1. **The new block.** The current variants of item 61's block (the `shipcraft` and `npx -- shipcraft` forms) change as follows. Every other line stays, in its order. Below, `<cmd>` is `shipcraft`, or `npx -- shipcraft` in the local variant, as item 61 writes every command.
   - After the line about writing the failing test first, a new line: `- Before writing a RED test, read the existing tests that cover the behavior you change, and never contradict them unless the change says that behavior changes. Passing control tests next to the failing ones are fine.`
   - The GREEN line's ending, `A test it finds wrong is reported back, and a RED session fixes it with \`<cmd> red --redo\`.`, becomes: `A test it finds wrong is reported back with the reason. The session the user talks to sets the GREEN's work aside (\`git stash push --include-untracked\`; it saved something only if \`git rev-parse -q --verify refs/stash\` changed, and \`git status --porcelain\` must then print nothing), starts a new RED session given the intent, the test and that report, never the GREEN's code, which fixes the test with \`<cmd> red --redo\` and commits; then, if something was saved and \`refs/stash\` still names it, it restores it with \`git stash pop\`; if nothing was saved, nothing is restored. If the GREEN already committed code after the RED commit, the work cannot be set aside, a step fails, or the restore conflicts, it stops, keeps the stash, and tells the user. If the repair would leave every locked test passing, or renames a test, \`red --redo\` refuses: report it to the user.`
   - After the line `A session told it is the RED or the GREEN ...`, a new line: `- Start each phase with the agent, model and effort that \`<cmd> status\` names on its roles line ([roles] red and green), when the project set them; if status refuses or that agent is not available, tell the user instead of choosing another. Each phase follows its brief in .shipcraft/briefs/ (red.md, green.md); where a brief is missing or disagrees with this block, this block wins.`
2. **Known blocks.** The two variants item 61 wrote (the exact bytes of both current variants at tag `v2.22.0`) join the known blocks, so `init` replaces them with the new block under item 61's rules. The 2.14.0 block stays known, and every other case is unchanged. `CLAUDE.md`'s block does not change.
3. **The phase briefs.**
   - **The files.** `init` writes two files, `.shipcraft/briefs/red.md` and `.shipcraft/briefs/green.md`. Their text is built into the binary, in a `shipcraft` and an `npx -- shipcraft` variant chosen as item 61 chooses the block's (`<cmd>` below). They use LF line endings and end with a newline. Their last line is `<!-- written by shipcraft X.Y.Z -->`, with the full package version, without the build hash.
   - **The RED brief says:**
     - write the failing tests from the intent, never from an implementation;
     - write two cases per rule, the ordinary one and a boundary a rushed GREEN would get wrong;
     - read the existing tests that cover the behavior the change touches first, and never contradict them unless the intent says that behavior changes;
     - each test written for the new behavior must fail for the right reason before `<cmd> red`; passing control tests are fine and are locked as controls;
     - fake external tools with their real output;
     - commit the tests, their stubs and the lock in one commit, and stop;
     - list ambiguities instead of picking a reading silently.
   - **The GREEN brief says:**
     - start from the RED commit;
     - never edit a locked file or a lock;
     - change product code, dependencies or config until the locked tests pass;
     - running the other tests the change touches is a check, not a gate: report a failure outside the locked tests, and let the hook's verdict decide the commit, since CI runs the rest;
     - stop and report a test that looks wrong, with the reason, instead of working around it;
     - commit.
   - **Writing them.** For each file:
     - absent: it is written;
     - a regular file whose bytes equal the built-in text of either variant: kept;
     - a regular file that reads as UTF-8 with other text: kept, with `shipcraft: .shipcraft/briefs/<name> differs from this version's, kept: compare it with docs/spec.md`. No marker in it is read, and nothing is appended;
     - a file that is not valid UTF-8, a symlink, an entry that is not a regular file, or a read failure: kept, with `shipcraft: .shipcraft/briefs/<name> could not be read, kept`.
   - **When.** The briefs are written only after everything else `init` writes has succeeded, `.shipcraft/locks` included. A failure before that point is today's failure: the same exit, the same rollback, and no brief written. A blocking `.shipcraft` file stays fatal, as items 25, 58, 61 and 67 require.
   - **The folder.** `.shipcraft` and `.shipcraft/briefs` are each checked with `lstat` and never followed. A symlinked `.shipcraft` that `init` accepted for the locks still gets no briefs. If either is a symlink or not a folder, a check fails with an error other than not-found, or it cannot be created, neither brief is written, and `init` prints `shipcraft: .shipcraft/briefs not written: <reason>`.
   - **A write failure.** Writing goes through a temporary file in the same folder, renamed into place. If writing a brief fails, the old file, if any, keeps its bytes; the temporary file and any partly written new file are removed; `init` prints `shipcraft: .shipcraft/briefs/<name> not written: <reason>` and goes on with the other brief.
   - **What never fails `init`.** No brief case above (the folder, a kept file, a write failure) changes `init`'s exit or rolls back what it wrote: it goes on, and exits as it would have.
   - **The closing guidance.** It names each brief `init` wrote, inside the existing `shipcraft: ready. Commit ...` line, after `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, red before green. No line is added, and the final CI line stays last.
   - **No special status.** The briefs are ordinary project files, versioned with the project. When a suite's globs select them, they are locked like any other selected file. This item changes no lock rule.
   - **`--lang other`** (item 67) writes them too.
4. **Touches.** These earlier items' cases cover what this item shares or changes.
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:**
     - item 61's expected block text and its new known-block upgrades (`v1-61.*`, `acceptance/lib/item61.sh`, `pontos/61.md`);
     - the untracked-files allowlist of `python::init-files`, `node::init-files` and `go::init-files` (`acceptance/run.sh`), which gains exactly the two brief paths;
     - the new-files allowlist of item 67's successful-init cases (`acceptance/lib/item67.sh`, from `v1-67.plain` to `v1-67.no-report-exit`), which gains exactly the two brief paths.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - `v1-2.5-agents-novo`, `v1-2.5-agents-acrescenta`, `v1-2.5-agents-uma-vez`, `v1-2.5-agents-link`;
     - item 64's `init-*` and `status-*` cases (the roles line the block points to);
     - `v1-27.mensagem-local`, `v1-27.mensagem-desligado`, `v1-27.mensagem-github` (the CI line stays last), and item 25's closing lines;
     - the rollback cases `v1-20.rollback`, `v1-25.husky.rollback`, `v1-25.pre.rollback`, `v1-61.refused-late`, `v1-67.rollback`, `v1-67.rollback-kept`, item 15a's (`v1-15a.1-*`) and item 58's `v1-58.rollback`, `v1-58.lefthook.rollback`, `v1-58.husky.rollback`, `v1-58.pre.rollback`: a refused `init` writes no brief;
     - item 67's refusal cases;
     - item 46's redo cases, which define the limits the block now names.
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:**
     - for an adopter, wrong locked tests fixed by a new RED session with the GREEN's work set aside, rather than by the session that saw the code, counted in draken-platform's next 5 items;
     - for a fresh agent, POC-021's "after" run: does it read the touched tests, and does it hand a wrong test to a new RED?
   - **Threshold:** in POC-021's after run, both behaviors are observed in at least 1 of 2 runs per agent.
   - **Revert:** a release drops the block's new lines and the briefs. Projects keep the files they have.
6. **Known limits.**
   - The battery checks the text written, not whether an agent follows it.
   - Setting work aside with `git stash` hides it from the new RED's checkout, but not from someone who goes looking in the stash. Ignored files (build output) stay in the checkout.
   - A GREEN that committed code before finding the wrong test cannot hand it to a new RED this way; the user decides.
   - A project that edited its block or its briefs keeps its own text.
   - Briefs written by an older version are not upgraded, because there is only one version so far.

### `init` is the required next step after install (item 69)

POC-021's after runs on 2.24.0 (2026-10-30): `init` ran in 3 of 4 runs, against 1 of 4 before item 61. In the fourth, Codex added the package with `pnpm add -D shipcraft`, checked it, reported "setup is available via pnpm exec shipcraft init", and stopped. Item 61's hint names `init` as a possibility, with two commands to choose from and an `npx` without `--` (fixed point 39). This item makes it one command, said as the required next step.

1. **The hint.** The conditions are item 61's, unchanged: `shipcraft --version` and the usage (no arguments, `help`, `-h`, `--help`), inside a git work tree whose root has no `.shipcraft.toml` (`lstat` fails with not-found). The root is read as today's hint reads it: the bytes of `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, less exactly one final newline, as a Unix path; a failed or empty output prints no hint. The line stays one line on stderr, after the usual output. Its text changes to:

   `shipcraft: next step (required): run `<command>` at the repository root to set up Shipcraft; until init runs, Shipcraft checks none of your commits; if init refuses, follow its Next line`

   with one pair of backticks around `<command>`, which is chosen as follows:
   - when the running binary is the project's install, by item 58's rule as `init` applies it (the binary and the root canonicalized; the binary under `<root>/node_modules/`, a pnpm store path under it included; `<root>/node_modules/.bin/shipcraft` an executable regular file, its link followed):
     - `pnpm exec shipcraft init` when `<root>/pnpm-lock.yaml` is a regular file (`lstat`, never followed, never read) and `pnpm` is found on `PATH` by the lookup `init` uses for review agents;
     - `npx -- shipcraft init` otherwise;
   - `shipcraft init` in every other case: a machine install, a binary inside a package's own `node_modules` or outside the root, or any failure to resolve the binary or the root.

   A failure while deciding whether the binary is the project's install selects the `shipcraft init` form. Once it is the project's install, a missing, non-regular or unreadable `pnpm-lock.yaml`, or `pnpm` not found (a lookup error counts as not found), selects the `npx -- shipcraft init` form. None of this prints a notice, creates a file or folder, runs a test, check, build or reviewer, or changes stdout or the exit code.
2. **No install script.** The npm packages keep running nothing at install time. pnpm runs no dependency scripts unless they are allowed, and `--ignore-scripts` is common, so a message printed from a script would reach only some installs. The hint and the docs are the touch points.
3. **Docs.**
   - The README's quick start says step 2 is required, right after step 1: `init` sets up Shipcraft and wires its hooks, and until it runs Shipcraft checks none of your commits. Its commands use `pnpm exec shipcraft init` or `npx -- shipcraft init`.
   - `npm/README.md` says the same, and its `npx` gains `--`. The npm package keeps carrying the root `README.md`, as `release/npm.sh` packages it today.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `--version` prints `shipcraft X.Y.Z (<hash>)` to stdout and exits 0: keep.
   - The usage prints to stdout and exits 0: keep.
   - The hint's conditions, its root reader, its one line on stderr after the output, no config read, no run-log row, and no file written: keep. Its text: change, as in point 1.
   - Outside a work tree, when git fails, or when `.shipcraft.toml` exists in any form (a symlink, a folder, an invalid file), no hint is printed: keep.
   - Invalid arguments print `shipcraft: error: invalid arguments: ...` with the usage, with no hint, and exit 2: keep.
   - The npm packages declare no install script: keep.
   - The Node launcher: it prints nothing of its own when the native binary runs; a missing platform package prints its platform diagnostic and exits 2; a spawn failure prints `could not run ...` and exits 2; a native signal is passed on, and any other status returned (a missing status exits 1): keep. The hint is printed by the native binary only, so a launcher that fails before running it prints no hint.
   - `init`'s own output, its refusals (item 67's `lang-unrecognized` and `lang-multiple` included), item 58's install detection and the commands the hooks call: keep.
5. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change:**
     - item 61's expected hint text (`HINT` in `acceptance/lib/item61.sh`) and its fixed point (`acceptance/pontos/61.md`), for `v1-61.hint-version`, `hint-noargs`, `hint-help`, `hint-h` and `hint-longhelp`. Their binary runs from outside `node_modules`, so the expected text is the `shipcraft init` form;
     - `v1-61.hint-outside`, which today checks that `HINT` is absent: it checks that neither the old text nor the new form is printed.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - `v1-61.hint-config`, `v1-61.hint-git-fails`;
     - the version line, read from `nucleo/Cargo.toml`: `v1-21.versao-fora`, `v1-21.versao-min-numerica`, `v1-62.version`, `v1-63.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-65.main` and `v1-65.many` (every profile), and item 66's cases (the shared `--version` check at the end of their driver);
     - item 58's install cases (`v1-58.*`): the same rule decides the install.
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** in POC-021's next after runs, every run executes `init` (4 of 4).
   - **Revert:** a release that restores item 61's text.
7. **Known limits.**
   - Yarn and Bun projects get the `npx -- shipcraft init` form, which also works there when Node is installed.
   - The `pnpm exec` form is chosen from a lockfile and a `PATH` lookup; it does not prove this contributor installed Shipcraft with pnpm.
   - In a layout plain `init` cannot recognize (item 67), the command starts setup and `init` refuses with the flags to add.
   - A binary installed inside a package's own `node_modules` gets `shipcraft init`, which may not be on `PATH`. `init` itself says how that install is wired.
   - An agent that never runs `--version` or `--help`, and never reads the README, is not reached.

### A path when a phase's session cannot be started (item 70)

POC-021's after runs on 2.25.0 (2026-10-31): Codex, running in its `workspace-write` sandbox, tried to open the RED session the block asks for with a nested `codex exec`, which failed with `Operation not permitted`, and stopped. Stopping was right: doing the RED in its own session would break the block's rule. But it stopped without telling the user how to go on, so the cycle ended there. This item adds the missing path to the block.

1. **The new line.** The current variants of item 68's block (the `shipcraft` and `npx -- shipcraft` forms) gain one line, right after the line that starts `- Start each phase with the agent, model and effort`. Every other line stays, byte for byte, in its order. In the machine variant the line is:

   ``- If you cannot start a phase's session (your tool cannot start one, or the start is refused, for example a sandbox refusing a nested `codex exec` with `Operation not permitted`), never do that phase yourself: stop and give the user the exact command to paste in a POSIX shell (sh, bash, zsh) where this project's tests run. It starts with `cd` to this checkout's absolute path, then starts a new session (an interactive one counts) with the agent, model and effort `shipcraft status` names for the phase (`shipcraft status` must exit 0; a GitHub lookup notice is fine), each flag left out when not set (none named: the agent you would have started; another agent: its own command and flags), and a prompt that names the phase, its brief and the change; for a wrong-test repair, the test, the GREEN's report and `shipcraft red --redo`, after the GREEN's work is set aside as above. Write each argument you fill in (the path, the model, the effort, the prompt) in single quotes, each `'` in it written `'\''`, for example `cd '<checkout>' && codex -m '<model>' -c 'model_reasoning_effort=<effort>' 'You are the RED session. Follow .shipcraft/briefs/red.md. The change: <intent>'` or `cd '<checkout>' && claude --model '<model>' --effort '<effort>' 'You are the GREEN session. Follow .shipcraft/briefs/green.md. The change: <intent>'`. That session still does its phase only. If the failed start may have begun the session (it printed output of its own, or a new lock or commit appeared), say so instead of giving a command. Continue only when the user says it finished and the phase's commit is on this branch; if it is not, tell the user.``

   The outer double backticks above only quote the line; the block holds it from `- If` to `tell the user.`, one line, ending with `\n`. `<checkout>`, `<model>`, `<effort>` and `<intent>` are literal text in the block, and `'\''` is those four characters. The local variant is built by item 61's rule unchanged: every `` `shipcraft `` becomes `` `npx -- shipcraft ``, so the line names `npx -- shipcraft status` and `npx -- shipcraft red --redo` there; `codex` and `claude` are not changed. Item 61's root line stays where that rule puts it.
2. **Known blocks.** The two current variants at tag `v2.25.0` (the exact bytes `init` 2.25.0 writes, machine and local) join the known blocks, so `init` replaces them with the new block under item 61's rules: kept bytes before and after, mode kept, the temporary-file write. The v2.22.0 variants and the 2.14.0 block stay known and are now upgraded to the new block. Every other case is unchanged (an edited block is kept with the differs notice). `CLAUDE.md`'s block does not change.
3. **The briefs do not change.** `.shipcraft/briefs/red.md` and `green.md` keep item 68's body and its rules. Their last line keeps naming the running version, as item 68 writes it, so a 2.26.0 `init` writes `<!-- written by shipcraft 2.26.0 -->` and keeps an unedited 2.25.0 brief with item 68's differs notice, as any version change does today. A session told it is the RED or the GREEN starts no other session, so the path belongs to the session the user talks to, which reads the block.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `init` creates `AGENTS.md` with the block, appends it once, replaces a known block, keeps an equal block with `already has the shipcraft block`, and keeps anything else with the differs notice; symlinks, non-regular files, invalid UTF-8 and read or write failures are kept with today's notices and never fail the init: keep. The block's text: change, as in point 1. The set of known blocks: change, as in point 2.
   - `CLAUDE.md`'s import block, when it is handled and the `CLAUDE.md not written` notice: keep. `CLAUDE.md` is still handled only when `AGENTS.md` holds the current block after `init`, which is now the new block.
   - The briefs: their paths, body, the version-derived last line, the byte comparison against this version's text, folder checks, notices and the rule that they never fail `init`: keep.
   - `shipcraft status`, which the new line asks the session to read: it reads the working-tree config, prints the branch, layers, roles, hooks and open-lock lines, reads the locks, asks GitHub about branch protection and prints its answer or a lookup notice; it starts no agent and runs no test; a config it cannot read refuses before the roles line; a lock folder or file it cannot read fails after the roles line with `shipcraft: error: ...` and exit 2, and a lock in a format it does not know refuses after the roles line with exit 1, the earlier lines already printed in both cases; a lock that does not parse is listed as open and status exits 0; it writes no file and no run-log row: keep.
   - The closing guidance (`shipcraft: ready. Commit ...` with `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and the briefs listed when written, the final CI line last): keep.
   - `init`'s exit codes, refusals and rollbacks (a refused `init` writes neither file nor any brief): keep.
   - `init`'s other output, the config it writes, the hooks and item 58's install detection: keep.
5. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** the expected block text, which gains the line of point 1 in both variants, in item 68's block cases (`v1-68.block`, `block-local`, `block-append`, `block-local-append`), its upgrade cases from v2.22.0 (`v1-68.upgrade-{bare,local}-{bare,local}`) and from 2.14.0 (`v1-68.legacy`, `legacy-local`), and item 61's exact-text checks in `v1-61.block` and `v1-61.local-block` (`acceptance/lib/item61.sh`, `acceptance/lib/item68.sh`, `pontos/61.md`, `pontos/68.md`).
   - **The version:** `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists read §11's contract, now 2.26.0, and the binary's version: the release raises `nucleo/Cargo.toml` to 2.26.0, and those cases keep their assertions.
   - **New cases:** upgrades from the v2.25.0 variants in both directions (bare and local input, bare and local install), keeping item 68's checks on prefix and suffix bytes, mode and the `CLAUDE.md` import.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-68.*` case (the briefs, their folder and write-failure cases, the closing lines, the `equal-*` cases, `core-*`); every other `v1-61.*` case; `v1-2.5-agents-novo`, `v1-2.5-agents-acrescenta`, `v1-2.5-agents-uma-vez`, `v1-2.5-agents-link`; `python::init-files`, `node::init-files`, `go::init-files`; item 67's successful-init cases and their allowlists; item 64's `init-*` and `status-*` cases; item 62's and item 5's status cases (`v1-62.status`, `v1-62.status-empty`, `v1-5.4-status-nivel-de-protecao`); item 25's closing-line cases (`v1-25.*`), which item 68 also kept; item 46's redo cases (`v1-46.*`), whose limits the line's repair path relies on; `v1-27.mensagem-local`, `v1-27.mensagem-desligado`, `v1-27.mensagem-github`; the rollback cases item 68 lists.
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** in POC-021's next after runs, no run stops without telling the user how to continue: every run that cannot start a phase's session gives the user a command to paste for that phase.
   - **Threshold:** 0 runs that stop silently, against 1 in the 2.25.0 after runs.
   - A run whose failed start may have begun the session counts as telling the user when it says so instead of giving a command.
   - **Revert:** a release drops the line. Its block joins the known blocks so `init` upgrades it back.
7. **Known limits.**
   - The battery checks the text written, not whether an agent follows it.
   - The example commands are examples: their flags belong to Codex and Claude Code and may change; the agent adapts them to what `status` names.
   - The user starts the phase by hand, so the session that asked cannot check the phase was run with the role `status` names.
   - The command carries the folder, not the shell environment (a virtualenv, a Node version manager, `GOWORK`): the line asks for a terminal where the project's tests run, and a wrong one makes the phase fail or refuse, not pass.
   - A project that edited its block keeps its own text.
### `red` and the commit run outside an agent's sandbox (item 74)

Our own items 70 to 73 (2026-10-31): the Codex RED ran in its `workspace-write` sandbox. There `shipcraft red` took 2919 s for 113 cases, against 42 to 213 s outside it. The sandbox forbids `nice`, and git could not write its index lock in a linked worktree, so the RED could not commit. Item 70's line covers a phase session that cannot be started, not a session that runs but sits in a sandbox. An adopter's sandboxed agent hits the same wall at `red` or at the commit, whose hook runs the locked tests. This item adds the path to the block.

1. **The new line.** The current variants of item 70's block (the `shipcraft` and `npx -- shipcraft` forms) gain one line, right after item 70's line (the one that starts `- If you cannot start a phase's session`). Every other line stays, byte for byte, in its order. In the machine variant the line is:

   ``- If you run in a sandbox (for example Codex's `workspace-write`), run `shipcraft red`, `shipcraft red --redo` and `git commit` (its hook runs the locked tests) outside it when you can: inside, they can take many times longer, or fail when git cannot write its index. If you cannot, finish your files and stop (your files: those you added, changed or deleted, both sides of a rename), and give the user the steps to run in a POSIX shell where this project's tests run, each argument you fill in written in single quotes as above: first `cd` to this checkout's absolute path; for a RED, then `shipcraft red` (for a repair, `shipcraft red --redo`), which prints the lock's path; then `git --literal-pathspecs add -f --` with each of your files as its own argument and, for a RED, that lock path; then `git commit -m` with the message. A GREEN gets only the `cd`, `git add` and `git commit` steps. Tell the user to start from the first step that has not succeeded yet, and to stop and tell you if a step fails. If one of your files already had other changes before your phase, or `git diff --cached --name-only -z` lists a staged path that is not one of yours (or fails), say so and name those paths instead of handing the steps over. Change nothing after handing them over. Your phase ends there: you start no other phase, and a commit alone does not mean the lock closed (`shipcraft status` says).``

   The outer double backticks only quote the line; the block holds it from `- If you run` to `(`shipcraft status` says).`, one line, ending with `\n`. The local variant is built by item 61's rule unchanged: every `` `shipcraft `` becomes `` `npx -- shipcraft ``, so the line names `npx -- shipcraft red` and `npx -- shipcraft red --redo` there; `git` is not changed. Item 61's root line stays where that rule puts it.
2. **Known blocks.** The two current variants of item 70's block join the known blocks: the exact bytes `init` writes at commit e642432 (item 70's merge, version 2.26.0, unchanged through 2.29.0), machine and local. `init` replaces them with the new block under item 61's rules: kept bytes before and after, mode kept, the temporary-file write. The v2.25.0, v2.22.0 and 2.14.0 blocks stay known and are now upgraded to the new block. Every other case is unchanged (an edited block is kept with the differs notice). `CLAUDE.md`'s block does not change.
3. **The briefs do not change.** `.shipcraft/briefs/red.md` and `green.md` keep item 68's body and rules, and their last line keeps naming the running version, as item 70 says.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14): item 70's list, point 4, unchanged in every entry, and the standing effects the spec-review brief lists (run-log rows, including the hook's row on every commit; lock files; the post-commit closing commit; `TMPDIR`, and a suite on a copy or in the working tree), and the hook's bookkeeping: `check --commit-msg` removes the previous per-worktree close mark, refuses `mark-failed` when it cannot write one, and writes a new mark for a passing lock; `close` can exit 0 while saying the lock did not close, keeping the mark for a manual retry, or report an index or tree update failure after HEAD moved; a lock ignored by `.gitignore` gets a warning that suggests `git add -f`, with the verdict unchanged. All keep: the line only tells an agent who runs the commands, and changes none of them. Two readings change:
   - the block's text: change, as in point 1;
   - the set of known blocks: change, as in point 2.
5. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** the expected current block text, which gains the line of point 1 in both variants, wherever an earlier case builds it:
     - item 61's exact-text checks (`v1-61.block`, `v1-61.local-block`);
     - item 68's block, upgrade and legacy cases (`v1-68.block`, `block-local`, `block-append`, `block-local-append`, `upgrade-{bare,local}-{bare,local}`, `legacy`, `legacy-local`);
     - item 70's block and upgrade cases (`v1-70.block-{bare,local}`, `upgrade-{bare,local}-{bare,local}`, `upgrade-brief-fails-{bare,local}`, `write-fails-{bare,local}`, `write-partial-{bare,local}`) and its fixed points.
     The RED adjusts only the expected text: `acceptance/lib/item61.sh`, `item68.sh`, `item70.sh` and their `pontos`. In `item70.sh` this includes `exact_block`'s structural check that the current block less item 70's line equals the v2.25.0 block: it now removes both item 70's and this item's line, and the v2.25.0 baseline stays as it is.
   - **The version:** `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists read §11's contract, now 2.30.0; the release raises `nucleo/Cargo.toml` to 2.30.0.
   - **New cases:** upgrades from item 70's variants in both directions (bare and local input, bare and local install), keeping item 68's and item 70's checks on prefix and suffix bytes, mode and the `CLAUDE.md` import.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-70.*` case (`edited-*`, `refused-*`, `status-*`), and everything item 70's Touches list keeps; item 71's and item 72's init cases, whose successful paths require `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and both briefs and whose rollback cases (`v1-71.rollback`, `v1-71.rollback-cache`, `v1-72.rollback-file`, `v1-72.rollback-hooks`) require no onboarding file after a core failure; item 17's close-mark cases (`v1-17.recusa-limpa-marca`, `v1-17.marca-falha`, `v1-17.worktree`, `v1-17.index-lock`, `v1-17.pos-ref-falha`), item 49's (`v1-49.post`, `v1-49.retry`, `v1-49.post-ref-failure`) and item 28's ignored-lock warnings (`v1-28.msg-gitignore`, `v1-28.msg-info`, `v1-28.msg-global`, `v1-28.msg-only-ignorado`).
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** in POC-021's next after runs, no sandboxed agent runs `shipcraft red` or a hooked commit inside its sandbox without saying so: each one runs them outside or hands the commands to the user.
   - **Threshold:** 0 RED phases over 600 s caused by a sandbox, in those runs and in our own next 5 items.
   - **Revert:** a release drops the line. Its block joins the known blocks so `init` upgrades it back.
7. **Known limits.**
   - The battery checks the text written, not whether an agent follows it.
   - Shipcraft does not detect a sandbox; the agent has to know it is in one.
   - A user who runs the handed-over commands is trusted to run them unchanged; the hook and `shipcraft/ci` still check the lock.
   - The steps are handed over as text: the user, not Shipcraft, decides which step already succeeded, reads the lock path `red` prints, and resolves a staged-content mismatch after a refused commit (a formatter that rewrote a file); the hook and `shipcraft/ci` still judge the result.
   - The session that hands the command over does not verify the commit; the session the user talks to checks it before the next phase, as item 70's line says.

### `report` with DORA (item 76)

Item 38's `report` measures the flow per lock from the run log alone. Three gaps show on this repository and on adopters:
- A lock merged through `gh` or the GitHub page has no `merge` row, so the report calls it `open` although its PR is on main.
- With `ci = "github"` there is no local `ci` row, so the waiting time is always empty.
- The report says nothing about releases, so DORA's keys live only in our private `metrics/dora.py`.

Victor's decision (2026-10-31): a local DORA readout in the product, from the run log, the git tags and the locks (item 75's fix data). A key without data says so with a count, and never guesses. Our private `dora.py` and `item-times.tsv` stay as they are. Reading CI and merge times from GitHub (`--github`) is a separate item (79): its spec review showed it needs its own readers (paginated checks, required-component completion, the config each PR was judged by). This item reads no network, and the waiting gap stays open here.

1. **What `report` reads.**
   - **The run log**, by item 38's rules, unchanged: found by walking up from the current folder (a `.git` file and `commondir` included), missing or unreadable read as empty, lossy UTF-8, BOM and CR stripped, blank lines skipped, `escape: true` rows ignored, gaps reported.
   - **Git**, through the `git` on `PATH`, read-only, from the repository the run log belongs to. Nothing is fetched:
     - every git call is `git -C <root>`, where `<root>` is the folder holding the `.git` (folder or file) the run log was found through, whatever the caller's folder; paths below are relative to that root;
     - every git call runs with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` in its environment, and without every repository-selecting variable `git rev-parse --local-env-vars` names (`GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY`, `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` and the rest), plus `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, so an inherited environment never selects another repository;
     - first, `git config --get-regexp '^(extensions\.partialclone|remote\..*\.promisor)$'`. A partial clone (where a promised object would be fetched from the remote) is any line for `extensions.partialclone` with a non-empty value, or any `remote.<name>.promisor` line whose value is `true` (git's boolean spellings: `true`, `yes`, `on`, `1`, any case). Recent git sets only the latter. Then no other git read runs, and every git-derived value is `null` with the reason `partial clone: report reads no network`. Exit 1 with no output means not a partial clone.
     - then `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository`. `true` (a shallow clone, where ancestry and tag history are incomplete) means no other git read runs, and every git-derived value is `null` with the reason `shallow clone: history incomplete`. `false` goes on; anything else is a failure.
     - *The default branch*: the first of `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`, `refs/remotes/origin/main`, `refs/heads/main`, `refs/heads/master` for which `git rev-parse --verify -q <ref>^{commit}` prints a commit. Its exit 1 with no output means that ref is absent and the next is tried; it is not a failure. None present: every git-derived value is `null` with the reason `no default branch`. The report names the ref used.
     - *Release tags*: `git for-each-ref refs/tags --format='%(refname:strip=2)'`, keeping names `v<X>.<Y>.<Z>` (decimal parts without leading zeros). For each, `git rev-parse --verify -q refs/tags/<name>^{commit}`: a commit printed is its commit; exit 1 with no output (a tag on a blob or tree, as an imported tag can be) means not a release and the tag is skipped, not a failure. A release is such a tag whose commit is an ancestor of the default branch (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`; exit 1 means not an ancestor, not a failure). A release's time, in Unix seconds:
       - its tagger date, from `git for-each-ref refs/tags/<name> --format='%(objecttype) %(taggerdate:unix)'`, when the object type is `tag` and the date is present;
       - else (a lightweight tag, or an annotated tag without a tagger line) its commit's committer date, from `git -c log.showSignature=false log --no-show-signature -1 --format=%ct <commit>` (no signature verifier is ever started);
       - output that is not a decimal integer is a failure.
     Releases are ordered by that time; ties by version, comparing major, then minor, then patch as decimal numbers of any length (no leading zeros, so the longer is larger, else byte order). Tag strings are never compared lexically.
     - *Lock files* at a commit: `git ls-tree <commit> -- .shipcraft/locks/` and `git cat-file blob <commit>:.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`. A path absent at a commit is absent, not a failure.
     - *A failure* (git not found, a non-zero exit other than the ref-absent, non-commit tag and ancestry "no" answers above, a missing object, unreadable output, a release without a readable time) makes every git-derived value `null` with the reason in the report's `git` field. The rest is item 38's. `report` still never refuses and exits 0.
   - **A lock file** is read only when it is a valid lock by this binary's rules (format 2, 3 or 4, seal included, item 75). An invalid one counts as unreadable for that lock, never as a feature.
2. **Lock states and fix data.**
   - **Lock rows.** One lock row per lock id found in the run log, in first-seen order as today, then one per lock file at the default branch's tip that the log does not name, in id byte order. The latter have no run-log times.
   - **The DORA population** is wider than the rows: every lock id found in the log, at the default branch's tip, or at any release tag. A lock removed after it shipped still counts for the release that shipped it.
   - **State.** `merged` when its `merge` row passed (as today). Otherwise `merge_not_observed` when its lock file is at the default branch's tip, else `open`. A `merge_not_observed` lock keeps empty local merge times, and the day rows count only `merged` locks, as today.
   - **Change kind.** `change_kind` is the `kind` of its valid lock file at the default branch's tip, else at the latest release tag that holds it, else at HEAD, else in the earliest passed non-redo `red` row (ties in file order). It is `null` when none says, or the lock read is format 2 or 3. `reported_at` and `fixes_release` come from the same source, else `null`.
3. **The DORA object.** `--json` prints one line `{"kind":"dora", ...}` after the `day` lines and before any `gap` line, also when the log is empty. The text output prints a `DORA` block after the days, with the same content. Durations are seconds. Medians and p90 are item 38's. The fields:
   - `"git"`: `{"default_branch": "<ref>" | null, "releases": <int> | null, "reason": null | "<line>"}`. `releases` is the repository total of release tags on the default branch, whatever `--since` says; the text block's coverage line and `deploys.n` give the releases read.
   - `"since"`: the `--since` tag, or `null`.
   - **The releases read:** every release tag, or with `report --since <tag>` those whose time is at or after that tag's. `--since`'s value is checked in this order:
     - not of the `v<X>.<Y>.<Z>` grammar above: the usage refusal, exit 2, before any git read;
     - git evidence unavailable (any of point 1's `null` reasons): the report prints as usual with `since` kept, every DORA metric `null` with that reason, exit 0;
     - git read, and the name is not among the release tags: `report: --since <tag> is not a release tag on <default branch>` on stderr, nothing on stdout, exit 2. A release *ships* the locks whose release it is, a lock's release being the earliest release by time whose tree holds its lock file (first appearance only). The *shipped locks* are those shipped by the releases read.
   - Every metric below is `{"value": ..., "n": <int>, "missing": <int>, "reason": null | "<line>"}`. `value` is `null` whenever `n` is 0, or a rule below says so, and `reason` then says why.
   - `"deploys"`: `value` is `{"count", "first", "last", "per_day"}`, where `per_day` = count ÷ ((last time − first time) / 86400), or `null` with fewer than 2 releases or a zero span. `n` is the releases read and `missing` is 0.
   - `"lead_time"`: `value` is `{"median", "p90"}` of release time − RED time, over the shipped locks with a passed non-redo `red` row (the earliest). `n` is those samples. `missing` counts the shipped locks with no such row, and samples where the release is not after the RED, with the reason.
   - `"rework_rate"`: `value` is the share (0 to 1) of the releases read that ship at least one lock and ship a lock with `change_kind: "fix"`. `n` is the releases read that ship at least one lock. `missing` is the shipped locks with `change_kind: null`; when above 0, `value` is `null`.
   - `"time_to_recover"`: `value` is `{"median", "p90"}` of release time − `reported_at` (item 75's grammar and offset), over the shipped `fix` locks that have one. `n` is those samples. `missing` counts shipped `fix` locks without `reported_at`, and non-positive samples.
   - `"change_failure_rate"`: `value` is the share of the releases read named by the `fixes_release` of some shipped `fix` lock. `n` is the releases read. `missing` counts the shipped locks with `change_kind: null`, plus the shipped `fix` locks whose `fixes_release` is `null` or not a release tag; when above 0, `value` is `null`. Everything here is scoped to the releases read: with `--since`, a lock shipped before it neither names a failure nor counts as missing.
   - **The coverage lines.** The text block opens with two lines. The first gives the run log's span (first and last row) and says it holds this machine's runs only. The second gives the default branch, the releases read (and `--since`), and that lock rows without log times come from the branch tip.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `report`'s run-log reader, discovery, gaps, `meta`, per-lock times, reviews, local waiting, hook and `ci` stats, day rows, lock order (first seen in the log), `--json` one object per line, and exit 0 always: keep.
   - `main.rs` accepting only `report` and `report --json`: change, to `report [--json] [--since <tag>]` in any order. Any other spelling, or a repeated flag, still prints invalid arguments with the usage and exits 2. A `--since` naming a tag that is not a release is a new exit 2, as point 3 says; `report` otherwise never refuses.
   - "No git" (`v1-38.offline`'s trap) and "an empty log prints only `meta`": change, as points 1 and 3 say. No network: keep.
   - `open` for a lock merged without a `merge` row: change, as point 2 says.
   - `report` writes nothing, appends no run-log row, and runs no test, agent or network call: keep.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists, met by the fixtures: keep.
5. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
     - `v1-38.offline`: its git trap may now be called; it still asserts no network and no write (the partial-clone case adds a real-git check of both);
     - `v1-38.empty`, `missing-log`, `escape-only` and `blank-only`: exactly two objects now, `meta` then `dora`;
     - the shared allowed-kind list and the kind-order assertion in `acceptance/lib/item38.sh` (used by `timeline` to `nonobject`): `meta`, `lock`, `day`, `dora`, `gap`, in that order;
     - `acceptance/pontos/38.md`'s "no git" and "only meta" readings;
     - any `v1-38.*` fixture whose lock file sits at the default branch's tip without a `merge` row, which now shows `merge_not_observed`. The RED lists each.
   - **The version:** 2.34.0 in §11 (2.32.0 was never released; item 80 took 2.33.0 on main first), `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-38.*` assertion; `v1-46.report-zero` and `v1-46.report-count`; item 54's reused-review cases; item 75's cases (`v1-75.*`); item 10's run-log check. No item 62 reader is used.
   - **Shared lock reader, kept:** item 43's validity and canonical-seal rules, which `report` now applies when it reads a lock (`v1-43.seal`, `rewrite`, `legacy-close`, `mixed`, `legacy-prefixed`, `open-bare`, `open-uppercase`, `open-short`, `open-prefix`, `open-whitespace`, `closed-bare`, `closed-uppercase`, `closed-short`, `closed-prefix`, `closed-whitespace`) keep passing unchanged. Only what is done with an invalid lock differs: the gate refuses, and `report` treats it as unreadable for that lock.
   - **New cases** use fixture repositories with real annotated and lightweight tags, a release line where a lock ships, format 3 and format 4 locks (feature and fix, with and without `reported_at` and `fixes_release`) (including a closed lock removed after its release), run logs written by the battery, a default branch reached through `origin/main` only and through local `main` only, `--since` (valid, malformed, not a release, git unavailable), a tag on a blob, an annotated tag without a tagger line, two releases in the same second (`v1.9.0` and `v1.10.0`), `report` run from a package subfolder (same output as at the root), `report` with `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` pointing at another repository (same output as without them), a signed commit with `log.showSignature=true` and a recording `gpg.program` that is never started, a real shallow clone (`--depth=1`) that gets no DORA readout, and a real partial clone (`git clone --filter=blob:none` from a local origin with `uploadpack.allowFilter`) where `report` fetches nothing and writes no object.
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** on this repository, `report --since <the first release all of whose shipped locks were red as format 4>` (likely v2.32.0; v2.31.0 still ships format 3 locks red before item 75) gives `deploys`, `lead_time`, `rework_rate` and `change_failure_rate` with `missing` 0 once a release after it ships, and each item's RED→release within one minute of `metrics/dora.py`.
   - **Revert:** a release that drops the DORA object, the git reads and `--since`, and restores item 38's `open`.
7. **Known limits.**
   - One release line: tags are `vX.Y.Z` at the repository root. Monorepo or package tags (`@acme/api@2.1.0`, `services/api/v1.2.0`) are not read, and a backport to an older line is counted at its own tag time.
   - A tag's time is when it was tagged (or committed), not when it was deployed.
   - Locks from before item 75 have no kind, so `rework_rate` and `change_failure_rate` read `null` while any of them ships within the releases read; `--since` starts the window after them.
   - The waiting time stays empty for `ci = "github"` projects until item 79.
   - A run log covers one machine: locks red elsewhere have no `red` time here.
   - A stale local default branch makes merged locks look `open`; nothing is fetched.
   - A partial or shallow clone gets no DORA readout; CI checkouts are usually shallow, so `report` is for a full local clone.

8. **Open, no case (the GREEN's audit, 2026-10-31).** Readings the spec leaves open; the code's choice stands, and no case pins it:
   - A `red` row's `kind`, `reported_at` and `fixes_release` are read one by one: a hand-written row with an invalid `kind` but a valid `reported_at` gives `change_kind: null` with that `reported_at`. `red` never writes such rows.
   - JSON `git.releases` is the repository total; the text coverage line counts the releases read (see point 3).
   - `default_branch` is the full ref name (`refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`), also in the `--since` refusal.
   - With no `.git` found, the reason is `no git repository found`.
   - An invalid lock file at the tip still makes a lock row and `merge_not_observed`; only its fix data falls through to the next source.
   - `--since` reads by time: a release tagged in the same second as the `--since` tag is read even when it sorts before it.
   - A `red` row without a numeric `at` is ignored as a source of fix data and of lead time.
   - A negative tag or commit time is accepted as a decimal integer.
   - `extensions.partialclone` set with no value is not a partial clone.
   - The cleared environment variables are a fixed list (today's `git rev-parse --local-env-vars` plus `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`), not read at run time.
### `red` warns when it locks on the default branch (item 100)

Found on Hands-off on 2026-11-09 (POC-044, item 1). The session ran `red` and committed the RED on `main`, then the GREEN. At the end, `shipcraft pr` refused `pr-not-on-branch`. The work was finished but stuck, and moving it to a branch took a coordinator. `red` is the item's first step and already knows the branch, so the warning belongs there. Victor's decision (2026-11-09): "Ok", to applying this directly.

1. **The warning.** After `red` writes a lock (not on a refusal), `red` prints one line on stdout when all of these hold:
   - HEAD is the default branch: `git symbolic-ref -q --short HEAD` equals the branch `pr` refuses on, which is the same value as `pr`'s default-branch lookup: the target of `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` without `origin/`, or `main` when that ref is absent or unreadable. That fallback is not a failure.
   - A remote named `origin` exists: `git remote get-url origin` succeeds. Without it `pr` cannot run.
   - The config `red` already loaded has an effective `[execution] ci` or `[execution] review` other than `off`. Defaults count: a config with no `[execution]` table has both `local`. `red`'s existing config validation and refusals are unchanged.

   The line goes **right before the `next:` line**, which stays `red`'s last line (item 64). Item 98's controls line stays right before `RED locked at`:
   `shipcraft: warning: this lock is on <branch>, the default branch; shipcraft pr refuses to open a PR from it. Move the RED to its own branch before the GREEN (git switch -c item/<name>).`
   - It is a warning only: exit code, lock, run-log row and every other line are unchanged.
   - The same applies to `red --redo` when it rewrites the lock: the line again goes right before `next:`. An unchanged redo prints nothing new.
   - With both layers `off`, nothing is printed: such a project commits on its default branch and never runs `pr`.
   - On a detached HEAD (`symbolic-ref` exits non-zero), or with no `origin` remote (`get-url` exits non-zero), nothing is printed. The check never fails `red` and never changes its exit code.
2. **`pr`'s detached-HEAD text in English.** `pr-not-on-branch` on a detached HEAD says `shipcraft pr needs a working branch, and HEAD is at nenhum branch`, a leftover from before the English rewrite (2026-10-26). That first line becomes exactly `shipcraft pr needs a working branch, and HEAD is detached (no branch)`. The text on a named branch, the `Why:` and `Next:` lines, the code `pr-not-on-branch` and the exit status are unchanged.
3. **Current behavior** (rule 14):
   - `red` prints nothing about the branch: change, as point 1 says.
   - Everything else `red` and `red --redo` do: keep. That covers:
     - the lock file as written today: `red` writes the lock path directly and creates the lock directory, and `red --redo` replaces it through a temporary file and a rename;
     - the run-log row, the run-log directory's creation, and the logging-failure behavior;
     - `[build]`, run before the suites, with its output;
     - the temporary selection and JUnit files and their cleanup, under the inherited `TMPDIR`;
     - every other stdout and stderr line, in its present order, item 98's controls line right before `RED locked at`/`RED redone at` included;
     - the exit codes and every refusal.
   - `pr`: its fetch, its run-log rows, its refusal on a named default branch, and everything after the branch check: keep. Only point 2's detached-HEAD text changes.
   - Hooks (`check`, `close`, post-commit) are untouched.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** the cases of items 53, 55, 60 and 98 that check `red`'s lines, item 98's `notice()` adjacency included, and item 64's last-line cases `v1-64.red-unset`, `red-set`, `redo-unset`, `redo-set` and `red-log-fails` (`next:` stays last). Most fixtures run `red` with no `origin`, so they print no warning. A fixture with an `origin` and HEAD on its default branch now gets the line at the end. The RED searches `acceptance/` for cases that assert `red`'s whole stdout, its line count, or the line right before `next:`, and lists each one it adjusts.
   - `pr-not-on-branch` has no acceptance case today, so point 2 adds one.
   - **The version:** 2.50.0 in §11, and `v1-87.version` with the version cases item 69 lists.
   - **New cases**, about 10: each condition of point 1 alone (default branch with origin and ci local; review local with ci off; both off; a non-default branch; detached HEAD; no origin; `origin/HEAD` naming a branch other than `main`), redo rewrite and unchanged redo, the line right before `next:` with `next:` still last, item 98's controls line still right before `RED locked at`, exit code and lock unchanged, and point 2's detached-HEAD text.
5. **Known limits.** `red` reads the working tree's config and fetches nothing, while `pr` fetches and judges by the base branch's config. So a stale `origin/HEAD`, or a working-tree config whose layers differ from the base's, can make the warning and `pr` disagree. The warning is a hint, and `pr`'s refusal stays the gate.
6. **Measure and revert.** Metric: 0 `pr-not-on-branch` refusals after a `red` that passed, on adopters' run logs (Hands-off, Draken, draken-platform), over the next 20 locks. Revert: drop the line.

### `report` reads the project's release tags (item 99)

Item 76's DORA readout counts only `vX.Y.Z` tags as releases. Adopters measured on 2026-11-09:
- Hands-off deploys on its own runner and tags each deploy `deploy-<date>-<time>`.
- draken-platform tags `draken-platform-v0.1.0`.
- Draken does not tag at all.

On all three, every DORA metric reads `n 0`. The project, not Shipcraft, knows what its release tag looks like. Victor's decision (2026-11-09): "Vamos fazer isso?", answering the proposal that `report` read each project's release tag pattern from `.shipcraft.toml`.

1. **The key.** `[report] release_tags = "<pattern>"` in `.shipcraft.toml`.
   - **Default:** with the key absent, the `[report]` table absent, or no `.shipcraft.toml` at the root, releases are item 76's `v<X>.<Y>.<Z>` tags, unchanged.
   - **Pattern grammar:**
     - a non-empty string of printable ASCII (bytes 0x21 to 0x7E);
     - with no `?`, `[`, `]` or `\`;
     - holding at least one byte other than `*`.
   - **Matching:** `*` matches any run of bytes, empty included, `/` included. Every other byte matches itself. The whole tag name, as `git for-each-ref refs/tags --format='%(refname:strip=2)'` prints it, must match. Examples:
     - `deploy-*` matches `deploy-20261107-1730`, not `deployed/prod`;
     - `draken-platform-v*` matches `draken-platform-v0.1.0`;
     - `v*` matches `v2.48.0` and `v2`.
   - **Validation:** any other value, or a value that is not a string, is refused `config-invalid` wherever the config is read and validated today: hook, `ci`, `red`, `pr`, `merge`, `status`. The refusal names the key and what is wrong. Other keys in `[report]` are refused the same way, so a typo is not ignored.
2. **What `report` does with it.**
   - **Reading the config.** `report` reads only the `[report]` table of `<root>/.shipcraft.toml`, where `<root>` is item 76's folder, from the working tree. It never refuses on the config:
     - The default reading (item 76's `vX.Y.Z`, exactly as today) applies when any of these holds:
       - the path is absent;
       - it is not a regular file or cannot be read;
       - it is not UTF-8;
       - it does not parse as TOML;
       - it has no `[report]` table;
       - it has a `[report]` table with no keys at all.

       Nothing here is reported, so `v1-76.invalid-config` keeps its successful git evidence. The hook and the other commands refuse such a file anyway.
     - A file that parses and has a `[report]` that is not a table, holds a key other than `release_tags`, or holds an invalid `release_tags` makes every git-derived value `null`, with the reason `[report] in .shipcraft.toml is invalid: <problem>`. The run-log part prints as usual and the exit is 0, as with item 76's git failures. `<problem>` is exactly one of these, the first that applies in this order:
       - `[report] is not a table`;
       - `unknown key <key>`, naming the first unknown key in byte order;
       - `release_tags is not a string`;
       - `release_tags is empty`;
       - `release_tags has a byte outside 0x21-0x7E`;
       - `release_tags has ?, [, ] or \`;
       - `release_tags is only *`.
     - Every other key of the file (suites, `shipcraft_min`, `[execution]`) is not read by this reader.
   - **Release tags.** With the key set, a release is a tag whose name matches the pattern, whose `^{commit}` is a commit, and whose commit is on the default branch: item 76's reads, unchanged except the name filter. A matching tag on a blob or tree is skipped, as today. The time is item 76's (tagger date, else committer date).
   - **Tag names stay exact.** Git refuses `*`, `?` and `[` in ref names (`git check-ref-format`), and a tag `a` and a tag `a/b` cannot coexist. So item 76's per-tag reads (`rev-parse`, and `for-each-ref refs/tags/<name>` for the tagger date) select exactly one tag, under any pattern.
   - **Order.** Releases are ordered by time, ties by tag name in byte order. Item 76's version tie-break applies only without the key.
   - **`--since <tag>`.**
     - With a valid `release_tags`: before any git read, the grammar check is the pattern. A name that does not match is the usage refusal, exit 2. A name that matches but is not a release is item 76's `not a release tag` refusal, exit 2.
     - With the default reading, whatever caused it (an absent, unreadable or unparseable file included): item 76's `v<X>.<Y>.<Z>` check and git evidence, unchanged.
     - With an invalid `[report]`: `--since` is not checked against any grammar. The report prints with `since` kept and every DORA metric `null` with point 2's reason, exit 0, as item 76 does when git evidence is unavailable.
   - **`fixes_release` is unchanged:** item 75's lock field and `red --fixes-release` still take only `v<X>.<Y>.<Z>`. Item 76's `change_failure_rate` code is kept as it is:
     - a shipped `fix` lock counts in `missing` when its `fixes_release` is `null` or names no release of the repository, all releases under the active selection and not only the releases read;
     - the numerator counts the releases read that are named.

     So `v1-76.since`, `since-order` and `failure-scoped` are unchanged. Example: with `release_tags = "v*"`, a fix naming the release `v1.0.0` is a measured failure. With `deploy-*`, a fix's `vX.Y.Z` names no release and counts as missing.
3. **What the report says.**
   - The `git` object gains `"release_tags"`. Keys serialize in byte order as today, so the object reads `default_branch`, `reason`, `release_tags`, `releases`. Its value is the pattern string when `release_tags` is valid, else `null`: the default reading and an invalid `[report]` both give `null`, and in the latter case `reason` says why. The field is present on every `dora` line, also when git made the rest `null`.
   - The text block's second coverage line ends with `; release tags: <pattern>` when `release_tags` is valid, or `; release tags: vX.Y.Z` otherwise (the default reading, or an invalid `[report]`, whose reason the same line already shows), replacing its final period.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - Every item 76 rule without the key: keep. Release reads, order, `--since`, every metric, coverage lines, partial and shallow clones, the environment scrubbing, exit codes.
   - `report` without `--github` reads no `.shipcraft.toml` today: change, as point 2 says, reading only `[report]`. It still writes nothing, appends no run-log row, and runs no test, agent or network call: keep.
   - `report --github` (item 79, `relatorio_gh.rs`) already reads and validates the whole working-tree config for its GitHub enrichment, and gives selected locks `config unreadable` when that fails: keep. Since an unparseable file leaves item 76's evidence as it is (point 2), `v1-79.unobserved-config` keeps its selection and reason. With an invalid `[report]`, that whole-config validation now fails too (point 1), so the locks `--github` selects get its existing `config unreadable`. DORA is `null` in that case anyway, so only `merged` locks are selected.
   - The config reader ignores unknown tables, so `[report]` is silently accepted today: change. `[report]` is validated (point 1). Any other unknown table or key: keep as today.
   - An older binary reading a config with `[report]` ignores it, so no `shipcraft_min` raise is needed: keep.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
5. **Touches.**
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - every `v1-76.*` case, `v1-76.invalid-config` included (point 2's default reading);
     - every `v1-79.*` case, `v1-79.unobserved-config` included;
     - every `v1-38.*` case;
     - `v1-75.*`;
     - the config refusal cases of items 31 and 45, and E1's legacy-key cases;
     - item 10's run-log check.
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:** item 83's byte snapshots `v1-83.json-local`, `json-release`, `json-github`, `json-metrics` and `json-fraction`, and their fixed points in `acceptance/pontos/83.md`. Each gains `"release_tags":null` in the `git` object, between `reason` and `releases`. The RED lists every other case that compares the whole `git` object byte for byte.
   - **The version:** 2.49.0 in §11, `v1-87.version` and the version cases item 69 lists.
   - **New cases**, at most 40, in item 76's fixture style (real repositories, annotated and lightweight tags, battery-written run logs):
     - `deploy-*` releases giving `deploys`, `lead_time` and `rework_rate` with `missing` 0;
     - a prefixed line (`draken-platform-v*`) beside plain `v1.0.0` tags, which the pattern excludes;
     - a matching tag off the default branch, and one on a blob, both skipped;
     - two matching tags in the same second, ordered by name;
     - a pattern that matches a `/` name;
     - `--since` matching and a release, matching but not a release (exit 2), and not matching (usage, exit 2);
     - each invalid value refused `config-invalid` by the hook: empty, only `*`, `?`, `[`, `\`, a space, a non-string, and an unknown `[report]` key;
     - `report` with each invalid `[report]`: DORA `null` with point 2's exact reason, exit 0;
     - `report` with a config that is not TOML: item 76's default reading, exit 0;
     - `[report]` with only the typo `release_tag = "deploy-*"`: `unknown key release_tag`, not the default reading;
     - `release_tags` in the JSON `git` object and in the coverage line, with and without the key;
     - under `deploy-*`, a shipped `fix` lock counted in `change_failure_rate.missing`; under `v*`, a fix naming a release counted as a failure.
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** on Hands-off, `report` with `release_tags = "deploy-*"` gives `deploys` and `lead_time` with `missing` 0 once a deploy tag ships a lock. On draken-platform, `release_tags = "draken-platform-v*"` counts its 1 release. Read on the first release that carries this item, in each repo that sets the key.
   - **Revert:** a release where `report` ignores `[report]` again and the config reader stops validating it. A project that set the key then gets item 76's `vX.Y.Z` reading back, with no refusal.
7. **Known limits.**
   - One pattern per project. A monorepo with one release line per package reads them all as one line.
   - `fixes_release` still takes only `vX.Y.Z`. Under a pattern that `vX.Y.Z` names cannot match (`deploy-*`), `change_failure_rate` is `null` while a shipped `fix` lock exists. Lifting that changes the lock grammar and is a separate item.
   - A tag's time is when it was tagged, as in item 76. A project whose tag is written after the deploy finishes gets the deploy time.

### Fix data in the lock (item 75)

DORA's change failure rate and time to recover need two facts per item: whether it fixes behavior a release already shipped, when the defect was first reported, and which release shipped it. Our private `metrics/item-times.tsv` holds them as hand-typed `kind` and `reported_at` columns, which an adopter does not have and which are often approximate. Victor's decision (2026-10-31): record them where the cycle starts, in the lock, for every RED, so no data is lost from here on. Item 76's `report` reads them. The release that introduced a defect is given by the RED, from the spec's wrong-verdict note (`--fixes-release`). Finding it by running the tests against earlier tags is a separate, parked item (77): its spec review (2026-10-31) showed it needs decisions this item does not take, such as which locked files are tests, how a tag's dependencies are prepared, and which release series counts.

1. **`red --fix [--reported-at <time>] [--fixes-release <tag>]`.**
   - **The options.** `shipcraft red` accepts `--fix`, and `--reported-at <time>` and `--fixes-release <tag>` only together with `--fix`; each at most once, in any order with today's `--base` and `--all`. `--redo` takes none of them: a redo keeps the lock's own fix fields. `<tag>` is `v` followed by `X.Y.Z`, each part a decimal number without leading zeros (`v2.25.0`); it is recorded as given, and its existence is not checked. A repeated option, `--reported-at` or `--fixes-release` without `--fix`, any of the three with `--redo`, a missing value, or a value outside the grammar below take today's invalid-arguments path (`shipcraft: error: invalid arguments: ...` with the usage, exit 2), before anything runs, is written or is logged.
   - **The time grammar.** `<time>` is `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM` or `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS`, then `Z` or `+HH:MM` or `-HH:MM`. It is ASCII digits only, with an uppercase `T` and `Z`. It must be a real calendar date (leap years by the Gregorian rule), with hours 00–23, minutes 00–59, seconds 00–59 and offset hours 00–23. There are no fractional seconds and no leap second, and `-00:00` is accepted like `+00:00`. This is Shipcraft's own grammar, close to RFC 3339 (which requires seconds), and the same check reads it back from a lock.
   - **Lock format 4.** Every `red` now writes a format 4 lock: format 3's fields and rules, plus, after `failing_at_red` and before `seal`, in this order:
     - `kind`: `"fix"` with `--fix`, else `"feature"`;
     - `reported_at`: the `<time>` exactly as given, or `null`;
     - `fixes_release`: the `<tag>` exactly as given, or `null`.

     A `feature` lock has `reported_at` and `fixes_release` both `null`. The seal covers the three fields like every other field, in that fixed order.
   - **Judging, closing and redoing.** A format 4 lock is judged, closed and redone like a format 3 lock. Closing (`close`, and `check` when it closes) changes only `state` and `seal`. `red --redo` on a format 4 lock writes format 4 with the same three fields. `red --redo` on a format 2 or 3 lock writes format 3, exactly as today's binary does (it already upgrades format 2 to format 3, item 46), with no fix field: a cycle opened before this item keeps an unknown kind and is never guessed as a feature. This supersedes item 46's "the current format" only for those legacy locks.
   - **Reading.** This binary reads formats 2, 3 and 4, each lock by its own `format`.
     - In format 4, `kind` must be `"feature"` or `"fix"`; `reported_at` `null` or a string the grammar above accepts; `fixes_release` `null` or a string the tag syntax above accepts; and both are `null` when `kind` is `"feature"`. A missing field, another value, or a key that format 4 does not name makes it not a valid lock, in the same way as a lock whose seal is broken: every reader handles it exactly as it handles such a lock today, so the hook and CI refuse it as `lock-edited`, while `red`, `status` and `init` treat it as they treat a hand-edited lock now.
     - A format 2 or 3 lock that holds a `kind`, `reported_at` or `fixes_release` key, even `null`, is not a valid lock in the same way. Formats 2 and 3 carry no kind: readers that need one (item 76) treat it as unknown. Their other reading rules are unchanged.
     - An older binary refuses a format 4 lock with `lock-format`, as item 43 says for format 3. Binaries up to 2.30 word that refusal as "only knows formats 2 and 3" with a Next to rerun `red`; that text cannot change, so the 2.31.0 release notes say that every lock it writes needs Shipcraft 2.31 or newer and that a team upgrades its pinned version together.
     - From this item on, a lock whose format is above the highest this binary knows is refused `lock-format` with: `lock <path> needs a newer shipcraft (format <n>; this one knows formats 2 to 4)`, Why `a lock written by a newer shipcraft cannot be judged by an older one`, and Next `upgrade shipcraft to the version the project pins (package.json, or shipcraft_min), then run the command again`. A format that is not a positive integer, or below 2, keeps today's wording.
   - **The run log.** Every `red` row of a non-redo invocation, passed or refused, carries `kind` (`"fix"` with `--fix`, else `"feature"`), `reported_at` and `fixes_release` (as given, or `null`). An invocation refused as invalid arguments writes no row, as today. `--redo` rows carry none of them.
2. **The block.** The current variants of item 74's block gain one line, right after the line that starts `- A behavior change: write the failing test first`. In the machine variant:

   ``- When the change fixes behavior a release already shipped, the RED runs `shipcraft red --fix`, with `--reported-at <time>` when the time the defect was first reported is known (for example `2026-10-31T00:36-03:00`) and `--fixes-release <tag>` when the release that shipped it is known (for example `v2.25.0`), so the lock records them.``

   One line ending with `\n`; `<time>` is literal text. The local variant is built by item 61's rule, so it reads `npx -- shipcraft red --fix` there. Item 74's block joins the known blocks: both variants, exactly as `init` writes them at item 74's merge (`f4220f1`, version 2.30.0). `init` upgrades it, and the older known blocks stay known and upgrade to the new block.
3. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `red` without `--fix`: its runs, narrowing (items 55, 65, 66), refusals and output (with the GREEN guidance as its last stdout line, item 64), row and exit codes: keep. Its lock: change, from format 3 to format 4 with `kind: "feature"`.
   - `red`'s options parser and its invalid-arguments path: keep, with the two new options.
   - Reading formats 2 and 3, the seal, `lock-format` (its wording for a format above the known ones: change, as point 1 says), `lock-edited`, closing (only `state` and `seal`), `red --redo` (item 46) and its refusals: keep, extended to format 4 as point 1 says.
   - The hook and `check`: keep, extended to format 4 like format 3. This covers the per-worktree close mark (an old one removed, `mark-failed` when it cannot be written, a new one for a passing lock), a `check` that writes and stages a closed lock, and the warnings for a lock outside the commit or ignored by git.
   - `close`: keep, extended to format 4 like format 3. It can exit 0 without closing and keep the mark for a retry, or move HEAD and then report a failed index or tree update. It keeps a working-tree lock edited since judging.
   - `ci`, `pr`, `review` and `merge`: keep; they read a format 4 lock like a format 3 one, including the docs-only shortcut an open lock prevents (item 40), a closed lock admitted only through a proved squash (item 42), and suite checks (item 62).
   - `status`: keep. It prints the layers, roles and hook lines before it reads the locks; an unknown format refuses after them; lock JSON that does not parse is listed as open, with exit 0; a lock folder it cannot read exits 2. It runs no project command, writes no file and logs no row.
   - `init`'s duplicate-integration refusal, which adds "close the open lock" when HEAD's lock is open or unreadable: keep.
   - `shipcraft report` (item 38): keep; the two new `red` row fields are extra keys it does not read.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
   - `init`'s block handling: keep, with point 2's new line and known block.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** the expected current block text, which gains point 2's line, wherever an earlier case builds it. That is item 61's `v1-61.block` and `v1-61.local-block`, item 68's block, upgrade and legacy cases, item 70's block and upgrade cases, and item 74's block, append, upgrade, brief-fails, write-fails and write-partial cases, with their `pontos`. It also covers the structural checks of `item70.sh` and `item74.sh`, which now also remove this item's line. Historical fixture bytes and the mode, prefix and suffix checks stay.
   - **Assertions that change, for the new lock and row shape, and nothing else in them:**
     - `v1-9.2-formato-da-trava`: the generated lock's format becomes 4. Its legacy and unknown-format refusal checks stay.
     - item 43's helper `i43_json` and the cases that use it (`v1-43.*`): a lock this binary writes is format 4, with a seal over the nine fields. A fixture built to be format 2 or 3 is built without the three new keys, so each digest or seal refusal is still caused by what the case is testing.
     - item 46's `draken`, `lists` and `log-fails` (through `i43_json`) and `passes-hook`, whose hand reseal now covers the nine fields, keeping its `red-already-passes` refusal.
     - item 59's `state()` and `event()` key sets (`v1-59.files*`, `v1-59.suites*`, `v1-59.stderr-closed`, `v1-59.ci*`) gain exactly the three new keys on a non-redo `red` lock and row.
     - item 65's exact non-redo `red` row key set (`v1-65.*`) and item 66's exact non-redo row and lock shapes (`v1-66.*`) gain exactly the three new keys with their values.

     Redo row shapes, selection, hashes, case counts, refusal precedence and row counts stay as they are.
   - **The version:** 2.31.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - `v1-30.2-antiga-hook` and `v1-30.2-antiga-ci`, and every assertion of the cases above other than the shapes named there;
     - item 53's second run (`v1-53.*`), item 55's and 60's `red` cases, and every other item 46 case;
     - item 64's `red-unset`, `red-set`, `redo-unset`, `redo-set` and `red-log-fails`, where the GREEN guidance stays the last stdout line;
     - item 17's close-mark cases (`v1-17.recusa-limpa-marca`, `marca-falha`, `worktree`, `index-lock`, `pos-ref-falha`), item 49's `post`, `retry` and `post-ref-failure`, and item 28's `msg-gitignore`, `msg-info`, `msg-global` and `msg-only-ignorado`;
     - `v1-20.trava`, `v1-40.trava-aberta`, item 42's `closed-ok`, `closed-no-review-waived`, `closed-no-ci`, `closed-no-review`, `closed-waiver-no-ci`, `closed-direct`, `closed-offline`, and item 62's `ci-open` and `ci-sealed`;
     - item 38's report cases (`v1-38.*`) and item 10's run-log check (`v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`), which accept extra row keys;
     - every other `v1-61.*`, `v1-68.*`, `v1-70.*` and `v1-74.*` case.
   - **New cases** include:
     - a format 4 lock written by plain `red` (`kind: "feature"`) and by `red --fix`, with and without `--reported-at` and `--fixes-release`, then read, judged, closed and redone by this binary;
     - a format 3 lock and a format 2 lock opened before this item, each redone by this binary into format 3, with no fix field;
     - the new `lock-format` wording for a format 5 lock;
     - the grammar's boundaries;
     - a format 4 lock with a missing, wrong-typed or extra key refused `lock-edited`, and a format 3 lock holding `kind` refused the same way;
     - the `red` row fields on a passed and on a refused `--fix` run.
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** every lock written from 2.31.0 on carries `kind`; of our own next 5 fix items, at least 4 also carry `reported_at` and `fixes_release`, with no hand-typed TSV value needed for item 76's report.
   - **Revert:** a release where `red` writes format 3 again and `--fix` is an invalid argument. Locks already written in format 4 stay readable.
6. **Known limits.**
   - Nothing checks that a `--fix` item really fixes a shipped defect, that `reported_at` is true, or that `fixes_release` names a real tag; they are the RED's statement, sealed in the lock.
   - A team must upgrade together: a binary older than 2.31 refuses every new lock (`lock-format`), with its old wording.
   - Locks written before this item carry no fix data; item 76 counts them as missing data, never as features.
   - Only the `red --redo` command keeps the fix fields. The hook and CI do not check that a redo commit's lock keeps them: a hand-generated lock that drops or changes them is judged by item 46's rules alone.

### `fixes_release` names the project's own release tags (item 105)

Found in the field (Hands-off, 2026-11-12, binary 2.53.0): Hands-off releases are tags like `deploy-20261112-200036`, read through `[report] release_tags = "deploy-*"` (item 99). `shipcraft red --fix --fixes-release deploy-20261112-200036` refuses with `invalid arguments` and no reason, so its fix locks carry `fixes_release: null` and `report`'s change failure rate stays `null` there. This is item 99's known limit ("`fixes_release` still takes only `vX.Y.Z` ... a separate item"), not a defect of a release: item 99 shipped it on purpose (v2.50.0). This item lifts it. Item 75's grammar for `vX.Y.Z` projects does not change.

1. **The tag name grammar.** A *tag name* is Shipcraft's own lexical grammar, checked on the string alone (git is never consulted): at least 1 byte, every byte in `0x21`-`0x7E`, none of `~`, `^`, `:`, `?`, `*`, `[`, `\`; it does not start with `-`, is not `@`, holds no `..` and no `@{`, does not start or end with `/` and holds no `//`, does not end with `.`, and no `/`-separated component starts with `.` or ends in `.lock`. There is no length limit. Every string of item 75's tag syntax (`v` and `X.Y.Z`, no leading zeros, any number of digits) is a tag name. A tag git accepts outside this grammar (non-ASCII bytes, for example) cannot be named.
2. **`red --fix --fixes-release <tag>`.**
   - A `<tag>` that is not a tag name takes item 75's invalid-arguments path, as today: before anything runs, is read, is written or is logged.
   - **The selection check** runs right after the arguments parse and before anything else `red` does today (before it loads and validates the rest of the config, before the open-lock, install and suite checks): `red` reads the release selection with item 99's reader of `.shipcraft.toml` in the working tree and its three outcomes (a missing, unreadable or unparseable file is the default reading). The tag must be a release name of that selection: under a valid `release_tags`, it matches the pattern (item 99's matcher); under the default reading, it is of item 75's tag syntax; under an invalid `[report]`, no tag is accepted. A tag name that fails refuses, exit 2, with no row and nothing written, with these three lines on stderr:
     - `shipcraft: --fixes-release <tag> is not a release tag of this project (release tags: <pattern>)`, where `<pattern>` is the pattern, or `vX.Y.Z` under the default reading, or `invalid: <item 99's reason>` under an invalid `[report]`;
     - `Why: report counts a fix only against a release it reads, so this lock would name a release report never counts.`;
     - `Next: pass the tag of the release that shipped the defect, or set [report] release_tags in .shipcraft.toml, then run red again.`
     So this refusal wins over every refusal `red` gives after it today (an invalid `shipcraft_min`, a suite root, an open lock, an uncommitted install). A tag that passes goes on to today's `red`, unchanged.
