   - Existence is still not checked (item 75): a matching name that is not yet a tag is recorded as given.
   - `--redo` is unchanged: it takes no fix option and keeps the lock's own fields.
3. **Lock format 6, written only when needed.**
   - **Format 6** is format 5's fields, order, prefixed `files` hashes, `controls`, seal and rules, with one difference: `kind` is `"fix"` and `fixes_release` is a tag name *outside* item 75's tag syntax (never `null`). A format 6 lock with any other `kind` or `fixes_release` is not valid, handled as a hand-edited lock today (`lock-edited` from the hook and CI).
   - **Which format each writer writes:** a new `red` writes format 6 when its `--fixes-release` is outside item 75's syntax, else format 5 (byte for byte as today). `red --redo` writes format 6 from a format 6 lock, format 5 from a format 4 or 5 lock, and format 3 from a format 2 or 3 lock (as today). Closing (`close`, `check`) changes only `state` and `seal`, in every format. An unchanged `--redo` and the controls notice behave for format 6 as for format 5.
   - This binary reads formats 2 to 6, each by its own `format`; format 5 keeps item 75's syntax for `fixes_release`. Its refusal for a format above 6 reads `... (format <n>; this one knows formats 2 to 6)`. A binary that knows formats only up to 5 refuses a format 6 lock with item 75's `lock-format` text (`formats 2 to 5`).
   - **`report`** reads a format 6 lock's fix fields as format 5's, and the run log's `red` rows: a row's `fixes_release` is read when it is `null`, of item 75's syntax, or a tag name. Item 76's `change_failure_rate` code is unchanged: a shipped `fix` lock whose `fixes_release` names a release under the active selection counts. So with `release_tags = "deploy-*"`, a fix naming `deploy-20261112-200036` is a measured failure when that tag is a release, and item 99's known limit on `fixes_release` (and its sentence "`fixes_release` is unchanged") is superseded by this item.

**Known limits.** The tag is checked against the selection in the working tree when `red` runs; a project that later changes `release_tags` keeps the names its locks hold, and `report` reads them under the new selection (a name that no longer matches counts as `missing`, as item 76 does for a name that names no release). Item 99's one-release-line limit stays: in a monorepo with `release_tags = "packages/*"`, a fix lock naming `packages/api/v1.0.0` is counted as shipped by any later matching release whose tree holds it, whatever package that release is. A team that writes a format 6 lock needs every machine and CI that judges it on 2.54 or newer; the release notes say so.

**Metric:** on Hands-off, the next fix locks carry a `deploy-*` `fixes_release`, and `report --since <the first deploy tag after the first such lock>` gives a non-null `change_failure_rate` once one ships and no shipped fix lock in that window lacks `fixes_release`. Revert: a release where `red` takes only item 75's syntax again; it keeps reading format 6, so locks already written stay valid.

**Touches.** Read first, and keep unless listed as changed:
- Item 75: the `v1-75.*` cases keep passing, except these, which change:
  - `v1-75.invalid-tag-prefix`, `invalid-tag-missing-part`, `invalid-tag-extra-part`, `invalid-tag-leading-major`, `invalid-tag-leading-minor`, `invalid-tag-leading-patch`, `invalid-tag-negative`, `invalid-tag-prerelease`, `invalid-tag-build`: their tags (`2.25.0`, `v2.25`, `v02.25.0`, `v2.25.0-rc1`, `v2.25.0+build`, ...) are tag names, so under the default selection they now get point 2's three-line refusal (still exit 2, no row, nothing written) instead of the usage; a case with an unparseable `.shipcraft.toml` reads it as the default selection. A tag that is not a tag name (with a space, `..`, or empty) still takes the invalid-arguments path.
  - `v1-75.future-hook`, `future-ci`, `future-status`, `future-red`: the unknown format moves from 6 to 7, and the text to `formats 2 to 6`.
  - `v1-75.legacy2-redo`, `legacy3-redo`, `legacy2-unchanged`, `legacy3-unchanged` are kept: legacy redo still writes format 3.
- Item 98 (format 5, `controls`): every format 5 case; locks without a non-`vX.Y.Z` `fixes_release` stay format 5 byte for byte.
- Item 43 and item 46: format refusals and redo upgrades.
- Item 76: `v1-76.*`, `change_failure_rate`, `missing`, `since`.
- Item 99: `v1-99.*`, including `v1-99.invalid-precedence` and the reader's reasons.
- Item 10: `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`; the version cases (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version` and others): the contract goes to 2.54.0.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `red` parses its arguments before reading anything; an invalid one exits 2 with the usage and writes nothing. Keep. Change: the selection check of point 2 comes right after, before `red` loads and validates the config and before every logged `red` refusal.
- `red` then loads and validates the whole config, checks open locks, the install and the suites, and logs its refusals. Keep, after the selection check.
- The lock writer picks the format from the current-format constant and writes digests, `controls`, the canonical serialization and the seal; `--redo` compares the rebuilt lock to the old one for an unchanged redo and prints the controls notice. Keep for formats 2 to 5. Change: format 6 as point 3 says, with the same serialization, seal, digest prefix, `controls` and notice.
- The hook, `ci`, `close`, `check`, `status`, `init` and `merge` read locks through one reader. Keep; that reader gains format 6.
- `report` reads `[report] release_tags` with item 99's reasons; red rows' and locks' `fixes_release` are read with item 75's syntax. Change: also tag names (point 3).

### The release publishes the binaries CI tested (item 78)

POC-023 ran `release-shadow.yml` beside `release/publish.sh` on two releases. On 2.25.0 the CI path was ready 12.6 min earlier, and on 2.30.0 20.0 min earlier. The battery verdicts were identical (2488/2488 and 2694/2694), with the same version line and the same smoke run. Its automatic rule says keep (2026-10-31). Today `publish.sh` builds both targets on the releaser's Mac and runs the whole battery there (item 21's release cases, `v1-21.release-*`). This item makes it publish the binaries the shadow workflow built and tested at the tagged commit. The local path stays as an explicit fallback.

1. **The CI path, by default.** `release/publish.sh <version>` keeps every check item 21 makes before building, with their three-line refusals:
   - the version syntax;
   - macOS;
   - the tools on `PATH`;
   - HEAD equal to the remote's `main`;
   - a clean tree;
   - the version equal to `nucleo/Cargo.toml`'s;
   - the tag still free on the remote.

   It also keeps the `publish.sh: <tag> from commit <sha>` line. Then, instead of the fresh checkout, the build and the battery, it does the following inside its temporary work folder (`shipcraft-publish.XXXXXX` under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}`, removed on exit, as today). Every refusal in this point is item 21's three-line form with exit 1. It happens before any `gh release create`, so nothing is published.
   - **The repository.** Every `gh` call of the script (run lookup, download, release create, release delete) passes `-R github.com/<owner>/<repo>`, whatever `GH_HOST` says, resolved once from `git remote get-url origin` (`git@github.com:<o>/<r>(.git)`, `https://github.com/<o>/<r>(.git)` or `ssh://git@github.com/<o>/<r>(.git)`). Any other URL refuses. This changes item 21's create and delete, which inferred the repository, on both paths, `--local-build` included; their behavior is otherwise kept.
   - **The run.** It runs `gh run list -R <repo> --workflow release-shadow.yml --branch main --event push --commit <sha> --json databaseId,status,conclusion --limit 20`.
     - The output must be a JSON array of objects, each with a positive integer `databaseId` and string `status` and `conclusion` (`conclusion` may be the empty string while running). Anything else refuses, naming the output as unreadable. A failing `gh` refuses (`could not list the release-shadow runs`).
     - It takes the first row, in the CLI's own newest-first order, whose `status` is `completed` and `conclusion` is `success`. It is chosen once: an older run is never tried after a later failure.
     - No such row refuses (`no successful release-shadow run for <sha>`). Its Next names waiting for the push run, `gh run rerun` of the failed push run, and `--local-build`.
   - **The artifact.** It runs `gh run download <id> -R <repo> -n shipcraft-darwin-<sha> -D <work>/ci`. The folder must then hold regular files (`lstat`, no symlink) `aarch64-apple-darwin/shipcraft` and `x86_64-apple-darwin/shipcraft`, and a `SHA256SUMS` with exactly one line for each of those two paths, in `shasum` format (64 lowercase hex, two spaces, the path) and no other line. `shasum -a 256 -c SHA256SUMS` run in that folder must exit 0. A failed download, a missing or non-regular binary, a malformed, missing or duplicate checksum line, or a failed check refuses, naming the run. The binaries are then given mode 755.
   - **The version line.** The host is read as item 21 reads it (`sysctl -n hw.optional.arm64`; `1` means arm64, including under Rosetta). On arm64, `aarch64-apple-darwin/shipcraft --version` must exit 0 with stdout exactly `shipcraft <version> (<first 12 characters of the pinned commit>)` and a newline; stderr is ignored. Anything else refuses. When the query does not answer `1` the check is skipped, as on an Intel Mac, and the notes say so.
   - **Packaging and publishing.** As item 21 does today:
     - one `tar.gz` per target, holding only the executable `shipcraft` (a copy of the downloaded binary);
     - a `SHA256SUMS` over the two archives;
     - `gh release create`, with the same handling after creation: a release whose URL came back is deleted on a later error; with no URL, nothing is deleted and the three-line inspection guidance is printed.

     The release notes say that the battery ran green in release-shadow run `<id>` (5 macOS shards, against the uploaded arm64 binary), and that the x86_64 binary was only built. The script writes nothing in the repository's working tree, runs no battery and runs no fixture command.
2. **`--local-build`.** `release/publish.sh <version> --local-build` is item 21's path, unchanged: a fresh checkout of the pinned commit, both builds, the whole battery against this machine's target (with `acceptance/reuso.sh`'s reuse), packaging and publishing, with today's notes. Any other second argument, or more than two, is the usage refusal.
3. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - The checks before building, their three-line refusals, the start line, the temporary work folder under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}` removed on exit, exit 2 when it cannot be made, exit 130 on INT or TERM, and that a refusal before `gh release create` publishes nothing: keep.
   - With one argument, the fresh checkout, both local builds and the local battery: change, as point 1 says. With `--local-build`: keep. That includes the battery's own effects: its logs and fixture work under the caller's `TMPDIR`, pruning of older work, the `~/.cache/shipcraft-acceptance/<tree-id>` result and the attempted `acceptance/local` status on a green full run (a failure there only warns), and what its fixtures meet (run-log rows, locks, closing commits, suites on a copy or in the tree).
   - Packaging (only the executable, one archive per target, `SHA256SUMS`), `gh release create`, deleting a half-made release whose URL came back, the three-line guidance when no URL came back, and the final `publish.sh: <tag> published at commit <sha>: <url>` line: keep.
   - It publishes nothing to brew, crates.io or npm: keep. `release/npm.sh` reads the GitHub release, so it is unchanged.
   - `install.sh` and the installation cases (`v1-21.instala-*`, `v1-21.remove`): keep.
   - `release-shadow.yml`: keep its jobs, artifact name and layout, and its `retention-days: 30`. Only its header comment changes.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change:** these ten cases now run `publish.sh` with `--local-build`, through the shared helper in `acceptance/tools/item21.py`, and keep every assertion: `v1-21.release-comum`, `release-prefixo`, `release-preso`, `release-build`, `release-bateria`, `release-rollback` and `release-incerto`, and item 37's `v1-37.release-comum`, `release-prefixo` and `release-preso`, which run the same helper and keep their archive and xattr checks. Item 37's probe that `dest.chmod(0o755)` appears exactly once in the helper may be adjusted only if the helper's insertion point moves. `acceptance/pontos/21.md` and `37.md` describe the fresh-checkout path as the `--local-build` fallback. The cases that refuse before the build (`release-formato`, `release-suja`, `release-versao`, `release-head`, `release-tag`, `release-remoto`, `release-tags-falha`) stay unchanged and run without the flag. Their fake `gh` gains `-R` handling only if the refusal happens after a `gh` call. The fixture's fake `git` may answer `remote get-url origin` with a synthetic `https://github.com/<o>/<r>.git` URL, while clone, push and `ls-remote` keep using the real local bare origin; nothing reaches the network.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** `v1-21.versao-fora`, `v1-21.versao-min-numerica`, every `v1-21.instala-*` case and `v1-21.remove`.
   - **New cases:** the CI path with a fake `gh` that answers `run list` and `run download` with the real JSON and artifact shapes:
     - a successful run is published, with downloaded binaries that lack the execute bit;
     - no run, a running run and a failed run refuse;
     - a `gh` failure refuses;
     - a missing or non-regular binary, a checksum mismatch, and a `SHA256SUMS` missing a line or holding an extra one refuse;
     - unreadable `run list` output refuses;
     - an origin URL that is not GitHub refuses;
     - a version line naming another commit refuses;
     - the Intel host skips the version check;
     - `--local-build` takes item 21's path;
     - a wrong second argument gets the usage refusal.
     Every refusal before `gh release create` publishes nothing.
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** release ready (the tag published) at most 15 min after the merge to main, on the next 3 releases.
   - **Revert:** `--local-build` for one release; a PR that restores the previous `publish.sh` for good.
6. **Known limits.**
   - The battery runs against the arm64 binary only, as it did locally on an arm64 Mac. The x86_64 binary is built in CI and not tested.
   - The workflow keeps artifacts 30 days (`retention-days: 30`). After that, a successful run may still be listed while its artifact is gone, so the download refuses. Rerun that push run (`gh run rerun <id>`), which keeps its `push` event, or use `--local-build`.
   - The binaries are not reproducible builds: the published bytes are CI's, not a local rebuild's.

### `--local-build` reuses the battery GitHub ran on the same commit (item 106)

Found in the field (2026-11-12 and 13, releases 2.53.0 and 2.54.0): when GitHub has no macOS runner, release-shadow's shards are cancelled with no step run, and item 78's default path refuses. The fallback, `--local-build`, then ran the whole battery on this Mac: 3,815 cases in 19,637 s (5 h 27) for 2.53.0. The same commit had already passed the whole battery on GitHub. The `acceptance` workflow's `push` run on `main` runs every case in 18 Linux shards (`acceptance/scope.sh --full`), and its `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` runs add 5 macOS shards. `acceptance/reuso.sh`'s reuse missed because it only reads results this Mac wrote (`~/.cache/shipcraft-acceptance/<tree-id>`). RED → release went from a median of 85 min to 553 over the last 3 releases. This item lets `--local-build` take a green GitHub run of the pinned commit instead of the local battery. A release built this way names the run it trusted and the platform the battery ran on.

1. **When it looks, and which runs count.** Only with `--local-build`, after both local builds and after `acceptance/reuso.sh`'s local reuse misses, and only when `SHIPCRAFT_ACCEPTANCE_REUSE` is not `0` (with `0`, it never calls `gh` for this and runs the local battery, as today). A local cache hit wins, as today, and makes no `gh` call for this. The run must be of the **pinned commit itself** (`headSha` equal to the full 40-character SHA the release pins), on `main`: the workflow file, `release/`, `docs/spec.md` and the commit embedded in `--version` are then the ones the release builds. No tree comparison is made. It runs `gh run list -R <repo> --workflow acceptance.yml --branch main --commit <sha> --json databaseId,headSha,event,status,conclusion --limit 20` (item 78's `-R`).
   - **The list reader** (Python's `json`, as `acceptance/reuso.sh` reads its cache): the output must parse as a JSON array, else the lookup ends with `unreadable run list`. A failing `gh` (non-zero exit) ends it with `gh run list failed`.
   - **Candidates:** each element is read alone, in the CLI's newest-first order. An element counts only when it is an object with `databaseId` a JSON integer above 0 (any size), and `headSha`, `event`, `status` and `conclusion` strings, with `headSha` equal to the pinned SHA, `status` `completed`, `conclusion` `success`, and `event` one of `push`, `schedule` or `workflow_dispatch`. A `pull_request` run covers only the PR's impact, so it never counts. Any other element is skipped and the rest are still read; extra fields are ignored.
2. **Which run it takes.** For each candidate, in order, it runs `gh run view <id> -R <repo> --json jobs`.
   - **The jobs reader:** the output must parse as a JSON object whose `jobs` is an array. A failing `gh`, or output of another shape, removes only that candidate, and the lookup goes on. A job is an element that is an object with `name` and `conclusion` strings (other fields ignored). An element of any other shape, or with a `null` or missing `conclusion`, is an *unreadable job*.
   - **The shard sets.** A *macOS shard* is a job whose name matches `macos <k>/<n>`, and a *Linux shard* one matching `linux <k>/<n>`, with `<k>` and `<n>` canonical decimals (no sign, no leading zero, at least 1). Each set is judged whole, never after dropping members. The run is a *macOS proof* when its macOS shards are exactly `macos 1/5` to `macos 5/5`, each once, all `success`. It is a *Linux proof* when its Linux shards share one `<n>`, are exactly `linux 1/<n>` to `linux <n>/<n>`, each once, all `success`. A duplicate, a gap, a second denominator or a shard not `success` voids that platform's proof. Any unreadable job voids both proofs of that run. A run can be both proofs. Jobs of other names (`build`, `scope`, `acceptance`) are ignored.
   - **The choice:** the first candidate that is a macOS proof. When none is, the first that is a Linux proof. It views every candidate it needs to establish that preference (at most 20 `gh run view` calls). Without either proof, the lookup ends with `no green run of this commit`.
   - **Never a refusal.** When the lookup takes nothing, it prints one line, `publish.sh: no GitHub battery to reuse (<reason>); running it here`, and runs the local battery exactly as today. `<reason>` is one of the three above.
3. **What it prints and publishes when it takes a run.**
   - It prints `publish.sh: battery reused from GitHub: acceptance run <id> at <sha>, <platform>`, where `<platform>` is `5 macOS shards` or `<n> Linux shards`. It runs no battery and no fixture command, writes nothing in `~/.cache/shipcraft-acceptance`, and sets no `acceptance/local` status.
   - It packages and publishes the locally built binaries as `--local-build` does today. In the notes, the header line (`shipcraft <version>, from commit <sha>.`) and the install paragraph stay. The two-line battery paragraph is replaced by `The whole battery (acceptance/run.sh all) ran green in GitHub acceptance run <id> at this commit, on <platform>, against a binary built there.` and `Both macOS targets were built here; the battery did not run against them.` Under a Linux proof a third line follows: `No macOS battery ran for this commit.`
   - Every other `--local-build` step, message and refusal keeps today's form (see Current behavior).

Cases: about 16, all in a new `acceptance/lib/item106.sh` on item 78's fakes (`acceptance/tools/item21.py` and `item21_fake.py`: fake `gh`, fake `git remote get-url`, a fake battery that records whether it ran). Nothing reaches the network.

**Known limits.** A Linux proof does not test the macOS binary; a macOS-only defect can ship, and the notes say which platform ran. GitHub's binary and the local one come from the same commit and `Cargo.lock` but are different bytes. A release commit with no green `push`, `schedule` or `workflow_dispatch` run yet (the `push` run takes about 30 min) runs the local battery, as today.

**Metric:** over the next 5 releases that take `--local-build`, the time from `publish.sh` start to the release is at most 20 min whenever a green `push` run of the commit exists (2.53.0: 5 h 27). Also, no release in the next 20 is later fixed for a macOS-only defect that a Linux proof let through. Revert: `SHIPCRAFT_ACCEPTANCE_REUSE=0` for one release; for good, a PR that restores the previous `publish.sh`.

**Touches.** Read first, and keep unless listed as changed:
- Item 21 (`v1-21.release-comum`, `release-prefixo`, `release-preso`, `release-build`, `release-bateria`, `release-rollback`, `release-incerto`, and the refusals before the build) and item 37 (`v1-37.release-comum`, `release-prefixo`, `release-preso`): every assertion stays. Their fake `gh` does not answer `run list --workflow acceptance.yml` with a reusable run, so they reach the local battery as today. Only the fake's answer to that call may be added, and only so it names no reusable run.
- Item 78 (`v1-78.*`): the default path is unchanged. `v1-78.local-build` and `v1-78.local-rollback` run with `SHIPCRAFT_ACCEPTANCE_REUSE=0`, so they make no lookup.
- `acceptance/reuso.sh` and its users (`run.sh`, `review.sh`): unchanged.
- The version cases (`v1-62.version`, `v1-63.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-78.version`, `v1-87.version`) and item 103's contract-version check: the contract goes to 2.55.0.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- Everything item 78's and item 21's lists name for `--local-build`, kept: the preflight refusals (exit 1, three lines), the work folder under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}` (exit 2 if it cannot be made, removed on exit, exit 130 on INT/TERM), the `publish.sh: <tag> from commit <sha>` line, the fresh checkout, both builds, archives and `SHA256SUMS`, `gh release create` with deletion of a created release on a later error and the inspection guidance when no URL came back.
- It sources `acceptance/reuso.sh` and calls `acceptance_cached_result "$SRC" HEAD`; a hit prints `battery reused for this tree`. Keep.
- On a miss it runs `nice -n 10 bash acceptance/run.sh "$BIN" all` and refuses on red. That battery's own effects stay as item 78 lists them (logs and fixture work under the caller's `TMPDIR`, pruning, the cache record, the attempted `acceptance/local` status, and what its fixtures meet). Change: point 1's lookup comes first; the local battery, with those effects, runs only when the lookup takes nothing.
- The notes' battery paragraph says `ran against <platform>: <battery>` and `<other> was only built`. Change: under a GitHub proof, point 3's lines.
- `gh` calls name `-R github.com/<owner>/<repo>` from `origin`. Keep for the new calls.

### Workspace suites run from the root when the root vitest config defines the projects (item 71)

Wrong-verdict note: since item 9 (2.0.0), `init` writes each Node workspace suite to run inside its package. Found on the Forge, the second adopter (2.25.0, deluge-forge-platform PR #805, 2026-10-31): its root `vitest.config.ts` carries the worker caps (pool options vitest reads only from the root config), a `globalSetup` that points `TMPDIR` at one folder per run and a guard against writes to the real home folder, and its `vitest.workspace.ts` lists one project per package. A run from inside a package skips all of them. The Forge rewrote every suite by hand to run from the root with `--project <name>`. The battery should have caught it: item 51's fixtures have a root workspace file, but they write `.shipcraft.toml` before `init`, so no case ran `init` on such a layout.

1. **Candidates.** `init` (no `.shipcraft.toml` yet, not `--lang other`) plans a Node workspace as item 9 does, and runner inference stays as today. The *candidates* are the planned suites whose runner is vitest. The probe below runs only when there is at least one candidate and the repository root holds at least one regular file (`lstat`, never followed) named `vitest.workspace.<ext>`, `vitest.projects.<ext>`, `vitest.config.<ext>` or `vite.config.<ext>`, `<ext>` one of `ts`, `mts`, `cts`, `js`, `mjs`, `cjs`, `json`. Otherwise every suite keeps today's form, `init` runs no vitest and prints nothing new. Jest suites are never candidates and get no new line.
2. **The probe.** `init` runs, once, from the repository root, `npx --no -- vitest list --filesOnly --json`, with the environment every command `shipcraft` starts gets (the one `init` received, less `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES`, which `shipcraft` removes at start, section 6), stdin from `/dev/null`, stderr discarded, in its own process group. stdout is read while the command runs (no deadlock on a full pipe). The whole probe, reading included, has a 60 s limit; at the limit the group is killed. The probe runs after `[build]` is decided (item 16b's `tsc -b`) and item 56's judgement, and before `init` writes anything; a hook refusal that today happens before `[build]` still happens first, with no probe.
   - **The probe fails** when it cannot start, reading its stdout or waiting for it fails (no retry), it exits non-zero or by a signal, reaches the limit, or its stdout is not valid UTF-8, does not parse as a JSON array of objects each with a string `file`, or holds a `file` that is not an absolute path. Then every candidate keeps today's form, and no part of the answer is used.
   - **Reading an entry.** `projectName` is read when it is a string; any other value or its absence reads as the empty name. Each `file` is canonicalized; when that fails its path is used as given. The suite folder is the repository root canonicalized, joined with the suite's root. A file *belongs to* a suite when its path, compared component by component, lies under that folder.
3. **When a candidate converts.** A candidate converts to the root form when all of these hold:
   - at least one entry belongs to it, and all of its entries carry the same name `<project>`;
   - `<project>` is not empty, does not start with `-`, and is made only of `A-Z a-z 0-9 @ / . _ -`;
   - every entry whose name equals `<project>` ignoring ASCII case, in the whole answer, belongs to this suite (vitest 2.1 matches `--project` without regard to case, so `api` also selects a project `API`; a project that also lists files of another folder converts no suite);
   - the suite's folder path holds none of the characters `* ? [ ] { } ! ( )` and no control character, so the prefixed globs below read it literally.
   Every other candidate keeps today's form; its reason (point 5) is the first condition above, in this order, that fails.
4. **The root form.** For a converted suite with folder `<dir>`:
   - `root = "."`;
   - `command = "npx --no -- vitest run --project=<project> --reporter=default --reporter=junit --outputFile.junit={junit}"`: today's vitest command with `--project=<project>` right after `run` (one word, so vitest never reads the name as an option);
   - `globs`: each of today's globs for the suite, in today's order, prefixed with `<dir>/` (`**/*.test.*` becomes `<dir>/**/*.test.*`);
   - `check`: when today's form would write `<bin>tsc --noEmit` (after item 56's judgement), the root form writes `cd ./<dir> && npx --no -- tsc --noEmit`, `./<dir>` shell-quoted when it holds a character other than `A-Z a-z 0-9 / . _ -` (the `./` keeps `cd` from reading `CDPATH` or an option), so the check runs inside the package as today and finds the same `tsc` and the same tsconfig; no check when today's form writes none;
   - its name and its place in the suite order: as today.
   A workspace config with at least one converted suite is always written in the `[[suite]]` form, with every suite named, even when only one suite exists, so case ids keep their `<name>:` prefix.
5. **What `init` prints.** On stdout, after the `workspace node with N suite(s)` line, only when the probe ran:
   - the probe failed: one line `shipcraft: suites run inside their packages: \`vitest list\` <how>; the root vitest config does not apply to them`, `<how>` one of `could not start`, `could not be read` (reading its output or waiting for it failed), `exited <code>`, `was stopped by signal <n>`, `timed out after 60 s`, `printed no file list`;
   - otherwise one line per candidate, in suite order: converted, `shipcraft: suite <name> runs from the repository root with --project=<project>, so the root vitest config applies`; kept, `shipcraft: suite <name> runs inside its package: <why>`, `<why>` one of `vitest listed none of its files`, `vitest listed its files in more than one project` (files that all lack a name, or all have an empty one, fail the next condition instead and get its reason), `its vitest project name is not one shipcraft writes in a command`, `its vitest project also lists files outside it`, `its folder name cannot be written in a glob`.
   `<name>` and `<project>` are escaped as in item 52's lists.
6. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - Suite planning (item 9): one suite per workspace package with tests, named after its folder, `root` the package folder, globs relative to it, the vitest command with `npx --no -- `, the jest command, and `check = "<bin>tsc --noEmit"` where a `tsconfig.json` exists: keep for every suite that does not convert, and for every jest suite.
   - Runner inference (`Lang::profile`, vitest as the default without a runner dependency): keep.
   - A single-package (non-workspace) Node project, Python, Go, `--lang other`, and an existing `.shipcraft.toml` (not rewritten): keep, and no probe runs.
   - A config with exactly one suite at root `.` written in the top-level form: keep for non-workspace projects; change for a workspace with a converted suite, as in point 4.
   - `[build]` inference and its proof run, item 56's judgement and lines, and the order of their lines: keep.
   - Everything `init` writes and prints after planning: `.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep` and its folders, the hooks (own hooks with their permission bits, lefthook, husky, pre-commit, and their installed or kept notices, item 58's project-local calls), the `shipcraft_min` raise, the reviewer choice and its notices, `--github` and its notices, the `AGENTS.md` block, the `CLAUDE.md` import, the briefs, the closing guidance with the CI line last: keep.
   - Exit codes, refusals, and the rollback on a failed write, which removes only the paths this run created: keep. Like `tsc -b`'s output today, anything the probe's vitest writes (a cache) is not removed.
   - `init` writes no run-log row: keep.
   - Downstream, for a converted suite: `red` and `ci` run its command and check from root `.` (the check entering the package with `cd`); a failing check still refuses before the tests with the same code, exit, stderr forwarding and CI `no-report` row, and its refusal text now quotes the new check and names root `.`: change, as point 4 implies. For a suite that does not convert: keep.
   - `shipcraft` removes `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` from its environment at start, so no command it starts inherits them (item 22): keep, and the probe gets that environment too.
7. **Touches.**
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** item 9's workspace cases (`v1-9.1-init-pnpm`, `v1-9.1-init-workspaces-do-package-json`, `v1-9.1-init-go-work`, `v1-9.1-monorepo-casos-por-suite`), whose fixtures have no root vitest file, so no probe runs; item 12's and 13's node init cases (`v1-12.1-npx-tsc-com-traco`, `v1-12.1-todo-npx-com-traco`, `v1-13.1-init-*`); item 16b's (`v1-16b.1-*`); item 56's (`v1-56.*`, `v1-56.workspace` included); item 51's (`v1-51.*`), whose fixtures have a root `vitest.workspace.ts` but write `.shipcraft.toml` before `init`, so no probe runs; item 15a's (`v1-15a.1-recusa-antes-de-perguntar-e-escrever`, `v1-15a.1-decide-antes-e-desfaz-escrita-no-meio`, `v1-15a.1-falha-preserva-config-simbolica`); item 25's closing lines (`v1-25.*`); item 27's (`v1-27.monorepo`, `v1-27.mensagem-local`, `v1-27.mensagem-desligado`, `v1-27.mensagem-github`); item 58's (`v1-58.plain`, `v1-58.kept`, `v1-58.rollback`); item 62's (`v1-62.init`, `v1-62.init-preserve`); `v1-53.environment`, `v1-60.monorepo`, `v1-60.overlap`, `v1-65.monorepo-check`; item 67's and 68's init cases; item 2's and 5's init screens (`v1-2.3-tela-do-init`, `v1-2.3-padrao-primeiro-instalado`, `v1-5.3-init-github-marca-obrigatorios`, `v1-5.3-init-github-plano-gratis`); item 61's onboarding cases (`v1-61.block`, `v1-61.local-block`, `v1-61.edited`, `v1-61.claude-link`, `v1-61.claude-dangling`, `v1-61.refused-late`); item 55's selection cases (`v1-55.*`, every profile), which now also see root-relative package globs; item 45's check refusals (`v1-45.red-*`, `v1-45.ci-*`, `v1-45.ci-log-code`), for their fixtures; item 22's environment cases (`v1-22.ambiente-red`, `v1-22.ambiente-ci`, `v1-22.ambiente-verifica`, `v1-22.ambiente-build`).
   - **Fixed points:** README 17 (suite commands run in their root), 40, 57 and 58 (a check where a tsconfig exists, from the package) hold: a converted suite's root is `.` and its check enters the package with `cd`.
   - **New cases:** a fake `npx` on `PATH` answers `vitest list` (canned answers for the failure and boundary cases) and records its arguments and folder; and at least one case with the real vitest 2.1.9 the battery's cache already holds for item 51: a pnpm workspace with a root `vitest.config.ts` and a `vitest.workspace.ts` naming two package projects, where `init` writes the root form and a later `red` on a failing test in one package locks it under that suite.
8. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** on the Forge's layout, a fresh `init` writes the root form for each package suite, with no hand edit; 0 adopter reports of a suite skipping the root config over the next 5 adopter inits.
   - **Revert:** a release drops the probe; projects keep the config they have.
9. **Known limits.**
   - Running from the root applies the root config's run-wide options (pool options, `globalSetup`). Options a project must inherit (its own `setupFiles`, timeouts) apply only when its config extends the root one; `init` does not check that.
   - `vitest list --filesOnly` needs vitest 2.1 or later and installed dependencies; without them every candidate keeps today's form, with the line that says why. A kept suite may still not run correctly inside its package (for example a project that only works from the root); `init` says it ran inside its package and does not prove it.
   - A package whose tests run in two projects (say `node` and `browser`) keeps today's form; the user writes those suites by hand.
   - The root vitest config and workspace file are not in any suite's globs, as today, so they are not locked.
   - The project name comes from vitest at `init` time; renaming a project later leaves the suite pointing at the old name, and vitest then runs nothing for it, which `red` refuses as `suite-without-tests`.
   - The worker caps the Forge sets in its command's environment are the project's choice; `init` writes none.
### `status` says review off, and `pr`'s CI wait prints each state change (item 73)

Two small field findings. The Forge (2.25.0, `review = "off"`, no `[roles] review`) saw `shipcraft status` print `review off (agent codex)` and `review codex`, naming a reviewer that never runs. And `shipcraft pr` with `ci = "github"` prints one `waiting for ...` line and then nothing until CI ends or the limit passes (often 3 to 6 minutes on this repository), so a person or an agent watching cannot tell a slow check from a stuck one.

1. **`status` with review off.** When `[execution] review` is `"off"`:
   - the layers line ends `; review off`, with no `(agent ...)`;
   - the roles line's review entry is `review off`, whatever `[roles] review` holds.
   With review `"local"` (or any other value that is not `"off"`) both lines stay as today. Nothing else in `status` changes. This supersedes item 64's display of the review role when review is off; item 64's validation and defaults of `[roles] review` are unchanged, and `pr`, `review` and `merge` still read it as today.
2. **`pr`'s GitHub CI wait.** While `pr` waits for GitHub CI (`ci = "github"` with review local, the `waiting for ... at <sha> (up to N s)` line of today), it prints one line on stdout each time what it reads changes, including the first read:
   `shipcraft: CI at <elapsed> s: shipcraft/ci <state>[; <check> <word>]...`
   - `<elapsed>` is the whole seconds since the wait started, rounded down;
   - `<state>` is the `shipcraft/ci` state as today's reader (`gh::statuses`) returns it (`pending`, `success`, `failure`, `error`, or any other string GitHub sends, as read), `no status` when that reader finds no `shipcraft/ci` entry, and `no state` when it returns an empty string; a `gh` failure in that reader ends `pr` as today (exit 2, no line);
   - then one `; <check> <word>` per name in `[execution] suite_checks`, in config order, `<word>` one of `green`, `failed`, `pending`, `missing`, `unreadable` as the wait reads them today;
   - when `<state>` is `failure` or `error`, the wait stops today before reading any check: the line then has no check entries, and no check is read;
   - `<elapsed>` is sampled once per read, after all of that read's calls return and just before printing; a slow read may print a value above the limit;
   - a line is printed only when the read's state vector (the `shipcraft/ci` state or its absence, then each check's word in config order) differs from the last one printed; status descriptions, run ids and ordering never trigger a line, and an unreadable check is the word `unreadable` whatever the reason.
   The lines that end the wait (CI failed, a check failed, the limit passed) and the wait's outcome, its exit code, its run-log row and its polling interval stay as today. The line is printed before the wait acts on what it read, so the read that ends the wait also prints its line when its states changed.
3. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `status`: the branch line, the layers line with `ci ...` (and item 62's suite checks), the roles line with item 64's red and green entries, the hook line, the open-locks line, the protection line, exit codes, no file written, no run-log row: keep. The review part of both lines when review is `"off"`: change, as in point 1.
   - `pr`'s wait: the `waiting for` line, polling every 2 s up to `ci_timeout`, stopping on `failure` or `error` of `shipcraft/ci` or a failed check, the `ci-github` and `ci-github-no-status` outcomes and their text, the review running only after CI is green, and everything `pr` prints before and after the wait: keep. New lines during the wait: change, as in point 2.
   - `pr` with `ci = "local"` or `"off"`, `review`, `merge` and `ci`: keep; they get no new line.
   - A `gh` error while reading `shipcraft/ci` during the wait: `shipcraft: error: ...` on stderr, exit 2, no review and no summary comment; the push and the PR created or updated before stay; a `pr` row with `code: "error"` is appended to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl` (a logging failure is ignored), and nothing is rolled back: keep.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change:** `v1-64.status-off` (`acceptance/lib/item64.sh`), which expects the roles line's review entry `review codex` with review off: it now expects `review off`.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-64.status-*` case; `v1-64.min-valid`, `v1-64.min-patch`, `v1-64.min-leading-zero`, `v1-64.min-legacy`, `v1-64.min-no-roles`; the hook-state status cases `v1-20.status.*`, `v1-15c.status-sem-exec-borda`, `v1-25.husky.status-completo`, `v1-25.pre.status-completo` and the other item 20 and 25 status variants; `v1-62.status`, `v1-62.status-empty`, `v1-5.4-status-nivel-de-protecao`; item 62's GitHub wait cases (`v1-62.*`) and item 3's wait cases in `acceptance/lib/v1.sh` (the review runs once after the status arrives; the wait's limit), whose assertions on the wait's outcome and lines stay; item 41's local CI queue cases.
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** no adopter report of a reviewer named while review is off; on this repository's next 5 `pr` runs, each CI wait prints at least one state line before its outcome.
   - **Revert:** a release drops the lines.
6. **Known limits.**
   - A state that changes and changes back between two reads is not seen, as today.
   - The local CI queue's wait (item 41) prints no new line.

### `red` refuses when the install is not committed (item 80)

POC-021's after runs on 2.31.0 (2026-10-31, `after2-claude-existing`): Claude ran `shipcraft init` and did not commit what it installed. It then wrote a test and a stub and ran `shipcraft red`, with `.shipcraft.toml`, `.husky/commit-msg`, `.husky/post-commit`, the briefs and `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep` all untracked. `red` ran every suite (`fallback: "config changed"`, because the untracked config is a changed path) and wrote a lock that hashes `.shipcraft.toml`. The hook refused the RED commit `locked-file-deleted` (`locked file .shipcraft.toml was deleted in the index`), and no RED commit could pass without carrying the install in it. The full run also locked a test that already failed in a file the RED never touched (item 81). This item makes `red` say so before it runs anything, unless the install is committed or staged.

1. **The install paths.** These paths of the repository root, the ones `init` writes and git tracks:
   - `.shipcraft.toml`;
   - `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, `.shipcraft/briefs/red.md`, `.shipcraft/briefs/green.md`;
   - the hook integration files item 15c.2 and item 25.4 protect, as `init` writes them: `.husky/commit-msg`, `.husky/post-commit`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, and the lefthook files `lefthook.yml`, `.lefthook.yml`, `lefthook.yaml`, `.lefthook.yaml`.

   `.git/hooks/commit-msg` and `.git/hooks/post-commit` (plain git) are not tracked by git and do not count. Nor do `lefthook-local` files (never written by `init`), `.husky/_/`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, or the package manifest and lockfile of a project-local install.
2. **The rule.** An install path is *uncommitted* when it exists in the working tree (`lstat` succeeds, whatever the entry's type; `lstat` failing with `ENOENT` or `ENOTDIR` means absent; any other `lstat` error, such as `EACCES` or `ELOOP`, counts the path as uncommitted, and the refusal's first line adds `(could not be read: <error>)` after it, so an unreadable install never passes) and neither HEAD's tree nor the index has an entry at that path. A staged path is not uncommitted, whether or not HEAD has it: a commit that carries the staged install together with the RED passes the hook, since the hook reads the locked files from the index. When HEAD is unborn (no commit yet), only the install paths that exist and are not staged count, so a repository's first commit can hold both the install and the RED. A path absent from the working tree is never uncommitted: a project installed before item 68 has no briefs and needs none. Only presence counts: a path that HEAD holds and the working tree changes is not uncommitted (see the limits).
3. **How it is read.** HEAD is read with `git rev-parse -q --verify HEAD`, as `Repo::head` reads it today: exit status 1 means unborn. HEAD's entries are read with one `git ls-tree -z --name-only HEAD -- <paths>` over the install paths that exist (not run when HEAD is unborn), and the index's with one `git ls-files -z --cached -- <paths>` over the paths HEAD lacks (not run when HEAD holds them all). A path either command lists is installed. Any other failure of the three commands (an exit status other than 0, and other than 1 for `rev-parse`; a signal; a failure to start git; output that is not NUL-terminated paths) is today's git failure: `shipcraft: error: ...`, exit 2, with the run-log row as for any error. No `rev-parse <ref>^{commit}` is added, so item 66's candidate resolution and its order stay exactly as they are.
4. **When.** `red` judges it after the config is read and validated and after `lock-open`, and before anything else: item 66's base, item 55's changed paths, item 59's first warning, `[build]`, any `check` or suite, and any write other than the best-effort run-log row of point 6. `red --redo` judges it after HEAD's lock is read (`redo-without-lock`, `lock-open`, `lock-edited`, `lock-format`), and before the same steps. Those earlier refusals keep their precedence.
5. **The refusal.** When any install path is uncommitted, `red` refuses `install-not-committed`, exit 1, with the paths in byte order, all of them, separated by a space in the command and by `, ` in the first line:

   ```
   shipcraft refused: the Shipcraft install is not committed or staged: <paths>
   Why: the lock protects .shipcraft.toml and the hook judges the commit's index against HEAD, so an install file the commit lacks reads as a locked file deleted, or a hook file created while the lock is open, and the RED commit is refused
   Next: stage the install (`git add -- <paths>`), with the other files init's `shipcraft: ready. Commit ...` line named when they changed (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, and package.json with its lockfile for a project install), or commit them; then run `shipcraft red` again
   ```

   For `--redo`, the `Next:` ends with ``run `shipcraft red --redo` again``. `init`'s closing line is the list the `Next:` refers to: `shipcraft: ready. Commit .shipcraft.toml<hook files>, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, .shipcraft/briefs/red.md, .shipcraft/briefs/green.md and .shipcraft/locks/;` as `init` prints it, with the parts it did not write left out.
6. **What it leaves.** No lock is written or changed, no suite, build or check runs, no stdout line of item 55, 65 or 66 is printed, and item 59's warnings are not printed. The run log gets one `red` row, `result: "refused"`, `code: "install-not-committed"`, without `selection`, `selected_files` and `fallback` (the selection was not decided, as for `lock-open`); a non-redo row carries item 75's `kind`, `reported_at` and `fixes_release` as every non-redo row does; a redo row has `redo: true`.
7. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `red` and `red --redo` read the config first (`Config::load`: `no-config`, `config-invalid`, `shipcraft-min`, the legacy-config refusal), then `lock-open` (`refuse_if_a_lock_is_open`) or HEAD's lock (`head_lock`): keep, before the new check.
   - With an install path uncommitted, `red` today goes on: item 66's base (on an unborn HEAD, `no base`), item 55's changed paths (an untracked or staged `.shipcraft.toml` is a changed path, so `config changed` and the full run), the full run, item 53's second run, and a lock whose `files` hash the working tree's `.shipcraft.toml` (`locked_files` in `red.rs`), with exit 0, its stdout lines and a `passed` row: change, it refuses as in point 5.
   - The hook's judgment of the RED commit: `check::run` reads each locked file from the index through `check_files` (`contract.rs`), where `Repo::read` takes a path `git ls-files` does not list as absent, and refuses `locked-file-deleted` (`locked file <file> was deleted in the index`, Why `RED is the GREEN contract; deleting the test or config deletes the contract`); CI does the same on each commit's tree through `git ls-tree`. `.shipcraft.toml` is a locked file of every lock: `locked_files` adds `CONFIG_FILE` to the suites' test files. Keep.
   - `check_lefthook` (`contract.rs`) is skipped for a commit that opens a lock and, on every later commit while a lock is open, refuses `hook-config-changed` for a hook integration file whose index bytes differ from every parent's, `was created` when no parent has it: keep. This is why an uncommitted hook file is a trap: it cannot be committed while the lock is open.
   - A staged `.shipcraft.toml` that differs from HEAD's is validated by the hook even with no lock open (item 31): keep.
   - `red`'s other effects (the `.git/shipcraft/tmp` list folder created and removed, the reports, `[build]` and `check` output, the lock written with `fs::write`, nothing rolled back on a refusal since nothing was written, the GREEN guidance as the last stdout line): keep on every path that passes the check.
   - `init`: what it writes, its closing guidance, its exit codes and rollbacks: keep.
   - The run-log row is best effort (`medicao::record`): it creates the common git directory's `shipcraft/` folder if missing, appends one line, and ignores any failure to create, open or write (a `runs.jsonl` that is a directory, a read-only folder): keep, also on this refusal, which stays exit 1 whatever happens to the row.
8. **Section changes.** None. Item 66's unborn `HEAD` (`no base`, the full run) is still reached when the install is staged, and its metric stands.
9. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:**
     - `v1-59.suites-base-missing` (`acceptance/lib/item59.sh`): the only case whose `red` runs with an install path neither in HEAD nor staged (the rewritten `.shipcraft.toml` on `feature/item59` is untracked). Its fixture stages that file (`git add -- .shipcraft.toml`) before `red`. Every assertion stays: a staged config is still a changed path since the base, so the run is the same full run with `config changed`, and the base still has no config.
   - **The version:** 2.33.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - the cases that run `red` in an unborn repository with the install staged: `v1-14.1-primeiro-red-sem-commit` (python, node, go; `git add -A` before `red`; the first commit holds the install and the RED, and GREEN closes it), `v1-17.primeiro-commit-verde-recusa` and `v1-17.primeiro-red-green-fecha` (`setup(unborn=True)` in `acceptance/tools/item17.py`, `git add -A` before `red`), and `v1-66.unborn` and `v1-66.unborn-remote` (HEAD pointed at an unborn branch, the index still holding the committed install; the `no base` full run). Their fixed points (`acceptance/pontos/17.md`, `57.md`, `66.md`) and README fixed point 54 stay;
     - every case that runs `red` after `setup_init`, `setup_red`, `pr_main` or a fixture commit of the install: `run.sh`'s main table, items 16a, 22, 25, 30, 32, 33, 38, 40, 42, 43, 44, 45, 46, 47, 50, 51, 52, 53, 55, 57, 58, 60, 62, 64, 65, 67, 71 and 75, and the other `v1.sh`, `v1-17.*` and `v1-66.*` cases;
     - `v1-66.git-head`, `v1-66.git-invalid`, `v1-66.git-status` (their shim fails only `HEAD^{commit}`, which this item does not call) and the five `v1-66.<ref>-first` cases, which assert the exact sequence of `^{commit}` resolutions;
     - `r-legacy-config-red`, whose config is moved aside but still in HEAD, and which refuses the legacy config first;
     - item 55's `v1-55.config` and `v1-55.removed` and item 59's `files-config`, `files-invalid` and `suites-base-bad`, which change tracked files only;
     - every `init` case (`init-*`, `v1-58.*`, `v1-61.*`, `v1-67.*`, `v1-68.*`): `init`'s output does not change.
   - **New cases** include: the field's shape as a first commit: an unborn repository with husky, `init`, the install and the RED's test and stub staged together, `red` accepted, the hook accepting that first commit with no `locked-file-deleted` and the lock committed open, then a GREEN commit that closes it; each install path alone untracked (config, `.gitkeep`, each brief, each husky file, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, a lefthook file); a staged config with a commit behind HEAD (accepted); an unborn HEAD with one install path unstaged (refused); a failing `git ls-files` (exit 2); a project without briefs (not refused); a tracked config changed in the working tree (not refused); `--redo` with a husky file untracked; the refusal's precedence after `lock-open`, `no-config` and `redo-without-lock`; the row's fields; a failing `git ls-tree` (exit 2); an unreadable `.husky` folder (refused, the path named with its error); `.husky` as a regular file (`ENOTDIR`, absent); a `runs.jsonl` that is a directory (still exit 1, no row).
10. **Measure and revert.**
    - **Metric:** RED commits refused `locked-file-deleted` for an install path in POC-021's next after runs (1 in the 2.31.0 runs), and `install-not-committed` refusals in our own and adopters' run logs that the user did not follow by staging or committing the install and a successful `red`.
    - **Threshold:** 0 such RED commits; 0 `install-not-committed` refusals reported as false (a refusal where every install path was in fact committed or staged) over the next 5 items and POC-021's next runs.
    - **Revert:** a release drops the check. There is no per-run switch: staging or committing the install is the way on.
11. **Known limits.**
    - Only presence is checked. An install path HEAD holds with other bytes in the working tree passes: `init` appending its commands to a tracked `.husky/commit-msg` or `lefthook.yml` and not committing them leaves a hook that works in this checkout but not in a clone, and a commit of that change while a lock is open is refused `hook-config-changed`.
    - `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `package.json` and its lockfile are not checked: none of them is locked or judged by the hook.
    - Plain git hooks in `.git/hooks` are not tracked, so a clone never has them; this item cannot see that.
    - An install path the project ignores (`.gitignore`) still counts; the `git add` in the `Next:` then needs `-f`, which the line does not say.
    - A path staged when `red` runs and unstaged (or never committed) before the RED commit still makes the hook refuse `locked-file-deleted` for `.shipcraft.toml`, or leaves a hook file to be created later and refused `hook-config-changed`. `red` judges the index at its own time, not the commit's.
    - A staged config that HEAD lacks is still a changed path. Since item 129 an added `.shipcraft.toml` (the merge base has none) is not `config changed` unless the merge base holds the legacy `.sc2.toml` or that read fails; `red` then narrows, subject to every other fallback of item 55, as on an unchanged config. Item 81 keeps failures in test files the RED did not change out of the lock.

### `red` does not lock a failure in a test file the RED did not change (item 81)

POC-021's after runs on 2.31.0 (2026-10-31, `after2-claude-existing`). The fixture has a cart test that already fails, `cart: applies the bulk discount from 10 units`, which the project's `CLAUDE.md` says another team owns. The RED added `packages/pricing/src/discount.test.ts` and a stub, and ran `red`. With no remote, item 66 took `main` as the base, which is `HEAD`. But `.shipcraft.toml` was untracked (item 80), so it was a changed path, and `red` ran every suite (`fallback: "config changed"`). The lock took the cart failure into `failing_at_red`, beside the 8 new pricing cases. A GREEN can close that lock only by making a test it must not touch pass.

What `red` does today:
- **The base** is item 66's commit, and item 55's changed paths are measured from its merge base with `HEAD`.
- **The selection** (items 55, 59, 60, 65) runs the changed test files. Only cases that map to a selected file are kept, so a narrowed run never locks a case of a file the RED did not change, except the lock files a redo adds (item 60).
- **Every fallback** (`--all`, `no base`, `no merge base`, `git failed`, `config changed`, `protected file removed`, `unreadable path`, `nothing selected`, `no report`, `case without file`, `file without cases`) runs every suite. It locks every failing case (`failing_at_red` in `red.rs`, then item 53's `confirm`), wherever it lives.
- **`red` never runs a test at the base.** It reads the base only through git (`narrow::base`, `narrow::changed`), and it runs suites only on the working tree.

So the failure comes from the full run. With item 80 the field run would have narrowed to `discount.test.ts` and locked no cart case. A full run still happens for every other reason above, for example a RED that changes only a helper in the globs (item 65's `file without cases`) or a RED on a branch with no merge base.

1. **The rule.** After the first run is read and judged as today (through `red-already-passes`, item 52's `group-failed` and the earlier refusals), let `F` be its failed cases, suite-prefixed as `failing_at_red` writes them. A case of `F` is *outside the RED* when all of these hold:
   - `red` ran without `--all`;
   - item 55's base and changed paths were read: the run is not `no base`, `no merge base` or `git failed`;
   - for `--redo`, the case is not in HEAD's lock `cases`;
   - the hook's mapper (§6, `coverage_map`, as item 55 uses it to judge coverage), applied once per suite to that suite's cases of `F` over its locked files on the working tree, finds a single map, and it maps the case to exactly one file. When any case of the suite's `F` has no holder, the mapper finds no map, and no case of that suite is outside the RED;
   - that file is not one of item 55's changed paths, and it is in the merge base's tree, as read below.

   A case that does not map, a case of a suite whose map fails, and a case in an added, changed or untracked file are never outside the RED.

   **The base tree.** The files the other conditions leave are read in one call, `git ls-tree -r -z --name-only <merge base> -- <paths>`. `<merge base>` is the commit item 55 computed. `<paths>` are those files' repository-relative paths (the suite's root prefix plus the mapped path), deduplicated and passed as they are. A file is in the base tree only when an output entry equals its path byte for byte; output is NUL-terminated, so no quoting applies. The call is not made when no file is left to read. If the read fails (an exit status other than 0, a signal, a failure to start git), `red` retains conservatively: no case is left out in this run, and the lock is today's. When `red` writes the lock, it prints one line on stdout, where point 4's line would go: `shipcraft: every failing case is locked: could not read the base tree (<error>)`. `<error>` is git's first stderr line, or the exit status or signal. The row then has `unchanged_dropped: null`, which no other row has, so the log can count these runs. This is a stdout line, not an item 59 warning, because it says what the lock holds.

   **Same file as a locked case.** The hook runs the test files that hold `failing_at_red` cases, and it blocks on every failure in those files, whether or not the case is locked (`Selection::blocks` in `suite.rs`, `judge_suite` in `contract.rs`, README fixed points 49 and 50). So a case outside the RED is left out only when no case that stays in `failing_at_red` maps to the same file. Otherwise it stays locked, as today, because the hook would refuse it anyway (`case-broke`) and the lock could never close. "Stays" is judged on `F` less the cases outside the RED, before item 53's second run. One pass is enough: a case kept by this rule lives in a file that already holds a locked case. This matters most with the redo exemption. HEAD's lock holds a case of an unchanged file, and another case of that file was skipped at RED (so it is not in `cases`) and fails now. That second case stays locked.
2. **The guard.** After the same-file rule, the cases outside the RED are left out only when at least one case of `F` is not outside the RED. When every case of `F` is outside the RED, nothing is left out, and `red` locks them as today. That covers a RED whose tests were committed before `red` ran, the item 66 known limit, and its battery fixtures.
3. **What is locked.** The cases left out are removed from `failing_at_red`, and also from `cases`. Item 46's `redo-drops-case` compares the lock's `cases` with later runs, and CI refuses `case-broke` for a case in `cases` that fails without being in `failing_at_red`, so a failing case cannot stay in `cases` alone. Then, on the cases that remain:
   - item 30.2's `suite-without-tests` is judged on them;
   - item 53's second run reruns only their suites and judges only them, so `red-not-reproduced` means every remaining case passed;
   - `files` is hashed as today, so the files of the cases left out stay protected: while the lock is open, the hook and CI refuse any change to them `test-changed` (`check_files`, rule 4), and deleting one `locked-file-deleted`. A case left out is not required to pass to close the lock, but its test cannot be edited to make it pass. No new lock field is needed: `files` already expresses "protected", and `cases`/`failing_at_red` "required".
4. **Reported.** When cases are left out, `red` prints one line on stdout, after item 53's `not locked, passed on a second run` line (when printed) and before `RED locked at` (`RED redone at` for `--redo`), only when it writes the lock:

   `shipcraft: not locked, failing in test files this RED did not change: <cases>; CI runs them`

   `<cases>` are byte-sorted, with item 52's list formatting and control-character escaping. The `red` row gets `unchanged_dropped`, the number of cases left out, only on rows that write a lock and only when that number is above 0. Rows without it read as 0. This keeps the exact row-key checks of items 59, 65, 66 and 75 unchanged wherever nothing is left out.
5. **Why this rule, and not a run at the base.** The first proposal reran, at the base, the suites that hold such cases, in a temporary `git worktree add --detach` checkout, and left out only the cases that failed there too, by the same case id. It was weighed and not taken:
   - **Cost.** It is a second full run of those suites, on top of item 53's.
   - **Dependencies.** A fresh worktree has no `node_modules`, virtualenv or build output. The suite runs there only after the project's install command, which Shipcraft does not know (item 75's note on parked item 77: how a tag's dependencies are prepared is undecided). Without it the base run errors, and the answer is "unknown".
   - **The unknown case.** A base run that cannot run or errors must not drop silently, so it would lock the case, exactly as today. In the projects that need this rule most (Node workspaces), that would be the common outcome.
   - **The same answer.** In the battery, the base run gives the same answer as this rule in every case but one (`v1-59.ci-longest`, where a test file rewritten with identical bytes fails only because of the RED's change).

   This rule reads only git, costs nothing, and its wrong answer is bounded. A case the RED broke through a file it changed (point 6) is left out instead of locked, so it costs a CI round trip, never an escape, like item 55's and item 65's limits.
6. **A RED whose stub breaks an existing test through a support file.** For example, the RED changes a product stub or a helper, and an unchanged test file now fails. That case is outside the RED, so this item leaves it out and lists it, and the GREEN is not bound to make it pass. This is the outcome the spec picks:
   - item 55 already gives it on a narrowed run, where that test does not run at all;
   - the line names it, so the RED session sees it and can stop;
   - CI runs every suite at HEAD and refuses `suite-red` once the lock closes with the case still failing.

   The line says "failing in test files this RED did not change", not "already failing", because Shipcraft has not run the base and cannot tell the two apart.
7. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `red`'s base, changed paths, selection, fallbacks and their order, the selection line, item 66's comparison line and item 65's support line: keep.
   - The narrowed run's kept cases (only those of selected files): keep. Item 81 changes nothing there, except a redo's lock-file cases, which point 1 exempts.
   - The first run's refusals and their order (`[build]`, `check`, `no-report`, `error-before-assertions`, `group-failed`, `red-already-passes` on `F`, and for `--redo`, `redo-drops-case` before it): keep. `red-already-passes` is still judged on all of `F`.
   - `suite-without-tests`, item 53's second run and `red-not-reproduced`: keep, on the remaining cases (change: they no longer see the cases left out).
   - The lock: `format` 4, `files` hashed after the second run, `kind`, `reported_at`, `fixes_release`, the seal, `fs::write` of a new file, or for `--redo` the temporary file renamed over the old one. Keep. `cases` and `failing_at_red` lose the cases left out: change.
   - stdout: `RED locked at ...`, `RED redone at ...` with its three lists, the commit line, item 59's base warning and the GREEN guidance as the last line: keep. The new line: change.
   - The `red` row: `result`, `code`, `secs` (still the whole command), `selection`, `selected_files`, `fallback`, `rerun_dropped`, `redo`, item 75's fields: keep. `unchanged_dropped` is added as point 4 says.
   - The hook, CI, `close`, `status`, `report`, `pr` and `merge`: keep. CI still runs every suite at HEAD: a case left out that still fails makes `ci` refuse `suite-red` once no cycle is open, as it does today for any failure outside the lock.
   - Locks already written: keep.
   - `red --redo` when the new lock reads the same as HEAD's: it prints `shipcraft: RED unchanged: lock <path> already reads the same; nothing written.` and returns. It prints no `RED redone at` line, no three lists, no item 53 line and no GREEN guidance, and its row has no `rerun_dropped`: keep. Point 4's line is printed only when a lock is written, so it is not printed here, and that row has no `unchanged_dropped`. Cases left out still count in the comparison, so a redo that only leaves out cases HEAD's lock did not hold reads as unchanged.
   - The run-log row is best effort (`medicao::record`): it creates the common git directory's `shipcraft/` folder if missing, appends one line, and ignores any failure to create, open or write. One `red` row per command, whatever happens to it, and the exit code never depends on it: keep, also with `unchanged_dropped`.
   - Suite commands inherit `red`'s environment, `TMPDIR` included, and whatever they write in the working tree stays there. `red` creates each temporary JUnit report (`shipcraft-junit-<pid>-<nanos>.xml` under the temporary folder) and item 22's list files under `.git/shipcraft/tmp` (a folder it created is removed if empty), and removes them after each run: keep. The new base-tree read is one git call that writes nothing.
   - After a GREEN commit closes the lock, the post-commit hook (`close`) adds the closing commit `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>` and writes its own run-log row. The battery's GREEN-commit cases see both the commit and the extra row: keep. A lock with fewer cases can close in an earlier commit, but how it closes does not change.
8. **Section changes.** Item 53's "a test already failing on the branch is still the RED's to fix" now holds only when it is in a file the RED changed, or when every failing case is outside the RED (point 2). Item 55's known limit "a failure already on the branch that lives in a selected file is still locked" stays as written.
9. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:**
     - `v1-55.config` and `v1-55.removed` (Python and Node; `acceptance/lib/item55.sh`, `i55_verify`). `legacy55` is committed at `origin/HEAD` and unchanged, and it is left out next to the untracked failing `fresh55`. The check `any('legacy55' ...) == bool(reason)` becomes: `legacy55` is in neither key for these two modes, and it stays in both for `all`, `all-log-fails`, `support` and `committed`. `acceptance/pontos/55.md` changes with them.
     - `v1-65.monorepo-helper`, `v1-65.overlap-uncovered` and `v1-65.unmapped` (`acceptance/lib/item65.sh`): `legacy65` (api and jobs; suite b's in `overlap-uncovered`) is left out next to a failing case of an untracked or modified file. The expected `cases` and `failing_at_red` lose exactly those cases, and the row expects `unchanged_dropped` with their count. `acceptance/pontos/65.md` changes with them.
     - `v1-66.warnings` (`core66` and `landing66` left out; `jobs66` kept) and `v1-66.warnings-local` (`core66` left out) (`acceptance/lib/item66.sh`), with the same row field. Their warnings are unchanged. `acceptance/pontos/66.md` changes with them.
     - `v1-59.ci-longest` (`acceptance/lib/item59.sh`, `state()`): the api `test_anchor`, whose file is rewritten with the base's bytes and fails only because `api/value` changed, is left out (point 6's case). Its row key set gains `unchanged_dropped`. Its `ci` assertions (the missing `api:v2` cases) are unchanged, and so is `acceptance/pontos/59.md`'s line on it.

     No other assertion changes. The RED adjusts only the expected lock keys, the expected row keys and the new line in these cases.
   - **The version:** 2.35.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - **The redo exemption:** `v1-46.unchanged`, `v1-53.redo` (Python and Node; the flaky case is in HEAD's lock `cases`), `v1-60.lock-only`, `v1-60.growth`, `v1-65.redo-lock-only`, `v1-75.redo-unchanged`, `v1-75.legacy2-unchanged`, `v1-75.legacy3-unchanged`, and every other case of items 46 and 60.

       - `v1-65.helper-only`, `v1-65.helpers-only`, `v1-65.unmapped-helper`, `v1-66.committed`, `v1-66.committed-local`;

These are also item 53's wrapper cases (`v1-22.ambiente-red`, `v1-22.ambiente-build`, `v1-22.bash`, `v1-16a.1-red-build`).

   - **New cases** include:
     - the field shape: an untracked new failing test beside a committed failing test of another suite, on a full run;
     - the same with the committed failing test in the same suite;
     - a RED whose stub breaks an unchanged test while its own test fails, with the line listing it;
     - every case of `F` outside the RED, where nothing is left out;
     - each condition of point 1 turned off on its own (`--all`, `no base`, an unmapped case, an added file, an untracked file, a redo case of HEAD's lock);
     - a case left out of `cases` and `failing_at_red`, with `files` still hashing its file;
     - a GREEN commit that edits the test file of a case left out is refused `test-changed`, and one that deletes it `locked-file-deleted`, also in `shipcraft ci`;
     - the second run not rerunning a suite whose only failing cases were left out;
     - the row field present only when cases are left out;
     - a failing `git ls-tree` at the merge base (a git shim): nothing left out, the stdout line, `unchanged_dropped: null`;
     - a path whose base-tree entry is a folder of the same name, or a longer path with the same prefix: not in the base tree;
     - the same-file rule: a pytest `red --redo` whose HEAD lock holds a failing case of an unchanged file, and another case of that file was skipped at RED and fails now. The second case stays in `failing_at_red`, and the redo commit is judged as today. A `red` whose untracked test fails, and an unchanged file holds a failing case beside a case of `F` that is not outside the RED (an unmapped one), keeps that case too.
10. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:**
     - in POC-021's next after runs, locks that hold a case of a test file the RED did not change (1 in the 2.31.0 `after2-claude-existing` run);
     - over our own and adopters' next 10 REDs with `unchanged_dropped`, the cases left out that the RED had in fact broken, found when `ci` refuses `suite-red` on a case the line named and the base did not fail.
   - **Threshold:** 0 such locks in POC-021's runs; at most 1 case wrongly left out in those 10 REDs. Above that, the run at the base (point 5) is reconsidered as its own item.
   - **Revert:** `red --all` locks as today for one RED. A release drops the rule.
11. **Known limits.**
    - Shipcraft does not run the base, so a case the RED broke through a changed product or helper file is left out like one that already failed (point 6). CI catches it after the lock closes.
    - When the base equals `HEAD` (no remote, or working on the default branch), "unchanged since the base" means "already committed". A test committed before `red` alongside an uncommitted one is left out if it fails, although it may be the RED's own.
    - The guard keeps today's behavior when every failing case is outside the RED. A RED that changes only a helper, on a branch where an old test already fails, still locks that old failure.
    - A pre-existing failure that is left out still fails the project's CI (`suite-red`) after the lock closes. This item only keeps it out of the lock; making the base green is the project's work.
    - A case whose test file the RED rewrote with the same bytes is unchanged for git, and so outside the RED.

### The review lists every Blocking item it reproduces (item 82)

Since item 70, every review that blocked on this repository found exactly one Blocking item: PR #90 (item 78) blocked twice and PR #92 (item 76) three times, one finding per round, each a different real bug. Each round costs a RED, a GREEN, CI and another review. Neither built-in brief tells the reviewer to stop at the first Blocking item, but none tells it to go on either. It reproduces one and writes the review. Victor's coordinator decided (2026-10-31): ask for all of them in one round.

1. **The new paragraph.** Both built-in briefs, `nucleo/src/brief.md` and `nucleo/src/brief_after_ci.md`, gain one paragraph at the end of "## Rules for a Blocking item", after the sentence that ends `otherwise it is CHANGES REQUESTED.`, separated by a blank line:

   ```
   After you reproduce a Blocking item, keep going: check the rest of the diff against the
   spec and the intent, and list every Blocking item you reproduce, not only the first.
   Blocking: <n> counts all of them.
   ```

   Nothing else in either brief changes: the steps, the format, the ground rules, the placeholders and their substitution stay byte for byte.
2. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - Which brief is chosen (item 63: after green CI, or not), the substitutions (`{{WHAT}}`, `{{TITLE}}`, `{{SHA}}`, `{{BASE_SHA}}`, `{{TREE}}`, `{{WORK}}`, `{{COMMANDS}}`, `{{BODY}}`, `{{SPEC}}`, `{{DIFF}}`, `{{BATTERY}}`, `{{MINUTES}}`), the brief written to the review's work folder as `brief.md` and passed to the agent: keep.
   - The verdict parsing (first line, `Blocking: N`), fail closed, the statuses, reuse by `diff_sha256` and `text_sha256` (item 54), the run-log `review` rows and `after_green_ci`, the reviewer's environment (item 63 point 4), the queue: keep. A verdict that lists several Blocking items is parsed as today: only the first two lines count.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
   - Reuse: a verdict from before this item is still reused when the diff and the text match, since the brief's text is not part of either key: keep.
3. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** item 63's exact-bytes check of the brief that is not after green CI (`i63_brief` in `acceptance/lib/item63.sh`, the SHA-256 of the normalized brief, used by `v1-63.brief-off`, `brief-off-spoof`, `brief-review`, `brief-review-pr` and `brief-review-pr-spoof`): the pinned hash becomes the new brief's. Every other assertion of `i63_brief` stays, and its after-green-CI checks (`brief-local`, `brief-github`, `brief-local-docs`, `brief-github-docs`) keep passing unchanged, since the new paragraph does not mention the suite.
   - **The version:** 2.36.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-63.*` case; item 54's reuse cases (`v1-54.*`); item 64's brief and prompt checks (`acceptance/lib/item64.sh`); item 40's `src/brief.md` cases, which are about a project file of that name, not this brief.
   - **New cases:** each brief (after green CI and not) carries the paragraph, once, right after the CHANGES REQUESTED sentence and before `## Review format`; a fake reviewer that answers `Blocking: 2` with two sections gives `CHANGES REQUESTED`, `failure`, and a row as today.
4. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** Blocking items per blocking review round (today 1 in 5 of 5), and review rounds per blocked item, over the next 5 items whose review blocks at least once; and review `secs` (non-reused rows), which may at most double (about 60 to 90 s today after green CI).
   - **Revert:** if rounds per blocked item do not go down, or review time more than doubles, a release restores both briefs' text.
5. **Known limits.** The reviewer still has a time budget (`{{MINUTES}}`); a long list can cut its own checks short, and the brief already tells it to write what it has. Nothing makes the reviewer find more: the paragraph asks, and the metric says whether it does.

### `report --github` fills the waiting and the merge time from GitHub (item 79)

Item 76 left one gap open. With `ci = "github"` there is no local `ci` row, so every lock's `waiting` is empty: on this repository and on draken-platform, `report` has never shown a waiting time. And a lock merged outside `shipcraft merge` (through `gh` or the GitHub page) shows `merge_not_observed` with no merge time, so its `close_to_merge` and `red_to_merge` are empty too. Victor's decision (2026-10-31): `report --github` asks GitHub, through `gh`, for exactly those values. Without `--github`, `report` reads no network and prints what it prints today. Item 76's first draft had this point; its spec review split it out because it needs its own readers: paginated statuses and check runs, the completion of every required component, and which PR a lock went through.

1. **What `--github` asks, and for which locks.** `report [--json] [--since <tag>] [--github]`, flags in any order, each at most once; any other spelling, or a repeated flag, is today's usage refusal (exit 2, before any git or `gh` call). With `--github`, after item 76's reads, `report` *selects* each lock row whose `state` is `merged` or `merge_not_observed` and whose `waiting` is `null`, in the order the lock rows print. No other lock is asked about, and a value the run log already gives is never replaced.
   - **The PR.** For a `merged` lock, the `pr` of the `merge` row that gave its `merge_at`. For a `merge_not_observed` lock, the `pr` of the rows of layer `pr`, any result, that name the lock (`lock` or `locks`). A `pr` that is not a positive JSON integer is ignored. No PR left: no PR. Two or more distinct PR numbers: the lock is ambiguous (a later PR may have carried the lock again), and no call is made for it.
   - **The calls.** Every `gh` call runs in item 76's `<root>`, with stdin from `/dev/null`, stdout read, stderr discarded, and `report`'s environment after startup (`SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` already removed, as for every command, section 6) less item 76's repository-selecting git variables (so `gh`'s own git calls read `<root>`'s repository) plus `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED=1`.
