Which GitHub repository `gh` picks (its remotes, `gh repo set-default`, `GH_REPO`, `GH_HOST`) is `gh`'s choice, the same one `shipcraft pr` and `merge` made when they wrote the PR numbers. The calls:
     - `gh pr view <pr> --json number,state,mergedAt,headRefOid,mergeCommit`;
     - `gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<headRefOid>/status?per_page=100`;
     - for a `suite_checks` name, `gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<headRefOid>/check-runs?check_name=<name, percent-encoded as item 62 does>&filter=latest&per_page=100`.
     Each call runs in its own process group and has a 60 s limit covering its start, its output and its exit: at the limit the whole group is killed, and `report` stops waiting for the output, even when a descendant still holds the pipe. The call then counts as `gh timed out`, and no further `gh` call is made in this run: every selected lock not yet finished counts as `gh timed out` too, even one whose answers are already in hand. Each call's outcome (its answer, or `gh failed`, or `unreadable answer from gh`), keyed by its full argument list, is reused for every lock that needs the same call: two locks on one PR ask once, also when the call failed.
   - **Reading an answer.** stdout must be valid UTF-8 and parse as JSON (`serde_json`; on a duplicate key the last value wins); otherwise, or when its shape is not the one below, the answer is `unreadable answer from gh`. A call that cannot start, whose output cannot be read or whose exit cannot be waited for, or that exits non-zero or ends by a signal is `gh failed`, whatever its stdout holds; its process group is killed and reaped, any partial answer is discarded, and later calls go on.
   - **A time** is GitHub's UTC form `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`: a real Gregorian date with year 0001 to 9999, hours 00–23, minutes and seconds 00–59, read as Unix seconds with no normalization. Any other text is not a time.
2. **The steps for one lock, in order.** The first step that fails gives the lock's one `missing` reason, and no later step runs for it.
   1. *No PR:* `no pr in the run log`; several: `several prs in the run log`. No call is made.
   2. *The PR answer:* it must be an object with `number` an integer equal to `<pr>`, `state` a string, `mergedAt` a string or `null`, `headRefOid` a string, and `mergeCommit` either `null` or an object whose `oid` is a string; else `unreadable answer from gh`. Then `state` other than `MERGED`: `pr not merged`. Then `mergedAt` not a time, or `headRefOid` not 40 lowercase hex characters: `unreadable answer from gh`.
   3. *For a `merge_not_observed` lock only:* `mergeCommit.oid` must be present (`mergeCommit` not `null`) and 40 lowercase hex characters (else `unreadable answer from gh`), and `git merge-base --is-ancestor <oid> <default branch commit>` (item 76's git rules and default branch) must exit 0; exit 1 or any other exit (the commit is not in this clone): `pr not merged into <default branch>`. Once this passes, the lock's `merge_at` is filled with `mergedAt`, and `close_to_merge` and `red_to_merge` are computed from it by item 38's rules (`null` where their other end is). These stay filled whatever the later steps find. Its `state` stays `merge_not_observed`, and it still does not enter the day rows. A `merged` lock skips this step; its `merge` row already went into the default branch, and its `merge_at` stays the local one.
   4. *The config:* `<root>/.shipcraft.toml`, read once per run directly from that path (symlinks followed; no repository discovery, no `Repo::open`), and parsed by `Config::parse`, unchanged (its legacy-key, `shipcraft_min`, suite, path and role validation). The file missing, not readable, not valid UTF-8, or any error from `Config::parse`: `config unreadable`, with nothing printed. A config whose `[execution] ci` is `"off"`: `ci off`, since its `shipcraft/ci` status is an administrative `success`, not a CI run.
   5. *The required components,* in this order: `shipcraft/ci`, then each `suite_checks` name in config order. For each, its *matches* at `headRefOid` are the combined status's `statuses` entries (every page) whose `context` is its name and, for a `suite_checks` name only, the check runs (every page of its own call) whose `name` is its name. The status call is made at the first component; a component's check-run call when that component is reached. For each component in turn:
      - a call's answer must be a non-empty JSON array of pages, each an object with the same non-negative integer `total_count` and an array (`statuses`, or `check_runs`), the entries of all pages numbering that `total_count` (an entry seen on two pages counts twice), and every entry an object with string `context` and `state` (statuses) or string `name` and `status` and a string or `null` `conclusion` (check runs); else `unreadable answer from gh`;
      - no match: `<name> missing at head`;
      - a match that is not a status with `state` `success` or a check run with `status` `completed` and `conclusion` `success`: `<name> not green at head now`;
      - its *completion* is the latest, among its matches, of a status's `created_at` or a check run's `completed_at`; one that is not a time: `unreadable answer from gh`.
   6. *The waiting:* the CI time is the latest completion among the components. `waiting` is `merge_at` − the CI time; a negative value is not used: `ci finished after merge`. Otherwise `waiting` is filled, and a `merged` lock enters its day's `waiting_median` with it.
   Nothing else changes: the DORA object, `meta`, `gap` lines and every other field are computed as without `--github`, except the filled lock fields and the day rows' `waiting_median`.
3. **The output.**
   - **Per lock.** With `--github`, every `--json` lock row gains `"github": [...]`: the fields that went from `null` to a number through point 2, in the order `merge_at`, `close_to_merge`, `red_to_merge`, `waiting` (empty when none). In text, a lock line with any such field ends with `; from github: <names, comma-separated>`. `unobserved`, local stats and every other lock field are unchanged. Without `--github`, neither appears.
   - **The summary.** With `--github`, `--json` prints one `{"kind":"github", "selected": <int>, "merge_filled": <int>, "waiting_filled": <int>, "missing": {"<reason>": <int>, ...}}` line after the `dora` line and before any `gap` line. `merge_filled` and `waiting_filled` count lock rows (two locks on one PR count two); `missing`'s keys are sorted by byte order, in JSON and text. The text output prints one `github: selected <n>, merge filled <n>, waiting filled <n>; missing <reason> <n>, ...` line (or `; missing none`) after the DORA block. Every selected lock whose `waiting` was not filled counts once in `missing`, under its reason; `<name>` and `<default branch>` stand for the component's name and the default branch's ref as item 76 prints it.
   - **Failures** never refuse: `report` still exits 0 (item 76's `--since` refusal and the usage refusal excepted), and no `gh` output or error text is printed.
   - `report --github` writes nothing and appends no run-log row; `gh` is the only network it uses, and only with `--github`.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `report` without `--github`: keep, byte for byte, in text and `--json`, every item 38 and item 76 rule included (its run-log and git reads, `--since`'s refusal, exit 0 otherwise, nothing written).
   - `main.rs`'s `report` arguments: change, to also accept `--github` once, in any order.
   - `Config::load`, `Repo::open` and every command's config reading: keep; `report --github` calls neither `load` nor `Repo::open`, and writes nothing, runs no suite, check or build.
   - `main.rs`'s shared usage text, printed by `help`, `-h`, `--help` and no arguments (stdout, exit 0, followed by item 61's setup hint on stderr when it applies) and by every invalid-argument error (one stderr diagnostic, `shipcraft: error: invalid arguments: <args>; ` followed by the usage text on the same line, exit 2, no hint lookup): change only its `report` line, to `shipcraft report [--json] [--since <tag>] [--github]`; everything else in those paths: keep. The command table in §3 changes to match.
   - Item 76's git reads, their environment, the partial and shallow clone rules and the default branch: keep; `--github` adds only point 2's ancestry check, under the same rules. When git evidence is unavailable (item 76's `null` reasons), there is no `merge_not_observed` lock; `merged` locks are still selected.
   - `gh.rs`'s existing readers (`statuses`, item 62's `check`) and the commands that use them (`pr`, `status`, `merge`): keep; the new readers are separate.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists, met by the fixtures: keep.
5. **Touches.**
   - **The version:** 2.37.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:** `v1-76.arguments-unknown` (`acceptance/lib/item76.sh`) uses `--github` as its unknown flag; it now uses `--gitlab`, with the same assertions (exit 2, usage, no git call).
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-38.*` and `v1-76.*` case (none passes `--github`); `Config::parse`'s cases, since it is reused unchanged (`v1-30.1-raiz-absoluta`, `raiz-pai`, `raiz-pai-interno`, `glob-vazio`, `glob-absoluto`, `glob-pai`; `v1-47.*`; item 64's role and `shipcraft_min` cases); the usage-text cases, which do not pin the `report` line (`v1-61.hint-noargs`, `hint-help`, `hint-h`, `hint-longhelp`, `hint-config`, `hint-outside`, `hint-git-fails`; `v1-69.machine-noargs`, `machine-help`, `machine-h`, `machine-longhelp`, their `machine-nested-*` counterparts, `v1-69.invalid` and `invalid-local`); item 62's GitHub wait and check cases (`v1-62.*`); item 73's wait-line cases (`v1-73.*`); `v1-46.report-zero` and `v1-46.report-count`; item 54's reused-review cases; item 75's cases (`v1-75.*`); item 10's run-log check (`v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`); item 22's environment cases (`v1-22.ambiente-red`, `ambiente-ci`, `ambiente-verifica`, `ambiente-build`) and README fixed point 19, whose startup removal `--github`'s calls inherit; item 43's shared lock-reader cases that item 76 lists (`v1-43.seal`, `rewrite`, `legacy-close`, `mixed`, `legacy-prefixed`, `open-bare`, `open-uppercase`, `open-short`, `open-prefix`, `open-whitespace`, `closed-bare`, `closed-uppercase`, `closed-short`, `closed-prefix`, `closed-whitespace`).
   - **New cases** use fixture repositories with real git (item 76's style), run logs written by the battery, and a fake `gh` on `PATH` that answers from files with the real JSON shapes (`pr view` with `number`, `state`, `mergedAt`, `headRefOid`, `mergeCommit.oid`; the slurped combined status, an array of pages with `total_count` and `statuses[].context`, `state`, `created_at`; the slurped check runs, an array of pages with `total_count` and `check_runs[].name`, `status`, `conclusion`, `completed_at`), records each argv, and is never the network. They cover: a `merged` lock with `ci = "github"` filled from `shipcraft/ci` plus one suite check, the later completion winning; a `merge_not_observed` lock whose PR comes from its `pr` row, with `merge_at`, `close_to_merge`, `red_to_merge` and `waiting` filled; the same with a missing check, where the merge fields stay filled and `waiting` does not; a status list and a check-run list split over two pages; each `missing` reason; two locks on one PR asking once, also when that call fails; a lock named by two PRs' `pr` rows (`several prs in the run log`, no call); a lock with local `waiting` asked nothing; `report` without `--github` making no `gh` call; `--github` repeated (usage, exit 2, no call); a `gh` missing from `PATH` (exit 0, `gh failed`); a `gh` that sleeps past the limit (killed, `gh timed out`, no further call), and one whose background child keeps stdout open past the limit (same outcome, within the limit plus a few seconds); `ci = "off"` (`ci off`).
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** on this repository, `report --github` fills `waiting` for every selected lock merged from item 76 on that has one PR in the run log and whose components all completed green before its merge, each within 60 s of `mergedAt` minus the latest completion of `shipcraft/ci` and `acceptance` that `gh` shows (the local `merge` row is written after `gh pr merge` returns); and on draken-platform it shows a waiting time for the first time. The `missing` reasons are recorded per run, to see which limit costs most.
   - **Revert:** a release drops `--github`; `report` without it is unchanged.
7. **Known limits.**
   - The checks are read as they are now, not as they were at merge: `suite_checks` comes from today's config (a check added later reads `missing at head`; a check dropped since is not waited for, so when it was the last to complete the waiting comes out longer), only the head's latest check runs count (`filter=latest`; a later rerun replaces the run the merge saw), and a status's time is its latest post (`created_at` of the combined status's entry; a CI that posts `success` again moves it).
   - The GitHub repository is the one `gh` selects at `<root>`. Run `report --github` with the same selection (`GH_REPO`, `gh repo set-default`) as the `pr` and `merge` runs that wrote the PR numbers; a `merged` lock is not checked against git, so another selection can read another repository's PR of the same number.
   - A `merged` lock's `waiting` ends at the local `merge` row, written after `gh pr merge` returns, so it includes that command's latency and the machine's clock.
   - A lock whose `pr` row was written on another machine has no PR here.
   - `ci` comes from today's config too: a project that turned CI off counts every lock as `ci off`, also those judged while CI ran.
   - Squash or rebase merges: the ancestry check needs `mergeCommit` in this clone; a stale local default branch makes it fail, as item 76's states do.
   - GitHub rate limits and private-repository access are `gh`'s; a failure counts as `gh failed`.
8. **Readings fixed after the spec review (round 4).**
   - After a timeout, every selected lock processed later counts as `gh timed out`, also one that would fail at step 1 with no call; this overrides point 2's first-failure rule.
   - An OS or pipe error while reading `gh`'s output is `gh failed`; bytes read in full and then rejected (not UTF-8, not JSON, wrong shape) are `unreadable answer from gh`, whatever library call reads them.
9. **Open, no case (the GREEN's audit, 2026-10-31).** Readings the spec leaves open; the code's choice stands, and no case pins it:
   - A `gh` call that succeeds is not killed; its process group is killed only on a failure or the limit.
   - A local `merge_at` with a fraction, minus GitHub's whole-second CI time, gives a fractional `waiting`.
   - A green match whose `created_at` or `completed_at` is absent is not a time: `unreadable answer from gh`.
   - A `merged` lock's `mergeCommit` shape is still checked (`null`, or an object with a string `oid`); its hex form and ancestry are not.
   - `ci = "local"` is asked like `"github"`; only `"off"` gives `ci off`.
   - In text, `; from github: ...` ends the lock line, after `; not observed here: ...`.
   - The text `github:` line is printed also when nothing is selected (`selected 0, ...; missing none`).
   - The config is read once per run, whether or not it reads.

### `report`'s text output is readable (item 83)

On this repository, `report`'s text output prints machine values. Times are Unix seconds (`first row at 1793218604, ... (Unix seconds)`, `red at 1793220313`). Hook latencies are raw floats (`p50 12.331499999999998 s`), and so are shares (`approved on first review 0.6666666666666666`). Every duration is in seconds (`red to merge 4226 s`). Victor's decision (2026-11-01): the text output prints these values for people. `--json` stays the machine format, byte for byte.

1. **Times.** These times print in UTC as `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, for example `2026-10-29T14:16:44Z`:
   - the run log's first and last row, on item 38's opening line and on the DORA block's first coverage line (item 76);
   - a lock's `red at`, `close at` and `merge at`, including a `merge at` filled by `--github`;
   - the DORA `deploys` line's `first at` and `last at`.

   A time with a fraction prints its whole second, rounded down: 100.9 prints `1970-01-01T00:01:40Z`. The machine's time zone (`TZ`) never changes the output. Where both coverage sentences say `(Unix seconds)`, they now say `(UTC)`. A time outside years 0000 to 9999 is not pinned.
2. **Durations.** These durations print as `<h> h <m> min <s> s`:
   - a lock's red to close, close to merge, red to merge and waiting;
   - a day row's median red to merge and median waiting;
   - DORA's lead time and time to recover, median and p90.

   The value is first rounded to the nearest whole second; a value exactly halfway is not pinned. Leading zero units are left out, and inner ones are kept: `0 s`, `59 s`, `1 min 0 s`, `59 min 59 s`, `1 h 0 min 0 s`, `1 h 10 min 26 s`, `27 h 3 min 4 s`. Hours never roll into days. A negative duration that does not round to 0 prints `-` and then the form of its absolute value, for example `-1 min 5 s`.
3. **Hook and CI latencies.** Each p50 and p90, on the `totals:` line and in each lock's `hook` and `ci`, prints in seconds with exactly one decimal: `p50 0.6 s`, `p90 54.5 s`, `p50 9.0 s`, `p90 1245.3 s`. The value is rounded to the nearest tenth; a value exactly halfway is not pinned. They stay in seconds because each is one step's run time, not a span between steps.
4. **Shares and rates.**
   - These shares print as a whole percent, rounded to the nearest; a value exactly halfway is not pinned. Examples: `0%`, `67%`, `71%`, `100%`. The shares are a day row's `approved on first review`, DORA's `rework rate` and DORA's `change failure rate`.
   - DORA's `per day` prints with exactly two decimals, for example `0.33` or `2.00`, rounded to the nearest hundredth; a value exactly halfway is not pinned.
5. **Unchanged.** Everything else in the text output stays as it is today:
   - the lines, their order and their wording outside the values above;
   - counts, and `-` for an empty value;
   - `first approved true`/`false`;
   - the refusal maps (`refusals {"no-report":2}`);
   - `; not observed here: ...` and `; from github: ...`;
   - the `github:` line;
   - the DORA lines' `(n ..., missing ...; reason)` tails;
   - `gap:` lines.

   `--json` is unchanged, byte for byte, with every value still Unix seconds, seconds and raw shares. Exit codes, the usage refusal, `--since`'s refusal and stderr are unchanged. `--since` and `--github` select and fill exactly what they do today; only the text form of the values changes.
6. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `relatorio.rs`'s `text` and `dora_text` print every value with `serde_json`'s number form. Times, durations, latencies, shares and `per day` change as points 1 to 4 say. Everything else they print is kept.
   - The `--json` output and every computed value (`meta`, `lock`, `day`, `dora`, `github`, `gap`): keep.
   - `report`'s reads, its exit 0 and its refusals: keep. That covers the run-log reader (item 38: `serde_json`, a field of the wrong type read as empty, never a gap), repository discovery and git reads (item 76), and `--github`'s config read, `gh` calls and their environment (item 79, point 4, every verdict there kept). The formatting adds no validation or coercion of its own. It writes nothing, appends no run-log row, and makes no network call without `--github`.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists, met by the fixtures: keep.
7. **Touches.**
   - **The version:** 2.38.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
     - `v1-38.text` (`acceptance/lib/item38.sh`) checks that the first line holds `100` and `200`. It now checks for `1970-01-01T00:01:40Z` and `1970-01-01T00:03:20Z`, and still checks `machine` or `local` and the `lock-38-A` row.
     - `v1-76.text` (`acceptance/lib/item76.sh`) has two assertions: `T1-100` and `T3-800` appear as numbers on the opening line, and again on the DORA coverage line. Both now check their UTC forms.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - every other `v1-38.*` and `v1-76.*` case, including `v1-76.text-empty` and `since-text-coverage`, which check counts and words, not values;
     - item 79's text cases, `v1-79.text`, `text-unobserved`, `text-missing`, `summary-sorted` and `no-github-text`, which check only the `github:` line and the `; from github: ...` ending;
     - every `--json` case of items 38, 46, 76 and 79 (`v1-46.report-zero`, `report-count` among them);
     - item 75's cases (`v1-75.*`);
     - every case item 79's Touches list marks "must keep passing unchanged" (its config reader, item 43's lock reader, item 22's environment and item 10's run-log check among them).
   - **New cases** use run logs written by the battery after the fixture's last commit (replacing any hook rows), as item 38's do, and item 76's release fixtures for the DORA lines. Each runs with `TZ` set to a zone other than UTC. They cover:
     - each time position;
     - a fractional time;
     - each duration boundary above;
     - a negative duration;
     - one-decimal latencies, including a whole number (`9.0 s`);
     - each share as a percent, including `0%` and `100%`;
     - `per day` at two decimals;
     - `--github`'s filled `merge at` and waiting in the new forms;
     - `--json` against the same fixture: the exact line text, with today's number spelling (a whole number without `.0`, for example `"red_at":100`);
     - `-` still printed for an empty value.

     Cap: 40 new cases.
8. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** on this repository, every time, duration, latency and share in `shipcraft report`'s text reads as above (no Unix seconds, no float with more than the fixed decimals in those positions). The 2.37.0 and 2.38.0 binaries, run on the same frozen checkout and run log, print identical `--json` bytes.
   - **Revert:** a release that restores the old text form; `--json` is untouched either way.

### The flow rules `init` writes are matched to risk (item 84)

Field data from the Draken session (2026-11-01): from 11:29 to 16:15 (4 h 46 min) it shipped 8 small PRs, and a Shipcraft command ran during only 44% of that time. The rest was flow its agents invented, because the block `init` writes and the shipped briefs describe only the RED/GREEN cycle. Every change took the full cycle, even a name change (2 redos, 2 review rounds, 65 min). A test review before the lock applied every finding (rounds of 9, 12 and 21 findings). The full suite ran 3 times per change (the GREEN's ~2,000 tests, the hook, CI). Every PR review finding was applied, so non-blocking points opened new rounds. Our own flow already fixed all four (items up to 40 cases: median 13 min, p90 23 min). Victor's decision (2026-11-01): the block and the GREEN brief carry that flow, in as few words as carry it, because an adopter's agents read every line on every session.

1. **The new lines.** The current variants of item 75's block gain six lines, right after the line that starts `- Cycle:`, in this order, before the line that starts `- A behavior change:`. Every other line stays, byte for byte, in its order. Each is one line ending with `\n`:

   ``- Pick the lane first: a mechanical change, one that changes no behavior (text, config, data, content, a rename, docs), is committed directly once the tests it affects pass in one run, with no RED or GREEN; a behavior change takes the cycle.``

   ``- A change that needs more than about 40 new test cases is split before its RED.``

   ``- A review of the RED's tests before the lock takes at most one round and fixes contract bugs only (a test that asserts the wrong behavior).``

   ``- Locally, the GREEN runs only the tests the change affects; the full suite runs once, in CI (where CI is off, once before the PR).``

   ``- In PR review, only Blocking findings open another round; the others go to notes.``

   ``- Small fixes from one request go in one PR.``

   The outer double backticks only quote each line. None of the six names a `shipcraft` command, so item 61's rule leaves them the same in the local variant; item 61's root line stays where that rule puts it (right after the heading, before `- Cycle:`).
2. **Known blocks.** Item 75's block joins the known blocks: both variants, exactly as `init` writes them from item 75's merge (`c3a919d`, version 2.31.0) through 2.38.0, unchanged in that range. In the code it is the old block named for that range (for example `AGENTS_BLOCK_2_31`). `init` replaces it with the new block under item 61's rules: kept bytes before and after, mode kept, the temporary-file write. Every block that was known before this item (item 74's, item 70's, v2.25.0's, v2.22.0's, 2.14.0's) stays known and is now upgraded to the new block. A block that matches none of them (an edited block) is kept with the differs notice, as today. `CLAUDE.md`'s block does not change.
3. **The GREEN brief.** `.shipcraft/briefs/green.md`'s line that starts `- Running the other tests the change touches` is replaced, in place, by:

   ``- Run the locked tests and the other tests the change affects, not the full suite unless the change affects all of it: CI runs it once (where CI is off, once before the PR). Report a failure outside the locked tests; it is not a gate: the hook's verdict decides the commit.``

   Every other line of `green.md` stays, and its last line keeps naming the running version (item 70). `red.md`'s body does not change (none of its lines disagrees with the new block); a fresh `init` stamps it with the running version, 2.39.0, like `green.md`. `init`'s brief handling is unchanged: a missing brief is written; an existing brief equal to this version's (either variant) is kept silently; any other existing brief, including one an older version wrote, is kept with the differs notice (item 68). So an existing install keeps its old `green.md`, and the block, which wins where a brief disagrees, carries the rule.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `init`'s block handling (item 61, 68, 70, 74, 75): create, append once, replace a known block, keep an edited block with the differs notice, leave a symlink or non-regular file alone, the temporary-file write, kept prefix, suffix and mode, `CLAUDE.md` written only when `AGENTS.md` holds this version's block, and a failure that never undoes the init: keep. Two readings change: the block's text (point 1) and the set of known blocks (point 2).
   - `init`'s brief handling (item 68): the folder checks, the per-brief notices, the closing line naming only written briefs, the version stamp: keep. One reading changes: `green.md`'s text (point 3).
   - Item 70's point 4 list and item 74's point 4 list, every entry: keep.
   - Item 71's probe: with an npm workspace config, `init` runs the project's runner (`npx --no -- vitest list --filesOnly --json`) at the root before it writes the onboarding files, with the caller's environment and `TMPDIR`, waits up to 60 s, kills the process group on failure and prints its conversion or fallback notice; the runner may leave its own output or cache files, which are not in `init`'s rollback set. A core write failure rolls back what `init` recorded; an onboarding or brief failure never does. Keep.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists (run-log rows, including the hook's row on every commit; lock files; the post-commit closing commit; `TMPDIR`; a suite on a copy or in the working tree): keep. The fixtures meet them.
   - Nothing else changes: no command, refusal, hook verdict, lock or run-log row. The lines tell agents how to choose and size work; Shipcraft enforces none of them.
5. **Touches.**
   - **The version:** 2.39.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
     - the expected current block text, which gains point 1's six lines in both variants: item 61's `expected_block` (`acceptance/lib/item61.sh`), used by `v1-61.block`, `v1-61.local-block` and by every case of items 68, 70, 74 and 75 that builds the current block;
     - the structural checks `exact_block` in `item70.sh`, `item74.sh` and `item75.sh`, which remove the lines later items added to reach their historical block: each now also removes point 1's six lines, and its historical baseline stays as it is;
     - `BRIEF_BODIES['green.md']` (both variants) in `item70.sh`, `item74.sh` and `item75.sh`, which assert this version's brief after `init`: it gains point 3's line in place of the old one. Where those files write `BRIEF_BODIES` bytes stamped `2.25.0` as an older version's briefs, the old body stays (that is what 2.25.0 wrote).
     - item 68's `brief_contract` concept patterns for `green.md` (`acceptance/lib/item68.sh`): `CI.*?rest` becomes `CI runs it once`; every other pattern stays, and point 3's line meets each of them.
     - their `pontos` where they quote the block or the brief.

     In `item70.sh`, `item74.sh` and `item75.sh` one `BRIEF_BODIES` dictionary serves both this version's expected briefs and the briefs a fixture writes stamped `2.25.0`: split it into a current and a historical body before changing the current one, so no historical fixture changes.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** item 80's install-guard cases that read the onboarding files (`v1-80.red-brief`, `green-brief`, `unborn-one`, `index-once`, `absent-briefs`, `excluded`); every other case of items 61, 68, 70, 74 and 75 (the `edited-*`, `refused-*`, `status-*`, `folder-*`, `write-fails*`, `write-partial*`, `core-*` cases among them), and everything item 74's and item 75's Touches lists keep; item 71's and item 72's init cases, whose successful paths require `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and both briefs, and whose rollback cases (`v1-71.rollback`, `v1-71.rollback-cache`, `v1-72.rollback-file`, `v1-72.rollback-hooks`) require no onboarding file after a core failure.
   - **New cases** (cap: 40):
     - the current block's exact text after `init` on a fresh repository, machine and local;
     - upgrades from item 75's block in all four directions (bare or local input, bare or local install), checking the exact new block, the prefix and suffix bytes, the mode and the `CLAUDE.md` import;
     - an edited item 75 block kept with the differs notice and no `CLAUDE.md` import;
     - one older known block (item 74's) upgraded straight to the new block;
     - `green.md`'s exact text after `init` (machine and local), `red.md`'s body unchanged with the 2.39.0 stamp, and an existing 2.38.0 `green.md` kept with the differs notice;
     - a control: `init` on a repository already holding the new block keeps it with the "already has" notice.
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** in the first adopter sessions that run with this block (Draken's next 8 PRs after it moves to 2.39.0 or later), the share of session time spent outside Shipcraft commands drops from 56% (the 2026-11-01 sample), no mechanical change runs the RED/GREEN cycle, and no PR opens a review round for a non-Blocking finding.
   - **Threshold:** median wall time per small PR at most 18 min (half the sample's mean: 4 h 46 min over 8 PRs).
   - **Revert:** a release restores item 75's block and the old `green.md` line; the new block joins the known blocks so `init` upgrades it back.
7. **Known limits.**
   - The battery checks the text written, not whether an agent follows it.
   - "Mechanical", "the tests it affects", "Blocking" and "about 40 new cases" are the agent's judgment (spec review round 1 raised each; they stay planning rules, not checks); Shipcraft does not classify a change, and the hook still allows a commit with no open lock as today.
   - An existing install keeps its old `green.md` (point 3); only the block reaches it on `init`.
   - Draken stays on 2.31.0 until the coordinator moves it, so the metric starts then.

### Cleanup after the merge: a rule in the block and a line from `merge` (item 85)

Field data (2026-11-03, one Mac): 38 worktrees in Draken's repository, 29 of them on merged PRs; 15 merged item worktrees in Shipcraft's; about 200 GB of leftover battery folders in `/private/tmp`; the disk at 96%. Nothing tells an agent to clean up after a merge, and whoever creates a resource is the only one who knows it is theirs. Shipcraft stays a generic gate: it does not learn about worktrees, folders or servers, it deletes nothing and checks nothing. It states the rule and says it again at the moment it applies.

1. **The new line in the block.** The current variants of item 84's block gain one line, right after the line `- Small fixes from one request go in one PR.`, before the line that starts `- A behavior change:`. Every other line stays, byte for byte, in its order. It is one line ending with `\n`:

   ``- After a merge, clean up what this change created: its worktree, temp folders, and servers or databases you started. Never remove what you did not create, what other sessions may be using, or anything with unmerged work: report those instead.``

   The outer double backticks only quote the line. It names no `shipcraft` command, so item 61's rule leaves it the same in the local variant.
2. **Known blocks.** Item 84's block joins the known blocks: both variants, exactly as `init` writes them from item 84's merge (`1397349`, version 2.39.0). In the code it is the old block named for that version (for example `agents_block_2_39`). `init` replaces it with the new block under item 61's rules: kept bytes before and after, mode kept, the temporary-file write. Every block already known stays known and goes straight to the new block.
3. **The line from `merge`.** After a successful merge, right after the line `shipcraft: PR #<n> merged into <base>...`, `shipcraft merge` prints one more line on stdout, with and without `--no-review`:

   ``shipcraft: clean up what this change created (worktree, temp folders, servers you started).``

   It is advice: nothing reads the state of the machine, it authorizes no deletion by itself, and the exit code stays 0. It prints after a merge into any base, the default branch or not. A refused merge, or a merge that fails in `gh`, does not print it.
4. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - Item 84's point 4 list, every entry, the npm workspace probe among them: keep.
   - `init`'s block handling (items 61, 68, 70, 74, 75, 84): create, append once, replace a known block, keep an edited block with the differs notice, leave a symlink or non-regular file alone, the temporary-file write, kept prefix, suffix and mode, `CLAUDE.md` written only when `AGENTS.md` holds this version's block, and a failure that never undoes the init: keep. One reading changes: the block's text (point 1) and the set of known blocks (point 2).
   - `init`'s brief handling (item 68, 70, 84): the briefs' bodies do not change; a fresh `init` stamps them with the running version, 2.40.0; an existing 2.39.0 brief gets the differs notice, as any older brief does: keep.
   - `merge` (items 5, 42, 62, 73, 79): its refusals, the order of its checks, the `--match-head-commit` pin, the first success line, and captured `gh` output: keep. One run-log row on a successful merge and none on a refused or failed one, overriding the standing-effects wording for this command: keep. The fetch of the base before the status checks, the `--no-review` comment posted before the merge attempt and left in place if the pinned merge then fails, and the head fetch for attribution: keep. `merge` deletes no branch, as today: keep.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists (run-log rows, lock files, the post-commit closing commit, `TMPDIR`, a suite on a copy or in the working tree): keep.
   - Nothing else changes: no refusal code, hook verdict, lock or config.
5. **Touches.**
   - **The version:** 2.40.0 in §11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
     - the expected current block text, which gains point 1's line in both variants: item 61's `expected_block` (`acceptance/lib/item61.sh`), used by `v1-61.block`, `v1-61.local-block` and by every case of items 68, 70, 74, 75 and 84 that builds the current block;
     - the structural checks `exact_block` in `item70.sh`, `item74.sh` and `item75.sh`, which remove the lines later items added to reach their historical block: each now also removes point 1's line, and its historical baseline stays as it is;
     - item 84's cases that assert "the block after `init`": they get the new block; its cases that write item 75's block as input keep it;
     - the version stamp in the expected briefs of items 70, 74, 75 and 84, where they read the running version; a brief a fixture writes stamped with an older version stays;
     - their `pontos` where they quote the block.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other case of items 61, 68, 70, 74, 75 and 84; item 80's install-guard cases that read the onboarding files; every case of items 5, 62 and 79 that runs `merge`; `v1-42.squash`, `absent`, `merge`, `head-config`, `no-review`, `invalid-string`, `invalid-number`, `invalid-bool`, `missing-ci`, `missing-review`, `no-review-missing-ci`, `merge-fails`, `closed-no-review-waived`; `v1-64.reader-merge`; `v1-73.control-merge`. The spec review found no case that asserts the whole stdout of a successful merge.
   - **New cases** (cap: 40, estimate 14):
     - the current block's exact text after `init` on a fresh repository, machine and local;
     - upgrades from item 84's block in all four directions (bare or local input, bare or local install), checking the exact new block, the prefix and suffix bytes, the mode and the `CLAUDE.md` import;
     - an edited item 84 block kept with the differs notice and no `CLAUDE.md` import;
     - one older known block (item 75's) upgraded straight to the new block;
     - a control: `init` on a repository already holding the new block keeps it with the "already has" notice;
     - `merge` success prints the new line right after the merged line, exit 0, with and without `--no-review`;
     - `merge` refused (no CI status) and `merge` failing in `gh` do not print it.
6. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** over each adopter's next 5 merges after it runs a block with this line (Draken, draken-platform): worktrees on merged PRs left in that repository: each branch from `git worktree list --porcelain`, looked up with `gh pr list --state merged --head <branch>`. A detached worktree, or a branch whose lookup fails, is not counted, and the readout says how many were left out.
   - **Threshold:** at most 1 left, against 29 of 38 on 2026-11-03.
   - **Revert:** at the first report of an agent removing someone else's work because of this rule, a release restores item 84's block and drops the `merge` line; the new block joins the known blocks so `init` upgrades it back.
7. **Known limits.**
   - The battery checks the text written, not whether an agent follows it.
   - "What this change created" and "unmerged work" are the agent's judgment: after a squash merge the branch's commits are not ancestors of the base though the change landed, so an agent that is unsure reports. Shipcraft neither classifies nor verifies it.
   - A resource shared between sessions, like one test database server, stays: the rule says not to remove it, and a leak inside it is that project's to fix.
   - The line reaches an existing install only on its next `init` after a release; `merge`'s line reaches it when the binary is upgraded.

### Old tests do not pin the whole block (item 86)

Item 85 added one line to the block and had to edit 5 older test files by one line each, plus 6 fixed points, because they compared the block byte for byte. `shipcraft red` selects each edited file and runs it whole: 380 cases instead of 24, 395 s instead of about 150 s. This item changes tests only: no product code, no version.

1. **Old blocks stay literal.** A frozen block from an old item stays a literal fixture for upgrades from that known version, which keep working.
2. **Tests of the current block check what they own.** The start and end markers stay exact, and every line the test owns is there once, in its frozen order, as an ordered subsequence of the block's lines. Lines newer items add are allowed. `has_lines` in `acceptance/lib/item61.sh` does this, for the bare and the local variant.
3. **Upgrades compare to the current block.** A case that upgrades a known old block gets the current block from a public `init` in a clean project (`clean_block` in `acceptance/lib/item61.sh`), and its prefix, current block and suffix stay byte exact.
4. **One pin.** `v1-86.exact-bare` and `v1-86.exact-local` in `acceptance/lib/item86.sh` are the only cases that compare the whole current block byte for byte, so an unexpected line is still caught. An item that adds a block line adds it to `PIN` there, in one line, with a note in `acceptance/pontos/86.md` if the rule changes. Items 61, 68, 70, 74, 75, 84 and 85 and their fixed points are not edited. A line removed or changed on purpose still edits the tests that own it.

- **Metric:** the next item that adds a block line edits 0 of `acceptance/lib/item{61,68,70,74,75,84,85}.sh`, and its `red` selects only its own file plus the pin holder.
- **Revert:** revert the PR.

### A failed review says why, and a passing failure is tried again (item 87)

Wrong-verdict note. Introduced in v2.0.0 (commit 4f9d784, checked with `git log -S`). Found on 2026-11-04 at 18:40 -03, on PR #104 here and in draken-platform, during a Codex failure: `shipcraft pr` printed `the reviewer exited with code 1: 44,605`, which is the token count, while the session's error was `stream disconnected before completion: Unable to verify model access right now. Please retry.` The earlier layer that should have caught it is the battery: the `quota` stub writes one line to stderr, so taking the last line looked right.

Field data (run logs of this Mac, 2026-11-04): 7 reviews of 371 ended without a verdict by a non-zero exit, 6 of them in that failure, and a person reran each. That failure lasted at least 15 minutes, so the retry below would not have ended it: the retry is for short failures, and the reason line is the certain gain.

1. **The reason.** On a non-zero exit, the reason after `the reviewer exited with code <code>: ` comes from the reviewer's stderr, read as bytes and decoded lossily as UTF-8 (one bad byte no longer loses the stream; an unreadable file reads as empty), split on `\n`, each line trimmed of ASCII whitespace. It is the last line that starts with `ERROR:` (exact case, no ANSI stripping); when none does, the last line that holds an ASCII letter and does not start with `tokens used` (any case); when none does, empty. Nothing else about the message changes. A timeout, a start failure and a malformed verdict keep their reasons.
2. **The retry.** When the exit is non-zero and that reason holds the exact bytes `Please retry`, the review runs again, in a new session and a new isolated copy, up to 3 attempts in all.
   - Between attempts it waits `[review] retry_wait` seconds and holds no machine slot: each attempt enters the queue as a review does today, so a wait does not block other runs. The first line of stderr each attempt prints today stays, and before a wait it prints one line: `shipcraft: review attempt <n> of 3 gave no verdict (<reason>); trying again in <s> s`.
   - Every other outcome ends the review as today, with no retry: approval, changes requested, a refusal or malformed verdict with exit 0, a timeout, a start failure, and a non-zero exit whose reason lacks those bytes (quota, for example). A review that fails closed still never approves.
   - The last attempt's outcome is the review's outcome. Its text, blocking count and reason are published, and the verdict, the status and the exit code are built from it as today.
   - All attempts use the original request: the same sha, base, title, body, config and reuse keys, whatever changes during a wait. `[review] timeout` applies to each attempt on its own; queue waits and retry waits have no overall limit.
   - `<n>` is the failed attempt's number from 1. The line prints only when another attempt will run, never after attempt 3 and never with `retry_wait = 0`.
   - The slot guard is dropped before the wait, and the next attempt takes a new ticket. An error that today returns before a row is written (the queue, the temp folder, the worktree, the brief) still returns as today on any attempt: it writes no row, and it ends the retries.
   - `shipcraft pr`, `shipcraft review` and `shipcraft review --pr` all retry. The refusal's why line becomes `review fails closed: without a well-formed verdict there is no approval, and shipcraft retries only a "Please retry" failure`.
3. **`[review] retry_wait`.** Whole seconds, default `120`, checked as `[review] timeout` is. `0` turns the retry off: one attempt; point 1's reason stays. The config table gains the row. Any explicit value, `0` included, needs `shipcraft_min` of `2.41` or more, refused the way item 62's `suite_checks` and item 64's `[roles]` are, because an older binary ignores the key and would reach another verdict.

**The run log.** Each attempt writes the `review` row it writes today, with its own tokens and no new field, so `report` counts each failed attempt as an error. A row's `secs` holds its queue time and its run, as today, and no retry wait. The `pr` row stays wall clock, so it holds the waits.

**Metric:** on the next failures with `Please retry`, the share of reviews that reach a verdict with no rerun by a person, and 0 reasons without a letter in `pr` rows. Cost: up to 2 waits of 120 s, and the tokens a failed attempt spends (up to about 49,000 seen). **Revert:** `retry_wait = 0` in a repository, or revert the PR.

**Touches.** Read first, and keep:
- Item 4: `v1-4.3-recusa-do-revisor`, `v1-4.3-saida-nao-zero` and `v1-4.3-tempo-esgotado` stay one attempt. The stub's `quota` text has no `Please retry`.
- Item 7: `v1-7.1-fila-um-slot`, `v1-7.1-fila-dois-slots`, `v1-7.2-fila-posicao`, `v1-7.2-fila-tempo-libera`.
- Item 24: `v1-24.json-zero`, `v1-24.json-null`.
- Item 63: `v1-63.publish-fails-local`, `v1-63.publish-fails-review-pr`.
- Item 64: `v1-64.row-error`, `v1-64.row-error-pr`, `v1-64.row-no-start`, `v1-64.row-no-start-pr`.
- Item 82: `v1-82.two-local`, `v1-82.two-off`, `v1-82.two-review-pr`, `v1-82.publish-local`, `v1-82.publish-review-pr`.
- `acceptance/README.md`, fixed point 5 ("Each PR invocation runs the reviewer once"): superseded. It gains "except a `Please retry` failure (item 87)".
- Items 54 and 63: reuse reads only `passed` and `refused` rows, and `after_green_ci` stays on each row.
- Item 38 and 76: `report` reads `review` rows as today.
- Items 62 and 79: `pr`'s `review-error` refusal and `merge`'s checks.
- Item 69 and the version cases: 2.41.0.

**Current behavior**, each marked keep or change:
- `revisao::run` enters the queue once and writes one row per call: change to once per attempt.
- `execute` makes a new copy and removes it at the end: keep.
- The non-zero reason is the last non-empty stderr line: change as point 1.
- "The reviewer runs once per `shipcraft pr` and is not retried automatically": change as point 2.
- `shipcraft pr`, `shipcraft review` and `shipcraft review --pr` all call `run`, so all retry: change.
- The `review-error` refusal's why line says shipcraft does not retry automatically: change as point 2.
- `execute`'s temp folder, its `brief.md`, `ultima-mensagem.md`, `saida.txt` and `erros.txt`, the forced worktree removal and the prune: keep, per attempt.
- `fila::enter`'s queue files, its position line and the unlock on drop: keep, per attempt.
- `shipcraft pr` pushes and creates or edits the PR before the review, and those stay when the review fails: keep. Its summary comment and its own row: keep, once.
- `medicao::record` ignores an append failure: keep.
- The pending status posted before the review and the final status are posted once per review, not per attempt: keep.

### `export` makes a branch without Shipcraft's files, for a project that does not use Shipcraft (item 135)

Found on 2026-11-18 by POC-051 (simulated adopters, `poc/051-simulated-adopters/FICHA.md`, queue item 2, seen in four of six repositories). Runs 1 to 4 were contributor clones of open-source projects that do not use Shipcraft (fastapi, excalidraw, cli/cli, httpx). In each, the branch meant for upstream carried Shipcraft's setup commit (`.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/`, the `AGENTS.md` block, a `CLAUDE.md`, a hook manager change), the lock inside the RED commit and the `chore(shipcraft): close lock` commit: in fastapi 6,068 of the 6,080 diff lines were Shipcraft's, and in cli/cli the block was appended to the project's own tracked `AGENTS.md`. Every run had to strip them by hand. This is a new command, not a fix: no earlier contract said Shipcraft keeps its files out of a branch for another project (`kind = feature`, 2.78.0).

**Reproduction, on main (2.77.0):** a scratch "upstream" repository with `src/calc.py`, `tests/test_calc.py`, `AGENTS.md` (`# Upstream rules`) and `.gitignore` (`.venv/`) on `main`, cloned; in the clone, a `.venv` with pytest, a branch `fix`, `shipcraft init --lang python`, `git add -A && git commit -m "add shipcraft"`, a failing `tests/test_sub.py` and a stub, `shipcraft red`, the RED commit, the GREEN commit (the hook closes the lock in its own commit). Then `git log --oneline origin/main..` lists 4 commits (`add shipcraft`, `test: sub`, `feat: sub`, `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>`) and `git diff --stat origin/main...` lists 9 files, 7 of them Shipcraft's (`.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/briefs/green.md`, `.shipcraft/briefs/red.md`, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, `.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`); only `src/calc.py` and `tests/test_sub.py` belong upstream. No command removes them.

**Where the lock lives, and the choice: a separate clean branch, settled here.** Three ways were weighed.
- **(a) A contributor mode chosen at `init`**, where the config, briefs and the `AGENTS.md` block are untracked (`.git/info/exclude` or the git directory) and locks are stored outside the tracked tree. It weakens the guarantee: the hook judges a commit against a lock committed with the RED, and CI re-checks every commit against the locks in git history (section 2); a lock outside the tree has no history, so CI, a fresh clone and a second worktree cannot see it, a GREEN commit made with `--no-verify` is no longer caught, and the audit trail lives in one machine's git directory. It also adds a second mode to every command that reads locks or the config (`red`, `check`, `close`, `ci`, `status`, `report`).
- **(b) Keep today's commits on the working branch, and add one step, `shipcraft export`, that writes a second branch** holding the same commits without Shipcraft's paths and without the commits that change only those, and names what it left out. The guarantee is unchanged: the working branch still has the lock in the RED commit, the hook judged every commit there and `shipcraft ci --base <ref>` re-checks them as today. The exported branch is outside the guarantee by design: it has no lock, no hook runs while it is written, and the project it goes to does not run Shipcraft; it is derived only from commits the guarantee already judged, after the lock is closed (point 1's `export-lock-open`). The audit trail is the working branch, which export never changes. Existing configs need nothing: `export` reads no config, and no file format changes.
- **(c) A mix** (untracked install files, tracked locks, plus export) still needs (b)'s step for the lock and the close commit, and keeps (a)'s second install path.

(b) is chosen: it is the only one that leaves section 1 as it is, adds no mode, and gives the clean branch this item is for. Untracked install files (part of (a)) can be a later item if adopters ask for a working tree without them.

Everything in sections 1 and 2 and in items 3, 110 and 130 holds unless a point below changes it: locks, the hook, `ci`, `pr`, `merge`, their refusals, rows and files written.

1. **`shipcraft export --base <ref> <branch>`.** Also accepted in the order `shipcraft export <branch> --base <ref>`. `<ref>` and `<branch>` are single arguments that do not start with `--`. Any other command line starting with `export` (no `--base`, `--base` twice or without a value, no branch or two, an unknown flag) fails as other commands' invalid arguments do: `shipcraft: error: invalid arguments: <args>; <usage>` on stderr, exit 2, nothing else run. Outside a git repository it stops as `ci` does today. `export` reads no `.shipcraft.toml`, so it runs in a repository without one.
   - **How it reads git.** Every git command `export` runs gets `-c core.hooksPath=<an empty temporary folder>` (so no hook runs, `reference-transaction` included) and the environment `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` and `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` (no object is fetched on demand, and replace refs and grafts are ignored). A git command that fails where this section does not name an outcome (a missing or unreadable object or tree, a partial or shallow clone missing an object `export` needs, a full disk, a ref that cannot be created) is a failure: `shipcraft: error: <what failed>`, exit 2, before anything is printed on stdout; the branch is not created, the temporary index and folder are removed (best effort), and objects already written may stay unreachable. Absence is decided only from a successful read: a tree that cannot be listed is a failure, never "no lock" or "no config".
   - **Range.** `<base>` is the commit `git rev-parse -q --verify <ref>^{commit}` prints; when that fails, `shipcraft: error: --base <ref> is not a commit in this repository`, exit 2 (`ci`'s text). HEAD must be a commit; an unborn HEAD fails `shipcraft: error: HEAD has no commit`, exit 2. The range is `git rev-list --reverse --topo-order <base>..HEAD` (oldest first). The **fork point** `F` is the first `parent` of the oldest commit of the range as its raw object names it (`git cat-file commit`, not `rev-list --parents`, so a shallow boundary still names it), or HEAD itself when the range is empty. When the oldest commit's object has no `parent` header (`<ref>` and HEAD share no commit), `shipcraft: error: <ref> and HEAD share no commit`, exit 2, judged at its place in point 2's order. When `F` is named but its object is missing (a shallow clone), reading it is a git failure as above.
   - **Shipcraft's paths** are these paths at the repository root, compared byte for byte: `.shipcraft.toml`, `.sc2.toml`, `.shipcraft` and every path under `.shipcraft/`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.husky/commit-msg`, `.husky/post-commit`, and the lefthook files `lefthook.yml`, `.lefthook.yml`, `lefthook.yaml`, `.lefthook.yaml` with their `lefthook-local` names (`lefthook-local.yml` and so on): the files `init` writes or edits, and the hook integration files section 2 already names. Each of these names also covers every path under it as a folder (`.shipcraft/locks/x.json`, and `AGENTS.md/guide.md` when a commit turns `AGENTS.md` into a folder), so a name and the paths under it are always taken from the same tree. A path with the same name in a subfolder (`docs/AGENTS.md`, `pkg/.shipcraft.toml`) is not one of them.
   - **The exported tree** of a commit `C` of the range is `C`'s tree with every entry at or under a Shipcraft path removed, and `F`'s entries at or under the same paths put in their place (same object and mode). Every other path, mode, symlink and submodule entry is `C`'s. One exception keeps the tree possible: when `C`'s tree has a non-folder entry (a file, symlink or submodule) at a folder above a Shipcraft path (`.husky` turned into a symlink while `F` has `.husky/commit-msg`), `F`'s entries under that folder are not put back and `C`'s entry stays.
   - **The exported commits.** For each `C` of the range, oldest first: when its exported tree equals the tree of the previous exported commit (`F`'s tree for the first), `C` is left out; otherwise a new commit object is written, with `git hash-object -t commit -w --stdin`, whose bytes are exactly `tree <tree>\nparent <previous exported commit, or F>\nauthor <A>\ncommitter <K>\n\n<M>`. `C` is read raw with `git cat-file commit`: its headers end at the first empty line, `<A>` and `<K>` are the bytes after `author ` and `committer ` on their header lines, and `<M>` is every byte after that empty line. Every other header of `C` (`gpgsig`, `encoding`, `mergetag`, and their continuation lines, which start with a space) is not copied. A commit object `export` cannot parse this way (no `author` or `committer` line) is a git failure as above. So the same range always exports to the same commit ids. A commit that changes only Shipcraft's paths, an empty commit, and the closing commit are left out; the RED commit keeps its tests without its lock.
   - **The branch.** `refs/heads/<branch>` is created at the last exported commit with `git update-ref --no-deref refs/heads/<branch> <tip> ""` (created only if absent, never through a symbolic ref; when it appeared since point 2's check, or `refs/heads/<branch>/...` or a prefix of it exists, update-ref fails and so does `export`, exit 2). Nothing else changes: HEAD, the current branch, every other ref, the index and the working tree stay byte for byte as they were; no hook runs; nothing is fetched or pushed; no run-log row is written (like `init`). The new branch's reflog entry and the new objects are the only writes. The exported trees are built with a temporary index file outside the repository's (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`), removed before `export` ends.
   - **Output**, on stdout, exit 0, in this order:
     `shipcraft: exported <n> of <t> commit(s) in <ref>..HEAD to <branch> (<tip>), on <F>, without Shipcraft's files`
     then one line per left-out commit, oldest first: `shipcraft: left out <c> <subject>: no change outside Shipcraft's files`
     then, when at least one commit of the range changes an entry at or under a Shipcraft path (compared with its own parent, in the original history; a change undone by a later commit still counts): `shipcraft: kept as in <F>: <paths>; add any change of yours to them by hand`
     then: `shipcraft: next: push <branch> to your fork and open the PR from it; this branch keeps the locks and the hook's history`
     `<tip>`, `<F>` and `<c>` are 7-character ids (`git.rs`'s `short`). `<subject>` is the first line of the commit's message (up to the first `\n`), decoded as UTF-8 with invalid bytes replaced, each control character replaced by one space; `(no subject)` when that line is empty. `<paths>` lists those Shipcraft paths in byte order, joined by `, `, each shown once by its name from point 1's list (a change under `.shipcraft/`, or to `.shipcraft` itself, shows as `.shipcraft/`; a change under `AGENTS.md/` shows as `AGENTS.md`).
2. **Refusals**, each on stderr with exit 1, writing no ref, no index, no working tree change and no row (an object written to the object store before a refusal may stay, unreachable). They are judged in this order, after the argument, repository, `<ref>` and HEAD checks of point 1:
   - `export-uses-shipcraft`, when `<base>`'s root tree has an entry `.shipcraft.toml` or `.sc2.toml` (`<file>` is `.shipcraft.toml` when it has both):
     `shipcraft refused: <ref> has <file>, so this project uses Shipcraft and its files belong in the PR`
     `Why: export leaves out Shipcraft's files for a project that does not use Shipcraft; nothing was written`
     `Next: open the PR from this branch with shipcraft pr`
   - `export-lock-open`, when HEAD's tree holds a lock that is not closed: an entry of type blob (any mode, symlinks included) directly in `.shipcraft/locks/` whose name ends with `.json` (`.json` alone included), and whose content is not closed. Closed means: the bytes are valid UTF-8 with no byte order mark, parse with `serde_json` as one value (on a duplicate key the last value counts, as `serde_json` reads it), that value is an object, and its `state` is the string `closed`. Anything else counts as not closed; the seal and the rest of the lock are not checked here. `<path>` is the first such path in byte order:
     `shipcraft refused: HEAD has an open lock, <path>, so its locked tests do not pass yet`
     `Why: the branch you send upstream should hold finished work, and the lock closes only when its cases pass; nothing was written`
     `Next: finish the GREEN (the hook closes the lock when its cases pass), then run shipcraft export again`
   - `shipcraft: error: <ref> and HEAD share no commit` (exit 2) from point 1 is judged here.
   - `export-merge`, when the range holds a commit with two or more parents; `<c>` is the first that `git rev-list --reverse --topo-order --min-parents=2 <base>..HEAD` prints:
     `shipcraft refused: <c> in <ref>..HEAD is a merge commit, so export cannot replay the branch as one line of commits`
     `Why: export writes each commit again on top of the one before it, and a merge has more than one parent; nothing was written`
     `Next: rebase the branch onto <ref> (git rebase <ref>), then run shipcraft export again`
   - `<branch>` must not start with `-` and must pass `git check-ref-format refs/heads/<branch>`; otherwise `shipcraft: error: <branch> is not a valid branch name`, exit 2.
   - `export-branch-exists`, when `refs/heads/<branch>` exists (the current branch included): `git rev-parse -q --verify refs/heads/<branch>` exits 0, or `git symbolic-ref -q refs/heads/<branch>` exits 0 (a symbolic ref, even one whose target is gone); any exit of either other than 0 and 1 is a git failure:
     `shipcraft refused: branch <branch> already exists`
     `Why: export never moves or overwrites a branch; nothing was written`
     `Next: pick another name, or remove <branch> yourself if you no longer need it, then run shipcraft export again`
   - `export-empty`, when the range is empty or every commit of it is left out:
     `shipcraft refused: no commit in <ref>..HEAD changes a file outside Shipcraft's files`
     `Why: the branch would hold nothing to send upstream; nothing was written`
     `Next: commit your change on this branch, then run shipcraft export again`
   - `<ref>` and `<branch>` in these lines are printed as given, `<c>` as a 7-character id; `<path>` and the `kept as in` paths are read as raw bytes (`git ls-tree -z` and `git diff-tree -z`, never git's quoted output, so `core.quotePath` does not matter) and printed decoded as UTF-8 with invalid bytes replaced. In all of them each control character is replaced by one space.
3. **`pr` points at it, and the docs.**
   - `pr`'s `origin-not-writable` refusal (item 130, point 2), the one a contributor clone of an upstream project gets, keeps its first two lines; its `Next:` line gains, at its end: ``; for a project that does not use Shipcraft, `shipcraft export --base <base> <new branch>` first makes a branch without Shipcraft's files`` (`<base>` as item 130 prints it; `<new branch>` literally). `no-origin` and `origin-unverified` do not change.
   - The usage text's line ``     | shipcraft ci --base <ref> [--pr]`` becomes ``     | shipcraft ci --base <ref> [--pr] | shipcraft export --base <ref> <branch>``; nothing else in it changes.
   - Section 3's table gains, after the `ci` row: `` | `export --base <ref> <branch>` | For a project that does not use Shipcraft: writes `<branch>` with the commits of `<ref>..HEAD` without Shipcraft's files and without the commits that change only them, and names what it left out (item 135). Changes nothing else. Refuses `export-uses-shipcraft`, `export-lock-open`, `export-merge`, `export-branch-exists`, `export-empty`. | ``
   - Section 7's table gains five rows, in its alphabetical order, between `execution-invalid` and `foreign-hook`: `export-branch-exists` (`export` to a branch that exists, item 135), `export-empty` (`export` with no commit that changes a file outside Shipcraft's files, item 135), `export-lock-open` (`export` while HEAD has a lock that is not closed, item 135), `export-merge` (`export` over a range with a merge commit, item 135), `export-uses-shipcraft` (`export` when the base has `.shipcraft.toml` or `.sc2.toml`, item 135).
   - The README's Commands table gains, after the `shipcraft ci` row: `` | `shipcraft export --base <ref> <branch>` | For a project that doesn't use Shipcraft: writes `<branch>` with your commits since `<ref>`, without Shipcraft's files (config, locks, briefs, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, hook manager files) and without the commits that change only them. Push it to your fork and open the PR from it. | ``. And a subsection right before `### Removing Shipcraft`: `### Contributing to a project that doesn't use Shipcraft`, then a blank line, then: "Work as usual on your branch: `init`, RED, GREEN, `shipcraft ci --base <base>`. The setup, the locks and the closing commits stay on that branch, which keeps the guarantee and its history. When the lock is closed, `shipcraft export --base <base> <branch>` writes `<branch>`: the same commits on the same base, without `.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and the hook manager files, and without the commits that change only them. It lists what it left out; a change of yours to one of those files has to be added by hand. Push `<branch>` to your fork and open the PR from it; `shipcraft pr` is for projects that use Shipcraft." Nothing else in the README changes.

**Known limits.** `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` are reset as whole files: a contribution that also edits the project's `AGENTS.md` loses that edit in the export (named on the `kept as in` line); reading only the `<!-- shipcraft -->` block out is a later item. The same holds for a hook manager file the contribution changes. A branch with merge commits is refused, not linearized. The export starts at the fork point, not at the base's tip: rebasing it onto a newer base is the user's. Signatures and `encoding` headers are not copied. `init` and `status` still say nothing about contributing to another project, the `AGENTS.md` block does not mention `export`, and `pr` in a fork clone whose origin is the user's fork (writable) still pushes the working branch with Shipcraft's files: pointing there, and refusing a PR whose base repository is not origin, are later items. The exported branch is not checked by Shipcraft (no lock, no hook): run the project's own checks on it if it has any.

**Metric:** on the release, the reproduction above: `shipcraft export --base origin/main fix-upstream` exits 0, `git diff --stat origin/main...fix-upstream` lists only `src/calc.py` and `tests/test_sub.py`, `git log origin/main..fix-upstream` lists `test: sub` and `feat: sub`, and the working branch is unchanged. Over the second arm of POC-051 (the same four upstream clones): 0 Shipcraft paths and 0 Shipcraft-only commits in the branch proposed upstream, with no hand stripping. Revert: a release without the command; it writes nothing a later binary reads.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.78.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-69.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change (mechanical, the RED updates each and lists it):**
  - the usage text, pinned whole: `acceptance/lib/item110.sh` (`usage` at line 176, read by `v1-110`'s invalid-argument cases `duplicate`, `flag-value`, `unknown`, `missing-title`, `missing-body`, `no-review`) and `acceptance/lib/item121.sh` (`usage` at line 78, read by `v1-121.usage` and the `args-*` cases that compare an invalid-argument stderr); each gains point 3's text and nothing else;
  - `v1-128.readme-once` pins the hash of the whole README without item 128's sentence; point 3's README row and subsection change it, so its expected hash becomes the same formula over this item's README;
  - item 130's `origin-not-writable` `Next:` text (`acceptance/lib/item130.sh`, `nxt` at line 193, compared whole at lines 193 to 204), read by `v1-130.read`, `triage`, `none`, `empty`, `null`, `fallback-name`, `controls-json`, `multi-stop`, `pushurl`, `rewrite`, `userinfo`, `scp`, `url-bytes` and `duplicate-json`, gains point 3's ending; the first two lines, the order, the absence of a push and of a `pr` row stay, and `url-bytes`'s `origin-unverified` variant keeps its text.
  - Spelled out for the usage text: `v1-110.duplicate`, `flag-value`, `unknown`, `missing-title`, `missing-body`, `no-review` (`item110.sh` lines 176 and 177), and `v1-121.usage`, `args-invalid`, `args-repeat`, `args-github` (`item121.sh` lines 78 and 242); `v1-121.args-order` and `args-bounds` do not reach it.
- **Assertions that change:** none other found. `v1-130.docs-refusals` checks its own three rows and their order, which the five new rows (placed before `foreign-hook`) keep. If the RED finds another, it reports it before `red` instead of editing it (rule 11).
- **Must keep passing unchanged:** items 3, 5, 110 and 130's `pr` and `merge` cases (only the `Next:` text above moves); item 128's `ci`, `pr` and `merge` lines; items 116 to 119's structural cases (every file they cap at most 600 lines, so `export` goes in a new module, not in `main.rs` beyond its parse arm); every `init`, `red`, `check`, `close` and `ci` case (none is touched).

**Current behavior**, each marked keep or change:
- `shipcraft export ...` is an unknown command: `invalid arguments`, exit 2: change (point 1).
- The usage text: change (point 3), nothing else in it.
- `pr`'s `origin-not-writable` `Next:`: change (point 3); its other lines, the check order and "nothing pushed": keep.
- Commands that read locks, the config and the hooks: keep, untouched; `export` reads locks only to refuse.
- The README requirement line of item 130 and item 128's sentence: keep.
- Standing effects the new cases meet while building fixtures, all keep: the hook appends a row on every commit even with no lock, `red` writes its lock, `init` writes `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, the post-commit hook adds the closing commit and one `close` row, and a `ci` control appends its row and may leave its suite's output where section 2 says. So the cases compare the run log, refs, index and working tree immediately before and after the `export` call, not across the setup or a control. In detail, all keep: `medicao::record` creates the common git directory's `shipcraft/` folder as needed and ignores a failure to create, open or append (no exit code or message changes); `close` always exits 0, writes one row per lock it closes and none when it closes nothing, and on a failure prints that the lock did not close and keeps the mark for a retry; suites, checks and `[build]` inherit the caller's `TMPDIR`, a clean-tree `ci` runs the suite in the working tree, a dirty tree without `[build]` on a copy, and a dirty tree with `[build]` refuses `build-dirty-tree`. The new cases build fixtures without `[build]` and run their `ci` control on a clean tree.

**New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item135.sh`, with real git fixture repositories (a "base" commit and a branch built by hand or with `init`), no network and no `gh`. Estimate: point 1, 15 (the reproduction's shape: exported commits, their trees (only `src` and tests differ from `F`), messages, author and committer identical, the four output lines exact; HEAD, index, working tree, every other ref and the run log unchanged, and a `commit-msg`, `post-commit` and `reference-transaction` hook that would write a marker not run; base `AGENTS.md` restored byte for byte and an `init`-created `CLAUDE.md` absent; the pre-commit, husky and lefthook files reset, a `.husky/pre-commit` change kept; a commit mixing `.shipcraft.toml` and `src` kept without the config change; `docs/AGENTS.md` and `pkg/.shipcraft.toml` kept; the base moved on after the fork point (parent is `F`, not the base tip); same ids on a second run after the branch is removed; detached HEAD; a file deletion and a mode change carried; an empty commit left out and listed; `<branch> --base <ref>` order; a branch without any Shipcraft path (two lines, no `kept` line); `.shipcraft/` shown once in byte order on the `kept` line; a multi-line message and a control character in a subject); point 2, 14 (each refusal's three lines and exit 1 with no ref written: `.shipcraft.toml` and `.sc2.toml` on the base, an open lock, an invalid-JSON lock and a lock whose duplicate `state` key ends on `open`, a merge in the range, an existing branch, the current branch's name, every commit left out, an empty range; the exit-2 failures: `<ref>` not a commit, an invalid branch name, no shared commit, invalid argument lists; and the order between neighbouring refusals); point 3, 4 (the usage line; the README row and subsection once; the five section 7 rows in place; item 130's `Next:` ending on an `origin-not-writable` refusal); 2 controls (`ci --base` on the working branch as today after an export; `pr`'s `no-origin` text unchanged). Cap: 40.

## 4. Configuration

`.shipcraft.toml` lives at the project root.


### A Blocking item is reachable, or a risk to data or access (item 88)

The brief asks for a reproduced violation of the spec or the intent, or a crash on valid input. It never asks whether anything reaches the defect or what it would cost. On draken-platform, 46 PRs merged from 2026-10-31 to 11-05 had 44 Blocking items in 35 refused rounds, and every item met the criteria. One agent classified them, with a partial check by the coordinator: 31 needed rare input, forced timing or a state no code creates yet, and 10 were worth a merge block. The 26 rounds with nothing worth a block cost about 6 hours and 1.9 million tokens. Victor's decision (2026-11-05, "Fecha e segue"): rare and unreachable defects become notes; a risk to data or access still blocks, however rare.

1. **The new rules.** In both built-in briefs, `nucleo/src/brief.md` and `nucleo/src/brief_after_ci.md`, the list that opens "## Rules for a Blocking item", from `A Blocking item must be BOTH:` to `unhandled error on valid input).`, becomes:

   ```
   A Blocking item must be ALL of these:
   - reproduced by you, with the exact commands and their observed output;
   - either a violation of a specific rule of the spec or of the stated intent (quote it),
     or a crash (panic, abort, or an unhandled error on valid input); and
   - either reachable or a risk to data or access; say which, and why. Reachable: a real
     user or system gets there in use, through an entry point of the product, a public API
     or documented configuration, from input it would really send or state it really holds
     (a supported older state counts). Timing counts if it can happen in use, even if you
     forced it to reproduce it. A risk to data or access: it can lose, corrupt or expose
     data, a secret included, or let someone act without permission, however rare the
     trigger.

   A reproduced defect that fails the last test is a note, never Blocking: a state nothing
   creates or has created, input nobody would really send, or timing that cannot happen in
   use. Finding no caller in this repository proves nothing about a public API or a
   documented configuration. The note says what would make it Blocking. A failing test that
   the project's suite runs is reachable.
   ```

   The paragraph that starts `A failing test of the battery` follows after a blank line, as today. Nothing else in either brief changes: the steps, the format, the ground rules, item 82's paragraph, the placeholders and their substitution stay byte for byte.
2. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - Which brief is chosen (item 63), the substitutions, the brief written to the review's work folder as `brief.md` and passed to the agent: keep.
   - The verdict parsing (first line, `Blocking: N`), fail closed, the retry (item 87), the statuses, the run-log `review` rows, the reviewer's environment, the queue: keep. An `APPROVE` with notes is parsed as today, and the notes reach the PR comment as today.
   - Reuse by `diff_sha256` and `text_sha256` (item 54): keep. The brief's text is in neither key, so a refusal from before this item is reused while the diff and the text match.
   - **What this means for a refusal already recorded:** the new rule applies to fresh reviews. To get one for an unchanged PR, change its code or its title or body.
   - `[review] spec`, the PR body and the reviewer's time budget: keep.
   - The review's work folder (`shipcraft-revisao-*` in the caller's `TMPDIR`, its worktree and output files) and its cleanup, the queue's files, what happens when a step fails before a row is written: keep.
   - Publishing: pending status, review and row, comment, final status, in that order; a failed comment leaves the pending status and the recorded verdict; reuse posts no new comment; the local `review` prints the text and publishes nothing; exits 0, 1 and 2: keep.
   - The sentence that makes style, naming, performance and missing tests notes: keep, and it wins over the last test. A failing test still counts as reproduced as today: name it and the rule it checks, with no separate probe. An outage from slowness is a performance note unless it loses, corrupts or exposes data.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
3. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** the pinned SHA-256 of the normalized briefs in item 63's `i63_brief` (`acceptance/lib/item63.sh`, used by `v1-63.brief-off`, `brief-off-spoof`, `brief-review`, `brief-review-pr` and `brief-review-pr-spoof`) and in item 82's `i82_brief` (`acceptance/lib/item82.sh`, both hashes).
   - **The version:** 2.42.0 in section 11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Fixed points that change with them:** the hash in `acceptance/pontos/63.md` and the statement in `acceptance/pontos/82.md` that only item 82's paragraph was added.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-63.*` and `v1-82.*` case; `v1-54.*`; `v1-4.1-brief-completa`, `v1-4.2-veredito-changes-requested`; `v1-30.1-brief`, `v1-30.1-brief-aspas`; `v1-35.4-externo`, `v1-35.4-textconv`, `v1-35.4-cor`; item 64's brief and prompt checks; item 87's cases; item 40's `src/brief.md` cases, which are about a project file of that name.
   - **New cases:** each brief (after green CI and not) carries the new list and the new paragraph once, between the heading and the sentence that starts `A failing test of the battery` in each brief; neither carries `must be BOTH`; item 82's paragraph still ends the section; a fake reviewer's `APPROVE` with a note section gives `success` and shows the note in the PR comment; a fake reviewer's `CHANGES REQUESTED` gives `failure` and a row as today.
4. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** over the next 20 draken-platform PRs whose reviews all ran on this version or later, counting one fresh completed verdict per round (no reused row, no error row): refused rounds per PR (0.76 today, 35 of 46), target at most 0.4; first review passed for PRs of 700 changed lines or more (12 of 30 today), target at least 60%; review `secs` per fresh row, nearest-rank p50 and p90 (146 s and 237 s), must not grow by more than half.
