The coordinator reads every note of those PRs once.
   - **Revert:** a defect that reached real use and that the coordinator matches to a note in its PR, or a data or access defect approved with no Blocking and found later, restores both briefs' text in a release. A target missed alone is not a revert: it opens the next cause (re-reviews with no memory of the earlier round).
5. **Known limits.** No test can check the reviewer's judgment: the cases pin the words, and the metric says whether they work. "Real user" and "without forcing" are the reviewer's reading, and reviews already differ between runs (draken-platform PR #77 was approved, then refused with only `.gitleaksignore` changed). The classification behind this item is one agent's. Notes live in the PR comment only: nothing tracks them after the merge, and a later review has no memory of them. It finds the defect again only if it looks there. The 31 above overstates what becomes a note, since it counts rare defects that risk data or access.


### `status` reads the hooks under `core.hooksPath` (item 101)

Found on Hands-off on 2026-11-09 (POC-044, finding 4). The project keeps its hooks in `scripts/hooks` (`core.hooksPath = scripts/hooks`). Its `commit-msg` runs `exec "$HOME/.local/shipcraft-2/bin/shipcraft" check --commit-msg "$1"`, and its `post-commit` runs `exec "$HOME/.local/shipcraft-2/bin/shipcraft" close`. Git runs both, and every RED commit was checked. Yet `shipcraft status` printed `shipcraft: missing hook: the commit does not go through `shipcraft check`; run `shipcraft init``. `init` refuses any `core.hooksPath` other than `.git/hooks` (item 15a), so the advice cannot be followed. Every agent that read `status` saw it, and one following it would change the project's hooks. Victor's decision (2026-11-09): "Sim, próximo item".

1. **When this applies.** `git config core.hooksPath` is set (exit 0), and all of these hold:
   - its raw value is not exactly `.git/hooks`;
   - it is not a husky path (item 25's `init_tool::husky_path`);
   - item 25's `init_tool::present` is false;
   - no lefthook file (`LEFTHOOK_FILES`) exists.

   Every other case keeps today's route and text: unset, `.git/hooks`, husky, pre-commit or lefthook.
2. **What `status` prints then.** It reads the two hook files git runs, `git rev-parse --git-path hooks/commit-msg` and `hooks/post-commit` (`Repo::hook_path`, which honours `core.hooksPath` and worktrees), following symlinks.
   - **Reading a hook.** The file is read as bytes and split on `\n`. A trailing `\r` is stripped from each line. Bytes that are not UTF-8 are compared as bytes, so a Latin-1 comment does not hide a call line. A file that exists but cannot be read (permission, I/O) counts as `missing or not executable`.
   - A hook **calls shipcraft** when it is a readable, executable regular file holding a *call line*. Executable means any execute bit set, after following symlinks, as the own-hook route reads it today. A call line, after leading spaces and tabs and an optional `exec` followed by spaces or tabs, starts with a command word and is followed by spaces or tabs and the arguments:
     - `check --commit-msg` in `commit-msg`;
     - `close` in `post-commit`.

     After the arguments comes the end of the line, or a space or tab. The command word is one shell word: consecutive segments, each either unquoted text (no space, tab or quote) or text in double or single quotes (no escapes; an unclosed quote is not a call), ending at the first unquoted space or tab. Its value is the segments joined without their quotes, and its last `/`-separated component must be exactly `shipcraft`. So `"$HOME"/bin/shipcraft close` calls shipcraft (value `$HOME/bin/shipcraft`). Lines whose first non-blank character is `#` are skipped. Nothing is expanded or run. Examples that call shipcraft:
     - `exec "$HOME/.local/shipcraft-2/bin/shipcraft" check --commit-msg "$1"`;
     - `shipcraft close`;
     - `	/usr/local/bin/shipcraft close`.

     Examples that do not: `# shipcraft close`, `echo shipcraft close`, `shipcraft-old close`, `shipcraft closed`.
   - **Both call shipcraft:** one line `shipcraft: hooks in core.hooksPath <dir> call shipcraft (commit-msg and post-commit)`, where `<dir>` is the raw value (lossy UTF-8, as `init`'s refusal prints it). In the problems below, `<dir>/<hook>` drops one trailing `/` from `<dir>` before joining. An empty value is printed empty.
   - **Otherwise:** one line `shipcraft: missing hook: <problems>; add the shipcraft calls there (shipcraft init does not write under core.hooksPath)`. `<problems>` holds, joined by `, `, in this order, each that applies:
     - `<dir>/commit-msg is missing or not executable`;
     - `<dir>/commit-msg does not call shipcraft check --commit-msg`;
     - `<dir>/post-commit is missing or not executable`;
     - `<dir>/post-commit does not call shipcraft close`.

     For each hook, only one of its two problems is listed: the first that applies.
   - **Git failures.** If `git config core.hooksPath` fails (an exit other than 0 or 1), point 1 does not apply and today's route and line stay. Once point 1 applies, a failing `git rev-parse --git-path` for either hook prints `shipcraft: missing hook: could not read the hooks under core.hooksPath <dir>` instead of the problems list. `status` still exits as today.
3. **Current behavior** (rule 14):
   - The `status` hook line with `core.hooksPath` outside `.git/hooks` and no husky, pre-commit or lefthook: change, as points 1 and 2 say.
   - Every other `status` line, its order, exit codes, and the husky, lefthook, pre-commit and own-hook routes: keep.
   - `init` and its refusal on `core.hooksPath` (item 15a): keep. Its wrong `Next:` line is a separate item.
   - `status` writes nothing and runs no hook: keep.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:**
     - item 15a's `core.hooksPath` refusal cases;
     - item 25's husky and pre-commit status cases;
     - item 20.3's lefthook status cases;
     - item 58's status cases;
     - item 89's AGENTS.md status line;
     - item 70's status cases `v1-70.status-config`, `status-folder`, `status-format` and `status-malformed`: exit codes, line order, unchanged files, no project command run and no run-log write.

     The RED searches `acceptance/` for `status` cases whose fixture sets `core.hooksPath` to another directory and lists any whose hook line changes.
   - **The version:** 2.51.0 in §11, and `v1-87.version` with the version cases item 69 lists.
   - **New cases**, about 15:
     - Hands-off's real hook text, both hooks: the hooks line;
     - the hook that is missing, not executable, a symlink to an executable, and a directory;
     - each non-call example above;
     - single quotes, no `exec`, and a tab indent;
     - the order of several problems;
     - a worktree of the same repository: the same line;
     - the husky, lefthook and unset routes unchanged (controls).
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** on Hands-off with this release, `shipcraft status` prints the hooks line, and no agent there runs `shipcraft init` because of `status`.
   - **Revert:** a release that goes back to the one `missing hook` line.
6. **Known limits.** The check is textual: a hook that calls shipcraft through a wrapper script or a shell function reads as `does not call`. A hook that has the call line but exits before it reads as calling.

### `status` says when `AGENTS.md`'s block is not this version's (item 89)

`init` writes the shipcraft block to `AGENTS.md`, and only a later `init` updates it. Nothing says when that is due. draken-platform kept its 2026-10-27 block until 11-05, without the lanes, the limit of about 40 cases or "only Blocking findings open another round", while its binary moved on many versions; its median PR on 11-04 was 871 changed lines against 271 on draken, which had those rules. Victor's decision (2026-11-05, "resolve", after this was recommended): `status` says so.

1. **The new line.** `status` prints one line right after the hook line and before the open-locks line. It reads `AGENTS.md` at the repository root in the working tree, from the root `Repo::open` finds (a linked worktree's own, even when `status` runs in a subfolder), with `init`'s reader and block rules (`find_block`, byte for byte, with no newline normalization). The rows are tried in this order:

   | `AGENTS.md` | Line |
   |---|---|
   | Has no start marker (stray end markers included), or does not exist | ``shipcraft: AGENTS.md block: none; run `shipcraft init` to add it`` |
   | Is not a regular file, a symlink included, or cannot be read as UTF-8 text | `shipcraft: AGENTS.md block: could not read it` |
   | Holds this version's block, in either the machine or the project-local form | `shipcraft: AGENTS.md block: current` |
   | Holds a block an earlier version wrote: any earlier block `init` knows, in either form, or the exact legacy block of v2.14.0 (a start marker with no end marker, followed by anything) | ``shipcraft: AGENTS.md block: from an earlier version; run `shipcraft init` to update it`` |
   | Anything else `init` keeps: edited, from a newer version, or other markers that do not pair | `shipcraft: AGENTS.md block: differs from this version's; init keeps it, compare it with docs/spec.md by hand` |

   A project-local install compared with the machine form of this version's block is `current`: that difference is the install's, not the version's. `init` still replaces it, as today.
2. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - Every failure before the hook line (outside git, invalid or missing config, `shipcraft_min` above the binary): keep, with no new line.
   - `status`'s other lines, their order, its refusals (an unknown lock format after the hook line, a lock folder it cannot read exits 2), and its exit codes: keep. The new line never changes the exit code and runs before the locks are read, so it prints in those cases too.
   - `status` runs no project command, writes no file and logs no row: keep. The new read writes nothing.
   - `init`: what it writes, replaces and keeps, and every message: keep. The block's text: keep. `CLAUDE.md` is not read by this line.
   - `merge`, `pr`, `red`, `ci`, `report` and the hook: keep. Only `status` prints the line.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
3. **Touches.**
   - **The version:** 2.43.0 in section 11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **The commands table** in section 3 gains the line in `status`'s row.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** `v1-5.4-status-nivel-de-protecao`; `v1-15c.status-sem-exec-borda`; `v1-20.status.*`; `v1-25.*.status-*`; `v1-70.status-*`; `v1-75.read-status`, `v1-75.future-status`; the `status` cases of items 58, 62, 64 and 73; the block cases of items 61, 68, 70, 74, 75, 84, 85 and 86. The spec review found none that breaks: no case pins the whole output or the hook line next to the locks line.
   - **New cases:** one per table row, each in a repository set up through public commands where it can be: `init` then `status` is `current`, on both the machine and the project-local install; each earlier block `init` knows gives `from an earlier version`, and after `init` gives `current`; an edited line, a missing end marker and two start markers give `differs`, and `init` leaves that file as it was; no `AGENTS.md`, and one with no markers, give `none`; a symlink and a folder and invalid UTF-8 give `could not read it`. In every case the `status` run keeps its exit code, changes no file and logs no row, compared right before and after it (setup commits may have written rows earlier), and the line sits between the hook line and the open-locks line. A CRLF copy of the current block gives `differs`; an end marker with no start gives `none`; a run from a subfolder reads the root's file; the exact v2.14.0 block, followed by other text, gives `from an earlier version`. With an unknown lock format the line still prints before the refusal.
4. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** adopter repositories whose block is older than their binary for more than 2 days, read by the coordinator from `status` on draken and draken-platform at each release: 0 from the next release on; and false lines: 0, meaning an `earlier version` line on a block that isn't one, or a `current` line on one that is.
   - **Revert:** a false line, or an adopter agent acting wrongly on it, removes the line in a release.
5. **Known limits.** The line tells; nothing runs `init`. `status` is only read when someone runs it, so an agent that never does never sees it. It says nothing of the phase briefs in `.shipcraft/briefs/`, which also change only through `init`. A newer block than the binary reads as `differs`, which is true but does not say to upgrade. The line says `shipcraft init` literally: on a project-local install, the user runs it the way they run `status`.


### `init` does not need the language when the config already exists (item 90)

Item 89's `status` says to run `shipcraft init` when the `AGENTS.md` block is old. On this repository, a Rust project with its own `.shipcraft.toml`, that `init` refuses `lang-unrecognized`. Passing `--lang other --command ... --glob ...` gets through, and then `init` itself says those values only seed a new config. Item 67 point 1 decided that a hand-written config does not lift the refusal, when no message told anyone to run `init` on such a project. Now one does. Victor's decision (2026-11-05, "Sim, abre").

1. **The change.** `init` with no `--lang`, `--command` or `--glob` (`--github` allowed) plans as today: workspaces first, then detection, with every read and refusal that planning has. Only the two refusals `lang-unrecognized` and `lang-multiple` change: when `symlink_metadata` on `.shipcraft.toml` succeeds, so a link, a broken link or a folder counts, `init` goes on instead of refusing. If that call fails for any reason, it refuses as today. Going on, it prints no language or runner line and not `test command run as written`, since it chose neither, and every later step runs as it does for a kept config today.
2. **What does not change.**
   - No config: `lang-unrecognized` and `lang-multiple` refuse as today, with their texts.
   - A plan that succeeds, with or without a config: as today, the language or workspace line and workspace precedence included.
   - `--lang python|node|go`, and an unknown `--lang`: as today, config or not. `--lang other` and its validation (item 67), and the seed notice: as today, config or not. `--command` or `--glob` without `--lang other`: refuses as today.
   - Every step after planning, in today's order: hook decisions and their refusals; `raise_min`, which refuses `shipcraft-min` on a linked or invalid config and errors on a folder or an unreadable one, so those configs do not reach `ready`; an open lock's refusal. Each of these still comes before anything is written, so `already exists, kept` does not print then.
3. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `.shipcraft.toml`: kept. The only write is today's: `raise_min` raising `shipcraft_min` for a lefthook or similar integration, with its message and its restore on failure.
   - `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`: ensured as today, its folders created, existing locks untouched; if `.shipcraft` is a file, the core failure as today.
   - Core write failures (config, lock placeholder, hooks): undo what this run created and exit 2, as today. Onboarding failures (`AGENTS.md` a link, `.shipcraft/briefs` a file): a notice, what was installed stays, and `ready` prints, as today.
   - `--github`: `protect` and its messages as today, its failure not failing `init`.
   - The final `ready` lines and the CI line from the kept config: keep.
   - Item 67 point 1's sentence that a hand-written config does not lift the refusal, and its point 3: superseded for this form only. Section 7's rows gain "and no `.shipcraft.toml` exists".
   - `status` and its line (item 89): keep. The advice now works here.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** `v1-67.detection-kept` in `acceptance/lib/item67.sh`, which pins this refusal, and `acceptance/pontos/67.md` points 9 and 14, which describe it. The case now asserts the new behavior.
   - **The version:** 2.44.0 in section 11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** every other `v1-67.*` case; `init-detect`, `init-files`, `init-workflow`, `init-lang-flag` in `acceptance/run.sh`; `v1-9.1-init-*`; `v1-20.rota.commit-msg`, `v1-20.rota.post-commit`; `v1-27.mensagem-*`; `v1-58.plain`, `v1-58.kept`, `v1-58.rollback`; `v1-75.read-init`; the block cases of items 61, 68, 70, 74, 75, 84, 85, 86 and 89.
   - **New cases:** with a valid hand-written config and no language file, `init` exits 0, keeps the config byte for byte, replaces an earlier `AGENTS.md` block, then `status` says `current`, with no language line and no `run as written` line; the same with two language files; a linked config and an invalid one still refuse `shipcraft-min` and a folder errors, each writing nothing; no config with no language file and with two still refuse with today's codes and an unchanged tree; two language files plus a tested workspace still print the workspace line; a detected language with a config still prints its language line; a foreign hook still refuses with no `already exists, kept` line; `.shipcraft` as a file undoes what the run created; `AGENTS.md` as a link prints the notice and `ready`.
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** the next time the block changes, `init` with no flags updates it on this repository and on any adopter with an unrecognized language; refusals of that form on a usable config: 0.
   - **Revert:** a project that needed the refusal with a config present: a release restores it.
6. **Known limits.** `init` does not check that the kept config's suites run: a parsable config with wrong commands gets `ready`, and the next commit says so. The `ready` line still says to commit `.shipcraft.toml`, as it does for any kept config.

### `init --lang node` says when vitest is only an assumption (item 91)

`--lang node` on a project whose `package.json` declares neither vitest nor jest writes `node_modules/.bin/vitest run ...` as the test command and prints `ready`, as item 9 decided. A project on another runner learns this only when `red` fails. Found on 2026-11-06 in a trial for a Node function with no dependencies that tests with `node --test`: `init` printed `language node (runner vitest)` and `ready`, with no vitest in the project. Victor's decision (2026-11-06, "Sim, abre o item"). The default stays; this item only says it is an assumption.

1. **The change.** When `init` writes a new `.shipcraft.toml` from `--lang node`, the plan is one suite at the root (no workspace), and today's runner inference (`node_runner` on the root) returns none, it prints one line on stdout, right before the `shipcraft: ready.` line:

   `shipcraft: warning: found neither vitest nor jest in package.json, so the test command assumes vitest. Install it, or for another runner, from the repository root, remove .shipcraft.toml and run: shipcraft init --lang other --command '<test command writing JUnit to {junit}>' --glob '<test files>'`

   The result is the inference's own, with no second reader: it follows symlinks, any failure to read or parse the file gives no runner, and a key under `dependencies` or `devDependencies` counts whatever its value (`"jest": null` is a runner). "One suite at the root" reads the resulting plan, not the manifests: `--lang node` does not look at workspaces today, so a root `package.json` with `workspaces` and no root runner plans one root suite and gets the line. The line is informative: at the same version, `init` writes the same files with the same bytes and exits as it would without it. Deciding and printing it starts no tool and looks at no `node_modules`; TypeScript build inference still runs as today.
2. **What does not change.**
   - The default itself: vitest without a runner dependency, the language line `language node (runner vitest)`, and every word of the config.
   - No line for: detection with no `--lang` (it needs a runner dependency to choose Node, and a detected workspace plans several suites); a `package.json` declaring vitest or jest, installed or not; a kept config (`.shipcraft.toml` exists in any form, item 90's reading); `--lang python`, `--lang go`, `--lang other`.
   - A run that fails before `ready` prints no line.
3. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, the hooks, hook integration files such as lefthook's and `shipcraft_min`'s raise and restore, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and the briefs: written as today. Keep.
   - Exit codes: a refusal exits 1, including an open lock's; a core write failure undoes what this run created and exits 2; an onboarding or briefs failure prints its notice and still reaches `ready` with exit 0, so the line prints there too; `--github`'s protection failure does not fail `init`. Keep.
   - TypeScript build inference, its `tsc -b` proof run and its output in the tree: keep.
   - The lines `init` prints today and their order, the closing guidance and the final CI line after `ready` included: keep, with this one added.
   - Item 16b's handling of an invalid `package.json` under `--lang node`, its warning and the absent `[build]`: keep; the new line also prints there.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** the version: 2.45.0 in section 11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** `init-detect`, `init-files`, `init-workflow`, `init-lang-flag` in `acceptance/run.sh`; `v1-9.1-init-*`; item 16b's cases in `acceptance/lib/item16b.sh`, `v1-16b.1-composite`, `v1-16b.1-references` and `v1-16b.1-build-reference` among them; `v1-72.root-composite`, `v1-72.root-references`; `v1-27.mensagem-local`, `v1-27.mensagem-desligado`, `v1-27.mensagem-github`; `v1-58.plain`, `v1-58.kept`, `v1-58.rollback`; `v1-75.read-init`; `v1-68.closing`, `v1-68.closing-red`, `v1-68.closing-green`, `v1-68.closing-none`; every `v1-67.*`; every `v1-90.*`.
   - **New cases:** `--lang node` with a `package.json` with no runner, with no `package.json`, and with an unparsable one: the line once, right before `ready`, exit 0, with a config whose text is the expected one for this version; `--lang node` with vitest and with jest (each in `dependencies` and `devDependencies`): no line; a kept config: no line; `--lang node` on a root `package.json` with `workspaces` and no root runner: the line; a plain `init` on a `package.json` with vitest: no line; `--lang node` that fails on a foreign hook: no line.
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** on the next Node adopter on another runner, the line appears at `init`, and `red` is not the first place the wrong runner shows. False alarms: the line printed when the root's runner inference returned a runner: 0. The known limits below are not false alarms.
   - **Revert:** one such false alarm, or an adopter on a known limit who reports the line as noise: a release removes it.
6. **Known limits.** A runner declared only in a parent folder or a workspace package, or installed globally, still gets the line, and so does a `package.json` that does not parse, even when its text names vitest. `init` does not check that vitest is installed when it is declared. Other runners keep going through `--lang other`; first-class support for `node --test` is not part of this item.

### `init` on a project with no runner yet (item 131)

Found on 2026-11-16 by POC-051 (simulated adopters, runs 5 and 6, the two vibe-coded apps: expense-compass from Lovable, barbearia from scratch). The persona follows the README: `npm i -D shipcraft` in an empty repository, then `npx -- shipcraft init`. Victor's decision (2026-11-18, "Pode resolver, tá?"; "Primeiro vamos focar no init individual do Shipcraft").

**Reproduction, on main (2.73.0),** each in a scratch repository with the binary installed as the project's (`node_modules/shipcraft/bin/shipcraft`, `node_modules/.bin/shipcraft` linking to it, item 58's rule):
- `package.json` is `{"name":"barb","version":"1.0.0","devDependencies":{"shipcraft":"^2.73.0"}}` with a `package-lock.json`, nothing committed. `node_modules/.bin/shipcraft init` refuses `lang-unrecognized` (`could not recognize the project language`, "found none it knows") although `package.json` is there, and its `Next:` says `shipcraft init --lang ...`, not `npx -- shipcraft init`, though the binary that answered is the project's: pasted as printed, it runs a global binary or is not found.
- `init --lang node` there exits 0 and prints item 91's warning (`Install it, ...`, no install command), then `shipcraft: ready. Commit .shipcraft.toml, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, .shipcraft/briefs/red.md, .shipcraft/briefs/green.md and .shipcraft/locks/;`, without `package.json` and `package-lock.json`, though item 80's refusal (`install-not-committed`) says that line names them "for a project install", and with nothing about `node_modules`, which `git status` shows untracked: the README cycle's `git add -A` commits the installed packages. Then `run \`shipcraft red\``, again without the prefix.
- A Lovable layout (`tsconfig.json` with `files: []` and `references` to `tsconfig.app.json` and `tsconfig.node.json`, each `composite` with a `tsBuildInfoFile` at the root, a type error in `src/a.ts`, a real `typescript` in `node_modules`, all committed): `init` prints the compiler's errors on stderr, then `shipcraft refused: did not write [build]: \`npx --no -- tsc -b .\` exited with 2` with `Next: fix the build and run shipcraft init again, or manually add ...`, then goes on to `ready` and exits 0. Running `init` again does not help: the config is now kept, and a kept config skips the `[build]` inference (today's `init.rs`). The probe leaves `tsconfig.app.tsbuildinfo` and `tsconfig.node.tsbuildinfo` untracked and not ignored; no line names them, and once `[build]` is written, `ci` refuses `build-dirty-tree` on a tree they make dirty.

**The slice.** This item takes the three points below, all on `init`'s first run on a Node project. Left for later items (POC-051 card, item 6): every other line `init` and the other commands print that names a command, in the project's invocation (hook and lefthook lines, `status`, `red`'s own Next lines); the test setup a React app needs beyond vitest (jsdom, Testing Library, a config `environment`), as documentation or a line; the other `[build]` notices (`cannot read ... as JSONC`, `cannot understand glob`, the manifest one), which keep `refused`; the vitest failure and DOM dumps that bury Shipcraft's own lines in `red` and the hook (run 5: ~130 KB, run 6: ~300 KB). **A guarantee hole, its own item:** vitest's `setupFiles` (run 6's `src/test-setup.ts`) and a `test` block inside `vite.config.*` are test support the GREEN can edit while the lock is open: the Node globs lock `**/vitest.config.*` and `**/jest.config.*` but neither a setup file outside them nor `vite.config.*`. Python's `conftest.py` is locked for the same reason. Locking `vite.config.*` wholesale would lock the app's build config, so the item needs its own design (read the setup paths from the config, or lock only the `test` block's files).

Everything in items 9, 16b, 56, 58, 61, 67, 68, 69, 72, 80, 90 and 91 holds unless a point below changes it: exit codes, files written and their bytes, refusal codes, the order of the other lines.

1. **A bare `package.json` is Node, and the lines name the invocation that ran.**
   - **Detection.** With no `--lang`, after the workspace step as today, the planner also takes Node when all of these hold: `Lang::detect` found no language (today's readers, unchanged: `pyproject.toml` or `go.mod` by `Path::exists`, so a folder of that name counts; any root entry named `requirements*.txt`; a `package.json` declaring vitest or jest through `node_runner`); `repo.path("package.json").exists()` is true (`Path::exists` follows links: a dangling link, a loop or any metadata error is absent; a folder named `package.json` is present, as for the other markers; its content is not read for this decision); `lang::workspace(root)` returns None (no `pnpm-workspace.yaml` that reads, no `workspaces` in a `package.json` that parses, no `go.work`: a runner-less workspace whose packages have no tests yet still refuses `lang-unrecognized`, as today); and `symlink_metadata` on `.shipcraft.toml` fails (no config in any form, item 90's reading: with a kept config the plan is item 90's, no language and no language line, as today). With `pyproject.toml`, a `requirements*.txt` or a `go.mod` beside a runner-less `package.json`, the result is that language alone, as today (no `lang-multiple`). A `package.json` that declares vitest or jest is Node as today, and with another marker beside it `lang-multiple` refuses as today. On the new path the plan is one suite at the root, the profile and config are today's `--lang node` ones byte for byte (vitest by default), the language line is `shipcraft: language node (runner vitest)`, and item 91's warning prints as for `--lang node` (its condition reads the plan, not the flag).
   - **The invocation.** When the running binary is the project's install (`Install::Project`, item 58's rule, the one item 69's `--version` hint applies), the lines below name the command as item 69's hint does: `pnpm exec shipcraft` when a regular `pnpm-lock.yaml` sits at the root and `pnpm` is on `PATH`, `npx -- shipcraft` otherwise. With any other install (`Machine`, `Nested`) they stay `shipcraft`, byte for byte as today. The lines:
     - the `Next:` of `lang-unrecognized` and `lang-multiple`: `specify the language: <inv> init --lang python|node|go; for any other: <inv> init --lang other --command '<test command writing JUnit to {junit}>' --glob '<test files>'`;
     - every other place `init` prints the `--lang other` form (item 67's `OTHER_FORM`: the `Next:` of item 67's own refusals in `custom.rs`, and item 91's warning): `<inv> init --lang other ...`;
     - the closing line: `shipcraft: then write the failing test and stub, and run \`<inv> red\`.`;
     The unknown-language-name refusal of `suites::choose_lang` (`linguagem desconhecida: <name>`, `Next: use --lang python, --lang node or --lang go`) names no command and keeps its text. Item 67's custom-form refusals in `custom.rs` also log the code `lang-unknown` (missing `--command` or `--glob`, an invalid command or glob, either flag without `--lang other`); they change as listed above whatever their code, and with a machine install they keep their text (`v1-67.refuse-missing-command`, `refuse-missing-glob`, `refuse-command-no-lang` and the command-validation cases keep their assertions). Item 69's `--version` hint keeps its text; it and these lines read one rule.
2. **The no-runner warning names the install, and `ready` names the install's files.**
   - **The install command.** Item 91's warning becomes, with the same condition and place (right before `shipcraft: ready.`, once):
     `shipcraft: warning: found neither vitest nor jest in package.json, so the test command assumes vitest. Install it with \`<install>\`, or for another runner, from the repository root, remove .shipcraft.toml and run: <inv> init --lang other --command '<test command writing JUnit to {junit}>' --glob '<test files>'`
     `<install>` follows the first of these root files whose `symlink_metadata` says regular file (a link, a folder or a metadata error skips that candidate), in this order: `pnpm-lock.yaml` gives `pnpm add -D vitest` (`pnpm add -D -w vitest` when `pnpm-workspace.yaml` exists at the root by `symlink_metadata`, since pnpm refuses a root install of a workspace without `-w`), `yarn.lock` gives `yarn add -D vitest`, `bun.lock`, then `bun.lockb`, gives `bun add -d vitest`; none of them, gives `npm install -D vitest`. No `PATH` lookup, no tool started. With any install (project or machine) the warning carries `<install>`.
   - **The install's files.** With the project's install, the `shipcraft: ready. Commit ...` line also names each of `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `yarn.lock`, `bun.lock`, `bun.lockb`, `package-lock.json`, in that order (every one present, both Bun files included), that exists at the root as a regular file by `symlink_metadata` and that git reports as not committed: `git status --porcelain=v1 -z -- <file>`, run at the root with literal pathspecs, exits 0 and prints something (untracked, staged or changed against HEAD). They go after the briefs and before ` and .shipcraft/locks/;` (`..., .shipcraft/briefs/green.md, package.json, package-lock.json and .shipcraft/locks/;`). A file git reports as committed and unchanged, an ignored one, an absent one, or one whose `git status` exits other than 0 is not named, and `init` goes on as today (no failure, no rollback, exit unchanged). With any other install the line is today's.
   - **`node_modules`.** With the project's install, whatever the language, when `git check-ignore -q -- node_modules` run at the root exits 1 (not ignored), `init` prints one line, on stdout, right before item 91's warning when it prints, else right before the `ready` line: `shipcraft: node_modules is not ignored by git: add it to .gitignore before committing, or \`git add -A\` stages the installed packages`. Exit 0 (ignored) prints nothing; any other exit, or git failing to start, prints nothing and changes nothing else. The line reads only the ignore rule: files under `node_modules` that are already tracked are not looked for (a known limit). `init` writes no `.gitignore`. The line is not printed on a run that stops before `ready`.
3. **A `[build]` probe that fails is a skipped optional step, and the probe's leftovers are named.**
   - **Quiet probe.** `init`'s `[build]` probe (item 16b, `tsbuild::infer`) runs the command as today (`sh -c` in the root, the caller's environment, both streams piped and drained as they arrive, the two-second wait for streams a leftover process holds open), but writes neither stream to `init`'s stdout or stderr, whether it passes or fails; only the first non-blank line of each is kept, as `suite.rs`'s `FirstLine` keeps it today (lossy UTF-8, at most 4096 bytes). Nothing else is buffered. A spawn or wait error fails `init` as today (exit 2, nothing written yet). A read error on either stream ends that stream's reading without failing, as today's reader does: the first line is taken from what was read before it, and the verdict is the exit status alone. `ci` and `red`'s `[build]` (`suite::build`) keep passing it through, as today.
   - **The skip.** When the probe fails, instead of today's three `shipcraft refused: did not write [build]: ...` lines, `init` prints, at the same place:
     `shipcraft: skipped the optional [build] step: \`<command>\` <how>: <first line>`
     `Why: init writes [build] only after it passes in the tree; a failing [build] would refuse every shipcraft ci and shipcraft red, so init goes on without it`
     `Next: when \`<command>\` passes (run it to see the errors), add a [build] section to .shipcraft.toml with command = "<command>"`
     `<how>` is today's (`exited with 2`, `killed by signal <n>`, `ended without an exit code`); `<first line>` is the first non-blank line of the probe's stderr, else of its stdout, folded to one line by `red::one_line` (whitespace runs, control characters among them, become one space; no other escaping); with neither, the first line ends at `<how>` with no `: `. In the `Next:`, `"<command>"` after `command = ` is the command written as a TOML basic string, as the `toml` crate serializes a string value (a `"` or `\` in a quoted workspace folder is escaped), so pasting it keeps `.shipcraft.toml` valid; the two backquoted mentions show the command as is. The `Next:` no longer says to run `init` again (a kept config skips the inference). `init` still writes no `[build]` and exits as today. No line of these three starts with `shipcraft refused:`.
   - **Leftovers.** Right before and right after the probe runs (passed or failed), `init` lists the untracked files git does not ignore with `git ls-files -z --others --exclude-standard` at the root (NUL-separated paths as git gives them: files, plus a folder entry ending in `/` for a nested git repository, which is named as given and not entered). The files in the second list and not in the first, sorted by bytes, are the leftovers. When there are any, it prints one line on stdout, right after the probe's own output (after point 3's three lines when the probe failed; before the language line either way): `shipcraft: the build left files git does not ignore: <list>; add them to .gitignore before committing`, where `<list>` is `refusal::short_list` of the paths, each folded by `red::one_line` and lossy UTF-8 (three names, then `and <n> more`). When either listing fails (git exits other than 0 or does not start), no line and no other change. Tracked files the build changed, and untracked files it rewrote, are not named (a known limit). It deletes nothing and writes no `.gitignore`. When the probe did not run (no signal, `--lang other`, a kept config), neither listing runs and no line prints. Since the probe runs before anything is written, the line has printed when a later step refuses or fails; that later step's exit and rollback are as today, and the build's files stay, as today.

**Metric:** on the release, a rerun of POC-051 runs 5 and 6 (`npm i -D shipcraft` in an empty repository; the Lovable layout) goes from `npx -- shipcraft init` to `ready` with no `refused` line and no line naming a bare `shipcraft` command among the lines above, and the `ready` list plus the two new lines name every root manifest and lockfile left uncommitted, `node_modules` when not ignored, and every file the probe added untracked (other project files are not inventoried). False alarm: a Python or Go project with a `package.json` beside its marker that gets Node or `lang-multiple`: 0. Revert: a release restores today's detection or lines; the config bytes never change.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.74.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-69.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else:**
  - item 91's exact warning line (`acceptance/lib/item91.sh`, the `warning_line` the cases compare): ` Install it,` becomes ` Install it with \`npm install -D vitest\`,` (or the fixture's lockfile command), and the `--lang other` form gains the prefix where a case runs the project's install; its "No line for detection with no `--lang`" (point 2 of item 91) changes for a bare `package.json`: a case that ran plain `init` on a `package.json` without a runner and expected `lang-unrecognized` now gets Node and the line;
    The cases that compare that line: `v1-91.no-runner`, `absent`, `invalid`, `invalid-utf8`, `manifest-directory`, `dangling`, `symlink-none`, `workspace`, `parent-only`, `installed-only`, `agents-fail`, `briefs-fail`, `github-fail`. Their fixtures have no lockfile and a machine install, so only ` Install it,` becomes ` Install it with \`npm install -D vitest\`,`; their config-byte, no-tool, once-right-before-`ready`, not-on-stderr and no-row assertions keep. A `v1-91` case that runs plain `init` on a runner-less `package.json` and expects no line (item 91 point 2's "detection with no `--lang`") changes to the line, and only that.
  - item 16b's failed-probe diagnostics: `v1-16b.1-build-type`, `v1-16b.1-build-reference`, `v1-16b.1-build-signal`, `v1-16b.1-build-signal-output` read the probe's refusal through `b16b_diagnostic` (exactly three lines starting `shipcraft refused:`, `Why:`, `Next:`). Only their failed-probe reader changes, to point 3's three lines (`shipcraft: skipped the optional [build] step:`, `Why:`, `Next:`) and to the absence of the build's output on stderr; the JSONC, glob and manifest diagnostics keep the old reader and text.
  - any other case the RED finds that pins the `ready` line, the closing line or the line before `ready` byte for byte under the project's install with a root `package.json` or lockfile uncommitted: the RED lists it before `red` (rule 11) and changes only the named file list and the prefix. The local-install fixtures of items 68, 70, 74 and 75 (`v1-68.briefs-local`, `v1-68.other-local`, the `*-local` cases of `acceptance/lib/item70.sh`, `item74.sh`, `item75.sh`) use `requirements.txt` and no `package.json`: the new `node_modules` line comes before `ready`, so their `closing` helper (`lines[-3] == ready`) keeps passing unchanged, and they are in the must-keep list below.
  - Not changed, despite their names: item 67's `detection` and item 90's `no-config` fixtures have no `package.json`, so they refuse as today.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - `init-detect`, `init-files`, `init-workflow`, `init-lang-flag` in `acceptance/run.sh`; `v1-9.1-init-*`;
  - item 25's `init_final` reader (`acceptance/lib/item25.sh`, the position of `ready` among the last lines) and its cases;
  - item 71's workspace-probe and rollback cases (`acceptance/lib/item71.sh`): workspace planning, item 71's conversion and its rollback are not touched;
  - the acceptance README fixed points 14 to 20, 39 to 41 and 57 to 65 (planning, writes, checks and rollback);
  - the `*-local` closing cases of items 68, 70, 74 and 75 named above;
  - item 45's stream-reader cases (`v1-45.red-stdout`, `v1-45.ci-stdout`, and their `-both`, `-late-stdout`, `-late-stderr` variants) and item 22's `v1-22.ambiente-build`: `red` and `ci` keep passing build and suite output through with today's first-line rules and environment; the quiet probe must not change the shared runner's behavior for them;
  - item 16b's passing-probe and no-signal cases (`v1-16b.1-composite`, `v1-16b.1-references`, `v1-16b.1-build-reference`) and its other notices (JSONC, glob, manifest: still `refused`); item 16a's `build-failed` refusal in `ci` and `red` (`acceptance/lib/item16a.sh`), whose build output still passes through;
  - items 56 and 72's check lines (`v1-72.root-composite`, `v1-72.root-references`);
  - item 58's `v1-58.plain`, `v1-58.kept`, `v1-58.rollback` (the local notice and its place) and its machine-install cases; item 69's `--version` hint cases (`acceptance/lib/item69.sh`);
  - item 80's `install-not-committed` refusal and its text;
  - every `v1-67.*` except the detection ones the RED lists; every `v1-90.*` except those whose fixture has a runner-less `package.json`;
  - `v1-27.mensagem-local`, `v1-27.mensagem-desligado`, `v1-27.mensagem-github`; `v1-75.read-init`; `v1-68.closing`, `v1-68.closing-red`, `v1-68.closing-green`, `v1-68.closing-none` with a machine install;
  - item 116 to 119's structural cases: every file at most 600 lines, no new file under `nucleo/src/config/`, `nucleo/src/relatorio/` or `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/`; item 117's `init` module layout.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item131.sh`, with real git fixture repositories, the binary copied as the project's install where the case says so, and a fake `tsc` script in `node_modules/.bin/` for the probe (one that prints errors, writes `*.tsbuildinfo` files and exits 2, one that passes and writes one), never the network. Estimate: point 1, 11 (bare `package.json` detected as Node with its language line, config bytes and item 91's line; `package.json` plus `pyproject.toml` is Python; plus `go.mod` is Go; `package.json` with vitest plus `pyproject.toml` still `lang-multiple`; a bare `package.json` with `workspaces` and untested packages still `lang-unrecognized`; a bare `package.json` with a kept config: no language line, as today; `lang-unrecognized` with no marker, project install: `npx -- shipcraft` twice in `Next:`; same with `pnpm-lock.yaml` and a fake `pnpm` on `PATH`: `pnpm exec shipcraft`; machine install: today's text; an item 67 refusal's `Next:` with the prefix; the closing line with and without the prefix); point 2, 10 (install command for each lockfile: none, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` with `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and `-w`, `yarn.lock`, `bun.lockb`; `ready` naming `package.json` and `package-lock.json` untracked; both committed: not named; machine install: not named; `node_modules` not ignored: the line, after `ready`; ignored: no line); point 3, 7 (the reproduction's three lines and no `refused`; no compiler output on stdout or stderr; `[build]` absent from the config; `killed by signal`; a probe with no output; a passing probe that leaves a file: the leftover line and `[build]` written; a failing probe that leaves four: three names and `and 1 more`; a quoted workspace folder with `"` in it: the `Next:` TOML string escaped; a failing probe then a core write failure: exit 2, rollback as today, the leftover line printed); 3 controls (machine install `lang-unrecognized` text; a `package.json` with vitest: no warning, `ready` as today with a committed manifest; `ci`'s `build-failed` still passes the build output through). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `init::run` reads the plan (`suites::plan`: workspace first, then `choose_lang`, which refuses `lang-unrecognized` or `lang-multiple` from `Lang::detect`), the install (`install`), the hook decision, `raise_min`, then `tsbuild::infer` unless the config is kept or `--lang other`, item 56 and 72's checks, item 71's probe, then prints the language line, writes the config, the lock placeholder and the hooks (undoing what it created on a failure, exit 2), then `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and the briefs, the `[build]` line, the check lines, the local notice, hook-manager notices, `--github`'s protection, the review line, item 91's warning, `ready`, the closing line and the CI line. The messages on these paths keep their text, condition and order: `write_once`'s `shipcraft: created <file>` and `shipcraft: <file> already exists, kept`; `install_hooks`'s installed and already-installed lines; the nested-install notice (`shipcraft: installed under <dir>; hooks call this binary by its path ...`); the seed notice (`.shipcraft.toml exists and was kept; --command and --glob only seed a new config`); the hook-manager notices. Change: detection (point 1), the texts of point 1's lines, item 91's warning text, the `ready` list, the new `node_modules` line and point 3's lines. Everything else: keep.
- `Lang::detect` returns Python for `pyproject.toml`/`requirements*.txt`, Node for a `package.json` declaring vitest or jest (`node_runner`), Go for `go.mod`, in that order. Change: point 1's bare `package.json`. `node_runner` itself: keep (item 91 reads it).
- `tsbuild::infer` reads tracked content only, decides the folders, runs `npx --no -- tsc -b <dirs>` once in the tree through `suite::run_build` (both streams to `init`'s stderr), writes `[build]` on success, and prints a `warn` (three lines, `shipcraft refused:` first) on failure. Change: point 3. The other notices: keep.
- `suite::build` in `ci` and `red`: the build output passes through to stderr, a failure refuses `build-failed`. Keep.
- `main.rs`'s item 69 hint (`init_command`): keep its text and its rule.
- Exit codes: refusals 1, failures 2; an onboarding or briefs failure still reaches `ready` with exit 0; a core write failure removes the files and folders this run created and exits 2; hook-manager writes keep their own restore paths; `tsbuild::infer`'s index reads and the probe's spawn or wait errors fail `init` (exit 2) before anything is written. Rows: `init` writes no measurement row. Keep all of it; the build's own files in the tree are never removed, as today.
- Standing effects the cases can meet: every `hook`, `red`, `ci`, `review`, `pr` and `merge` run appends a row to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl` (creating `.git/shipcraft/`), the hook on every commit even with no lock (a fixture commit after `init` installs hooks writes one), and a failed append changes no exit code; `red` writes `.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`; the post-commit hook makes `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>`; suites, checks and `[build]` run with the caller's `TMPDIR`, `ci`'s suite in the tree on a clean tree or on a copy on a dirty tree without `[build]`, and `ci` with `[build]` on a dirty tree refuses `build-dirty-tree` (the `build-failed` control commits its fixture first). Keep all of it; cases take their log and tree baselines after setup. `init` writes `.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, the hooks, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.shipcraft/briefs/`; the probe runs in the tree and leaves whatever the build writes there (point 3 names it, never removes it); review detection looks for `codex`, `claude` and `gemini` on `PATH`. Keep all of it. The new cases put a `PATH` without real agents where the review line matters, and never a real `npm`, `pnpm` or network.

### `init` and `status` with husky 7 or 8 and with prek (item 133)

Wrong-verdict note. In v2.0.0 (item 25's `nucleo/src/init_tool.rs`, `git tag --contains 4f9d784`, the oldest tag): `init` accepts husky only in husky 9's layout and the pre-commit framework only through the launchers `pre-commit install` writes. Every other husky layout refuses `foreign-hook` with a `Next:` whose manual branch (`or manually put ... in commit-msg and ... in post-commit`) leads back to the same refusal, since `init` never reads the hand-written hooks; and a launcher written by prek (a pre-commit drop-in) refuses `is not from pre-commit`, which `status` repeats as `missing hook` while the hooks run. Found on 2026-11-18 by POC-051 runs 2 (excalidraw, husky 7) and 1 (fastapi, prek), recorded in the POC-051 card (`poc/051-simulated-adopters/FICHA.md`, item 4, commit `18ba6ee`, 2026-11-18T16:20-03:00). The layer that should have caught it is the README, whose table says `husky` without a version, and item 25's cases, which cover husky 9 only and pin no Next that can be followed. A refusal whose Next loops and a `missing hook` for hooks that run are defects, so this is a fix (`kind = fix`, `red --fix --reported-at 2026-11-18T16:20-03:00 --fixes-release v2.0.0`). Victor's decision (2026-11-18, "Pode resolver, tá?"; "Primeiro vamos focar no init individual do Shipcraft").

**Reproduction, on main (2.75.0, the shared binary):**
- (A) husky 7, run 2's layout: a git repository with `core.hooksPath = .husky`, `.husky/_/husky.sh` (husky 7's helper), `.husky/pre-commit` (`#!/bin/sh`, `. "$(dirname "$0")/_/husky.sh"`, a comment), `package.json` naming `husky` 7.0.4 and `vitest`, one test. `init --lang node` refuses `this project does not use husky 9 (core.hooksPath `.husky/_`, `.husky/_/h`, no `husky.hooks` in package.json)`, `Next: shipcraft integrates with husky 9: update husky to 9, run `npx husky` and run `shipcraft init` again, or manually put ...`. After writing `.husky/commit-msg` (`npx --no -- shipcraft check --commit-msg "$1"`) and `.husky/post-commit` (`npx --no -- shipcraft close`), both 0755 and husky 7 style, `init` refuses the same three lines and writes nothing; `status` refuses `there is no .shipcraft.toml`, `Next: run `shipcraft init``.
- (B) prek, run 1's layout: a Python repository with a `.pre-commit-config.yaml` holding one local hook `lint` with no `stages`. `init` exits 0, appends the `shipcraft-check` and `shipcraft-fecha` hooks and says to run `pre-commit install -t commit-msg -t post-commit`. After `prek install -t commit-msg -t post-commit` (prek 0.5.3), `status` prints `shipcraft: missing hook: `shipcraft init` refuses: installed hook commit-msg (.git/hooks/commit-msg) is not from pre-commit`, and `init` refuses the same; yet a commit runs `shipcraft check` and `shipcraft close` through prek, and also runs `lint` twice (at commit-msg and at post-commit), since a hook with no `stages` runs at every hook type installed.

The first RED case reproduces (A) on main: it fails on 2.75.0 because `init` refuses.

Everything in items 15a, 20, 25, 58, 80 and 101 holds unless a point below changes it: the route order of `decide_hook_with`, husky 9's and pre-commit's plans and refusals, the transaction and rollback, the hook integration files the hook and CI protect (`.husky/commit-msg`, `.husky/post-commit`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`), item 58's project-local form, and `status`'s other lines.

1. **husky 7 and 8 are integrated like husky 9.**
   - **The layout.** A project is in the *husky 7 layout* (husky 5 to 8 share it) when the raw `core.hooksPath` is exactly `.husky`, `.husky/_/husky.sh` is a regular file (`symlink_metadata`, not a link) that `fs::read` reads and that holds husky 7's and 8's re-run lines (point 1's helper check below), `.husky/_/h` is absent (`symlink_metadata` fails with `NotFound`; any other result, a dangling link or a lookup error included, counts as present), and `package.json` has no `husky.hooks` (today's `old_husky` reader). husky 9's test (`.husky/_`, `.husky/_/h`, item 25's reader) is checked first and is unchanged; husky 9.1 also writes a `.husky/_/husky.sh` shim, which is why `.husky/_/h` must be absent.
   - **Why the helper matters.** In this layout git runs `.husky/commit-msg` and `.husky/post-commit` directly, so they must be executable, and only the helper makes a failing command stop the hook: husky 7's and 8's `husky.sh` runs the hook again under `sh -e` and exits with its status. A hook that does not source it ignores a failing `shipcraft check` when a later command succeeds. So every hook file this layout accepts sources the helper before any shipcraft line, and the helper itself must be husky's: `.husky/_/husky.sh` qualifies only when, split into lines on `\n` and each trimmed of spaces and tabs, it holds a line exactly `sh -e "$0" "$@"` and a line exactly `exit $exitCode` (husky 7.0.4's and 8.0.3's helper both hold them; a stub such as `:` does not, and the layout is then the "any other" refusal below).
   - **The helper line** of a file is found this way: after the `#!` line (the file must start with `#!`), skip lines that are blank or whose first non-blank character is `#`; the next line is the helper line when, with leading and trailing spaces and tabs removed and an optional trailing comment removed (a space or tab, `#`, then anything), its text is exactly husky 7's `. "$(dirname "$0")/_/husky.sh"` or husky 8's `. "$(dirname -- "$0")/_/husky.sh"`, compared as text (no other path, quoting or spelling qualifies). A comment line before it and a ` # comment` after it are allowed.
   - **The plan** is husky 9's (item 25.4, item 58), with these differences. A hook file that does not exist is written as `#!/bin/sh`, `. "$(dirname "$0")/_/husky.sh"`, an empty line and the command line (`shipcraft check --commit-msg "$1"` or `shipcraft close`, or item 58's `./node_modules/.bin/shipcraft` form), each line ending in `\n`, created with mode 0755. In an existing file that has no shipcraft line (point 2), the command line plus `\n` is inserted right after the helper line's `\n`; when the helper line is the file's last line with no `\n`, `\n`, the command line and `\n` are appended. Every existing byte is kept. An existing file keeps its mode. An existing file refuses `foreign-hook` when it holds a `\r` byte (`<file> has CR line endings, which sh reads as part of each line`, `Next: convert <file> to LF line endings and run shipcraft init again`), when it has no helper line (`<file> does not source .husky/_/husky.sh, so a failing shipcraft command would not stop the hook`, `Next: add . "$(dirname "$0")/_/husky.sh" as its first command and run shipcraft init again`), or when its shipcraft line is not the first command after the helper line, skipping blank and `#` lines (`<file> does not call shipcraft right after sourcing .husky/_/husky.sh`, `Next: put the shipcraft line right after the husky.sh line and run shipcraft init again`): a command before it (an `exit 0`, an `exec`) could skip it, and a call before the helper runs twice. The `CR` refusal is checked first, then the helper line, then the call's place. The `.husky/pre-commit` check (`TWICE`, with point 2's prefixes), the `already calls shipcraft another way` refusal, links, unreadable and binary files refuse as for husky 9.
   - **The writes are one transaction** with `shipcraft_min`'s raise, as today: a new file is written, then its mode set to 0755; when setting the mode fails, everything this run wrote is undone as for a failed write (new files removed, other files back to their bytes), and `init` fails with `set the mode of <file>: <error>` (exit 2, after `shipcraft: undid what this run created`).
   - **What is still missing** is each of `.husky/commit-msg` and `.husky/post-commit`, commit-msg first, that exists before the plan without an execute bit (a file the plan creates is 0755 and never missing), as `.husky/<hook> (not executable)`. The install command printed by `init`'s notice and `status` is `chmod +x` followed by those paths, in the same order, space-separated. With nothing missing, `status` prints `shipcraft: hook through husky (commit-msg)`, as for husky 9.
   - **Every other husky layout still refuses `foreign-hook`, with a Next that can be followed.** The manual branch is dropped from these two refusals, since `init` would refuse again after it:
     - `package.json` has `husky.hooks` (husky 4 and earlier): `shipcraft refused: this project configures husky in package.json (husky.hooks), the form of husky 4 and earlier`, `Next: update husky to 9 and run `npx husky init`, replace the `npm test` line it writes in .husky/pre-commit with your husky.hooks pre-commit command (or remove the file if there is none), move the other husky.hooks commands into .husky/<hook> files, remove husky.hooks from package.json and run `shipcraft init` again`;
     - any other (a `core.hooksPath` starting with `.husky` that is neither layout, or a `.husky/` folder without a husky `core.hooksPath`): `shipcraft refused: this project's husky setup is neither husky 9 (core.hooksPath .husky/_ with .husky/_/h) nor husky 7 or 8 (core.hooksPath .husky with .husky/_/husky.sh)`, `Next: run `npx husky` (husky 9) or `npx husky install` (husky 7 or 8) and run `shipcraft init` again`.
     The `Why:` line stays `foreign-hook`'s. The other `foreign-hook` refusals keep today's text, manual branch included.
2. **A husky line already calling shipcraft through a package runner is recognised.** In husky 9's and husky 7's plan, a line of `.husky/commit-msg` or `.husky/post-commit` is a *shipcraft line* when its words (split on spaces and tabs), after one optional *runner prefix*, start with `shipcraft` or `./node_modules/.bin/shipcraft`. The runner prefixes are the word sequences `npx --no`, `npx --no --`, `pnpm exec` and `pnpm exec --`, the longest that matches: each runs the project's installed binary and fails when it is missing, never fetches a package and never runs a `package.json` script. `npx` and `npx --` without `--no`, and `bunx`, may download a package of another version, and `yarn shipcraft` runs a `shipcraft` script first when the project has one, so none of them is a prefix. A shipcraft line *is the command* when its words after the prefix are exactly the words of the plain or the project-local command line. So `npx --no -- shipcraft check --commit-msg "$1"` is kept as it is (no second call added, nothing written), and `npx --no -- shipcraft check --other` still refuses `already calls shipcraft another way`. Lines without a prefix are judged as today, except that blanks between words no longer matter. A line holding only a prefix (`npx --no`) is not a shipcraft line. The `.husky/pre-commit` check (`init_tool::checks`, `TWICE`) uses the same words: after an optional `exec` and an optional prefix, `shipcraft` or `./node_modules/.bin/shipcraft` followed by `check`.
3. **prek's launchers are recognised like pre-commit's.**
   - **The reader.** A hook file is read with `fs::read_to_string` (as `is_launcher` reads today); a file that does not read as UTF-8 text, or cannot be read, is not a launcher. It is split into lines on `\n` (a trailing `\r` dropped). It is a *prek launcher* for `<hook>` when one line starts with `# File generated by prek` and one *launch line* exists: a line whose shell words (item 25's `shell_words`; a line they cannot read is not a launch line) are, in order, `exec`, `$PREK`, `hook-impl`, then the *options*: every word up to the first `--` word (or to the end), which must include the word `--hook-type=<hook>` exactly and include none that starts with `--config` or is `-c`, except one word that is exactly `--config=.pre-commit-config.yaml` after unquoting; words after `--` are not read (so `--config=".pre-commit-config.yaml"` qualifies, and `--config="other.yaml"`, `--config other.yaml` and `-c other.yaml` do not; an `echo` of the same words, or the launch line commented out, is not a launch line). prek 0.5.3 writes, for `prek install -t commit-msg`: `exec "$PREK" hook-impl --hook-dir "$HERE" --script-version 4 --hook-type=commit-msg -- "$@"`, and adds `--config="<file>"` before ` -- ` for `-c <file>`.
   - Wherever item 25 accepts a pre-commit launcher (`is_launcher`: the installed `commit-msg` and `post-commit`), a prek launcher for the same hook is accepted too; a launcher of either kind may sit beside one of the other. The refusal for an installed hook that is neither becomes `installed hook <hook> (<path>) is not from pre-commit or prek` (Next unchanged). The `pre-commit` hook check (`# shipcraft hook` or a `shipcraft check` line) is unchanged.
   - **The tool's name** is `prek` when the installed `commit-msg` or `post-commit` (`Repo::hook_path`, as item 25 reads them; a failure there fails as today) is a prek launcher for its own hook type, or the installed `pre-commit` is a prek launcher for `pre-commit`; else when neither `commit-msg` nor `post-commit` is a pre-commit launcher (`is_launcher`), `pre-commit` is not on `PATH` and `prek` is (`files::on_path`); otherwise `pre-commit`. A file that cannot be read counts as no launcher of either kind here. So a mixed pair names `prek`. Its install command is `<name> install -t commit-msg -t post-commit`. `status` prints `shipcraft: hook through <name> (commit-msg)`, and `init`'s notice and `status`'s missing line name `<name>`'s command. With no prek launcher and either a pre-commit launcher or no `prek` on `PATH` (or `pre-commit` on it), every line is today's.
4. **`init` says which of the project's pre-commit hooks will also run at commit-msg and post-commit.** pre-commit and prek run a hook at every installed hook type when neither its `stages` nor the top's `default_stages` names one, so installing commit-msg and post-commit makes those hooks run twice more per commit (run 1: `mypy` and `ty` failed at commit-msg, `ruff` printed `(no files to check) Skipped`). Shipcraft does not edit them. When `init` takes the pre-commit route and its writes succeed, it prints one line at the notice's place, right after the pre-commit notice (whether or not that notice printed; also on a run that writes nothing), and nothing when the run is refused or rolled back, if any hook of `.pre-commit-config.yaml` other than Shipcraft's has no `stages` or an empty one, and `default_stages` is absent or empty:
   `shipcraft: these pre-commit hooks have no stages in .pre-commit-config.yaml, so they also run at commit-msg and post-commit: <ids>; to keep them at pre-commit only, add stages: [pre-commit] to them or default_stages: [pre-commit] at the top`
   `<ids>` is `refusal::short_list` of their `id` values, in file order, deduplicated on the raw value before display, each then through `red::one_line` (a hook whose `id` is not a string is left out). Shipcraft's hooks are left out only by id: `shipcraft-check`, `shipcraft-close` and `shipcraft-fecha` (a differing one already refused, item 25). A hook whose remote manifest sets its own `stages` is listed anyway (a known limit: the manifest is not read). `status` prints nothing new.
5. **Shipcraft's post-commit hook id is `shipcraft-close`.** The pair `init` appends to `.pre-commit-config.yaml` is `shipcraft-check` and `shipcraft-close` (`shipcraft-fecha` was a Portuguese leftover); the other six keys are unchanged. **Existing configs, no migration:** a hook with id `shipcraft-fecha` that is otherwise exactly what `init` wrote (its six other keys, plain or project-local) counts as the post-commit hook, as `shipcraft-close` does: `init` keeps it and adds nothing, and `status` reads it as complete. A `shipcraft-fecha` that differs refuses as `shipcraft-close` does, naming `shipcraft-fecha`. A config holding both `shipcraft-close` and `shipcraft-fecha` refuses `foreign-hook`: `.pre-commit-config.yaml has both shipcraft-close and shipcraft-fecha (post-commit)`, with the YAML refusals' Next, since `shipcraft close` would run twice. This refusal is checked over the whole `repos` list before any per-hook comparison, so it wins whatever their order and even when one of them differs.
6. **README.** The hook managers row becomes `plain git hooks, lefthook, husky (9, 7 and 8), pre-commit and prek`.

**The version: 2.76.0.** A fix, but not a patch: `init` now writes into a layout it refused, prints a new line, and writes a new hook id. Nothing breaks a config, a lock, a command or a refusal code (`foreign-hook` keeps its code; old configs with `shipcraft-fecha` keep working), so it is minor. `init` writes `shipcraft_min = "2.76"` in a new config and raises a lower one as today (item 25's monotonic raise through `init_msg::raise_min`, kept by `v1-25.husky.min`, `v1-25.pre.min` and the higher minimum kept by `min-alto`).

**Known limits.** The other `foreign-hook` refusals (pre-commit with a `core.hooksPath`, an unreadable YAML, two hook tools, a foreign `commit-msg` hook) keep a manual branch that `init` does not read afterwards; a way to tell `init` the hooks are wired by hand (`init` writing everything but the hooks, and `status` reading the hand-written calls) is a later item. item 101's `status` route (hooks under a `core.hooksPath` no tool owns) does not learn point 2's runner prefixes. A `yarn shipcraft ...` line is not recognised (a project script of that name would win), so `init` adds its own line beside it. prek's own config files (`prek.toml`) and `.pre-commit-config.yml` are not read. A remote hook's manifest `stages` is not read (point 4). `pre-commit install` without `-t pre-commit` leaves the project's pre-commit stage uninstalled if it was; Shipcraft does not add it. A hook installed with `pre-commit install --hook-type` into a `core.hooksPath` still refuses as today.

**Metric:** on the release, reproductions (A) and (B) end with `init` exit 0 and `status` printing `hook through husky (commit-msg)` and `hook through prek (commit-msg)`, and (B)'s `init` prints point 4's line naming `lint`. POC-051's second arm (the same six repositories) counts `foreign-hook` refusals and `missing hook` lines for hooks that run: target 0 on runs 1 and 2. Revert: if a husky 7 or prek project gets a hook that does not run (a commit not checked while `status` says `hook through`), point 1 or 3 goes back to refusing that layout, in a patch.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** section 11 and `nucleo/Cargo.toml` (and `Cargo.lock`) go to 2.76.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-69.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11, the manifest or `shipcraft_min`).
- **Assertions that change** (the RED adjusts exactly these, mechanically):
  - `acceptance/lib/item25.sh`: the `hook(event)` helper's post-commit id becomes `shipcraft-close`, and the two id tuples in `integrated` (`('shipcraft-check', 'shipcraft-fecha')`) hold `shipcraft-close` (they may hold all three ids). This one helper change carries every case that goes through `integrated` or `add_pair` on the pre-commit route: `v1-25.pre.comum`, `sem-launchers`, `parcial-launchers`, `indentacao`, `parcial`, `min`, `min-alto`; `v1-25.yaml.cr`, `crlf`, `ls`, `ps`, `bom`, `comentario`, `literal`, `flow-outros`; the `v1-25.pre.estagios.*` cases that integrate; `v1-25.pre.estagios-lista.sobrepoe`; the `v1-25.pre.entry.local.*` and `entry.remoto.*` cases `wrapper`, `status`, `exec`, `check-extra`, `argumento-antes`; `v1-25.pre.entry-remoto-ausente`. The `igual`, `igual-flow-stages`, `igual-reordenado` and `status-*` fixtures then write `shipcraft-close` too; the old id is covered by this item's point 5 cases. The seven `v1-25.pre.diferente.sc2-fecha.*` cases keep their names, select the post-commit hook through `hook()`, and now expect `shipcraft-close` in the refusal;
  - `acceptance/lib/v1.sh`, case `v1-15a.2-husky-ou-pre-commit-na-raiz` (the `"prefix kept and the exact local pair of item 25"` check): `shipcraft-fecha` becomes `shipcraft-close`;
