### `init` runs pytest with the project's Python (item 134)

Wrong-verdict note. In v2.0.0 (`nucleo/src/lang.rs`'s Python profile, `git tag --contains 4f9d784`, the oldest tag): `init` writes `test_command = "python3 -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --junitxml={junit}"` for every Python project, so the hook, `red` and `ci` run whichever `python3` is first on `PATH` when they run, not the project's virtual environment. On a Mac whose `python3` is the system 3.9 without pytest, `red` refuses `no-report` with `No module named pytest` and a `Next:` that says to create the stub, which already exists. Found on 2026-11-18 by POC-051 runs 1 (fastapi: uv, `.venv` with Python 3.11) and 4 (httpx: `.venv`), recorded in the POC-051 card (`poc/051-simulated-adopters/FICHA.md`, item 5, commit `18ba6ee`, 2026-11-18T16:20-03:00); both users kept `.venv/bin` first on `PATH` by hand. The layer that should have caught it is item 6's Python profile and its cases, whose fixture puts the venv on `PATH` for every case, so a bare `python3` always found pytest. A test command that runs the wrong interpreter is a defect of `init`'s inference, so this is a fix (`kind = fix`, `red --fix --reported-at 2026-11-18T16:20-03:00 --fixes-release v2.0.0`). Victor's decision (2026-11-18, "Pode resolver, tá?"; "Primeiro vamos focar no init individual do Shipcraft").

**Reproduction, on main (2.76.0, the shared binary), with `PATH` holding `/usr/bin` (system `python3` 3.9.6, no pytest), the binary and nothing else:**
- (A) a git repository with `pyproject.toml`, `.venv/` (`python3 -m venv .venv`, pytest installed into it) ignored by `.gitignore`, and `tests/test_ok.py`. `init` exits 0 and writes `test_command = "python3 -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --junitxml={junit}"`. After committing, a stub `shouty.py` (`raise NotImplementedError`) and `tests/test_shout.py`, `red` refuses `suite . did not write the JUnit report: ... wrote to stderr: .../python3: No module named pytest`, `Next: create the stub: the function exists and raises NotImplementedError; then run shipcraft red again`.
- (B) the same with `uv.lock` and no `.venv`, `uv` (0.13.0) on `PATH`: `init` writes the same bare `python3` command and says nothing about uv.

The first RED case reproduces (A) on main: it fails on 2.76.0 because the command `init` writes starts with `python3`.

Everything in items 6, 16a.2, 56, 67, 90 and 131 holds unless a point below changes it: language detection, the pytest flags and globs, `--lang`, `--command`/`--glob` (written as given), a kept config (`init` never rewrites it), the transaction, and every other `init`, `red`, `ci`, hook and `status` line.

1. **`init` picks the project's Python.** When `init` writes a new config with the Python profile at the root (detected or `--lang python`; never with `--command`, never for a kept config), the command is `<python> -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --junitxml={junit}` where `<python>` is, first match:
   - `.venv/bin/python`, when `.venv/bin/python` at the repository root is an executable file (`files::is_executable`: `fs::metadata`, so a link to the base interpreter counts and a dangling one does not);
   - `venv/bin/python`, the same test on `venv/`;
   - `uv run --frozen python`, when `fs::metadata` of `uv.lock` at the repository root succeeds and is a file (a link to a file counts; a dangling link, a folder or any error does not; its content is never read, so an empty file counts) and `uv` is on `PATH` as `files::on_path` reads it (each `PATH` entry joined with `uv` and tested with `is_executable`; a relative entry resolves against `init`'s working directory, a known limit). `--frozen` runs the locked environment without rewriting `uv.lock`; `uv run` creates or syncs `.venv` when it has to;
   - `python3`, as today.
   `python -m pytest` is kept in every form (not a bare `pytest`): it puts the suite's root on `sys.path`, which tests that import the project's modules rely on. A venv beside `uv.lock` wins over uv: it is what `uv sync` made, and running it needs no uv at commit time. Windows' `Scripts\python.exe` is out of scope (Shipcraft ships for macOS; section 11). Poetry, pdm and hatch environments outside the project folder are not looked for (a later item); an in-project one named `.venv` is found by the first rule. Nothing checks that pytest is installed in the chosen interpreter.
2. **`init` says which Python it chose.** When point 1 applies, one line is printed right after `shipcraft: language python (runner pytest)`, together with it and like it before any write (so it also appears on a run that is later refused or rolled back, as the language line does):
   - `shipcraft: Python interpreter .venv/bin/python (the project's virtual environment); to use another, edit test_command in .shipcraft.toml` (or `venv/bin/python`);
   - `shipcraft: Python through uv run --frozen (uv.lock and uv on PATH, no runnable .venv/bin/python or venv/bin/python); to use another, edit test_command in .shipcraft.toml`;
   - `shipcraft: Python interpreter python3 from PATH (no runnable .venv/bin/python or venv/bin/python, and no uv.lock with uv on PATH), so the hook runs whichever python3 is first on PATH at commit time; to use another, edit test_command in .shipcraft.toml`.
   No such line with `--command`, with a kept config (item 90's cases forbid any line matching `^shipcraft: (python|node|go)\b` there), or for another language. When `<python>` is a venv, `init` runs `git check-ignore -q -- <dir>/bin/python` with the repository root as its working directory (the interpreter's own path, so a venv whose inner `.gitignore` holds `*`, as `python3 -m venv` writes on recent Pythons, counts as ignored); when it exits 1 (not ignored) and the run's core writes succeed (the config, the lock folder and the hooks; a later `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` or brief failure, which keeps exit 0 as today, does not stop it), it prints at item 131's `node_modules` line's place (right before `ready`) `shipcraft: warning: .venv/bin/python is not ignored by git, and the hook and ci run the suite in a copy that links only ignored files when the tree differs from what they judge: add .venv/ to .gitignore before committing` (or `venv`). Exit 0, any other exit, a signal or a failure to start git print nothing.
3. **A suite that wrote no report because its interpreter is missing says so.** Nothing runs earlier or differently: `[build]`, the suite's `check`, suite selection (items 55, 60, 65), the narrowed run and its fallback, and the command itself (with `sh -c`, so `||` fallbacks still work) are as today. Only when a suite's run ends in today's `suite <name> did not write the JUnit report: ...` (`suite::missing`, used by `run_suites`; the narrowed run's no-report suites reach it through the fallback full run as today) is the suite's command, as written in the config (before `{junit}` replacement and filter rewriting), read: its first word, split on ASCII whitespace (an empty command reads nothing). Then:
   - when that word is `<dir>/bin/python` or `<dir>/bin/python3` with a nonempty `<dir>` (so `bin/python` alone keeps today's refusal), and the path (a relative one joined to the suite's root in the folder where the suite ran, the tree or the checkout copy; an absolute one as it is) is not an executable file (`files::is_executable`), the refusal becomes (code `no-report`, exit 1, in the hook, `red`, `red --redo` and `ci` alike, never `red`'s stub `Next:`): today's first line (`suite <name> did not write the JUnit report: ...`, with the command and its stderr as today) followed by `; its interpreter <word> is missing or not executable at the suite's root <root>`; `Why: the suite's command names a Python interpreter that the folder where the suite ran does not have, as observed after it ran`; `Next:` for a relative word, `create it at the suite's root (python3 -m venv <dir> and install the test dependencies into it, or uv sync); if it exists in the working tree, add <dir>/ to .gitignore, since the hook's copy links only ignored files; or edit <key> in .shipcraft.toml; then try again`, and for an absolute word, `install that interpreter or edit <key> in .shipcraft.toml; then try again`. `<dir>` is the word without its `/bin/python` or `/bin/python3`; `<name>` and `<root>` are shown as the root check shows them; `<key>` is `test_command` for the top-level suite and `command of [[suite]] <name>` for a `[[suite]]`;
   - when that word is exactly `uv` and `uv` is not on `PATH` (`files::on_path`): today's first line followed by `; uv is not on PATH`, `Why: the suite's command runs through uv`, `Next: install uv or edit <key> in .shipcraft.toml; then try again`, same code and exit.
   Otherwise today's refusal is printed unchanged. A suite that writes a report is judged as today whatever its first word.
   **In a linked worktree, the main worktree's venv.** A new `git worktree add` has no ignored folders, so a relative venv path would refuse in every RED or GREEN worktree (as `v1-17.worktree` shows). Right before a suite's command runs (after its `check`, so a check that creates the venv wins), when its first word, read as above, is `<dir>/bin/python` or `<dir>/bin/python3` with a nonempty relative `<dir>`, is not an executable file at the suite's root in the folder where the suite runs, and the repository is a linked worktree (`git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir` differs from `--git-dir`, the common dir's parent is the main worktree, and the common dir's last component is `.git`), and `<main>/<suite root>/<word>` is an executable file: the command runs with that first word replaced by the absolute path (shell-quoted with `sh` single quotes), and Shipcraft prints to stderr, before `running the suite`, `shipcraft: suite <name> runs <absolute path> (this worktree has no <dir>; the main worktree's)`. The config is not changed. A checkout copy of a linked worktree resolves the same way (its main worktree is the repository's). In the main worktree, a bare repository, or when the main worktree has no executable interpreter either, nothing is replaced and point 3's refusal applies.
4. **`status` names a bare `python3` beside a project venv (read-only).** For each suite of the config, in order, whose command's first word (point 3's reader: ASCII whitespace, the config's text, an empty command reads nothing) is exactly `python3` or `python`, and whose root (in the working tree) holds an executable `.venv/bin/python` or, failing that, `venv/bin/python`, `status` prints, right after the `open locks` line: `shipcraft: suite <name> runs <word> from PATH, but <root>/<dir>/bin/python is the project's virtual environment; to use it, start <key> with <dir>/bin/python in .shipcraft.toml`, where `<root>` is shown as the root check shows it (`.` at the root), `<dir>` is `.venv` or `venv`, and `<key>` is point 3's (the path in the advice is relative to the suite's root, where the command runs). Exit code, other lines and rows unchanged. **Existing configs, no migration:** nothing rewrites a config; `init` on a kept config prints none of point 2's lines.
5. **The GitHub guide's Python setup creates the venv.** In `docs/github-ci.md`'s "Language setup", the Python block becomes: `python3 -m venv .venv`, `.venv/bin/python -m pip install pytest`, then today's loop (`for f in requirements*.txt; do if [ -f "$f" ]; then ... -r "$f"; fi; done`, the shell's glob order, the `-f` guard, a failing install stopping the step as today) through `.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r`, then `echo "$PWD/.venv/bin" >> "$GITHUB_PATH"`, so a config with `.venv/bin/python` and an older one with `python3` both run there. A sentence under it says the folder must be the one the committed command names: a project whose command starts with `venv/bin/python` replaces `.venv` with `venv` in the block. A second block, an alternative to the first with the same `if: steps.layer.outputs.run == 'true'` conditions, for uv projects: `astral-sh/setup-uv@v6`, then `uv sync --frozen` (it installs the default groups, `dev` included; a project whose pytest is in another group adds `--group <name>`), which creates `.venv` where `init`'s commands look for it (unless `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT` moves it, a known limit), then the same `GITHUB_PATH` line. The setup limits keep naming `pyproject.toml` and `requirements`. README unchanged.

**The version: 2.77.0.** A fix, but not a patch: `init` writes a different command and new lines, and the hook, `red` and `ci` refuse a run they used to start (under the existing `no-report` code). Nothing breaks a config, a lock, a command or a refusal code, so it is minor. A new config gets `shipcraft_min = "2.77"` as today; a `.venv/bin/python` config means the same to an older binary, which only lacks point 3's refusal.

**Known limits.** Windows venv paths; interpreters outside the project (poetry's and pdm's default cache, pyenv, conda, hatch); a venv in a subfolder other than the root; a command prefixed with `env` or variables; whether pytest is installed in the chosen interpreter; a uv project's `uv run` may download packages at commit time when `.venv` is missing or out of date; `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT` placing uv's environment elsewhere; a relative `PATH` entry read from `init`'s working directory; the missing-interpreter message reads only the command's first word (a word after `env`, a variable assignment or a quote is not read).

**Metric:** on the release, reproductions (A) and (B) end with `red` accepted and the lock written, without the venv on `PATH`, and `init` printing point 2's line. POC-051's second arm counts Python projects where the user puts a venv on `PATH` by hand or edits `test_command`: target 0 on runs 1 and 4. Revert: if a project's chosen interpreter runs the suite differently from the user's own `python -m pytest` (another result, not a missing venv), point 1 goes back to `python3` in a patch.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** section 11 and `nucleo/Cargo.toml` (and `Cargo.lock`) go to 2.77.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-69.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11, the manifest or `shipcraft_min`).
- **Assertions that change** (the RED adjusts exactly these, mechanically):
  - `acceptance/lib/item63.sh`, `i63_brief`: the review brief quotes the config's command, which `init` now writes as `.venv/bin/python -m pytest ...` on the battery's Python target (its `.venv` holds pytest), so `'python3 -m pytest' in s` becomes `' -m pytest' in s`, and the SHA-256 of the normalized brief (`7b4e46d5...`) is computed after one more normalization, `.venv/bin/python -m pytest` replaced by `python3 -m pytest`, so the expected hash stays: `v1-63.brief-local`, `brief-github`, `brief-local-docs`, `brief-github-docs`, `brief-off`, `brief-off-spoof`, `brief-review`, `brief-review-pr`, `brief-review-pr-spoof`.
  - The same one-line normalization (before hashing, the expected hashes unchanged) in every other case that hashes the review brief with `7b4e46d5346a968de2db5be396987bf35de813f21b0d3796ef9be1b53f0dc1da` or `773a99d73de349ea8f13e93fcf3f8ee86bbd61a8a7b805e59219a5fb0e151c16`: `acceptance/lib/item82.sh` (`v1-82.brief-local`, failing in the prototype run, and its siblings), `acceptance/lib/item96.sh` and `acceptance/lib/item102.sh`.
  - Explicitly supplied commands stay as written and are not rewritten: item 30's fixtures and `acceptance/tools/item30.py`, `v1-1.3-config-da-ponta-do-branch-base`, and the hand-written monorepo configs in `acceptance/lib/v1.sh`.
  - The RED searches `acceptance/` for `python3 -m pytest`, `test_command`, `language python`, `runner pytest`, `No module named` and `open locks` and lists, before `red`, every other case whose assertion this item changes (a literal `python3` command read back from a config `init` wrote, an exact `status` line count or order after `open locks`, an exact `init` line count or the line right after the language line), adjusting only those (rule 11). A prototype of points 1, 2 and 4 (not 3) passed the `python` profile 384 of 384; on `core` its failures were `v1-82.brief-local` (above) and `v1-17.worktree` (a linked worktree with no `.venv`, which point 3's worktree rule must keep passing unchanged).
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - item 6's per-language scenarios and the v0 cases on the Python target (`acceptance/run.sh python`, `init-detect`, `init-files`, `init-workflow`, `init-lang-flag`): the base target's `.venv` holds pytest and is git-ignored, so the new command runs the same suite; `init-files` lists the files `init` writes (no new file);
  - item 90's kept-config cases (`v1-90.*`): with no language chosen, no line may match `^shipcraft: (python|node|go)\b` (case-insensitive), which point 2's lines would; point 2 prints nothing for a kept config;
  - item 131's language-line and detection cases (`v1-131.*`), item 91's warning cases (`v1-91.*`), item 67's custom-command cases (`v1-67.*`: no inference, no line);
  - items 55, 60 and 65's selection, check, fallback and second-run cases (`v1-55.main`, `v1-55.error`, `v1-55.redo`, `v1-60.monorepo`, `v1-60.check-fails`, `v1-65.monorepo`, `v1-65.monorepo-check`, `v1-65.no-report`, `v1-65.no-report-unmapped`, `v1-65.second-run-fails`): point 3 only rewords a refusal that already happens;
  - item 16a.2's `no-report` text for a suite that runs and writes no report (`acceptance/lib/item16a.sh`), and item 45's failing-check refusal (`v1-45.*`): a `python3` or `pytest` first word never takes point 3's path;

  - item 61's and item 68's onboarding and write-failure cases (`v1-61.refused`, `v1-61.refused-late`, `v1-68.core-file`, `v1-68.core-refused`, `v1-68.core-hooks`, `v1-68.write-fails`, `v1-68.write-fails-kept`, `v1-68.write-partial` and the folder-failure cases): point 2's interpreter line prints with the language line, the warning only after the core writes succeed;
  - item 89's `status` line order (`v1-89.*`: its line right before `open locks`), item 73's and item 64's `status` cases;
  - item 27's guide checks (`v1-27.doc-*`, `acceptance/tools/item27.py`: the setup limits naming `pyproject.toml` and `requirements`, the workflow YAML);
  - item 128's README cases (`v1-128.readme`, `v1-128.readme-once`, a hash of the README): the README does not change;
  - item 30's and item 105's hand-written Python configs (`python3 -m pytest`, `pytest -q`): they run as today.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item134.sh` (ids `v1-134.<name>`), on real git fixtures with a real venv (`python3 -m venv`, pytest from the battery's pip cache) where an interpreter must run, and a `PATH` the case controls, whose `python3` cannot import pytest (a stub `python3` on `PATH` that exits 1 with `No module named pytest` stands in for the system one, so the case does not depend on the machine's Python), and a stub `uv` that records its arguments and runs `.venv/bin/python` or exits as the case needs (no real uv, no network). Estimate, 40: point 1, 12 (reproduction (A): `.venv/bin/python` written, `red` accepted with the stub `python3` first on `PATH`, lock written; `venv/` chosen when `.venv` is absent; `.venv` beats `venv`; a `.venv` without `bin/python`, or with a dangling link, or a non-executable one: next rule; `uv.lock` with `uv` on `PATH`: `uv run --frozen python ...` written, and a hook commit runs it through the stub; `uv.lock` without `uv` on `PATH`: `python3`; `uv.lock` beside `.venv`: the venv; neither: `python3`, control; `--lang python` takes the same choice; `--command` written as given and no line; kept config untouched and no line; the Python globs and flags unchanged); point 2, 9 (each of the three lines, in place right after the language line; the warning for an unignored `.venv` before `ready`; no warning when ignored; no warning on a rolled-back run; no line for node, control; the line's `venv/bin/python` variant); point 3, 12, the worktree rule's 3 included (a linked worktree without `.venv`: the main worktree's interpreter runs and the stderr line names it; neither has it: refused; a GREEN commit through the hook, with a lock open, after `.venv` is removed: refused with the three lines, the commit does not happen; `red` with the venv missing: the interpreter refusal, not the stub Next; `ci` the same; a venv present but not ignored, with a GREEN commit made while a tracked file has an unstaged change (so the hook runs in a copy): refused, while `red` in the tree runs; `bin/python` not executable; `/bin/python3` ending; an absolute missing path; `uv` first word with `uv` absent from `PATH`; `uv` present: runs, control; `python3` first word: today's no-report path, control; a hand-written `[[suite]]` in a subfolder whose `<root>/.venv/bin/python` is missing: `<name>` and `<root>` shown; a `check` that creates the venv before the command: accepted, control; a command `.venv/bin/python ... || python3 ...` whose fallback writes the report: accepted, control); point 4, 5 (a kept `python3` config beside `.venv`: the line, after `open locks`, exit 0; `python` word; `venv/` path; a `.venv/bin/python` config: no line, control; a subfolder suite: `<root>/.venv/bin/python`); point 5, 2 (the guide's Python block has the four steps and still parses as YAML where item 27 reads it; the uv block). The RED may trade cases between points within 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `Lang::profile` (Python) returns runner `pytest`, command `python3 -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider --junitxml={junit}`, no check, and the seven Python globs; `init::suites::plan` builds the root suite from it with `repo.root()`; Python has no workspace route. Change: point 1's command; runner, check and globs keep.
- `init::run` prints `shipcraft: language python (runner pytest)` (single suite), `shipcraft: test command run as written (filter = "command")` with `--command`, nothing for a kept config with no language; then the create/kept lines, hook writes and rollback, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, briefs, `[build]`, the local-install and hook-manager notices, `--github`, the review line, item 131's `node_modules` line, item 91's warning, `ready`, the guidance and the CI line. Keep; point 2 adds its lines at the places named.
- `suite::run_suites` skips (in the hook) suites with no locked case, checks the root (`suite <name> cannot run: root ... is not a directory`, a `Missing` at once), runs the suite's `check` (not in the hook), then the command with `sh -c` in the suite's root, and with no report gives `suite <name> did not write the JUnit report: ...` (`Missing`), which `red` turns into `no-report` with the stub `Next:` and the hook and `ci` (`contract::no_report`) into `no-report` with `run the suite manually ...`, exit 1. `suite::run_changed` (red's narrowed run) records a missing root as `Narrowed::NoReport` and goes on, runs every suite's check, skips the command of a suite with no selected file, and leaves a missing report to red's coverage and fallback. `red` and `ci` run `[build]` (`suite::build`) before any suite; its output stays in the tree. `ci` refuses `build-dirty-tree` on a dirty tree with `[build]` before any build or suite, and skips suites for `suite_checks` and docs-only changes with its own lines. Checks, `[build]` and suites run with the caller's `TMPDIR`. In `init`, a failed core write rolls back and exits 2, while an `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` or brief write failure prints its notice, removes the brief's temporary file and keeps exit 0. Keep all of it; point 3 changes only the text of the `did not write the JUnit report` refusal when its condition holds.
- `Repo::export` links into the checkout copy every path git ignores (but `__pycache__`), so an ignored `.venv` is reached through a link in the copy. Keep.
- `merge::status` prints branch, layers, roles, the hook line, the `AGENTS.md` line, `open locks`, then branch protection; exit 0, no row. Keep; point 4's lines go after `open locks`.
- Standing effects the cases can meet, all kept: `main::measured` and `medicao::record` create `.git/shipcraft/` and append to `runs.jsonl` best effort (a failed append changes no exit code); the hook writes a row on every commit, even with no lock; `red` and `red --redo` write `.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`; `close` appends one row per lock it closes and none when it closes nothing, and the post-commit hook makes `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>` when a commit's locked cases all pass; the hook and `ci` run the suite in the tree when it matches what they judge (no unstaged change and no alternate index for the hook, a clean tree for `ci`) and in a checkout copy otherwise; `red` writes `.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`; `init` writes `.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, the hooks, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.shipcraft/briefs/`; review detection looks for `codex`, `claude` and `gemini` on `PATH`; suites run with the caller's `TMPDIR`.
- New code goes in its own module (`nucleo/src/python_env.rs` or similar) so `lang.rs`, `suite.rs`, `init.rs` and `merge.rs` change by a few lines each.

### Single suite (top level)

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `shipcraft_min` | Minimum `shipcraft` version, `"X.Y"`. An older binary refuses (`shipcraft-min`). |
| `lang` | `python`, `node` or `go`. |
| `runner` | The runner name (`pytest`, `vitest`, `jest`, `gotestsum`). |
| `test_command` | The test command. `{junit}` is replaced by the report path. |
| `test_globs` | Test and support file globs. These files are locked. Any other file the GREEN must not change, such as the spec the tests were written from, can be listed too: it is protected and never run. |
| `check` | Optional project-wide check (type check, vet). Runs in CI and at RED, never in the hook. A failing check refuses `no-report` with a reason that names the check (item 45). |
| `filter` | Who filters the hook's run (section 6). |

### Sections

| Key | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `[[suite]] name` | Suite name. Prefixes its case ids. | the folder |
| `[[suite]] root` | Folder the command runs in. Globs are relative to it. | `.` |
| `[[suite]] command` | Test command, with `{junit}`. | required |
| `[[suite]] check` | Project-wide check for this suite. | none |
| `[[suite]] globs` | Test and support file globs, plus any other file to protect from the GREEN (see `test_globs`). | `[]` |
| `[[suite]] filter` | `"shipcraft"` or `"command"` (section 6). | `"shipcraft"` |
| `[build] command` | Runs once at the root before the first suite (section 6). | none |
| `[execution] ci` | Where CI runs: `local` (this machine, through `shipcraft pr`), `github` (GitHub Actions) or `off` (not run, not required). | `local` |
| `[execution] review` | `local` or `off`. `github` is reserved and refused (`review-github`). | `local` |
| `[execution] ci_timeout` | Local CI time limit, in seconds. | `1800` |
| `[execution] suite_checks` | Names of the project's own GitHub checks that run the suite; only with `ci = "github"` (item 62). | `[]` |
| `[execution] merge` | How `shipcraft merge` merges: `merge` (a merge commit) or `squash` (item 42). | `merge` |
| `[roles] review` | The review agent: `codex`, `claude` or `gemini`, or a table with `agent`, `model` and `effort` (item 64). | first one installed |
| `[roles] red`, `[roles] green` | The agent (string or table, as `review`) the orchestrator uses for each phase; shown, never run (item 64). | not set |
| `[review] spec` | Path of a project spec given to the reviewer. | none |
| `[review] timeout` | Review time limit, in seconds. | `1200` |
| `[review] retry_wait` | Seconds between review attempts after a `Please retry` failure (item 87); `0` turns the retry off. | `120` |
| `[review] after` | When `shipcraft pr` starts the review with `ci = "github"`: `ci` (after GitHub CI passed) or `push` (at once, beside GitHub CI; item 96). | `ci` |
| `[review] guide` | Path of a project file whose text the reviewer gets in its own section of the brief, read at the base commit (item 102). | none |
| `[report] release_tags` | The tags `report` reads as releases: a pattern where `*` matches any run of characters (item 99). | `v<X>.<Y>.<Z>` tags |
| `[findings] builtin_classes` | Whether `report --findings` groups by item 121's built-in classes after the project's own (item 122). | `true` |
| `[[findings.class]] name`, `keywords` | A class of review findings for `report --findings`: its name and the patterns that match a finding's title, tried before the built-in classes (item 122). | none |
| `[machine] slots` | Concurrent local CI and review runs on this machine. | `1` |
| `[pr] warn_lines` | Product lines above which the size line in the PR body (item 103) and the PR summary suggest a split; `init` writes it, and `report` counts PRs against it (item 107). | `500` |
| `[pr] shape_lines` | Fixes the file-size threshold of the shape warning (an integer above 0). | p90 of the project |

### Rules

- **The config is sealed by the lock:** its hash is in `files`, so nobody changes how the suite runs while a lock is open.
- **A PR is judged by the base branch's config** (`origin/<base>` as it is now), not its own. A PR cannot turn off its own review or change its own suite command.
- **A layer that is off is labeled:** its status is published as `success` with the description "off in .shipcraft.toml" on every PR, so the choice is visible, and the merge gate does not require it.
- **`shipcraft_min`:** an older binary refuses, in the hook and in CI, as it does a lock in an unknown format. Two machines never judge one commit differently without warning.
- **Validation:** unknown values, an absolute `root`, or a path or glob with `..` are refused. `.` and empty path components are normalized away.

### Roles per phase (item 64)

Shipcraft runs the review agent itself (`[roles] review`). The RED and the GREEN are written by sessions that the orchestrator starts, and the lock is what makes them honest. Item 64 lets a project write down which agent, model and effort each phase uses, so the choice lives in the versioned config and not in someone's memory. It also lets the review pick a model and an effort. Shipcraft still starts no RED or GREEN session. Why: on this repository the choice (RED by Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` at medium effort, GREEN by Claude, review by Codex) lived only in private notes, which blocked dogfood step 4.

1. **The keys.** `[roles] review`, `[roles] red` and `[roles] green`. Each is either a string, the agent alone (`"codex"`, `"claude"` or `"gemini"`, as `review` is today), or a TOML table with the keys `agent` (required), `model` and `effort` (both optional). Any TOML spelling of a table is the same value: inline `review = { agent = "codex" }`, a `[roles.review]` section, or dotted keys. The string `"codex"` and the table `{ agent = "codex" }` mean the same thing everywhere. `red` and `green` have no default: absent means the orchestrator chooses. `review` keeps its defaults: `init` writes the first agent installed, as a string, and when the key is absent at run time the reviewer is `codex`, as today.
2. **Validation.** It applies wherever the config is read: the hook, `red`, `ci`, `pr`, `review`, `merge` and `status`, each reading the same snapshot it reads today (staged, base or working tree). A refusal is `config-invalid`, and its reason names the exact key path, for example `roles.green.agent` for a table without `agent`, or `roles.red.flavor` for an unknown member. The order: everything that refuses today keeps its place and comes first (parse and structure errors, legacy keys, `shipcraft-min`, item 47's `[execution]` checks). Then come the `shipcraft_min` rule below, and `red`, `green` and `review`, in that order. Within one role: the value's type, then `agent` (missing or unknown), then unknown members in key order, then `model`, then `effort`. Each of these is refused:
   - a value that is neither a string nor a table;
   - an agent that is not `codex`, `claude` or `gemini`;
   - a table without `agent`, or with any key other than `agent`, `model` and `effort`;
   - a `model` or `effort` that is not a string, is empty, or starts with `-`, or that has a whitespace or control character anywhere. These are checked on the decoded TOML string, with Unicode's definitions (Rust's `char::is_whitespace` and `char::is_control`), so U+00A0 and U+0085 are refused;
   - an `effort` not in its agent's list. For `codex`: `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`. For `claude`: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`, as `claude --help` lists them. `gemini` takes no effort.
   A table form, or `red` or `green` in any form, also needs `shipcraft_min` of `2.20` or more, compared as `shipcraft_min` is compared today. A config that uses none of these is unaffected. Otherwise the config is refused `config-invalid`, and the reason names `shipcraft_min`: older binaries do not know these keys and would judge the project differently. Validation checks the shape only. Whether the model exists, and whether it accepts that effort, is the agent CLI's business: a reviewer that rejects them fails to start, which is a review `error`, as today, and is not retried.
3. **The review uses its model and effort.** With `[roles] review` as a table (read from the base config, as today), the reviewer starts with the model and effort given, and with neither when they are absent (as today):
   - `codex`: `-m <model>` and `-c model_reasoning_effort=<effort>`;
   - `claude`: `--model <model>` and `--effort <effort>`;
   - `gemini`: `-m <model>` (the flag of the gemini CLI's `--model`; it is not installed on the machine where this was written, so the battery checks it with its stub).
   Each one goes before the prompt, as separate arguments. The agent name in the review status, its comment and the row's `agent` stays the agent alone.
   - **Rows.** Every review row that is not reused (the same rows that item 63's `after_green_ci` goes on) gains `model` and `effort`: the strings the config asked for, or `null` when absent. They are not the provider's effective defaults.
   - **Reuse.** An earlier row is a source only when its `agent`, `model` and `effort` also equal this run's. A row without `model` or `effort` (every row from before this item), or with `null`, counts as `null` for each. A row whose `agent`, `model` or `effort` is present but is not a string (or `null` for `model` and `effort`) is never a source. Unreadable logs and lines are handled as today. So when the string form is unchanged, earlier verdicts are still reused, but a change of model or effort reviews again. A reused row records the `model` and `effort` of the row it reused.
4. **The RED and GREEN roles are shown, not run.**
   - `shipcraft status` prints one more line after the layers line, whether review is on or off: `shipcraft: roles: red <role>; green <role>; review <role>`. A `<role>` is the agent, followed by ` (<model>, effort <effort>)`, ` (<model>)` or ` (effort <effort>)` when they are given. An absent `red` or `green` reads `not set (the orchestrator's choice)`. `review` is the configured value, or `codex` when the key is absent, as today. The layers line is unchanged.
   - After `shipcraft red` or `shipcraft red --redo` writes a lock, it prints on stdout, after its last line today: `shipcraft: next: the GREEN, in a new session (<green role>, [roles] green)`, or `shipcraft: next: the GREEN, in a new session ([roles] green not set)`. A refusal prints neither line.
5. **What uses the roles.** The lock, the hook, CI and the gate validate `red` and `green` (point 2) but never use them to choose or check who wrote a commit. A session that is not the declared agent is not detected or refused; the lock stays the mechanical guarantee. The orchestrator reads the roles from `shipcraft status`, which the `AGENTS.md` block already tells agents to run. The block itself does not change in this item: telling the orchestrator to start each phase with its role belongs to a later item that changes the block, together with its upgrade path.
6. **Measure and revert.** The metric is false refusals of a valid `[roles]` config across the next 5 items (target 0), plus whether dogfood step 4 is written in `.shipcraft.toml`. The revert is to remove the new keys, or to go back to the string form. Product rollback: the previous release.

### A failing `check` (item 45)

Whenever a run reaches a suite's `check` (in `red`, and in `ci` on HEAD), the check and then that suite's test command run, suite by suite in declaration order, as today. When the check's shell (`sh -c`, the same as for the test command) ends unsuccessfully, by a non-zero exit or a signal that shipcraft observes, the run stops and refuses `no-report`, as today, but with its own reason. Before item 45 the reason said the suite "did not write the JUnit report" and quoted stderr only. In draken-platform the first `red` failed this way: the check was `tsc --noEmit`, which writes its errors to stdout, so the reason said "exited with 2 without writing to stderr" and pointed at the report.

- **Reason:** `suite <name>: the check `<check>` failed (<how it ended>): <line>`. `<name>` is the suite's name as in the other `no-report` reasons. `<check>` is rendered on one line as the command is today. `<how it ended>` is worded as today (for example `exited with 2`, or the signal). `<line>` is the first non-blank line the check wrote on stderr, found by today's stderr scanner; if stderr has none, the first non-blank line on stdout, found by the same scanner (same decoding, trimming and length cut). Without either, the reason ends after the parenthesis, with no colon.
- **Why and Next:** Why says the suite is judged only after the project passes its check. Next says to run the check at the suite's root (naming the root and the command), and to fix what it reports, which can be the code or stub, or a missing tool or dependency; in `red` it ends with "then run `shipcraft red` again".
- **Unchanged:** the refusal code stays `no-report` and the run log records it as before; the check's output still passes through to shipcraft's stderr, as a best effort, and a failure to read or forward it does not change the verdict. A test command that writes no report keeps today's reason, stderr only. `build-failed` still comes first; the hook never runs `check`; docs-only CI and every refusal that comes before the suites run as before; a check that passes changes nothing; an outer timeout (`ci_timeout`) keeps its own outcome.

## 5. Layers and the gate

**Layer values are checked like `merge` (item 47).** `[execution] ci` and `[execution] review` are checked by the same reader, at the same point and in the same snapshots as `[execution] merge` (item 42): when the text of a `.shipcraft.toml` is turned into a config, after it parses as TOML, by every command and in every snapshot that does so today. Each must be a TOML string equal to `"local"`, `"github"` or `"off"` (exact, no trimming or case folding); an absent key takes its default. Any other value, a non-string included, refuses `execution-invalid`, naming the key and the value, checked in the order `ci`, `review`, `merge`. Nothing else moves: malformed TOML, a malformed `[execution]` table and a bad `ci_timeout` keep `config-invalid`; `review = "github"` passes this check and is refused `review-github` where it is today; a deleted config, `close` (which reads no config) and which snapshot each command judges by are unchanged. Before item 47 only `pr`, `merge` and `status` checked these values, so `shipcraft ci` with `ci = "foo"` passed and printed `CI ok.` with no last line (item 44).

### Where each layer runs

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `local` | Runs on this machine, started by `shipcraft pr`. No cloud runner, service or key. |
| `github` | Runs in the project's GitHub Actions, from the documented workflow. Nothing runs locally for that layer. |
| `off` | Does not run. The status is `success` with the description "off in .shipcraft.toml", and the gate does not require it. |

With `ci = "github"` and `review = "local"`, `shipcraft pr` waits until CI is green at HEAD (item 62: `shipcraft/ci`, plus every `suite_checks` name) (up to `ci_timeout`) and reviews only on `success`.

### The suite in the project's own GitHub checks (item 62)

Shipcraft's own repository runs its battery in GitHub Actions as 10 parallel shards behind one required check, `acceptance` (about 4 minutes). With `ci = "github"` before this item, `shipcraft/ci` had to come from a workflow that runs `shipcraft ci`, and `shipcraft ci` runs the whole suite in one process: it requires every locked case in one report, so it cannot be split into shards. Measured on 10-29: 1245 s for the battery in one run on this machine, against about 4 minutes sharded. The only way to keep the shards was `ci = "off"`, which reads as "no CI" while CI does run. Item 62 lets a project say that its suite runs in GitHub checks it already has, and makes `shipcraft/ci` the commit re-check alone.

**What `ci` means.** `[execution] ci` says only where CI runs: `local` (this machine, through `shipcraft pr`), `github` (the project's GitHub Actions) or `off` (CI does not run, and the gate does not require it). It never says what runs; `suite_checks` does.

**The key.** `[execution] suite_checks` is a TOML array of strings, the names of GitHub checks that run the project's suite, its `[build]` and its suite `check`s. Absent means `[]`, today's behavior everywhere.
- It is read and validated by the reader and at the point of item 47, after `ci`, `review` and `merge`, and refuses `execution-invalid` naming the key when:
  - the value is not an array, or an element is not a string;
  - an element is empty, holds a control character (U+0000 to U+001F or U+007F), starts or ends with a character Rust's `char::is_whitespace` accepts, or equals an earlier element byte for byte;
  - an element is `shipcraft/ci` or `shipcraft/review`;
  - the array is not empty and `ci` is not `"github"`;
  - the array is not empty and `shipcraft_min` is not a TOML string of two or three dot-separated runs of ASCII digits that compares at or above `2.18` by today's `shipcraft_min` comparison (a binary older than 2.18 ignores the key, so the project must keep those binaries out). Today's `shipcraft-min` refusal, which comes before item 47's checks, keeps its place: a `shipcraft_min` above the running binary refuses `shipcraft-min` first.
- The value judged is the one in the config the command already judges by: for `pr` and `merge` the PR base's; for `ci` the config of `--base` with today's fallback; for `status` the working tree's.

**Reading a check at a commit.** For a commit `<sha>` and a name, shipcraft makes two `gh` calls and decodes each stdout as one JSON document:
- check runs: `gh api "repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<sha>/check-runs?check_name=<name, percent-encoded>&filter=latest&per_page=100"`. The response must be an object with an integer `total_count` and an array `check_runs` whose length equals `total_count`; each element must be an object with a string `name`, a string `status` and a `conclusion` that is a string or null. The runs whose `name` equals the name byte for byte are its matches.
- commit statuses: `gh api "repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<sha>/status?per_page=100"` (GitHub's combined status, one newest status per context). The response must be an object with an integer `total_count` and an array `statuses` whose length equals `total_count`; each element must be an object with a string `context` and a string `state`. The statuses whose `context` equals the name byte for byte are its matches.

The name is **unreadable** when either call fails or either response breaks its shape above. Otherwise it is **failed** when a match is done and not successful (a run with `status` `completed` and a `conclusion` other than `success`, null included; a status `failure` or `error`); else **pending** when a match is not done (a run whose `status` is not `completed`; a status whose state is not `success`, `failure` or `error`); else **green** when at least one match exists; else **missing**. Every match counts: two producers that report the same name must both be green.

**CI is green at a head** when `shipcraft/ci` is `success` there, read as today, and, when `suite_checks` is not empty, every name in it is green there. `pr` and `merge` use this one rule.

**`shipcraft merge`.** With `suite_checks` not empty, the gate checks `shipcraft/ci` first, as today, then each name in config order, then the review as today. The first name that is not green refuses `merge-without-ci` with the reason `PR #<n> has no green check <name> at its HEAD (<short sha>): <failed|pending|missing|unreadable>`; Why: merging requires the project's suite green on the latest PR commit; Next: wait for the check or fix what failed, then run `shipcraft merge <n>` again. `--no-review` still waives the review only.

**The merge is pinned to the head it checked.** `shipcraft merge` runs `gh pr merge <n> --merge` (or `--squash`) `--match-head-commit <head>`, where `<head>` is the `headRefOid` its gate read. If the PR moved, GitHub refuses and `gh` fails: shipcraft writes no `merge` row for that run and exits as it does today on a `gh` failure, with a message that names `<head>` (a `--no-review` comment already posted stays). This holds whatever `ci`, `review` and `suite_checks` are, and removes item 42's known limit that `shipcraft merge` does not pin the PR head it checked.

**`shipcraft pr` with `ci = "github"`.** When it waits before the review (today: `review = "local"`), each poll reads `shipcraft/ci` and every suite check, and it waits until CI is green at HEAD, up to `ci_timeout`, polling as today. At each poll, a failed `shipcraft/ci` or any failed suite check stops the wait as `ci-github`, naming `shipcraft/ci` or the first failed name in config order. An unreadable suite-check name is read again at the next poll; a failure to read `shipcraft/ci` stops `pr` as it does today. When the limit passes, the wait stops as `ci-github-no-status`, naming every context still not green with its state (`shipcraft/ci` first, with its state as read today; then the suite checks in config order, each `pending`, `missing` or `unreadable`). The limit is checked between polls, so one poll's calls can run past it. Both stops keep today's exit code, statuses and rows.

**`shipcraft ci` re-checks commits and leaves the suite to the checks.** When the config the run judges by has `suite_checks` not empty, `shipcraft ci`:
- checks every commit of the range exactly as today (lock transitions, sealed file hashes, the config under an open lock, hook integration files, the default-branch squash rule), with every refusal that part has today;
- judges docs only as today (`docs_only` keeps its value);
- then prints exactly one line, `shipcraft: suite not run here: it runs in the GitHub check(s) <names joined with ", ", in config order> ([execution] suite_checks)`, in place of `docs only: the suites do not run`, and runs no `[build]`, no suite `check` and no suite command; `build-dirty-tree` is not reached and no copy of HEAD is made;
- does not judge what needs the suite's report at HEAD: `lock-not-closed`, `case-broke`, `lock-closed-red`, `red-already-passes`, `case-missing` and `suite-red` are not reached, and a lock open at HEAD is accepted as an open cycle;
- on the default branch still runs the escape check, unchanged;
- then prints `shipcraft: CI ok.` and item 44's last line for the config's `ci`.

Every `ci` row of the run log has `suite_in_checks`, a JSON boolean: `true` when the run printed the line above, else `false` (refusals and errors before that point included).

**`shipcraft status`.** With `suite_checks` not empty, the layers line reads `ci github (suite in GitHub checks: <names joined with ", ">)` where it reads `ci github` today; otherwise unchanged.

**What `shipcraft/ci` proves, with `suite_checks`.** That every commit of the PR passed the commit checks the hook makes that need no test run: locks opened and closed only as `red` and the hook write them, sealed files unchanged under an open lock, the config unchanged under an open lock, hook integration files unchanged. What the suite proves at HEAD (locked cases pass, nothing else broke) comes from the named checks. Section 1's "CI repeats every check" holds for `ci` without `suite_checks`; with it, CI is `shipcraft/ci` plus the named checks.

**Docs and the config `init` writes.** `init` writes, above `[execution]`, these comment lines in place of today's one:
```
# where each layer runs: "local" = on this machine, started by shipcraft pr; "github" = in this
# project's GitHub Actions (docs/github-ci.md); "off" = it does not run and shipcraft merge does not
# require it. shipcraft/ci re-checks every commit of the PR and runs the suite at its HEAD,
# unless suite_checks names GitHub checks that run the suite (docs/github-ci.md).
```
`init` writes no `suite_checks`, and a second `init` that keeps an existing config does not rewrite its comments. `docs/github-ci.md` gets a section for a project whose suite already runs in its own workflow:
- the named checks must run on `pull_request` and report on the PR head commit (GitHub Actions does), always run (no path filter that can skip them), run the project's `[build]` and `check`s too, and fail when any job they stand for fails or is cancelled: the section gives an aggregate job (`needs:` the suite jobs, `if: always()`, failing unless each needed job's result is `success`; a needed job may be `skipped` only when a scope job that itself succeeded says, in an output the aggregate reads, that there was nothing for it to run, so a job skipped because a prerequisite failed fails the aggregate), the shape of Shipcraft's own `acceptance` gate (`scope` must succeed; `linux` may be skipped only when `scope` reports mode `none`);
- each name must be unique across the project's workflows and integrations;
- one reader decides which job publishes `shipcraft/ci`: a step that reads `.shipcraft.toml` at the same commit the job passes as `--base` (the PR's base SHA on `pull_request`, the `before` SHA on `push`; `git show <that sha>:.shipcraft.toml`) with a TOML reader (Python's `tomllib`, present on GitHub's Ubuntu runners), not `grep`. A base without the file falls back to HEAD's, as today; a file that exists but cannot be read or parsed fails the step. The full-suite job (today's guide, whose `grep` step this reader replaces) runs only when `execution.ci` is `"github"` and `execution.suite_checks` is absent or empty; the re-check job only when `execution.ci` is `"github"` and `execution.suite_checks` is a non-empty array. So exactly one job publishes `shipcraft/ci` for a PR, chosen by the config that judges it;
- the re-check job: the guide's workflow without the language setup, checking out `pull_request.head.sha` with `fetch-depth: 0`, `--base` the PR's base SHA, with `GH_TOKEN` and the guide's permissions kept (the default-branch squash rule and the escape check read GitHub);
- the order: first a PR that adds the re-check job and the reader above (the re-check job runs nothing while the base's `suite_checks` is empty; a project whose base already has `ci = "github"` keeps its full-suite job, language setup included, gated by the same reader), then a PR that sets `ci = "github"`, `suite_checks` and `shipcraft_min = "2.18"` (it is still judged by its base's config); after it merges, PRs are judged the new way and a later PR may remove the old full-suite job; then make the named checks and `shipcraft/ci` required where GitHub protects branches.

**Unchanged.** `ci = "local"` and `ci = "off"`; `ci = "github"` without `suite_checks`; how `shipcraft/ci` and `shipcraft/review` are read; `review`; the escape check, which still judges merged PRs by `shipcraft/ci` and `shipcraft/review` only; `init --github`, which still marks `shipcraft/ci` and `shipcraft/review` as required (not the suite checks).

**Known limits.**
- Which tests ran is the named checks' business: a check that runs a subset (by impact, as Shipcraft's own `acceptance` does) or skips a locked case is trusted, and so is whether it ran `[build]` and `check`.
- At HEAD, a lock left open while its cases pass (`lock-not-closed`, a sign of `--no-verify`) and a locked case missing from the run (`case-missing`) are not caught.
- A provider that reports its check on GitHub's test-merge commit rather than the PR head never makes the name green: it waits or refuses.
- The escape check does not read the suite checks, so a merge through GitHub's button with a red suite check and a green `shipcraft/ci` is not recorded as an escape.
- A name with more than 100 matching check runs, or a commit with more than 100 status contexts, is unreadable.

**Versions.** The binary goes to 2.18.0. No lock field changes; `suite_checks` is a new optional config key.

**Battery.**
- `v1-59.ci`, `v1-59.ci-mixed`, `v1-59.ci-present`, `v1-59.ci-unreadable`, `v1-59.ci-bad-config`, `v1-59.ci-longest`, `v1-59.ci-remedy` and `v1-59.files-many` assert the exact key set of the `ci` row: the RED adds `suite_in_checks` to it, and nothing else.
- `v1-42.squash` asserts `--squash` and no title or body, not the whole argument list: it must hold.
- The `gh` stub (`acceptance/stubs/gh`) enforces `--match-head-commit` against the PR's live head as GitHub does, and serves check runs (with `check_name` and `filter=latest`) and the combined status from state a case sets; `acceptance/README.md` lists both.

### `shipcraft pr`

1. Pushes and opens or updates the PR. The PR body is the intent the reviewer receives.
2. Local CI runs `shipcraft ci --base <merge-base> --pr` and publishes `shipcraft/ci` on HEAD.
3. Only if CI passed, the review runs and publishes `shipcraft/review` and a comment.
4. Posts a summary: runs, refusals and time per layer, review tokens, product lines changed (tests, support files, pure renames, lockfiles, binaries and, since item 108, files marked `linguist-generated` excluded), a split hint above `warn_lines`, and a warning for each product file that grows past the project's p90 file size. Warnings never change exit codes or statuses.

**Shipcraft's own files are not product (item 50).** Product lines, the split hint and the shape warning leave out two kinds of path: `.shipcraft.toml` at the repository root, and every path that starts with `.shipcraft/` from the repository root. Paths are the repository-relative paths git prints, matched literally byte for byte; the working tree, the current directory and suite roots play no part, so `packages/web/.shipcraft.toml` is still product. Classification uses the HEAD path, as in item 24, so a file renamed into `.shipcraft/` leaves the count: the `.shipcraft/` folder is reserved for Shipcraft. The shape warning drops these paths from its candidates and from its p90 population before reading any blob and before the 20-file minimum, so an excluded blob that cannot be read never makes the shape unmeasured; an explicit `[pr] shape_lines` still applies. The run log's `product_lines` and `shape_warnings` carry the new values; old rows are not rewritten. Hook integration files (item 40 protects them for docs-only PRs) stay product here. Like every warning, this changes no exit code, status or verdict.

Wrong-measurement note: since 2.0.0 (items 11 and 24) each lock counted as product, and a lock grows with the number of cases, not with the change. Found in draken-platform, where every PR from #18 to #23 got the split hint because of its locks (PR #23: 655 of its 672 "product lines" were the lock) and PR #24 got a shape warning naming its own lock. The spec review of item 11 should have caught it: its exclusion list named package managers' lockfiles and never Shipcraft's.

**`ci` publishes no status (item 44).** `shipcraft ci` never publishes a status, with or without `--pr`, because it cannot prove that its base is the PR's base or that it ran on the pushed HEAD. An executed CI result reaches `shipcraft/ci` only through `shipcraft pr` (local CI) or the documented GitHub workflow; `pr` still publishes the off-layer status of section 4. In draken-platform#18 an agent ran `shipcraft ci --base origin/main --pr` and `shipcraft review --pr 18` by hand, read "CI ok", and `shipcraft merge` refused for lack of `shipcraft/ci`.

So whenever `ci` exits `0` (suites green, docs only, an empty range, or an accepted open RED cycle), the line `shipcraft: CI ok.` is followed on stdout by exactly one more line, the last, with no blank line, chosen by the `ci` value of the config this run judges by:

| `ci` | Last line |
|---|---|
| `local` | `shipcraft: ci publishes no status; shipcraft pr runs CI and publishes shipcraft/ci on HEAD.` |
| `github` | `shipcraft: ci publishes no status; the GitHub workflow publishes shipcraft/ci on HEAD.` |
| `off` | `shipcraft: ci publishes no status; with ci = "off", shipcraft pr publishes shipcraft/ci as off.` |

Nothing else changes: refusals and errors print no such line, and exit codes, stderr, the run log and every status stay as they are. Inside `shipcraft pr` the line is the last of the CI child's output; `pr`'s own lines follow as before.

**Docs-only PRs (item 40).** With `--pr`, a PR is docs only when the diff from the merge-base to HEAD is not empty and every entry is a regular file added, modified or deleted (a rename counts as both paths; no link, submodule, mode or type change) whose path is a `*.md` file at the repository root or anything under the top-level `docs/`, and none of those paths is protected (a test or support file of any suite, `.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/`, hook integration files), and no lock is open at HEAD, and every suite's `root` is a chain of folders of HEAD's tree (no link, file, submodule, missing path, `..` or absolute root; without that, shipcraft cannot tell which `.md` a suite reads as a test). Then CI still checks every commit, skips `[build]`, `check` and the suites, and prints exactly one line, `docs only: the suites do not run`. Any doubt runs everything. There is no config: a `.md` outside the root and `docs/` (for example one embedded in the code) always runs the suites. Every `ci` row of the run log has `docs_only` (`true` or `false`).

### The size line in the PR body (item 103)

Found in POC-040 (private process repository, 2026-11-10): in Hands-off, with a wrapper that showed the agent its product lines against 400 before `shipcraft pr`, PRs above 400 product lines went from 17 of 20 to 2 of 20, with review rounds per PR not rising (2.40 to 2.37). Until now the product showed the size only in the summary comment posted after the review, when the PR is already open and reviewed. Now the PR body carries it, so the agent reads it when it opens the PR and the reviewer reads it in the brief. It is a line, never a refusal, and the review rules do not change: a missing reason for not splitting is never a Blocking item by this item.

1. **The block.** The size block is these lines, each ending in `\n`:
   - `<!-- shipcraft:size -->`
   - `Size: <N> product lines against a limit of <L> (+<A> / -<R>, counted as in the PR summary).`
   - only when `<N>` is above `<L>`: `Above the limit: if this change can ship in working slices, the first slice can be its own PR; if it cannot, a line here saying why helps the reviewer.`
   - `<!-- /shipcraft:size -->`

   `<A>` and `<R>` are the added and removed product lines of section 5's summary (item 11's rule 2, item 24 and item 50, unchanged: the same function, the same classification), from the PR's merge base with `origin/<default branch>` to HEAD; `<N>` is `<A> + <R>`; `<L>` is `[pr] warn_lines` of the config `pr` reads today (`Config::of_base`). "Above" is `<N> > <L>`, the same test as the summary's split hint. The summary comment, its `Size:` line and its hint do not change.
2. **The body.** `pr` computes the product lines after it has looked up the open PR for the branch and before it creates or edits anything (only that count moves; the shape warning stays where it is). A failure of that count is an error: nothing is created or edited, and, since the PR's locks are not looked up yet, no `pr` row is written (change: today that failure writes an error row).
   - **Lines.** The body is read as text split at `\n` only; a line may end in `\r`, which is ignored when a line is compared with a marker. U+2028, U+2029 and a lone `\r` are ordinary characters.
   - **Ownership.** Shipcraft owns only a trailing block: when the body ends with a line that is exactly `<!-- /shipcraft:size -->` (followed by nothing but spaces, tabs, `\r` and `\n`) and some earlier line is exactly `<!-- shipcraft:size -->`, the trailing block runs from the last such start line to the end of the body. Markers anywhere else (inside the text, in a code fence, an unmatched start) are the author's text and are never removed or paired.
   - **The rest.** The rest of a body is the body itself when it has no trailing block. When it has one, the rest is the text before the block's start line, with one `\n` removed from its end when that text ends in `\n\n` or is exactly `\n` (the blank line Shipcraft put there). Nothing else is trimmed or changed.
   - **The base text.** It is the `--body` file's text when given; otherwise the open PR's current body as `gh` returns it (`body` absent or `null` is empty); otherwise, for a new PR, the empty string. The new body is the block alone when the base text's rest is empty; otherwise the rest, then `\n` when the rest does not end in `\n`, then `\n` (a blank line), then the block. So taking the rest of the new body gives back the base text's rest, byte for byte, whenever that rest is empty or ends in `\n`.
   - **Unreadable body.** Without `--body`, when the open PR's `body` is present and is not a JSON string, `pr` writes no block, prints `shipcraft: the size line was not written: the PR body could not be read` and goes on as today (the review gets the body as today). With `--body`, the given text is the base text as above.
   - **Writing.** A new PR is created with the new body (one `gh pr create`); its failure is an error, as today. For an open PR, when `--body` is given, today's single `gh pr edit` carries the new body (and the title when given); its failure stays the error it is today. Otherwise, when `--title` is given, today's title-only edit runs as today; then, when the new body differs byte for byte from the current one, one more `gh pr edit` writes only the body; when it is equal, nothing is written. A failure of that body-only edit is a warning: `pr` prints `shipcraft: the size line could not be written to the PR body: <first line of the error>` and goes on; no exit code, status, verdict or row changes because of it.
   - **The body the review gets** (its brief) is the body `pr` submitted when the create or edit succeeded or the body was already equal, and the body it read otherwise. **Item 54's text key** is computed over the rest of that body (point 2's rest); a body with no trailing block keys exactly as today. `review --pr <n>` keys the body it reads the same way. So a verdict for a body ending in `\n` (or empty) is reused after `pr` adds or updates its block.
3. **stdout and the run log.** Right after `shipcraft: PR #<n>`, `pr` prints the block's `Size:` line and, when above, its `Above the limit:` line, each prefixed with `shipcraft: `. The `pr` row gains `product_lines` (`<N>`) and `warn_lines` (`<L>`), both numbers, on every `pr` row written today once the PR is known (passed, refused or error); a failure that writes no `pr` row today still writes none. Every other layer's row is unchanged.

**Known limits.** Shipcraft writes raw text; a body that ends inside an unclosed code fence or HTML comment shows the block inside it on GitHub, though the reviewer reads the raw text. A person's edit inside the trailing block is overwritten on the next `pr`. Between `pr` reading the body and writing it, another edit on GitHub is lost (last write wins). A non-empty body that does not end in `\n` keys differently once `pr` has added the block (its rest gains the `\n`), so that first run's review is not reused. A body near GitHub's length limit can exceed it with the block; creating or editing it with `--body` then fails as an error, as any create or edit failure does today.

**Metric:** in the next 20 merged PRs of an adopter that runs this binary without POC-040's wrapper, the share above its limit stays at or below 25% (Hands-off's window: 10%; baseline 85%) and review rounds per PR do not rise above that adopter's last 20. Revert: drop the block in a release; the summary comment is unchanged, so nothing else depends on it. `init` writing the limit and `report` counting it are item 107.

**Touches.** Read first, and keep:
- Item 11: every `v1-11.*` case, `sz_size` included: the summary's `Size: +A / -R product lines` line and the hint above `warn_lines` (default 500) are unchanged.
- Item 50: `acceptance/lib/item50.sh`: what counts as product, the shape warning and where it runs.
- Item 24: renames and HEAD-path classification of product lines.
- Item 54: `v1-54.*`: reuse keys; the text key of a body with no trailing block is unchanged.
- Item 96: `v1-96.reuse`, `v1-96.reuse-publish`: a verdict from `review --pr` is reused by the `pr` run that then adds the block (point 2's key), since their `body.md` ends in `\n`; both stay unchanged.
- Item 35: `v1-35.3-registro`, `v1-35.3-legado`, `v1-35.1-rebase-igual`: reuse across a rebase with the same diff.
- Item 38 and item 41: the `pr` row's fields, `locks`, `queue_secs`; `product_lines` and `warn_lines` are added beside them.
- Item 10: `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: rows stay valid JSON lines.
- Item 63, item 96, item 102: the briefs; the body inside them now ends in the block, and nothing else in them changes.
- Item 44: the line `ci` prints last inside `pr` stays the last line of the CI child's output.
- `english.sh`'s `e1-legacy-hook-pr-warn-lines` and `e1-legacy-ci-pr-warn-lines`: the old name `aviso_linhas` is still refused; only `warn_lines` is read.
- The `pr` cases that read the PR body or title through the fake `gh` (every `pr create`, `pr list` and `pr edit` call): a new PR's body now ends in the block, and a run without `--body` may now call `gh pr edit --body-file`.
- The version cases (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest): the contract goes to 2.53.0.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `pr` fetches, reads the config, refuses `pr-not-on-branch`, reads `--body`, pushes with force-with-lease, then lists the open PR for the branch. Keep. Change: the product-line count runs after that lookup and before any create or edit, and its failure writes no `pr` row.
- A new PR is created with the `--body` text or an empty body, and the title given or the last commit's subject. Change: the body gains the block.
- An open PR is edited only when `--title` or `--body` is given, and that edit's failure is an error. Keep. Change: a body-only edit can follow (point 2), and its failure is a warning.
- `gh::open_pr` parses `gh pr list` as today (a failed command or invalid JSON is an error; any other shape is read as it is read today). Keep; only point 2's non-string `body` is new.
- Product lines reach the `ci` and `review` rows through the environment; the shape warning is computed after the PR is known. Keep both.
- The review reads the body `pr` holds; the summary comment is posted at the end with the `Size:` line and the hint. Keep both.
- Nothing `pr` writes to GitHub is rolled back on a later failure. Keep.

### `init` writes the size limit, and `report` counts PRs against it (item 107)

The second half of POC-040's product item (private process repository, verdict keep, 2026-11-10); item 103 shipped the line in the PR body. Two things were left. The limit that line uses, `[pr] warn_lines`, is a default no project sees in its config, so nobody knows it can be set. And the share of PRs above the limit, the number POC-040's verdict and item 103's metric read, was counted by a private script. Now `init` writes the limit and `report` prints the count. Nothing is refused, and no exit code, status or verdict changes.

1. **`init` writes the limit.** The config `init` writes gains these four lines, right after the `[review]` table's `timeout = 1200` line and before `[build]` and any `[[suite]]`:

   - an empty line;
   - `[pr]`
   - `# product lines above which the PR body and the PR summary suggest a split; a line, never a refusal`
   - `warn_lines = 500`

   Each ends in `\n`. They are written in every config `init` writes (a language, `--lang other` with `--command`, a workspace with `[[suite]]` tables, with or without `[build]`), always with `500`, the default: a new project's behavior is the same as without the lines. Nothing else in the file changes, and `init` prints nothing new. A config that already exists is kept byte for byte, as today (item 90): `init` never adds the table to it.
2. **`report` counts the PRs.** The count reads the `pr` and `merge` rows of the run log `report` already reads, through the same reader, and adds no git read and no network call; the rest of `report` runs as today.
   - **A measured row** is a row whose `layer` is `"pr"`, whose `pr` is a JSON integer above 0, and whose `product_lines` and `warn_lines` are both JSON integers of 0 or more, whatever its `result`. "JSON integer" is as item 79 reads `pr`: a number written with no fraction and no exponent that fits 64 unsigned bits (`400`; never `400.0`, `4e2`, `"400"` or `-1`). Only a line the reader already reads as a JSON object reaches this test: a line it cannot parse (a number out of range such as `1e400` included) is the `gap` it is today and is not looked at again. A parsed row that is not measured is skipped and adds no gap.
   - **A measured PR** is a `pr` number with at least one measured row. Its size and its limit are the `product_lines` and `warn_lines` of its last measured row in the file's order (the log is append-only; `at` is not read). A row that comes, in the file's order, after the first `merge` row that counts for the same `pr` number (next bullet) is not a measured row: a `pr` run on a branch already merged never changes the PR's size, and a PR with rows only after its merge is not measured. It is **above** when `product_lines > warn_lines`, the same test as the PR body's block (item 103) and the summary's hint (item 11).
   - **A merged PR** is a measured PR whose number is the `pr` of some `merge` row that counts by item 38: `result` is `passed`, `base` and `default_branch` are equal strings, and the row is not an escape. The `merge` row's own `pr` is read as above (a JSON integer above 0). Its locks play no part, so a PR with no lock counts. The first such row in the file's order is the one the bullet above means.
   - **The line.** When at least one PR is measured, `--json` prints one more object, after the last `day` line and before the `dora` line, with exactly these keys: `kind` (`"size"`), `prs` (measured PRs), `above` (those above), `above_share` (`above / prs`), `merged_prs` (merged PRs), `merged_above` (those above), `merged_above_share` (`merged_above / merged_prs`, or `null` when `merged_prs` is 0). Counts are JSON integers; a share is a number as the `day` row's share is printed (a whole value as an integer: `0`, `1`; otherwise the fraction). The text output prints, at the same position, one line: `size: <prs> PR(s) measured, <above> above their limit (<share>); merged <merged_prs>, <merged_above> above (<merged share>)`, each share as item 83's whole percent (`25%`, `0%`, `100%`) and `-` for `null`.
   - **No measured PR:** no `size` object and no `size:` line. A run log without measured rows (none yet, or only rows from before 2.53.0) reports byte for byte as today, in both forms.
   - `--since` and `--github` do not change the line: it always covers the whole run log, and nothing in it is filled from GitHub.

**Known limits.** The count covers what ran on this machine, as the report's first line says: a PR opened or merged elsewhere (another clone, GitHub's button) is not measured or not counted as merged. A PR's size is the one its last `pr` run saw; commits pushed after it without `shipcraft pr` are not read. Each PR is judged by the limit its own last row carries, so a project that changes `warn_lines` mixes limits in one count. PR numbers are not checked against the repository: a run log copied from another repository counts its PRs. A project that wants POC-040's limit sets `warn_lines = 400` itself; `init` writes the default.

**Metric:** on an adopter's machine, `report`'s `size` line equals the count POC-040's script gives for the same PRs (checked once on Hands-off's run log after its next 20 merged PRs), and item 103's metric is then read from this line. Revert: a later release that drops the `size` line and stops writing the four config lines; a config that already has `[pr] warn_lines = 500` behaves the same under that release. Going back to an older binary is not the revert: a project `init` set up with 2.56 has `shipcraft_min = "2.56"`, which an older binary refuses (`shipcraft-min`) whatever the `[pr]` table says, as for every release.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.56.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:** item 91's frozen config text (`acceptance/lib/item91.sh`, the `expected` string its warning cases compare byte for byte: `v1-91.no-runner`, `absent`, `invalid`, `invalid-utf8`, `manifest-directory`, `dangling`, `symlink-none`, `workspace`, `parent-only`, `installed-only`, `agents-fail`, `briefs-fail`, `github-fail`) gains point 1's four lines after `timeout = 1200\n`.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - item 90 (`v1-90.*`) and item 91's `kept`, `kept-link` and `kept-invalid`: an existing config is kept byte for byte;
  - `init`'s build and rollback paths: `v1-16b.1-composite`, `v1-16b.1-references`, `v1-16b.1-build-type`, `v1-16b.1-build-reference`; `v1-56.solution`, `v1-56.workspace`, `v1-56.build-fails`; `v1-72.forge`, `v1-72.root-composite`, `v1-72.rollback-file`, `v1-72.rollback-hooks`; `v1-15a.1-decide-antes-e-desfaz-escrita-no-meio`, `v1-15a.1-falha-preserva-config-simbolica`;
  - `python::init-files`, `node::init-files`, `go::init-files`, item 67's and item 71's `init` cases, item 68's `equal-*` cases: they read keys or compare two `init` runs, not the whole text;
  - item 11 (`v1-11.*`), item 50 and item 103 (`v1-103.*`): the `Size:` lines, the hint, the block, and the `pr` row's `product_lines` and `warn_lines`; `warn_lines` read from a config with and without the key;
  - `english.sh`'s `e1-legacy-hook-pr-warn-lines` and `e1-legacy-ci-pr-warn-lines`;
  - every `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 79, 83, 99 and 105, text and `--json`: their run logs hold no measured row, so their output is unchanged;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field.
- **New cases** write the run log by hand after the fixture's last commit, as item 38's and item 83's do, and run `init` in fresh repositories. Estimate: 10 for point 1, 26 for point 2. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `init` without a config writes `.shipcraft.toml` from one template: the header comment, `shipcraft_min`, the top-level suite keys, `[execution]`, `[roles]`, `[review]`, then `[build]` when detected and the `[[suite]]` tables. Change: the `[pr]` table of point 1 after `[review]`. Its other files (`AGENTS.md`'s block, the briefs, the hooks, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`), its messages, its exit codes, its rollback of what it created when a later step fails, and its branch-protection call: keep. The commands `init` runs before it writes the config (`tsc -b` for composite TypeScript projects, `vitest list --filesOnly --json` for a vitest workspace), their order, the caller's `TMPDIR`, and the outputs they leave in the tree even when a later step fails: keep.
- `init` with a config keeps the file and reads it (item 90). Keep.
- `[pr] warn_lines` is read as a non-negative integer with default 500; the old name `aviso_linhas` is refused; `pr` reads it from the base's config. Keep.
- `pr` writes `product_lines` and `warn_lines` on its `pr` row (item 103); `merge` writes `pr`, `base` and `default_branch` on its row. Keep: this item adds no row and no field.
- `report` reads the run log's bytes; a file it cannot read (missing, a directory, no permission) is an empty log; the bytes are decoded as UTF-8 with each invalid sequence replaced by U+FFFD, never refused, so a row with an invalid byte inside a string still reads. Keep. It parses with `serde_json`, one object per line; a field of the wrong type is read as empty, never a gap; rows with `escape: true` and blank lines are ignored; a BOM and a trailing CR are stripped. Keep, and the `size` line reads through the same reader.
- `report` prints `meta`, the `lock` lines, the `day` lines, `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and the `gap` lines, in that order, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. Change: the `size` line between the `day` lines and `dora`, only when a PR is measured.
- `report`'s git reads (item 76), `--since`'s refusal, `--github`'s calls and the usage refusal: keep.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the new `report` cases run no hook after writing their log; the `init` cases meet `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep` and the hooks as today. Keep.

### Generated files are not product lines (item 108)

Found in the field (Hands-off and draken-platform, POC-040, private process repository, 2026-11-10): a PR that regenerates a client, a schema or a snapshot is counted by its generated lines, so the size line (item 103) tells the agent to split a change nobody wrote by hand, and `report`'s share of PRs above the limit (item 107) counts it as above. Projects already mark those files for GitHub with the `linguist-generated` attribute, which collapses them in the PR's diff. Now the product lines leave them out too. Nothing is refused, and no exit code, status or verdict changes.

1. **Which files are generated.** A file is *generated* when git answers, for its path and the attribute `linguist-generated`, the value `set` or `true` (the attribute written alone, or as `linguist-generated=true`). Every other answer is not generated: `unspecified`, `unset` (`-linguist-generated`), `false`, and any other string (`TRUE`, `yes`, `1`).
   - **The question.** `pr` asks once, after the `git diff --numstat` of item 11 and only when that diff names at least one path: `git check-attr --source=<head> -z --stdin linguist-generated`, in the repository, where `<head>` is the same head commit the numstat used. Its stdin is every path the numstat printed, each followed by one NUL byte, in the numstat's order: the bytes git printed for the path, and for a rename the new path (the HEAD path, as in item 24). The paths are kept as raw bytes for this call and for matching its answer, even when they are not valid UTF-8; the rest of the classification reads them as today (the lossy text of item 11's reader), and the shape warning's reader is not changed. The answer is read as raw bytes. It fits when, split at every NUL byte, it is exactly three fields per path asked followed by one empty remainder (so it ends in a NUL and has no byte left over), and for the path at each position the first field equals that path's bytes, the second is exactly `linguist-generated`, and the third is the value.
