Every path asked gets a record, also in a repository with no `.gitattributes` at all: there the value is `unspecified`, a fitting answer that marks nothing.
   - **What takes part.** The `.gitattributes` files of the head commit's tree, at every level, as git reads them with `--source`; the working tree's and the index's `.gitattributes` play no part, so an uncommitted attribute changes nothing. A path the PR deletes is still asked and is generated when a pattern of the head commit matches it. A PR that changes `.gitattributes` is counted by its head's version.
   - **When the question fails.** The question fails when git cannot be started, when writing its stdin, waiting for it or reading its output fails, when it exits non-zero or is ended by a signal, or when its answer does not fit as above. Then no file is generated, for the whole PR and never for some paths only: the count is the one of today. `<reason>` is `git check-attr gave an unreadable answer` when git exited 0 and the answer does not fit; otherwise the first non-empty line of git's stderr with its trailing CR removed, or `git check-attr failed` when there is none. When `pr` reaches the point where it prints the block's `Size:` line on stdout (item 103), it prints one more line right after the block's lines (after `shipcraft: Size: …`, and after `shipcraft: Above the limit: …` when that one is printed): `shipcraft: generated files were not left out of the size: <reason>`. A `pr` that ends before that point (a failed create, title edit or body-file read, as item 103 lists) prints no such line. The run goes on as today; no exit code, status, verdict, body text or row field changes because of the failure.
2. **What leaves them out.** Item 11's product lines, added and removed, skip every generated file, in addition to what they skip today (test and support files, pure renames, the lockfiles by name, binaries, Shipcraft's own files). One function gives the count, so every reader of it follows: the summary's `Size: +A / -R product lines` line and its hint above `warn_lines` (item 11), the PR body's block and the `shipcraft: Size:` line on stdout (item 103), the `pr` row's `product_lines` and the rows that carry it (item 10), and through that row `report`'s `size` line (item 107). A PR with only generated, test and excluded files is 0 product lines, as a PR with only tests is today. Old rows are not rewritten.
3. **What does not change.** The shape warning (item 24) keeps its candidates and its p90 population: a generated file can still get a shape warning, and still counts in the population. The docs-only rule (item 40), the lock, the hook and CI do not read the attribute. The review still gets the whole diff, generated files included, with the same brief template and the same reuse keys (items 54 and 96); only the size block inside the PR body it reads follows the new count. `[pr] warn_lines` and its default are unchanged, and no config key is added: the attribute is the switch, and a project without it counts as today, after the same one call.

**Known limits.** Git also reads `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes` and the user's and the system's attributes files, so an attribute set there counts on that machine only; the project's `.gitattributes` is what travels with the commit. Git's `--source` needs git 2.40 or later; with an older git the question fails and the first point's line is printed on every `pr`. The battery itself needs git 2.40 or later (CI's and the release machine's are later). Only `linguist-generated` is read: `linguist-vendored`, `linguist-documentation` and `-diff` change nothing. GitHub also treats some files as generated without the attribute (minified files, files with a "generated by" header); those stay product here. A path that is not valid UTF-8 is asked with its bytes, and is classified by today's rules for the rest.

**Metric:** on Hands-off's run log, the share of merged PRs above their limit in `report`'s `size` line (item 107), over its next 20 merged PRs after the upgrade, counts no PR whose hand-written lines are under the limit (checked once by reading the PRs counted as above). Revert: a later release that drops the `check-attr` call; rows written meanwhile keep the smaller `product_lines`, and a project can get today's count at once by removing the attribute.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.57.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Must keep passing unchanged** (their fixtures set no `linguist-generated` attribute, so each count is the one of today):
  - item 11 (`acceptance/lib/v1.sh`): `v1-11.1-linha-de-tamanho`, `v1-11.1-pr-so-de-teste`, `v1-11.1-tamanho-desde-o-merge-base`, `v1-11.2-binario-fora`, `v1-11.2-globs-de-suite`, `v1-11.2-globs-do-toml`, `v1-11.2-lockfile-fora`, `v1-11.2-renomeado-sem-mudanca-fora`, `v1-11.2-testes-e-apoio-fora`, `v1-11.3-*`, `v1-11.4-linhas-produto-no-jsonl`;
  - item 24 (`acceptance/lib/item24.sh`): every `v1-24.*` case, the `atributo-*` ones included: they write `.gitattributes` with `binary`, `diff` and `-diff`, never `linguist-generated`, and the shape warning and the rename classification are unchanged;
  - item 35's `.gitattributes` cases (`acceptance/lib/item35.sh`): the hook does not read the new attribute;
  - item 50 (`v1-50.*`): Shipcraft's own files stay out, by path, before and after;
  - item 103 (`v1-103.*`): the block, the `Size:` lines, the `pr` row's `product_lines` and `warn_lines`, `count-fail` included (a failing numstat is still the error it is today, and no `check-attr` runs after it);
  - item 107 (`v1-107.*`): `report` reads rows only;
  - item 63 and item 91 where they read the summary or the config text;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: the rows and their layer and result combinations; no row gains or loses a field;
  - item 38's `v1-38.pr-locks`, `pr-locks-many`, `pr-publication-passed`, `pr-publication-refused` and item 41's `v1-41.docs-root`, `docs-tree`, `product`, `protected`, `timeout`, `refuses`: the `pr` row, its attribution, timing and failure rows;
  - item 40's `v1-40.pr-local`: a docs-only PR through `pr`;
  - item 54's `v1-54.unchanged`, `reused-row`, `publish-failure` and item 96's `v1-96.reuse`, `reuse-publish`: a review is reused or published as today; their PRs have no generated file, so the body's block and the text key are the same.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item108.sh`, on item 103's fixture style (a disposable repository, `[pr] warn_lines` small, the fake `gh`), and commit `.gitattributes` in the PR's head or in its base as each case needs. A case that needs `git check-attr` to fail puts a `git` wrapper first on `PATH` that fails only for `check-attr` and passes every other call to the real git. Estimate: 14 for point 1, 10 for point 2, 4 for point 3. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `pr` computes the product lines once, from the PR's merge base with `origin/<default branch>` to the head, with `git diff --numstat -z -M <base> <head>`, after it has looked up the open PR and before it creates or edits anything; a failure of that diff is an error and ends `pr` as today. Keep. Change: one `git check-attr` call after it, when the diff names a path, and the files it marks leave the count.
- The count skips a path whose file name is one of the lockfiles, a path the config's suites take for a test or support file, a path git prints with `-` counts (binary), a pure rename, `.shipcraft.toml` at the root and paths under `.shipcraft/`. Keep, and each is skipped whatever the attribute says.
- `pr` prints `shipcraft: PR #<n>`, then the block's `Size:` line and, when above, its `Above the limit:` line; writes the block in the PR body (one `gh pr create`, or the edits of item 103); posts the summary comment at the end with its `Size:` line and the hint. Keep. Change: the numbers follow the new count, and the first point's warning line may come right after the block's lines (after `shipcraft: Size: …` and, when printed, `shipcraft: Above the limit: …`).
- The `pr` row carries `product_lines` (added plus removed) and `warn_lines`; the `ci` and `review` rows the same `pr` run causes carry `product_lines` and `shape_warnings`; `hook` rows carry neither. Keep which rows carry which field; change only the value of `product_lines`, as above.
- The shape warning reads blobs and `git diff` for its candidates and prints one warning per product file past the threshold. Keep, generated files included.
- Before it counts, `pr` fetches, pushes the branch with `--force-with-lease` and prints the published-head line; a later failure never undoes the push or a GitHub write. A failed `gh pr create`, title edit or `--body` file read ends `pr` before the `pr` row is written; a failed body-only edit is item 103's warning and the run goes on. `gh` gets its body and input through temporary files under the caller's `TMPDIR`. Keep all of it: this item adds no GitHub call, no file and no rollback.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists. A row is appended to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl` per measured run, best effort (a failed append changes no exit code); the hook appends its row on every fixture commit. `red` writes a lock under `.shipcraft/locks/` and a GREEN commit that closes it is followed by the automatic closing commit, so HEAD moves once more. `init` writes `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`. Local CI through `pr` runs the suite in a temporary copy under the caller's `TMPDIR`, and a `[build]` step can leave its output in the tree. Keep each.

### `pr` warns about new npm dependencies (item 115)

Slopsquatting: a coding agent writes a dependency whose name does not exist (it was invented), or a look-alike of a real one that someone registered recently; whoever owns that name runs code on every install. Nothing in Shipcraft looked at the dependencies a PR adds. Now, when the PR's diff adds a dependency to a `package.json`, `pr` asks the npm registry about it and writes a line in the PR body when the package does not exist there, or was first published less than 30 days ago. The agent reads it when it opens the PR, and the reviewer reads it in the brief. It is a warning only: it never blocks, never changes a status, a verdict or a refusal, and a registry that cannot be read costs one line. Its own failures never change the exit code; the only way it can is item 103's known limit, a body that the extra lines push past GitHub's length limit (see Known limits).

**What is left out, and why.** Only `package.json` and the npm registry: the three adopters (draken, draken-platform, Hands-off) declare their dependencies only in `package.json` files (pnpm workspaces), and so do the battery's Node fixtures. `Cargo.toml` (crates.io), `go.mod` (module proxy, where names are repository paths) and `requirements*.txt` / `pyproject.toml` (PyPI, several formats) are left out; each can be a later item with its own registry. Lockfiles are not read, so a dependency of a dependency is not checked. A near-miss test (a name one edit away from a well-known package) is left out: it needs a list of well-known names to ship and maintain, and a distance rule with false alarms on real packages; the age rule already catches the usual attack, since a squatted look-alike is new.

1. **The new dependencies.**
   - **The switch.** `[pr] new_dependencies` is `"warn"` (the default, also when absent) or `"off"`; `[pr] new_dependency_days` is an integer from 1 to 36500 (default `30`). Both are read from the config `pr` reads today (`Config::of_base`, the same one `warn_lines` comes from). Any other value or type is refused by `Config::parse` as `config-invalid`, through the same path and three lines as an invalid `[pr] shape_lines` today (item 31: the message names the line). With `"off"`, no manifest is read, no call is made, and no line of this item is printed or written. `init` writes neither key (item 107's `warn_lines` line is unchanged).
   - **The manifests.** The paths that `git diff --name-only -z --no-renames --diff-filter=AM <base> <head>` prints (NUL-separated raw bytes), where `<base>` and `<head>` are the merge base and head that item 103's count uses, whose last path component is exactly `package.json` (byte for byte, so `Package.json` is not one) and with no path component exactly `node_modules`. A path that is not valid UTF-8, or that contains a control character (U+0000 to U+001F, U+007F, U+2028 or U+2029), is skipped whole, so every path written in a line is one line of UTF-8. Paths are taken in byte order.
   - **Reading one.** For each of `<head>` and `<base>`, `pr` runs `git ls-tree -z <commit> -- <path>` with the path's raw bytes. An empty answer means the path is absent at that commit. One entry of type `blob` (mode `100644` or `100755`) is then read with `git cat-file blob <oid>`. Any other entry (a link, a folder, a submodule) means the manifest is skipped whole. Each file is read as UTF-8 JSON by `serde_json` after removing one leading UTF-8 BOM, if any. A manifest whose head or base file is not valid UTF-8, not valid JSON, or not a JSON object is skipped whole: none of its names is new, and no line says so.
   - **Its names.** The keys of the four top-level objects `dependencies`, `devDependencies`, `optionalDependencies` and `peerDependencies`. A section that is absent or not an object gives no name. A key that contains a control character (as above) is never a name.
   - **Registry names.** On either side, a key counts only when its value is a string that names a registry version: it does not start with `workspace:`, `file:`, `link:`, `portal:`, `npm:`, `git:`, `git+`, `github:`, `gitlab:`, `bitbucket:`, `http:` or `https:`, and contains no `/` (GitHub's `user/repo` shorthand and local paths such as `./pkg`). So `"^1.2.0"`, `"1.2.3"`, `"latest"`, `"*"` and `""` count; `"workspace:*"`, `"file:../core"` and `"npm:other@1"` do not. A key whose value is not a string does not count.
   - **New.** A name is new in a manifest when it counts at the head and does not count at the base in any of the four sections. So a version change (`^1` to `^2`), or a move from `devDependencies` to `dependencies`, is not new; a dependency that moves from `workspace:*` (or any other spec that does not count) to a registry version is new, since npm will now resolve it from the registry; a manifest the PR adds (no base file) makes every name that counts new. A manifest the PR deletes is not read.
   - **The list.** The new names of every manifest, each name once, in byte order; each is attributed to the first manifest path, in byte order, where it is new.
   - **When git fails.** When the diff, an `ls-tree` or a `cat-file` above cannot start, exits non-zero, ends by a signal, or its output cannot be read, nothing is called and point 3's git line is written instead of any other line of this item. It is never an error.
2. **The registry.**
   - **The call.** One per new name, in the list's order, one at a time: `curl` (found on `PATH`) with exactly these arguments, in this order: `--disable` (first, so no `.curlrc` or other curl config file is read), `--silent`, `--show-error`, `--compressed`, `--max-time`, `10`, `--header`, `Accept: application/json`, `--write-out`, `\n%{http_code}` (a newline, then curl's variable), and the URL `https://registry.npmjs.org/<name>`, where a scoped name `@scope/pkg` is written `@scope%2Fpkg` (only the `/` is escaped; every other byte is as the key has it). It runs in the repository root, with stdin from `/dev/null`, stderr discarded, in its own process group, and is killed with its group when it is still running after 15 s.
   - **A name the registry cannot hold.** A name that does not match `^(@[A-Za-z0-9~-][A-Za-z0-9._~-]*/)?[A-Za-z0-9~-][A-Za-z0-9._~-]*$`, or is longer than 214 bytes, is *not found* without a call (npm refuses such names, so no package can have it).
   - **The answer.** curl exits `0` and its stdout, read in full as bytes, ends with a `\n` and exactly three ASCII digits, the status. Then:
     - `404`: the name is **not found**.
     - `200`: the bytes before that last `\n` must be valid UTF-8 and, read by `serde_json`, a JSON object whose `time` is an object whose `created` is a string in the form `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS`, optionally `.` and 1 to 9 digits, then `Z`, a real UTC date. Its age is the whole days, rounded down, from that time to "now", read once from the system clock before the first call of the run and used for every name (seconds as 64-bit integers, so no threshold in range overflows); a time in the future is 0 days. The name is **young** when its age is less than `new_dependency_days`, and is not reported otherwise.
     - Anything else is a **failure**, with its reason: `curl could not start` (not on `PATH`, or not executable); `timed out` (curl's exit `28`, or the 15 s kill); `curl failed (exit <code>)` (any other non-zero exit), `curl failed (signal <n>)` (ended by a signal), `curl failed (output not read)` (reading its stdout or waiting for it failed); `the registry answered <status>` (any other status, such as `429` or `503`); `unreadable answer from the registry` (exit `0` without the status ending, or a `200` whose bytes are not the object above).
   - **No retry.** One attempt per name; the next `pr` run asks again (nothing is cached between runs). The first failure stops the check: no further call is made, and the name that failed and every name after it are *not checked*. Names answered before it keep their result.
3. **The lines.**
   - **In the PR body.** Item 103's block gains this item's lines between its `Size:` / `Above the limit:` lines and its end marker, in this order: one line per not-found or young name, in the list's order; then, only when some name is not checked, the not-checked line. The lines are:
     - `New dependency not in the npm registry: <name> (<path>). Check the name: anyone can publish a package under a name that does not exist yet.`
     - `New dependency first published <D> day(s) ago on npm: <name> (<path>). Check it is the package you meant.`
     - `New dependencies not checked: <k> of <n>; the npm registry could not be read (<reason>).`
     - the git line, only when git failed (point 1), alone: `New dependencies not checked: git could not read the package.json files.`

     `<name>` is the key as it is, `<path>` the attributed manifest's repository path, `<D>` the age in days, `<n>` the number of new names and `<k>` how many are not checked. With no new name, or with `"off"`, the block is today's byte for byte. The block's markers, ownership, rest and writing rules (item 103 point 2) are unchanged, so item 54's text key over the rest is unchanged and a reused review stays reused. The summary comment (item 11) is unchanged: it gains no line of this item.
   - **On stdout.** The same lines, each prefixed with `shipcraft: `, right after the block's `Size:` (and `Above the limit:`) lines and before item 108's line and item 103's warnings. They are printed also when the body could not be read or written (item 103's two warnings), exactly as the `Size:` line is.
   - **In the run log.** Every `pr` row that has `product_lines` (item 103) gains `new_dependencies` (the number of new names), `dependency_warnings` (not-found plus young names) and `dependencies_not_checked` (`<k>`, `0` when every name was answered), all numbers. With `"off"`, or when git failed, all three are `null`. No other row changes.
   - **When it runs.** Right after item 103's product-line count (and item 108's question), before anything is created or edited; so a failing count still writes no row and makes no call. `ci`, `review --pr`, `merge` and `pr --merge`'s merge step make no call of this item.

**Known limits.** Shipcraft does not read `.npmrc`: a package served by another registry (a private scope) is asked on registry.npmjs.org and reported as not found there; such a project can set `new_dependencies = "off"`. An `npm:` alias, a git, URL or file dependency, and a workspace package are not checked. A dependency added only to a lockfile, or brought in by another package, is not checked. A renamed `package.json` is read as a new file (renames are not followed), so all its names are asked, and only a missing or young one gets a line. Each `pr` run asks again, one call per new name, one at a time: a new `package.json` with 40 dependencies costs about 40 calls of about half a second each on a working network (each call is capped at 10 s, and the first failure stops the rest). The age is the registry's first publication of the name, not of the version asked for, and the system clock decides "now". A look-alike older than the threshold, or a real package that is itself malicious, gets no line. As in item 103, a body near GitHub's length limit can exceed it with the extra lines; creating or editing it with `--body` then fails as an error, as any create or edit failure does today.

**Metric:** over the adopters' next 30 days of `pr` rows after the shared binary moves to this release: of the rows with `new_dependencies` above 0, at most 1 in 10 has `dependencies_not_checked` above 0 for a reason other than an offline machine; and of the warnings written (read once by hand from the PRs), at most 1 in 5 is a false alarm (a correct name, such as a private scope). Revert: a release whose default is `"off"`; nothing is written outside the PR body and the run log, so nothing needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.63.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **The harness (shared files, by this spec's leave):** `acceptance/stubs/curl` is a new offline stub, and `stubs_for` in `acceptance/lib/v1.sh` copies it beside the fake `gh` into every v1 case's stubs folder, and `acceptance/run.sh` adds `curl` to the names its shadow `PATH` hides (`gh codex claude gemini` today), so no v1 case can reach a real registry. The stub never touches the network. Its contract: it logs each call as one JSON array of its arguments per line in `state/curl.log` (the `state` folder beside the fake `gh`), takes the name from the URL's last path segment with `%2F` read back as `/`, and answers from `state/registry.json`, a JSON object from name to entry, where an entry has at most one of: `"exit": <int>` (prints nothing, exits with that code), `"stdout": <string>` (prints it as is, exits 0), `"status": <int>` with optional `"body": <string>` (prints the body, then `\n` and the status, exits 0), or `"created": <string>` or `"created_ago": <seconds>` (prints `{"time":{"created":...}}` with that string, or with the stub's wall clock minus those seconds in the UTC form, then `\n200`, exits 0). A name absent from the table, or a missing table, answers `200` with a `created` ten years old, so a case that adds a dependency and does not set the table gets no line. A table that is not such an object makes the stub print `acceptance-stub: bad registry.json` to stderr and exit 99. Cases at the day boundary use `created_ago` one hour either side of it, never the exact second. Every case that runs `pr` today must keep passing unchanged with it.
- **Assertion that changes, and nothing else in it:** `acceptance/lib/item110.sh`'s `pr_keys` set (the exact key set of every `pr` row of its cases) gains `new_dependencies`, `dependency_warnings` and `dependencies_not_checked`. Every `v1-110.*` case reads it; no other assertion of item 110 changes.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - item 103 (`acceptance/lib/item103.sh`, every `v1-103.*` case): the block, its `Size:` and `Above the limit:` lines, the ownership and rest rules, the body edits and their warnings, the `pr` row's `product_lines` and `warn_lines`; no fixture there adds a dependency that the stub reports;
  - item 108 (`v1-108.*`): its line after the block's lines;
  - item 107 (`v1-107.*`): `init` writes `[pr] warn_lines = 500` and nothing of this item; `report` reads rows only and ignores the new fields;
  - item 11, item 24 and item 50 (`v1-11.*`, `v1-24.*`, `v1-50.*`): the summary comment and the product lines;
  - item 54 (`v1-54.*`), item 96 (`v1-96.reuse`, `v1-96.reuse-publish`) and item 35 (`v1-35.3-registro`, `v1-35.3-legado`, `v1-35.1-rebase-igual`): reuse keys;
  - item 38 and item 41 (`v1-38.pr-*`, `v1-41.*`): the `pr` row's fields, which gain the three fields beside them;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: rows stay valid JSON lines;
  - item 110 (`v1-110.*`): `pr --merge`, except the one assertion below;
  - item 63 (`v1-63.*`) and item 102 (`v1-102.*`): the review brief, its measurement environment and the project guide; the body inside the brief now ends in the block with this item's lines when there are any, and nothing else in the brief changes;
  - item 31, item 47 and `english.sh` (`e1-legacy-*`): config refusals; the new keys add no legacy name;
  - item 21 (`acceptance/tools/item21.py`), item 76, item 79 and item 99: their own `PATH` tripwires that forbid `curl`; none runs `pr`;
  - the Node cases (`v1-91.*`, and the language cases that build a Node fixture): a `package.json` already in the base is not new.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item115.sh`, on item 103's fixture style (a disposable repository, the fake `gh`, the curl stub's table), with a `package.json` in the base or the head as each case needs. Estimate: 13 for point 1 (the switch and its refusal, a non-default days value, a nested manifest, `node_modules` left out, an added manifest, a version change, a move between sections, each skipped spec kind, a non-string value, an invalid base or head manifest, a BOM, dedup across two manifests and attribution), 12 for point 2 (exact arguments and URL, scoped escaping, an invalid name without a call, 404, a young name, the day boundary, a future time, each failure reason, no call after the first failure), 10 for point 3 (line wording and order, the not-checked line, the block unchanged with no new name, stdout position, lines printed when the body is unreadable, the three row fields, `null` when off, no line in the summary comment, the text key unchanged), and 2 controls (a stub table entry that the case reads back; an old dependency gives no line). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `pr` fetches, reads the base's config, refuses `pr-not-on-branch`, reads `--body`, pushes with force-with-lease, lists the open PR, then counts the product lines (item 103) and asks `git check-attr` (item 108) before it creates or edits anything; a failure of that count is an error with no `pr` row. Keep. Change: this item's reads and calls come right after, and none of their failures is an error.
- The size block holds the `Size:` line and, above the limit, the `Above the limit:` line between its markers; `pr` prints those lines after `shipcraft: PR #<n>`, then item 108's line, then item 103's warnings. Keep. Change: this item's lines go after the block's lines, in the body and on stdout.
- The `pr` row carries `product_lines`, `warn_lines`, `queue_secs`, `review_after`, `locks`, `pr` and `base`. Keep. Change: three more fields.
- The summary comment and the review brief: the brief gets the body `pr` submitted (item 103 point 2). Keep; the brief now shows this item's lines because they are in the body.
- `Config::parse` reads `[pr] warn_lines` and `shape_lines` and ignores other keys under `[pr]`. Keep. Change: the two new keys are read and checked.
- Network: `pr` reaches the network only through `git` and `gh` (and the review agent). Change: it also runs `curl` against registry.npmjs.org, once per new name.
- Body submissions (item 103): `gh` gets the body through a temporary folder `shipcraft-gh` holding `corpo.md`, removed after the call; a failed create or `--body` edit ends `pr` with an error before the `pr` row; a failed body-only edit is item 103's warning, and the review gets the body read before. Keep, with the body now holding this item's lines.
- After the body: the shape warning, the measurement environment (`product_lines`, shape warnings), the local queue when CI is local, the CI and review (run or reused) with their pending and final statuses and the review comment, then the summary comment, the `ready` line or the refusal, the `pr` row, and with `--merge` the merge after that row. A later failure (a status or the summary comment) ends `pr` as today with the row written; nothing already pushed or written to GitHub is rolled back. Keep, every one, in that order.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists. Keep.

### `report` counts the `suite-red` runs that went green on the same commit (item 109)

POC-037 (private process repository, `poc/037-flake-or-real-red`, 2026-11-08) read the 45 `suite-red` rows of the two Draken repositories by hand: 17 were followed by a green CI on the same commit with no new commit, 104 min of CI spent on reds that were not the code's. That ground truth was counted by a private script, and since item 97 (2.47.0) each `suite-red` row names its failing cases, but nothing reads them. Now `report` prints the count and the cases those reds named. It is a reading of the run log only: nothing is rerun, nothing is refused, and no exit code, status or verdict changes. Calling a red flaky, and rerunning failed cases in `ci`, stay POC-037's open question.

1. **Which rows count.** The count reads the `ci` rows of the run log `report` already reads, through the same reader, and adds no git read and no network call; the rest of `report` runs as today.
   - **A red row** is a row whose `layer` is `"ci"`, whose `result` is `"refused"`, whose `code` is `"suite-red"`, and whose `commit` is a non-empty JSON string. Any other row is not a red row and adds no gap: a `ci` row with another code (`no-report`, `lock-not-closed`, `case-broke`, a build or `check` refusal), an `error` row (`timed-out` included), a red row with a missing, empty or non-string `commit`. Only a line the reader already reads as a JSON object reaches this test; a line it cannot parse is the `gap` it is today and is not looked at again; rows with `escape: true` are ignored, as today.
   - **A green row** is a row whose `layer` is `"ci"`, whose `result` is `"passed"`, whose `commit` is a non-empty JSON string, and that ran the suite: its `docs_only` is not JSON `true` (item 40 skipped the suite) and its `suite_in_checks` is not JSON `true` (item 62 left the suite to GitHub). A missing field, `false` or any other value counts as ran, so rows from before items 40 and 62 are green rows.
   - **Green again.** A red row is *green again* when some green row comes after it in the file's order (the log is append-only; `at` is not read) and has a `commit` equal to the red row's, byte for byte. Its `branch`, `lock`, `locks` and `secs` play no part. Each red row is judged on its own: two red rows on one commit followed by one green row on it are two red rows, both green again; a green row before the red row, or only on another commit, leaves it not green again. A red row that is not green again is only counted, never called real: a later fix may have come as a new commit, or the green ran elsewhere.
   - **Its CI time** is the red row's `secs` when it is a JSON number of 0 or more (an integer or a fraction, as `medicao` writes it); otherwise it adds 0 and is not a gap.
   - **Its cases** are the strings of the red row's `failed` when `failed` is a JSON array; an element that is not a string is skipped, and an id the same array holds twice is taken once for that row. A red row whose `failed` is missing or not an array (rows from before 2.47.0) names no case. `failed_count` is not read.
2. **The line.** When at least one red row exists, `--json` prints one more object, after the `size` object when there is one (item 107), otherwise after the last `day` line, and always before the `dora` line, with exactly these keys:
   - `kind`: `"reds"`;
   - `suite_red`: the number of red rows;
   - `green_again`: the number of those that are green again;
   - `green_again_share`: `green_again / suite_red`, printed as the `day` row's share is (a whole value as an integer: `0`, `1`; otherwise the fraction);
   - `green_again_secs`: the sum of the CI time of the green-again rows, printed as `num` prints the lock rows' durations (a whole value as an integer, otherwise the fraction), `0` when none;
   - `named`: the number of green-again rows whose `failed` is a JSON array, even an empty one;
   - `cases`: an array of at most 5 objects `{"id": <string>, "rows": <integer>}`, one per distinct case id named by at least one green-again row, where `rows` is the number of green-again rows that name it; ordered by `rows` from most to fewest, ties by `id` in byte order; the first 5 of that order; `[]` when no green-again row names a case. Ids of red rows that are not green again are never listed.

   Counts are JSON integers. The text output prints, at the same position, one line:
   `reds: <suite_red> suite-red, <green_again> green again on the same commit (<share>), <time> of CI; cases named in <named>: <cases>`
   with `<share>` as item 83's whole percent (`25%`, `0%`, `100%`), `<time>` as the lock line prints a duration (`0 s`, `45 s`, `2 min 5 s`, `1 h 0 min 3 s`), and `<cases>` as each case written `<id> (<rows>)`, joined by `, `, in the JSON order, or `none` when the array is empty. In the text, `<id>` is the id as a JSON string literal, exactly as `serde_json` writes it (double quotes; `\"`, `\\`, `\n`, `\r`, `\t`, `\b`, `\f`, and `\u00XX` with lowercase hex for the other control characters; every other character as is), so an id with a newline, a comma or a parenthesis stays on the line and distinct: `"tests.test_db::test_insert" (3)`. The `--json` object holds the id itself.
   - **No red row:** no `reds` object and no `reds:` line. A run log without red rows (none yet, only other `ci` rows, or no log at all) reports byte for byte as today, in both forms.
   - `--since` and `--github` do not change the line: it always covers the whole run log, and nothing in it is filled from GitHub.

**Known limits.** The count covers what ran on this machine, as the report's first line says: a CI that ran on GitHub or in another clone writes no row here, so a red fixed only by a GitHub rerun stays not green again. Green again is not proof of a flaky test: a red caused by the machine (a database down, a full disk) that passes once it is fixed is green again too, and that is the class POC-037 counted. A red row only names up to 50 cases (item 97), so a case past the cut is never listed. Case ids are compared byte for byte, so the same test under two suite prefixes is two ids. Commits are not checked against the repository: a run log copied from another repository counts its rows. The rows do not say which suites a run selected: a later `ci` on the same commit against another base whose config runs fewer suites (a monorepo whose older base has no `[[suite]]` for the failing package) passes and makes the red green again, so the line measures a later passing `ci` that ran a suite on the same commit, not the same suites passing.

**Metric:** on draken-platform's run log, `report`'s `reds` line equals the green-on-the-same-commit count POC-037's `out/ground_truth.py` gives for the same rows (checked once, after its next 10 `suite-red` rows), and POC-037's ground truth is then read from this line. Revert: a later release that drops the `reds` line; the run log is unchanged by this item, so nothing written meanwhile needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.58.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report`, which run `report` on their own logs;
  - every `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 79, 83, 99, 105 and 107 (`acceptance/lib/item38.sh`, `item46.sh`, `item75.sh`, `item76.sh`, `item79.sh`, `item83.sh`, `item99.sh`, `item105.sh`, `item107.sh`), text and `--json`: their run logs hold no `suite-red` row, so their output is unchanged; item 107's `size` line keeps its place between the `day` lines and `dora`;
  - item 97 (`v1-97.*`): the `suite-red` row's `failed` and `failed_count` are written as today; this item only reads them;
  - item 43's, item 81's and item 33's `suite-red` cases: the refusal, its printed lines and its row;
  - item 40's and item 62's `ci` cases that write `docs_only` and `suite_in_checks` (`v1-40.raiz-md`, `v1-40.sem-pr`, `v1-62.ci-light` among them): the fields are written as today; this item only reads them;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item109.sh`, write the run log by hand after the fixture's last commit, as item 38's, item 83's and item 107's do, and run `report` and `report --json`. One control case reaches a real `suite-red` row through `shipcraft ci` and a green `ci` on the same commit, then reads `report`. Its suite command is a case-owned adapter, as item 97's is, that fails or passes by an environment variable set by the case, so the tracked content and HEAD stay the same between the two runs (editing a tracked file would be judged on a copy of HEAD, or refused as `build-dirty-tree` with `[build]`; committing would change the commit); the case asserts the two rows carry the same non-empty `commit`. Estimate: 18 for point 1, 16 for point 2. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `report` reads the run log's bytes; a file it cannot read (missing, a directory, no permission) is an empty log; the bytes are decoded as UTF-8 with each invalid sequence replaced by U+FFFD, never refused. It parses with `serde_json`, one object per line; a field of the wrong type is read as empty, never a gap; rows with `escape: true` and blank lines are ignored; a BOM and a trailing CR are stripped. Keep, and the `reds` line reads through the same reader.
- `report` prints `meta` (whose `ci` totals already count every `ci` row and its refusals by code), the `lock` lines, the `day` lines, `size` when a PR is measured, `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and the `gap` lines, in that order, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. Change: the `reds` line after `size` (or after the `day` lines) and before `dora`, only when a red row exists.
- `report`'s git reads (item 76), `--since`'s refusal, `--github`'s calls, `[report]`'s config refusal path and the usage refusal: keep.
- `ci` writes a `suite-red` row with `failed` (at most 50 ids, byte order) and `failed_count` (item 97), and a passing `ci` row with no such field; both carry `commit` (HEAD's sha, or empty when there is none) and `secs`. Keep: this item adds no row and no field.
- How `ci` runs and reads a suite (item 97's list): each run's temporary report `shipcraft-junit-<pid>-<nanos>.xml` and its best-effort deletion after it is read, both runner output streams and the suite and check progress messages on stderr, the verdict taken from the report and not from the runner's exit status; `suite-red` prints its three-line refusal and exits 1; a passing `ci` prints its success lines and exits 0. Keep.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the new `report` cases run no hook after writing their log; a control case that runs `ci` appends its rows and the hook's as today. Keep.

### `report` counts the review rounds each PR needed (item 112)

Trilha C of 2026-11-17 (private process repository): a quality line in `report`, read from what Shipcraft already records. Three readings were asked for: the review's Blocking findings, the PRs that needed a second review round, and the runs of a `suite_checks` check (Shipcraft's own `lint`, item 111) that went red. Only the second is in the run log today: a `review` row says whether the review approved or requested changes, but not how many Blocking findings it named, and a GitHub check writes no row at all. **Split:** this item is the review rounds, from the run log only. The Blocking count (from the `shipcraft/review` statuses, whose description names it, with `--github`) and the red runs of the `suite_checks` checks (GitHub's check runs, with `--github`) are later items, each with its own GitHub reader and failure paths. It is a reading of the run log only: nothing is reviewed again, nothing is refused, and no exit code, status or verdict changes.

1. **Which rows count.** The count reads the `review` rows of the run log `report` already reads, through the same reader, and adds no git read and no network call; the rest of `report` runs as today.
   - **A round row** is a row whose `layer` is `"review"`, whose `pr` is a JSON integer above 0 (read as item 107 reads it: a number written with no fraction and no exponent that fits 64 unsigned bits; never `7.0`, `7e0`, `"7"`, `-1`, `0`, `true` or `null`), whose `result` is `"passed"` or `"refused"`, and whose `reused` is not JSON `true`. A missing `reused`, `false` or any other value counts as a fresh review, so rows from before item 38 are round rows. Any other row is not a round row and adds no gap: a review that gave no verdict (`result` `"error"`, `reviewer-no-verdict` included), a reused verdict (item 38: not a new round), a row with a missing, zero, negative, fractional, string or other non-integer `pr` (the local `shipcraft review` writes `pr: null`), and every row of another layer. Only a line the reader already reads as a JSON object reaches this test; a line it cannot parse is the `gap` it is today and is not looked at again; rows with `escape: true` are ignored, as today.
   - **A reviewed PR** is a `pr` number with at least one round row. **Its rounds** are the number of its round rows, wherever they are in the file; `at`, `commit`, `branch`, `lock`, `locks`, `agent`, `model`, `effort`, `code`, `after_green_ci`, `beside_ci`, `diff_sha256`, `text_sha256` and `secs` play no part, and neither do the `pr` and `merge` rows: a PR's rounds are counted whether or not it was merged. `shipcraft review --pr <n>` writes `pr: <n>` on its row, so a review asked by hand counts as a round of that PR.
   - **A changes-requested round** is a round row whose `result` is `"refused"`.
   - **A second round:** a reviewed PR with 2 or more rounds needed a second round.
2. **The line.** When at least one PR is reviewed, `--json` prints one more object, after the `reds` object when there is one (item 109), otherwise after the `size` object when there is one (item 107), otherwise after the last `day` line, and always before the `dora` line, with exactly these keys:
   - `kind`: `"review"`;
   - `prs`: the number of reviewed PRs;
   - `rounds`: the number of round rows, all PRs together;
   - `changes_requested`: the number of changes-requested rounds, all PRs together;
   - `second_round`: the number of reviewed PRs that needed a second round;
   - `second_round_share`: `second_round / prs`, printed as the `day` row's share is (a whole value as an integer: `0`, `1`; otherwise the fraction);
   - `most`: an array of at most 5 objects `{"pr": <integer>, "rounds": <integer>}`, one per PR that needed a second round, ordered by `rounds` from most to fewest, ties by `pr` from smallest to largest (as numbers); the first 5 of that order; `[]` when no PR needed a second round. A PR with one round is never listed.

   Counts and PR numbers are JSON integers. The text output prints, at the same position, one line:
   `review: <prs> PR(s) reviewed in <rounds> round(s), <changes_requested> requested changes; <second_round> needed a second round (<share>): <most>`
   with `<share>` as item 83's whole percent (`25%`, `0%`, `100%`; as there, a share exactly halfway between two whole percents is not pinned, so no case asserts one) and `<most>` as each entry written `#<pr> (<rounds>)`, joined by `, `, in the JSON order, or `none` when the array is empty: `review: 4 PR(s) reviewed in 7 round(s), 3 requested changes; 2 needed a second round (50%): #12 (3), #9 (2)`.
   - **No reviewed PR:** no `review` object and no `review:` line. A run log without round rows (none yet, only reused or error reviews, only rows without a PR number, or no log at all) reports byte for byte as today, in both forms.
   - `--since` and `--github` do not change the line: it always covers the whole run log, and nothing in it is filled from GitHub.

**Known limits.** The count covers what ran on this machine, as the report's first line says: a review run in another clone, or a person's review on GitHub, writes no row here. A round is a review that gave a verdict, not a review that was needed: a `pr` run after a commit that changed only a test or a doc reviews again unless the verdict is reused, and that round counts. A review attempt that gave no verdict (`error`) adds no round: an error followed by one fresh `passed` or `refused` verdict for the same PR is one round, and two errors are none. A verdict that was recorded and whose comment or status failed to publish afterwards still counts: the row is written before the publication. PR numbers are not checked against the repository: a run log copied from another repository counts its rows. How many Blocking findings a round named, and which `suite_checks` checks went red, are not read (the split above).

**Metric:** on Shipcraft's own run log, `report`'s `review` line counts, for the same PRs, the same second-round PRs as the rows of `metrics/item-times.tsv` whose `review_rounds` is 2 or more (private process repository; checked once, after the next 5 merged items), and the process metric is then read from this line. Revert: a later release that drops the `review` line; the run log is unchanged by this item, so nothing written meanwhile needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.60.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:** item 79's `v1-79.duplicate-prs` writes a fresh `review` row with `pr: 98` and `result: "passed"`, so its `--github` output gains the `review` object between the `day` lines and `dora`; the kind order its check reads (`acceptance/lib/item79.sh`, the list `['meta','lock','day','dora','github','gap']`) gains `review` between `day` and `dora`. Every other assertion of that case stays.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 83, 99, 105, 107 and 109 (`acceptance/lib/item38.sh`, `item46.sh`, `item75.sh`, `item76.sh`, `item83.sh`, `item99.sh`, `item105.sh`, `item107.sh`, `item109.sh`), and item 79's other cases, text and `--json`: their logs hold no round row (their `review` rows carry no `pr`), or their checks tolerate one more object (`v1-107.invalid-size` writes a passed `review` row with a positive `pr`, so its output gains the `review` object, and its assertions read only the `size` line), so their assertions are unchanged; item 107's `size` line and item 109's `reds` line keep their places;
  - `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report`, which run `report` on logs a real `review` or `pr` wrote and read only their own lines;
  - the review rows as written today: item 38's `v1-38.reviews`, `review-errors`, `reuse` (the lock line's `reviews` counts), item 54's and item 96's reuse cases (`reused: true`), item 64's, item 87's and item 102's review rows, item 63's `v1-63.brief-review-pr`, `brief-review-pr-spoof`, `publish-fails-review-pr` and item 82's `v1-82.brief-review-pr`, `two-review-pr`, `publish-review-pr` (fresh rows, their origin fields and publication outcomes): this item only reads them;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item112.sh`, write the run log by hand after the fixture's last commit, as item 38's, item 83's, item 107's and item 109's do, and run `report` and `report --json`. One control case reaches real round rows through `shipcraft review --pr <n>` with a fake reviewer, as item 87's do, and asserts that the row it wrote carries `pr: <n>` and is counted. Estimate: 16 for point 1, 14 for point 2. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `report` reads the run log's bytes; a file it cannot read (missing, a directory, no permission) is an empty log; the bytes are decoded as UTF-8 with each invalid sequence replaced by U+FFFD, never refused. It parses with `serde_json`, one object per line; a field of the wrong type is read as empty, never a gap; rows with `escape: true` and blank lines are ignored; a BOM and a trailing CR are stripped. Keep, and the `review` line reads through the same reader.
- `report` prints `meta`, the `lock` lines (whose `reviews` already count each lock's `passed`, `refused` and `error` review rows that are not reused, and whose `first_review_approved` reads the first), the `day` lines (`first_review_approved_share`), `size` when a PR is measured, `reds` when a red row exists, `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and the `gap` lines, in that order, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. Change: the `review` line after `reds` (or `size`, or the `day` lines) and before `dora`, only when a PR is reviewed.
- `report`'s git reads (item 76), `--since`'s refusal, `--github`'s calls, `[report]`'s config refusal path and the usage refusal: keep.
- `pr` writes one `review` row per review it runs or reuses, with `pr` (the PR's number), `result` (`passed`, `refused` or `error`), `code`, and `reused: true` on a reused verdict; `shipcraft review` writes `pr: null`; `shipcraft review --pr <n>` writes `pr: <n>`. Keep: this item adds no row and no field.
- `shipcraft review --pr <n>` (the control's path): fetches the base and the PR head, takes a review queue slot, runs the reviewer in a detached worktree under the caller's `TMPDIR` with its brief and output files, prints its announcement (and its queue position when it waits), posts the pending `shipcraft/review` status, records the row, then posts the comment and the final status, releases its slot and removes the worktree, as item 87's current behavior lists. A setup failure before the review writes no row; a publication failure after the row exits with an error and leaves the pending status and the row. Keep all of it: the control reads only the row and the `review` line, never the exit code against the count.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the new `report` cases run no hook after writing their log; the control case that runs `review --pr` appends its row as today. Keep.

### `report --github` counts the Blocking findings of each reviewed PR (item 113)

Item 112's split, first half. A `review` row says whether a review approved or requested changes, but not how many Blocking findings it named. The count is on GitHub: every fresh review `pr` or `shipcraft review --pr <n>` runs posts a `shipcraft/review` status on the reviewed commit whose description is `on this machine: APPROVE, Blocking 0 (<agent>)` or `on this machine: CHANGES REQUESTED, Blocking <n> (<agent>)` (since 2.0.0). With `--github`, `report` reads those statuses for the commits item 112's round rows name and adds the Blocking counts, in total and per PR, to item 112's `review` line. This changes item 112's sentence "`--github` does not change the line" for `--github` only. Without `--github`, nothing changes and no network call is made. It is a reading only: nothing is refused, no row is written, and no exit code changes. The red runs of `suite_checks` checks (item 112's split, second half) are not part of this item.

1. **Which commits are read.** Only with `--github`, and only when item 112's `review` object is printed (at least one round row, as item 112 defines it). Item 112's round rows are grouped by their `pr` and their `commit`: rows of the same PR whose `commit` is the same JSON value as `serde_json` compares it (so `1` and `1.0` differ, and `null` is a value), or which both lack `commit` (a missing `commit` is its own value, different from `null`), form one **reviewed commit**. Two rounds of one PR on one commit are one reviewed commit, read once and counted once; one commit under two PR numbers is two reviewed commits, each counted (the call is shared, point 2). A reviewed commit whose `commit` is missing, not a JSON string, or not exactly 40 lowercase hex characters is not read and makes no call: its reason is `no commit in the run log`, always, even when a timeout (point 2) stopped the calls. A reviewed PR's other row fields, the `pr` and `merge` rows, reused and error review rows, and `--since` play no part: the line covers the whole run log, as item 112's does.
2. **The call.** For each reviewed commit that has a commit, in the order of its first round row in the run log: `gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<commit>/statuses?per_page=100` (the list of every status, newest first, not the combined `status` item 79 reads). These calls come after every call item 79 makes for the locks, and run exactly as item 79's (item 79 point 1, "The calls" and "Reading an answer"): in `<root>`, with the same environment, stdin from `/dev/null`, stderr discarded, in their own process group, under the same 60 s limit; a call that cannot start, exits non-zero or ends by a signal is `gh failed`; output that is not valid UTF-8 JSON, or not the shape below, is `unreadable answer from gh`. Each outcome is reused, keyed by its full argument list, so a commit shared by two PRs is asked once, also when the call failed. **No retry:** one attempt per argument list per run; a failed call's reason stands, and running `report --github` again is the retry. **A timeout** (one call reaching 60 s, item 79's or this item's) makes no further `gh` call in the run: every reviewed commit not yet read counts as `gh timed out`, and so does every one that has a commit when the timeout happened in item 79's lock calls (one without a commit keeps `no commit in the run log`). Item 79's lock calls, their reasons and the `github` summary line are unchanged by this item: the status calls and their reasons count only in the `review` object.
3. **Which status counts.** The answer must be a non-empty JSON array of pages, each page a JSON array (possibly empty), each entry an object with a string `context`; else `unreadable answer from gh`. An entry whose `context` is exactly `shipcraft/review` is a review status, and must also have a `description` that is a string or `null` and a `created_at` that is a time (item 79's GitHub UTC form); else `unreadable answer from gh`. Entries of other contexts are not read further.
   - **A count:** a review status's `description` carries a count when it starts with exactly `on this machine: APPROVE, Blocking ` or `on this machine: CHANGES REQUESTED, Blocking `, followed by 1 to 9 ASCII digits, followed by the end of the description or a space. The count is that decimal number (`007` reads as 7). Ten digits or more carry no count, so every sum below fits a JSON integer. Any other description (`null`, the pending `review in progress`, `review error`, `verdict from ... reused`, `off in .shipcraft.toml`, another tool's text, `Blocking -1`, `Blocking 1x`, a lower-case `blocking`) carries no count.
   - **The latest count wins:** the reviewed commit's count is the count of the review status with a count whose `created_at` is latest; on equal times, the one that comes first in the answer (pages in order, entries in order). Statuses without a count are skipped, so a later `review error` or reused-verdict status never hides an earlier count. The verdict word, the status `state` and the row's `result` are not compared with each other or with the count.
   - **No review status with a count** at the commit (none at all, or only statuses without one): the reason is `no blocking count at commit`.
4. **The line.** With `--github`, item 112's `review` object gains these keys after `most`, in this order:
   - `blocking`: the sum of the counts of the reviewed commits that were read, all PRs together; `0` when none was read;
   - `blocking_read`: the number of reviewed commits whose count was read;
   - `blocking_commits`: the number of reviewed commits, read or not;
   - `blocking_missing`: an object from each reason to the number of reviewed commits not read for it, keys in byte order; `{}` when every reviewed commit was read. The reasons are `no commit in the run log`, `gh failed`, `unreadable answer from gh`, `gh timed out` and `no blocking count at commit`;
   - `blocking_most`: an array of at most 5 objects `{"pr": <integer>, "blocking": <integer>}`, one per reviewed PR whose read counts sum above 0, ordered by that sum from most to fewest, ties by `pr` from smallest to largest (as numbers); the first 5 of that order; `[]` when no PR has a Blocking finding read. A PR whose reviewed commits were partly read is listed with the sum of the read ones.

   Counts are JSON integers. Within the object, keys print in the order the serializer already gives every `report` object (byte order, as item 112's keys); no case asserts key order. The text line is item 112's line, unchanged, followed on the same line by
   `; Blocking <blocking> in <blocking_read> of <blocking_commits> reviewed commit(s): <blocking_most>`
   with each entry written `#<pr> (<blocking>)`, joined by `, `, in the JSON order, or `none` when the array is empty; and, only when `blocking_missing` is not empty, by `; not read: ` and each reason written `<reason> <n>`, joined by `, `, in key order:
   `review: 4 PR(s) reviewed in 7 round(s), 3 requested changes; 2 needed a second round (50%): #12 (3), #9 (2); Blocking 5 in 6 of 7 reviewed commit(s): #12 (4), #9 (1); not read: gh failed 1`.
   - **Without `--github`:** the `review` object has exactly item 112's keys and the line is item 112's, byte for byte.
   - **With `--github` and no round row:** no `review` object, no status call; the output is today's.
   - **When nothing can be read** (`gh` missing, every call failed, a timeout, no count on any commit): the keys and the suffix are still printed, with `blocking` `0`, `blocking_read` `0`, `blocking_most` `[]` and the reasons in `blocking_missing`. Every other line of the report prints as it does today, and the exit code stays 0. Nothing is written to stderr for these reasons.

**Known limits.** The count is what the `shipcraft/review` statuses on GitHub say now, for the commits this machine's run log names: a review run in another clone is not counted (item 112's limit), a status deleted with its repository or a commit GitHub no longer serves (a force-push followed by garbage collection answers an error) is `gh failed`, and a status any token with write access posts with the same description is counted as Shipcraft's. A run log copied from another repository asks this repository's GitHub for its commits. GitHub keeps at most 1000 statuses per context and commit; older ones are not read. One call is made per distinct reviewed commit, so a run log with a few hundred reviewed commits takes a few hundred calls (each under the 60 s limit; at about half a second each, a few minutes); `--since` does not narrow it. The count is per reviewed commit, not per round: two fresh reviews of one PR on one commit (a re-review after only the PR body changed, or `review --pr` by hand on the head `pr` reviewed) count once, with the later verdict's count, though item 112 counts two rounds; in Shipcraft's own flow a second round follows a fix commit, so it is a new reviewed commit. A status is posted on a commit, not on a PR: two PRs whose rounds reviewed the same head commit both read that commit's latest count, so each is counted with it, and the total counts it twice. A later item may have `pr` record the count on its `review` row and read it from the run log instead; that is a product decision for Victor and not this item.

**Metric:** on Shipcraft's own run log, for each of the next 5 merged items, `report --github`'s `blocking_most` and `blocking` agree with the Blocking counts the review comments of those PRs named, taking for each reviewed commit the latest verdict on it, (the metrics rows of the private process repository note them); the process metric then reads Blocking from this line. Revert: a release that drops the five keys and the suffix; nothing is written by this item, so nothing needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.61.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
  - item 112's `v1-112.github`: its round rows carry `commit: "foreign112"` (not a commit), so no status call is made and its call-list assertion stays; its `--github` `review` objects (with and without the `gh` fault) now equal the plain one plus `blocking: 0`, `blocking_read: 0`, `blocking_commits: 2` (PR 97 and PR 8 on `foreign112`), `blocking_missing: {"no commit in the run log": 2}`, `blocking_most: []`, and its `--github` text lines equal the plain lines with the `review:` line followed by `; Blocking 0 in 0 of 2 reviewed commit(s): none; not read: no commit in the run log 2`. Every other assertion of that case stays.
  - item 79's `v1-79.duplicate-prs`: its fresh `review` row (`pr: 98`, `commit` 40 `a`s) is a reviewed commit, so its expected call list gains one last call, `['api','--paginate','--slurp','repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa/statuses?per_page=100']`, after item 79's calls; the fake `gh` of `acceptance/lib/item79.sh` does not answer it, so it is `gh failed` there, and its `review` object gains the five keys. Its `comparable()` helper, which compares the `--github` report with the plain one leaving out the `github` and `lock` objects, also leaves out those five keys of the `review` object (only those: item 112's keys are still compared). Its lock assertions and kind order stay.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every other case of item 112 (`acceptance/lib/item112.sh`), including `v1-112.control-github` (no round row, so no status call) and the cases without `--github`;
  - every `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 79 (other than `duplicate-prs`), 83, 99, 105, 107 and 109: their `--github` cases hold no round row (`v1-107.github`, `v1-109.github`, `v1-83.github-*`), so they make no status call, or they run without `--github`;
  - `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report`, which run `report` without `--github`;
  - the `shipcraft/review` statuses as `pr` and `review --pr` post them today: item 63's and item 82's `review --pr` cases, item 38's, item 87's and item 96's review cases, item 54's `v1-54.unchanged` (the reused-verdict description holds `same file versions`), and every case that reads a status description: this item only reads them;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item113.sh`. They write the run log by hand after the fixture's last commit, as item 112's do, put a fake `gh` first on `PATH` that answers each argument list from a table and logs its calls, as item 79's and item 83's do (with item 79's `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED`, process-group and environment checks not repeated), and run `report --github` and `report --json --github`. One control case reaches a real status description: it runs `shipcraft review --pr <n>` with a fake reviewer, as item 112's `v1-112.control-live` does, captures the `shipcraft/review` description the fake `gh` received, serves it back from the status list, and asserts that `report --github` reads its count. Estimate: 6 for point 1 (grouping, invalid commits, shared commit), 6 for point 2 (call form, order after lock calls, cache, failure, timeout, no retry), 12 for point 3 (shape, pages, the count grammar's edges, latest by time, ties, skipped statuses, no count), 10 for point 4 (keys, ranking, top 5, partial, text with and without `not read`, no `--github`, no round row), 2 controls. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `report` reads the run log as item 112 lists (reader, gaps, `escape`, BOM, CR), prints `meta`, `lock`, `day`, `size`, `reds`, `review`, `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and `gap` in that order, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. Change: with `--github`, the `review` object gains the five keys and its text line the suffix.
- Without `--github`, `report` makes no network call. Keep.
- `report --github` (item 79) asks `gh pr view` and the combined `status` and `check-runs` for the selected locks, with its 60 s limit, process group, environment, cache by argument list and the timeout that stops every later call, and prints the `github` summary line with `selected`, `merge_filled`, `waiting_filled` and `missing` for the locks. Keep: the status calls of this item come after them, share the cache and the timeout, and never enter the `github` line.
- `pr` and `shipcraft review --pr <n>` post `shipcraft/review` statuses (pending `on this machine: review in progress (<agent>)`, then the verdict's description, or `verdict from <sha> reused (same file versions)`, or `off in .shipcraft.toml`), truncated by `gh::status` to 140 characters, and write their `review` rows with `commit` the reviewed head. Keep: this item only reads them.
- `report`'s git reads (item 76), `--since`'s refusal, `[report]`'s config refusal path and the usage refusal: keep.
- `shipcraft review --pr <n>` (the control's path), as item 112's current behavior lists it: it fetches the base and the PR head (updating the remote-tracking refs and `FETCH_HEAD`), takes a review queue slot, runs the reviewer in a detached worktree under the caller's `TMPDIR`, posts the pending status, records its row, posts the comment through `gh::comment` (a `shipcraft-gh-*` folder with `corpo.md` under the caller's `TMPDIR`, removed afterwards) and the final status, prints the `shipcraft: shipcraft/review <state>: <description>` line, and exits 0 on an approval. Keep all of it; the control reads only the description the fake `gh` received and the `report --github` output.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the new `report` cases run no hook after writing their log; the control case that runs `review --pr` appends its row and posts its statuses to the fake `gh` as today. Keep.

### `report --github` counts the red runs of the suite checks (item 114)

Item 112's split, second half. Item 62's `[execution] suite_checks` names the GitHub checks that run a project's suite (Shipcraft's own `acceptance` and, since item 111, `lint`). Their runs happen on GitHub and write no run-log row, so nothing in `report` says how often they went red, nor whether a red was the code's. With `--github`, `report` reads the check runs of those names on the commits of each reviewed PR (item 112) and prints, per name, how many runs went red, how many of those went green again on the same commit, and how many were fixed by a new commit. Without `--github`, nothing changes and no network call is made. It is a reading only: nothing is rerun, nothing is refused, no row is written, and no exit code changes. Calling a red flaky stays POC-037's open question, as in item 109.

**Why not item 109's reading.** Item 109 reads the local `ci` rows of the run log. A project with `suite_checks` runs its suite on GitHub: its `ci` rows say `suite_in_checks: true`, which item 109 already leaves out, and a check such as `lint` has no row at all. So the runs are read from GitHub. What is reused is item 109's meaning of *green again*: a later passing run on the same commit, each red run judged on its own, nothing called flaky. "Later" is by completion time here, since GitHub's runs have no file order. The *fixed by a new commit* count is new: GitHub gives the PR's commits, the run log does not.

1. **Which PRs and which names.** Only with `--github`, only when item 112's `review` object is printed (at least one round row), and only when `<root>/.shipcraft.toml`, read once per run as item 79 point 2 step 4 reads it (directly from that path, parsed by `Config::parse`, unchanged), reads and has a non-empty `[execution] suite_checks`. Otherwise there is no `checks` object, no `checks:` line and no call of this item; a config that cannot be read prints nothing of this item and writes nothing to stderr.
   - **The PRs** are item 112's reviewed PRs (a `pr` number with at least one round row, as item 112 point 1 defines it), in the order of their first round row in the run log. The rows' `commit`, the `pr` and `merge` rows, reused and error review rows, and `--since` play no part: like items 112 and 113, the line covers the whole run log, which is the report's period (its first and last row).
   - **The names** are the `suite_checks` entries, in config order. They come from today's config, not from the config each PR was judged by.
2. **The calls.** For each PR, in order:
   1. `gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/<pr>/commits?per_page=100`, the PR's commits;
   2. then, for each distinct commit of that answer in answer order, `gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<sha>/check-runs?filter=all&per_page=100`. `filter=all` lists the runs GitHub keeps for every check at the commit, reruns included (within GitHub's retention, see Known limits); item 79's `filter=latest` lists only the newest. There is no `check_name` parameter: one call serves every name.

   These calls come after every call of item 79 (the locks) and of item 113 (the review statuses), and run exactly as theirs (item 79 point 1, "The calls" and "Reading an answer"): in `<root>`, with the same environment, stdin from `/dev/null`, stderr discarded, in their own process group, under the same 60 s limit. A call that cannot start, exits non-zero or ends by a signal is `gh failed`; output that is not valid UTF-8 JSON, or not the shape below, is `unreadable answer from gh`. Each outcome (the raw answer, or the reason) is reused, keyed by its full argument list, so a commit that two PRs share is asked once, also when the call failed; the cached answer is validated and counted again within each PR, and a PR never contributes partly. **No retry:** one attempt per argument list per run; running `report --github` again is the retry. **A PR is read in full or not at all.** Its first call that fails gives its one reason, and no later call is made for it (the next PR's calls go on). **A timeout** (one call reaching 60 s, of item 79, item 113 or this item) makes no further `gh` call in the run: every PR not yet finished counts as `gh timed out`, also one whose calls are all already in hand and also when the timeout came before this item's first call; a PR that already got its reason (`gh failed`, `unreadable answer from gh`) before the timeout keeps it. Item 79's `github` line and item 113's keys are unchanged by this item: its calls and their reasons count only in the `checks` object.
3. **Reading the answers.**
   - **Commits:** the answer must be a non-empty JSON array of pages, each page a JSON array (possibly empty), each entry an object whose `sha` is a string of exactly 40 lowercase hex characters; else `unreadable answer from gh`. The commits are the entries' `sha`s, pages in order and entries in order; a `sha` listed again is read once, at its first position. A PR whose answer lists no commit is read, with no runs.
   - **Check runs:** the answer must be a non-empty JSON array of pages, each an object with the same non-negative integer `total_count` and an array `check_runs`, the entries of all pages numbering that `total_count`, every entry an object with a string `name`, a string `status` and a `conclusion` that is a string or `null` (item 79's check-run reader, unchanged); else `unreadable answer from gh`. An entry whose `name` equals a `suite_checks` name byte for byte and whose `status` is `completed` is a **run** of that name, and must also have a `completed_at` that is a time (item 79's GitHub UTC form); else `unreadable answer from gh`. Every entry must have that common shape, so a malformed entry of another check makes the answer unreadable; beyond it, entries of other names, and entries not `completed` (queued, in progress), are not read further. As in item 79's reader, entries are taken as listed: an entry that appears on two pages counts twice, and no `id` is read.
   - **A red run** is a run whose `conclusion` is `failure` or `timed_out`. **A green run** is a run whose `conclusion` is `success`. Any other conclusion (`cancelled`, which GitHub gives a run superseded by a newer push, `skipped`, `neutral`, `action_required`, `stale`, another string, `null`) is neither, and still counts as a run.
   - **Each red run** at commit `C` of a PR falls in exactly one of three groups, tested in this order:
     - **green again:** a green run of the same name at `C` has a `completed_at` later than the red run's (an equal time is not later);
     - **fixed by a new commit:** a green run of the same name, at a commit that comes after `C` in the same PR's commit list, has a `completed_at` later than the red run's (an equal time is not later; whatever that commit's later runs say);
     - **still red:** neither.

     Each red run is judged on its own: two red runs at one commit followed by one green run there are two red runs, both green again. A red run, and the commit it is at, are judged within each PR that lists the commit: a commit two PRs list is counted once per PR.
4. **The line.** `--json` prints one more object, right after the `review` object and before the `dora` line, with these keys:
   - `kind`: `"checks"`;
   - `prs`: the number of reviewed PRs;
   - `prs_read`: the number of reviewed PRs read in full;
   - `missing`: an object from each reason to the number of reviewed PRs not read for it, keys in byte order; `{}` when every PR was read. The reasons are `gh failed`, `unreadable answer from gh` and `gh timed out`;
   - `names`: an array with one object per `suite_checks` name, in config order, each with exactly the keys `name` (the name), `runs` (its runs at the commits of the PRs read), `red` (its red runs among them), `green_again`, `new_commit` and `still_red` (the red runs in each group, which sum to `red`). A name with no run has every count `0`.

   Counts are JSON integers. Within each object, keys print in the order the serializer already gives every `report` object; no case asserts key order. The text output prints, at the same position, one line:
   `checks: <prs_read> of <prs> reviewed PR(s) read; <name>: <red> red in <runs> run(s), <green_again> green again on the same commit, <new_commit> fixed by a new commit, <still_red> still red`
   with one `; <name>: ...` part per name, in config order, and, only when `missing` is not empty, `; not read: ` and each reason written `<reason> <n>`, joined by `, `, in key order. In the text, `<name>` is the name as a JSON string literal, exactly as `serde_json` writes it (item 109's rule for case ids), so a name with a colon, a comma or a semicolon stays distinct:
   `checks: 4 of 5 reviewed PR(s) read; "acceptance": 2 red in 9 run(s), 1 green again on the same commit, 1 fixed by a new commit, 0 still red; "lint": 1 red in 9 run(s), 0 green again on the same commit, 1 fixed by a new commit, 0 still red; not read: gh failed 1`.
   - **Without `--github`:** no `checks` object and no `checks:` line; the output is today's, byte for byte.
   - **With `--github`, and no round row, or no readable config, or an empty or absent `suite_checks`:** no `checks` object, no `checks:` line and no call of this item; the output is today's.
   - **When GitHub cannot be read** (`gh` missing, every call failed, a timeout): the object and the line are still printed, with `prs_read` `0`, every count `0` and the reasons in `missing`. Every other line of the report prints as it does today, item 113's keys and item 79's `github` line included, and the exit code stays 0. Nothing is written to stderr for these reasons.

**Known limits.** Only GitHub check runs are read: a `suite_checks` name that a commit status reports (item 62 accepts both; a Jenkins `tests` status, say) has no run here, so it shows `0` runs with its PRs read; the line counts Checks API runs only. GitHub lists check runs only for the most recent 1000 check suites of a commit and keeps a limited number of runs of one name per suite, so older runs of a heavily reused commit are not read and are not reported as missing. The report's opening line, which says reviews or CI that ran elsewhere are not counted, is unchanged: it describes the run-log lines, and the `checks:` line names its own source (`reviewed PR(s) read`). The runs are what GitHub shows now, for the PRs this machine's run log reviewed: a PR reviewed in another clone is not read (item 112's limit). GitHub lists at most 250 commits per PR, so later commits of a longer PR are not read; a commit force-pushed away no longer appears in the PR's list, so its runs are not read, and a red there is not counted. Check runs are listed per commit, not per PR: a run at a PR's commit that another branch's push triggered is counted too, and a workflow that runs on both `pull_request` and `push` (Shipcraft's own `lint` does) gives two runs per pushed commit, so one red push can count as two red runs. Green again is not proof of a flaky check: a run that failed for the runner's sake and passed on a rerun is green again too, as in item 109; "fixed by a new commit" says only that a later commit of the PR had a green run of that name, not that the commit fixed the cause. `suite_checks` comes from today's config: a name added later reads the older PRs' runs of that name, when there are any. One call is made per reviewed PR and one per distinct commit of it, so a run log with a hundred reviewed PRs of four commits each makes about five hundred calls (each under the 60 s limit; at about half a second each, about four minutes); `--since` does not narrow it. The GitHub repository is the one `gh` selects at `<root>` (item 79's limit).

**Metric:** once, after the next 5 merged items, on Shipcraft's own run log: the `checks` line of `report --github` agrees with a hand count, by a script in the private process repository that lists the same reviewed PRs' commits and check runs through `gh` with this item's rules, run at the same time; and that script's per-PR `lint` red count agrees with the "lint went red" note of each of those 5 metrics rows. The process metric then reads the red runs from this line. Revert: a release that drops the `checks` object and line; nothing is written by this item, so nothing needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.62.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:** item 79's `v1-79.duplicate-prs`: its config has `suite_checks = ["zeta suite/β"]` and its fresh `review` row (`pr: 98`) makes PR 98 a reviewed PR, so its expected call list gains one last call, `['api','--paginate','--slurp','repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/98/commits?per_page=100']`, after item 113's status call; the fake `gh` of `acceptance/lib/item79.sh` does not answer it (exit 91), so it is `gh failed` there, and the `--github` output gains a `checks` object right after the `review` object, with `prs` `1`, `prs_read` `0`, `missing` `{"gh failed": 1}` and `names` `[{"name": "zeta suite/β", "runs": 0, "red": 0, "green_again": 0, "new_commit": 0, "still_red": 0}]`.
