The kind order its check reads gains `checks` right after `review`, and its `comparable()` helper, which compares the `--github` report with the plain one leaving out the `github` and `lock` objects and item 113's five keys, also leaves out the `checks` object. Every other assertion of that case stays.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every other case of item 79: their run logs hold no round row (their `review` rows, where any, carry no `pr`), so no call of this item;
  - every case of items 112 and 113 (`acceptance/lib/item112.sh`, `item113.sh`): their fixtures have no `.shipcraft.toml`, or one with no `suite_checks` (item 113's `ci = "github"` config, and `ci = "off"` in its live controls), so no `checks` object and no call of this item;
  - every `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 83, 99, 105, 107 and 109, `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report`: no round row, or no `--github`;
  - item 62's `suite_checks` cases and item 111's `lint` cases: the key and the check are read as today; this item only reads them;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field;
  - item 79's whole must-keep list (its point 5), since this item reuses its call, cache, environment and config machinery: item 22's environment cases (`v1-22.ambiente-red`, `ambiente-ci`, `ambiente-verifica`, `ambiente-build`), `Config::parse`'s cases (item 47's, item 64's role and `shipcraft_min` cases, `v1-30.1-raiz-*`, `glob-*`), item 43's shared lock-reader cases it names, and the usage-text cases.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item114.sh`. They write the run log by hand after the fixture's last commit and a `.shipcraft.toml` with `ci = "github"`, `shipcraft_min = "2.18"` and a `suite_checks` of two names (one with a space or a non-ASCII character), put a fake `gh` first on `PATH` that answers each argument list from a table and logs its calls, as item 113's do, and run `report --github` and `report --json --github`. Estimate: 6 for point 1 (no round row, no config, empty `suite_checks`, config order, PR order, no `--github`), 9 for point 2 (call form and order after item 113's, one call per distinct commit, cache across PRs, a failure stopping only its PR, `gh` missing, a signal, timeout before and during this item's calls, no retry), 13 for point 3 (each answer's shape, pages, a repeated sha, entries not `completed`, a `completed_at` that is not a time, each conclusion, green again by time and the equal-time tie, fixed by a later commit, a green run at an earlier commit not fixing, still red, two reds before one green), 8 for point 4 (keys, every name listed with zeros, text with and without `not read`, the JSON-literal name, the position after `review`, nothing on stderr, exit 0), and 2 controls (an empty `suite_checks` with round rows makes no call; a readable config and round rows without `--github` make no call). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `report` reads the run log as item 112 lists, prints `meta`, `lock`, `day`, `size`, `reds`, `review`, `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and `gap` in that order, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. Change: with `--github`, the `checks` object and line right after `review`, under point 1's conditions.
- Without `--github`, `report` makes no network call. Keep.
- `report --github` (items 79 and 113) asks `gh pr view`, the combined `status` and `check-runs` (`filter=latest`) for the selected locks, then the `statuses` of item 113's reviewed commits, with the 60 s limit, process group, environment, cache by argument list and the timeout that stops every later call; its execution-config reader (`Asker::config`) reads `.shipcraft.toml` at most once per run, only when a lock reaches item 79's step 4. Keep: this item's calls come after them and share the cache and the timeout. Change: that same once-per-run reader is now also invoked by this item, whenever a reviewed PR exists and `--github` is given, even with no selected lock (a config already read is not read again, and one this item reads first is not read again by item 79). Keep: the separate `[report]` read of the working-tree config for the release selection (`Selection::read`, item 76), unchanged.
- `suite_checks` is validated by `Config::parse` (item 62: a non-empty array needs `ci = "github"` and `shipcraft_min` of 2.18 or more), and read by `pr`, `merge`, `ci`, `status` and `report --github` (item 79). Keep: this item reads it the same way.
- `report`'s git reads (item 76), `--since`'s refusal, `[report]`'s config refusal path and the usage refusal: keep.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the new `report` cases run no hook after writing their log. Keep.

### `report` reads each merged PR, by week and by size, and suggests a size limit (item 120)

On 2026-11-18 Victor read four weeks of review rounds across 452 merged PRs of four projects (Hands-off, Draken, draken-platform and Shipcraft): PRs of up to 600 product lines got 0.64 change requests each and 70% were approved on the first round; above 600 they got 1.69. That reading needed a private script, because `report` counts per lock, not per PR. A PR that delivers several stacked locks gives each lock its own `lock` row (item 38), and every review row of the PR carries all of its locks, so each lock's `reviews` counts and `first_review_approved`, and the `day` lines built from them, count every round of the PR once per lock: a PR with three locks and two refused rounds shows six refused reviews, which made Hands-off's per-lock numbers look worse than they were. This item adds four readings to `report`, per merged PR, from the run log `report` already reads. It is measurement only: the `lock` and `day` lines keep counting as today, no row or field is added to the run log, nothing is refused, and no exit code, status, verdict or `[pr] warn_lines` changes. A suggested limit is a line to read, never applied.

1. **One line per merged PR.** The reading uses the rows of the run log through the reader `report` already uses (item 38: blank lines and `escape: true` rows ignored, a line that does not parse is the `gap` it is today and is not looked at again), and adds no git read and no network call.
   - **A merged PR** is a number that is the `pr` of at least one `merge` row that counts by item 38 (`result` `"passed"`, `base` and `default_branch` equal strings, not an escape) and whose `pr` is a JSON integer above 0, read as item 107 reads it (a number written with no fraction and no exponent that fits 64 unsigned bits; never `7.0`, `7e0`, `"7"`, `-1`, `0`, `true` or `null`). **Its merge row** is the first such row with that number in the file's order (the log is append-only; `at` is not read to pick it). A `merge` row that does not count, or has no valid `pr`, makes no merged PR.
   - **Its locks** are the locks of its merge row, read as `report` reads a row's locks today: its `lock` when it is a non-empty string, then each non-empty string of its `locks` array, in that order, each once. Only the merge row is read for them; the `pr` rows' `locks` are not.
   - **`merge_at`** is the merge row's `at` when it is a JSON number (Unix seconds, as every `at`), else `null`. **`week`** is the ISO 8601 week of the UTC day of `merge_at` (the day `floor(merge_at / 86400)` days after 1970-01-01, as the `day` lines take it, so a fractional or negative time is floored, never truncated), written `YYYY-Www` with the ISO week-numbering year as four digits and a two-digit week (weeks start on Monday; week 1 is the week with the year's first Thursday): `2026-11-18` is `2026-W47`, `2027-01-01` is `2026-W53`, `2024-12-30` is `2025-W01`. `week` is `null` when `merge_at` is `null`, and also when `merge_at` is below 0 or at or above 253402300800 (`10000-01-01T00:00:00Z`): such a PR keeps its `merge_at`, its other keys and its place in the size table and the limit, adds no `gap`, and is in no week line.
   - **`product_lines`** is the PR's size by item 107: the `product_lines` of its last measured `pr` row before its first counting `merge` row, in the file's order; `null` when the PR is not measured by item 107. A measured row needs both `product_lines` and `warn_lines` as JSON integers (item 107), so a `pr` row without `warn_lines` measures nothing here either, although `warn_lines` is not shown and plays no other part.
   - **`rounds`** is the number of item 112's round rows whose `pr` is the PR's number (a `review` row, `result` `"passed"` or `"refused"`, `reused` not JSON `true`), wherever they are in the file, before or after the merge row. **`changes_requested`** is how many of them are `"refused"`. **`first_round_approved`** is `true` when the first of those round rows in the file's order is `"passed"`, `false` when it is `"refused"`, and `null` when `rounds` is 0. A PR with at least one round is **reviewed**.
   - **`red_at`** is the earliest `at` (a JSON number; equal times keep file order, as item 38's first step) of the rows whose `layer` is `"red"`, `result` `"passed"`, `redo` not JSON `true`, and whose locks (read as above) include one of the PR's locks: item 38's RED of each lock, the first across the PR's locks. **`red_to_merge`** is `merge_at - red_at`, `null` when either is `null` or when the difference is not a finite number (an overflow of two extreme times), never a gap; a negative value (clocks) is kept as it is. A `null` `red_to_merge` is left out of every median.
   - **The line.** One object per merged PR, ordered by PR number from smallest to largest (as numbers), with exactly these keys: `kind` (`"merged_pr"`), `pr`, `locks` (an array of strings, `[]` when none), `merge_at`, `week`, `product_lines`, `rounds`, `changes_requested`, `first_round_approved`, `red_to_merge`. Counts, PR numbers and line counts are JSON integers; times and durations are printed as the `lock` row prints them, by the same conversion (`rows::num`: a whole value below 9e15 in magnitude as an integer, any other as the number it is). The text output prints, at the same position, one line each:
     `pr #<pr>: merged at <merge_at> (<week>); locks <locks>; <product_lines> product lines; <rounds> review round(s), <changes_requested> requested changes, approved on round 1 <yes|no|->; red to merge <red_to_merge>`
     with `<merge_at>` as item 83's UTC time, `<red_to_merge>` as item 83's duration, `<locks>` joined by `, ` or `none`, and `-` for each `null` (`merged at - (-)` when the merge row has no time): `pr #140: merged at 2026-11-18T01:05:02Z (2026-W47); locks 8c042eb7c328; 1429 product lines; 1 review round(s), 0 requested changes, approved on round 1 yes; red to merge 10 min 13 s`.
2. **One line per week.** For each `week` that at least one merged PR has, one object, ordered by week from earliest to latest, with exactly these keys, each over the merged PRs of that week:
   - `kind`: `"week"`; `week`: the week as above;
   - `merged_prs`: how many merged PRs;
   - `red_to_merge_median`: the median of their non-null `red_to_merge` (item 38's median: the mean of the two middle values for an even count), `null` when none;
   - `reviewed_prs`: how many of them are reviewed;
   - `round1_approved_share`: the reviewed PRs whose `first_round_approved` is `true`, over `reviewed_prs`;
   - `changes_requested_per_pr`: the sum of `changes_requested` over the reviewed PRs, over `reviewed_prs`;
   - `three_rounds`: how many reviewed PRs have `rounds` of 3 or more; `three_rounds_share`: `three_rounds` over `reviewed_prs`.

   The three ratios are `null` when `reviewed_prs` is 0, and are printed as the `day` row's share is (a whole value as an integer: `0`, `1`; otherwise the fraction). A merged PR whose `week` is `null` is in no week line. The text output prints one line each:
   `week <week>: <merged_prs> merged PR(s), median red to merge <median>; <reviewed_prs> reviewed, approved on round 1 <share>, <per_pr> change requests per PR, 3 or more rounds <three_rounds> (<three share>)`
   with the shares as item 83's whole percent, `<per_pr>` with two decimals (`0.50`, `1.67`, `0.00`; as for item 83's percent, a value exactly halfway between two hundredths is not pinned, so no case asserts one), the median as item 83's duration, and `-` for each `null`. The 3-or-more-rounds count is the one this item exists for: an average hides the costly tail.
3. **By size, and a suggested limit.** Over the merged PRs whose `product_lines` is not `null` (**measured merged PRs**):
   - **The size table.** Eight objects, always all eight and in this order, one per bucket of product lines: `0-100`, `100-200`, `200-300`, `300-400`, `400-600`, `600-800`, `800-1200`, `1200+`. A PR is in the bucket whose `from < product_lines <= to`, except that the first bucket also holds 0 (`0 <= product_lines <= 100`) and the last has no `to` (`product_lines > 1200`); so 100 is in `0-100`, 101 in `100-200`, 600 in `400-600`. Each object has exactly these keys: `kind` (`"size_bucket"`), `from`, `to` (`null` for the last), `merged_prs` (its measured merged PRs), `reviewed_prs` (those reviewed), `round1_approved_share` and `changes_requested_per_pr` (as in the week line, over the bucket's reviewed PRs, `null` when there are none). The text output prints one line each: `size <from>-<to>: <merged_prs> merged PR(s), <reviewed_prs> reviewed, approved on round 1 <share>, <per_pr> change requests per PR`, the last as `size 1200+: ...`, with the week line's forms.
   - **The suggested limit,** Victor's rule of 2026-11-18, with two guards from the spec review. The **sample** is the measured merged PRs that are reviewed; `n` is its size. The edges are 100, 200, 300, 400, 500, 600, 800, 1000 and 1500; the edge before 100 is 0. For an edge `L`, **the PRs up to `L`** are the sample's PRs with `product_lines <= L`. An edge is **supported** when at least 30 PRs (the **minimum**) are up to it and at least one sample PR is in its own interval: `0 <= product_lines <= 100` for 100 (0 included, as the first size bucket), and above the edge before it and at most `L` for every other edge (so a limit is never suggested above every size the data has: 30 PRs of 20 lines, or of 0, support only 100). A supported edge **qualifies** when, over the PRs up to it, the mean of `changes_requested` is below 1 (strictly) and the share whose `first_round_approved` is `true` is at least 0.5. Then:
     - when no edge is supported (for example `n` below 30, or fewer than 30 PRs up to 1500), the limit is the default, 600, and `basis` is `"default"`;
     - otherwise, when at least one supported edge qualifies, the limit is the largest one, and `basis` is `"data"`;
     - otherwise (supported edges, none qualifies) the limit is `null` and `basis` is `"none"`.

     The minimum of 30 is a working heuristic for "enough PRs to read a rate from", not a statistical guarantee; it applies to the PRs each edge is judged on, not only to the whole sample, so 29 PRs of 2,000 lines and one of 50 give the default, not 100. The rule stays cumulative, as Victor's: an edge is judged on all the PRs up to it, so many small PRs can carry an edge whose own interval holds one costly PR (30 approved PRs of 20 lines and one of 1,400 lines with ten change requests give 1500). That is the rule's reading, accepted here; the size table beside it shows each interval on its own.

     One object with exactly these keys: `kind` (`"limit"`), `sample` (`n`), `minimum` (30), `default` (600), `limit`, `basis`. The text output prints one line:
     - `"data"`: `suggested limit: <limit> product lines, from <n> reviewed merged PR(s): the largest supported edge (30 or more PRs up to it, one in its interval) whose PRs up to it average under 1 change request each with at least 50% approved on round 1`;
     - `"default"`: `suggested limit: 600 product lines (the default): <n> reviewed merged PR(s), no supported edge (30 or more PRs up to it, one in its interval)`;
     - `"none"`: `suggested limit: none: at no supported edge (30 or more PRs up to it, one in its interval) do the PRs up to it average under 1 change request each with at least 50% approved on round 1 (<n> reviewed merged PR(s))`.

     On Victor's data of 2026-11-18 the rule, read without the two guards, gave Hands-off 600 and Draken 800; he set both at 600. The line only reports: `warn_lines` stays what the config says.
   - The size table and the limit are printed when at least one merged PR is measured, and not at all otherwise (a merged PR without a measured `pr` row is in the `merged_pr` and `week` lines only).

**Where the lines go.** In `--json` and in the text, right before `dora` (in the text, right before the `DORA` line), after whichever of today's lines are printed (`meta`, `lock`, `day`, `size`, `reds`, `review`, `checks`), in this order: the `merged_pr` lines, the `week` lines, the eight `size_bucket` lines, the `limit` line. **No merged PR:** none of them, and `report` prints byte for byte what it prints today, in both forms. The "byte for byte as today" sentences of items 107, 109, 112 and 114 (a log without measured rows, red rows or round rows; `report` without `--github`) hold for a log with no merged PR; a log with a merged PR gains this item's lines, and their own lines are unchanged. `--since` and `--github` do not change them: they always cover the whole run log, and nothing in them is filled from GitHub (`--github`'s `merge_at` for a `merge_not_observed` lock stays on that lock's row).

**Known limits.** The readings cover what ran on this machine, as the report's first line says: a PR merged elsewhere (another clone, GitHub's button) writes no `merge` row here and is not read, and a review run elsewhere is not a round. A PR's size is the one its last `pr` run before the merge saw. A round is item 112's: a review that gave a verdict, so a `pr` run after a commit that changed only a test reviews again and counts. The RED time is the earliest of the PR's locks, so a PR whose stack began days earlier carries that wait. The edges, the minimum of 30 and the thresholds are Victor's rule as constants, not config keys: making them, or the limit itself, a per-project setting with the spec review switchable is a later item (POC-050 reads this item's lines first). PR numbers are not checked against the repository.

**Metric:** POC-050 reads this item's `week` lines (the share of PRs with 3 or more rounds) and its `limit` line on Hands-off and Draken. Check once on each adopter's run log after the release: the `limit` line's sample and verdict match a by-hand count of the same rows. Revert: a later release that drops the four kinds; the run log is unchanged by this item, so nothing written meanwhile needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.64.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
  - item 79's kind order (`acceptance/lib/item79.sh`, the list `['meta','lock','day','review','checks','dora','github','gap']` its cases sort by): it gains `merged_pr`, `week`, `size_bucket` and `limit` between `checks` and `dora`, since its observed cycles write a counting `merge` row with `pr: 97`;
  - item 107's `v1-107.order`: its `merge` row has `pr: 1` and its `pr` row measures it, so its kind list gains `merged_pr`, `week`, eight `size_bucket` and `limit` between `size` and `dora`, and in the text the line after `size:` is `pr #1: ...` instead of `DORA`.
  - item 112's `v1-112.order-reds` and `v1-112.order-day` (found by CI after the lock): their `row()` helper gives every row `pr: 7`, so their `merge` rows (lock `A`, and `A` and `B` on two days) count with a valid `pr` and the log has a merged PR 7, measured in `order-reds` (its `pr` row has `product_lines` 9 and `warn_lines` 2) and not in `order-day`. Their exact kind lists gain, right before `dora`, `merged_pr` and `week`, and in `order-reds` also eight `size_bucket` and `limit`; in the text of both, the line after `review:` is `pr #7: ...` instead of `DORA`. Nothing else in them changes.
  - item 79's list is one shared line (`acceptance/lib/item79.sh` line 317): the cases that reach it with a counting `merge` row with a valid `pr` (every case whose cycle writes the observed merge with `pr: 97` and does not null it, such as `v1-79.merged`, `v1-79.text` and `v1-79.summary-sorted`) gain `merged_pr` and `week` lines; their fixtures measure no size, so most gain no `size_bucket` or `limit`. Only that list changes; no other assertion of item 79.
  - item 116's structural lists (`acceptance/lib/item116.sh`, `files` and `module_names`): the new code goes in one new file, `nucleo/src/relatorio/prs.rs`, declared `mod prs;` in `relatorio.rs`, so `files` gains `'relatorio/prs.rs'` and `module_names` gains `'prs'`. That changes `v1-116.contents` (the folder's exact contents) and makes `v1-116.root`, `unique` and `ceiling` read the new file too (its `mod` line, no table name of item 116 defined again in it, at most 600 lines). No other assertion of item 116; every other file keeps its table items, and `relatorio.rs`, `text.rs` and every file stay at most 600 lines.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every other `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 79, 83, 99, 105, 107, 109, 112, 113 and 114, text and `--json`: their logs hold no counting `merge` row with a valid `pr`, or their checks read only their own kinds;
  - item 109's kind-order cases `v1-109.order-size` and `v1-109.order-day`, whose `merge` rows carry no `pr`, and item 112's other cases; `v1-113.basic`, `v1-114.basic` and `v1-114.text`, whose logs hold review rows only;
  - `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report`, which run `report` on logs a real `pr` or `merge` wrote and read only their own lines;
  - item 103's `v1-103.row-refused` and `v1-103.row-error` (a `pr` row carries `product_lines` and `warn_lines` as integers whatever its result, and other rows carry no `warn_lines`): this item reads that row through item 107, so a measured `pr` row with a refused or error result still sizes the PR;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item120.sh`, write the run log by hand after the fixture's last commit, as item 107's and item 112's do, and run `report --json` and `report`. Estimate: 16 for point 1, 9 for point 2, 13 for point 3. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `report` finds the repository by walking up from the current directory to the first `.git` (a directory, or a file `gitdir: <dir>` leading to its git dir, then its `commondir` when it has one), and reads `<common git dir>/shipcraft/runs.jsonl`, the log every worktree shares; no `.git` found, or a `.git` file it cannot read, is an empty log and DORA's `no git repository found`, exit 0. Keep.
- `report` reads the run log's bytes; a file it cannot read (missing, a directory, no permission) is an empty log; the bytes are decoded as UTF-8 with each invalid sequence replaced by U+FFFD, never refused. It parses with `serde_json`, one object per line; a field of the wrong type is read as empty, never a gap; rows with `escape: true` and blank lines are ignored; a BOM and a trailing CR are stripped. Keep, and the new lines read through the same reader. `report` writes no file or folder, appends no row and has no rollback. Keep.
- `report` prints `meta`, the `lock` lines (each lock's `reviews` and `first_review_approved` counting every round of its PR, so stacked locks repeat the PR's rounds), the `day` lines (per lock), `size`, `reds`, `review`, `checks` (with `--github`), `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and the `gap` lines, in that order, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. Change: the `merged_pr`, `week`, `size_bucket` and `limit` lines between `checks` (or the line before it) and `dora`, only when a PR is merged.
- `report`'s git reads (item 76), `--since`'s refusal, `--github`'s calls, `[report]`'s config refusal path and the usage refusal: keep.
- `pr` writes `pr`, `product_lines`, `warn_lines` and `locks` on its `pr` row; `merge` writes `pr`, `base`, `default_branch` and `locks` on its row; `review` rows carry `pr` and `result`; `red` writes `lock`, `result` and `redo` on its row. Keep: this item adds no row and no field.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the new `report` cases run no hook after writing their log. Keep.

### `report --findings` prints the review findings ledger (item 121)

On 2026-11-18 Victor started POC-050 in Hands-off and Draken: a 600-product-line PR limit, a spec review before the RED, and a ledger of the Blocking findings past reviews raised, committed as `docs/review-findings.md`, that the RED reads before writing cases. Today a private script makes the ledger from the review comments `pr` and `review --pr` post on each PR. This item moves that reading into `report` as `report --findings`. It is a reading only: no refusal, brief, lock, row, status, comment or exit code of any other command changes, nothing is written to the repository, and the RED is not handed the ledger (a later item, after POC-050's verdict). The caller redirects stdout to the file.

1. **The arguments.** `report --findings [--json] [--since <pr>]`, flags in any order, each at most once.
   - With `--findings`, `--since` takes a PR number: 1 to 19 ASCII digits whose value is at least 1 and fits 64 unsigned bits (`007` reads as 7); anything else (`0`, `-3`, `v2.1.0`, `1.5`, an empty value, a missing value, 20 digits) is today's usage refusal. The release-tag grammar of item 99 is not applied, and `[report]` is not read.
   - `--findings` with `--github` is the usage refusal. So is any other spelling or a repeated flag, as today. The usage refusal is the one `main.rs` gives today (`shipcraft: error: invalid arguments: <args>; ` and the usage text on stderr, exit 2), before any git or `gh` call.
   - Without `--findings`, `report` is unchanged: `--since` is item 76's tag, and every line prints as today.
   - The usage text's `report` line becomes `shipcraft report [--json] [--since <tag>] [--github] | shipcraft report --findings [--json] [--since <pr>]` (the last line of the usage text; nothing else in it changes), and §3's command table gains a `report --findings [--json] [--since <pr>]` row.
2. **The calls.** The root is found by `report`'s discovery, unchanged (`relatorio::discover`, the first item of `report`'s current behavior in item 120): walking up from the current directory to the first `.git`, a directory, or a file read as `gitdir: <dir>` (and its `commondir` read when it has one, as today, though the run-log path it builds is not used). When it finds no root (no `.git`, or a `.git` file it cannot read or parse), no call is made and the result is not read with the reason `no git repository found` (point 5). No git subprocess runs, and the run log and `.shipcraft.toml` are not read. Every call runs exactly as item 79's (item 79 point 1): `gh` in `<root>`, with the same environment, stdin from `/dev/null`, stderr discarded, in its own process group, under the same 60 s limit, through the same asker as `report --github` (`relatorio_gh`'s `Asker`), so a call that cannot start, exits non-zero or ends by a signal is `gh failed`, output that is not valid UTF-8 JSON or not the shape below is `unreadable answer from gh`, and the first call that reaches 60 s is `gh timed out` and makes no further call in the run. One attempt per call; no retry.
   - **The merged PRs:** `gh pr list --state merged --limit 1000 --json number`. The answer must be a JSON array (possibly empty) of objects, each with a `number` that is a JSON integer above 0, read as item 107 reads `pr` (written with no fraction and no exponent, fitting 64 unsigned bits: `serde_json`'s `as_u64`, so never `7.0`, `7e0`, `"7"`, `-1` or `0`; other keys are ignored); else `unreadable answer from gh`. A failure of this call is the not-read result (point 5) with its reason. The PRs read are the distinct numbers of the answer that are at least `--since` (all of them without `--since`), from the largest to the smallest.
   - **Each PR's comments,** in that order: `gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/<n>/comments?per_page=100`. The answer must be a non-empty JSON array of pages, each page a JSON array (possibly empty) of objects, each with a string `body` (other keys are ignored); else `unreadable answer from gh`. The whole answer is checked before any comment is read, so a PR whose answer is not of that shape adds no finding and no count warning. A PR whose call fails, or comes after a timeout (no call is made then), is **not read**: it adds no finding, prints one line on stderr, `report: PR <n>: review comments not read: <reason>`, and is named in the output's not-read marker (point 5); the run goes on with the next PR, and the exit code stays 0. The reasons and their meaning are the asker's, unchanged: a pipe or read error on `gh`'s output is `gh failed`, and `unreadable answer from gh` is only for output read in full and rejected as not UTF-8, not JSON or not the shape.
3. **Which comments, and what a finding is.** A comment is a **review comment** when its `body` starts with exactly `**shipcraft: review executed by ` (the comment `pr` and `review --pr` post with a verdict, item 38); every other comment, the `gave no verdict` comment included, is not read. Review comments are read in the answer's order (pages in order, entries in order); a PR with several rounds has several. A body is split into lines at `\n`, and one trailing `\r` and then trailing spaces and tabs are removed from each line.
   - **A Blocking heading** is a line, starting at column 0, of one of these forms, where `<n>` is 1 or more ASCII digits not followed by another digit, and `<rest>` is the rest of the line (possibly empty):
     - 2 to 4 `#`, then one or more spaces, then `Blocking`, one or more spaces, `<n>` and `<rest>` (`## Blocking 1 — Title`, `### Blocking 2: Title`, `#### Blocking 3`);
     - `**Blocking`, one or more spaces, `<n>`, `<rest>` and `**` ending the line, with no `*` in `<rest>` (`**Blocking 1 — Title**`).

     `Blocking` is case-sensitive. A heading with no number (`## Blocking findings`, `**Blocking:**`), one with leading spaces, one that is not the whole line (`- **Blocking 1** — x`) and the review's `Blocking: 4` line are not Blocking headings. Each Blocking heading is one **finding**. Fenced code blocks are not tracked: a heading-shaped line inside one counts.
   - **The title** is `<rest>` with every leading space, tab, `:`, `.`, `-`, `—` (U+2014) and `–` (U+2013) removed, then trailing spaces and tabs removed; an empty title is written `(no title)`.
   - **The location.** A finding's text is the lines after its heading up to the next Blocking heading of the same comment, or the comment's end. Its location is taken from the first line of that text that starts with `Location:`, `Locations:`, `**Location:**` or `**Locations:**` (case-sensitive): the rest of that line, with leading and trailing spaces and tabs removed. A finding with no such line, or whose rest is empty, has no location. (The POC script read only `**Location:**`; the reviews of handsoff PR 160 and draken PR 13 write `Location:` and handsoff PR 152 writes `**Locations:**`.)
   - **The count check.** When a review comment has a line that is exactly `Blocking: <d>` (1 to 19 ASCII digits), the first such line is its declared count; when its value differs from the number of Blocking headings in that comment, `report` prints on stderr `report: PR <n>: the review says Blocking: <d>, <k> Blocking heading(s) read` (`<d>` as written, `<k>` the headings counted) and keeps the findings it read. A comment with no such line, or whose count agrees, prints nothing.
4. **The classes.** Each finding gets the first class of this table whose pattern matches its title (Rust's `regex` crate, case-insensitive, Unicode, matched anywhere in the title), or `Other` when none matches. The table is one constant in the code, in this order, so a later item can make it per project; its first five classes come from Hands-off's findings and the rest from Draken's (English and Portuguese titles), as the POC script has them:

   | Class | Pattern |
   |---|---|
   | Typed input lost (leave, reload, refresh over a dirty form) | `typed\|\binput\b\|dirty\|discard\|clobber\|\bleav\|unmount\|navigat\|\bform\b` |
   | Session, workspace or project boundary (sign-out, another user, slug, tenant) | `sign.?out\|sign.?in\|previous user\|another (user\|person\|workspace\|project)\|workspace\|tenant\|cross\|leak\|slug\|secret\|\bmix` |
   | Order and overlap (late or out-of-order response, overlapping saves, retry, during a refresh) | `overwrit\|delayed\|overlap\|concurren\|stale\|older\|newer\|\blate\b\|pending\|during\|while\|race\|retry\|out of order\|in.?flight\|queued\|twice\|duplicate\|settle` |
   | Failure paths (error, refusal, 4xx/5xx, partial failure) | `fail\|falh\|error\|erro\b\|refus\|reject\|throw\|\b[45]\d\d\b\|partial\|timeout` |
   | Permissions and roles | `permission\|admin\|role\|approver\|owner\|member\|access\|authori` |
   | Consent, tracking and conversions (pixel, cookies, attribution, events) | `pixel\|cookie\|consent\|recus\|revoga\|revocation\|convers\|_ga\|\blead\b\|oppref\|origem\|utm\|video_start\|whatsapp\|calend\|\bmeta\b\|refer` |
   | Markdown, MDX and link handling (a syntax form that slips past a check) | `markdown\|\bmdx\b\|link\|anchor\|âncora\|autolink\|\bhtml\b\|setext\|\bh[12]\b\|emphasis\|bold\|parênteses\|parenthes\|\burls?\b\|destin` |
   | Signature, review and publish state (draft, live, export, deploy) | `assinatura\|signature\|\bsign\|draft\|rascunho\|publica\|publish\|\blive\b\|no ar\|deploy\|export\|rollback\|manifest\|build\|archive\|prévia\|preview\|revis\|agend\|schedul` |
   | Dates, numbers and declared limits | `\bdata\b\|\bdate\|\bdia\b\|\bday\|\byear\|\bano\|numer\|número\|valor\|value\|percent\|deadline\|prazo\|limit\|expir\|lifetime\|threshold` |
   | Paths, files and locks (symlink, slash, backup, directory) | `symlink\|\bpath\|caminho\|diretório\|director\|slash\|backup\|\block\b` |
   | Setup, docs and deploy wiring (entry point, provisioning, migration) | `documented\|entry point\|provision\|firestore\|hosting\|migration\|onboarding\|target\|rewrite\|executable\|image` |

   (`\|` is the table's escape for `|`; each pattern is an alternation.) So `Secrets cross workspace boundaries when project slugs match` is a boundary finding, `Delayed approval overwrites a newer, confirmed answer` an order finding, `Numeric deadlines escape citation checks` a dates finding and `Bold formatting bypasses monetary-value verification` a Markdown finding.
5. **The output.** The **order** of the findings is: by class in the table's order with `Other` last; within a class, by PR from the largest number to the smallest; within a PR, in the order the comments and their headings came.
   - **Text** (without `--json`), on stdout, exactly:
     ```
     # Review findings

     Every Blocking finding the review raised on merged PRs, grouped by class, from the highest PR number down.
     Generated by `shipcraft report --findings`; do not edit by hand.

     ```
     (with `--since <pr>`, the command is written `shipcraft report --findings --since <pr>`, `<pr>` as its decimal value). The PR number stands for recency: no merge time is read. Then, only when at least one PR was not read (point 2), one line `Not read: PR <n> (<reason>), PR <m> (<reason>).`, the PRs in reading order (largest number first) joined by `, `, followed by an empty line, so a ledger saved from a partial run says so in the file. Then, for each class with at least one finding, in order: `## <class> (<count>)`, an empty line, one line per finding, and an empty line. A finding's line is `- <title> (<location>) (PR <n>)`, or `- <title> (PR <n>)` without a location. Every line ends with `\n`. With no finding (no merged PR, no review comment, no Blocking heading) and no PR not read, the output is the header alone; with no finding and at least one PR not read (every comments call failing included), it is the header and the `Not read:` line with its empty line.
   - **`--json`:** one JSON object per finding, one per line, in the same order, with exactly these keys: `kind` (`"finding"`), `pr` (a JSON integer), `class` (the class name, or `"Other"`), `title` (as above, `"(no title)"` included), `location` (a string, or `null`). Before the findings, one object per PR not read, in reading order: `{"kind":"pr_not_read","pr":<n>,"reason":"<reason>"}`. No header and no other line; no finding and no unread PR prints nothing. Object key order is not part of the contract (no case asserts it); the order of the lines is.
   - **Not read.** When the merged-PR list is not read (no root, `gh` missing, offline, a failure, an unreadable answer, a timeout), the output is one line on stdout and nothing else: `review findings not read: <reason>` in text, `{"kind":"findings_not_read","reason":"<reason>"}` with `--json`. The reasons are `no git repository found`, `gh failed`, `unreadable answer from gh` and `gh timed out`. Nothing is written to stderr for it.
   - The exit code is 0 in every case of points 2 to 5. `report --findings` writes no file or folder and appends no row.

**Known limits.** The ledger is what GitHub serves now: the comments of the merged PRs `gh pr list --limit 1000` returns, which are the 1000 most recently *created* merged PRs (gh orders by creation, not merge time), so in a repository with more than 1000 merged PRs an old PR merged recently can be left out with no marker; as they read today (an edited or deleted comment reads as edited or is gone), and a review run elsewhere that posted the same comment form is read as Shipcraft's. A review that names its findings in another form (`### 1. Title` under a `## Blocking` header, a list) adds none; the count check is the signal on stderr. A heading-shaped line in a code block is read as a finding. A PR is in the ledger whatever the branch it merged into. The classes are keyword guesses on the title, tuned on Hands-off's and Draken's findings; they are a constant in this item, and making them per project is a later item. A redirect of a not-read run over `docs/review-findings.md` replaces the ledger with the not-read line; the caller checks the line before committing.

**Metric:** POC-050's ledgers, checked by hand once, outside the battery. On the release, run `report --findings` on Hands-off and Draken and compare with the POC script's output on the same day: the same findings in the same classes, except the locations this item reads in forms the script missed. Revert: a later release that drops `--findings`; nothing is written by this item, so nothing needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.65.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
  - item 110's usage text (`acceptance/lib/item110.sh`, the `usage` string its invalid-argument cases `v1-110.duplicate`, `flag-value`, `unknown`, `missing-title`, `missing-body` and `no-review` compare with): its last line gains ` | shipcraft report --findings [--json] [--since <pr>]` after `[--github]`.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 79, 83, 99, 105, 107, 109, 112, 113, 114 and 120, text and `--json`: none passes `--findings`. Item 79's usage case (`acceptance/lib/item79.sh` line 296) checks that the usage contains `shipcraft report [--json] [--since <tag>] [--github]`, which it still does; item 79's invalid-argument cases (`repeat-github`, `repeat-json`, `repeat-since`, `unknown`) stay refusals;
  - item 76's and item 99's `--since` grammar cases, which run without `--findings`;
  - item 116's and item 119's structural cases (`acceptance/lib/item116.sh`, `item119.sh`): the new code goes in one new file, `nucleo/src/findings.rs`, declared in `main.rs`, outside `nucleo/src/relatorio/` and `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/`; the asker may gain a method inside `impl<'a> Asker<'a> {` (indented, so no new definition), and every file there stays at most 600 lines; `relatorio_gh/tests.rs` keeps exactly its 7 tests (unit tests of this item go in `findings.rs`);
  - the review comment as `pr` and `review --pr` post it (`**shipcraft: review executed by <agent>, on this machine**`, item 38's and item 82's cases): this item only reads it;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field;
  - the cases that print the usage text but compare only part of it or a same-run copy (spec review, 2026-11-18): item 61's `v1-61.hint-noargs`, `hint-help`, `hint-h`, `hint-longhelp`, `hint-config`, `hint-outside`, `hint-git-fails`; item 69's `v1-69.machine-noargs`, `machine-help`, `machine-h`, `machine-longhelp`, their `machine-nested-*` counterparts, `v1-69.invalid` and `invalid-local`; item 22's `v1-22.ambiente-*` (the startup environment, which the new entry point shares);
  - `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report`, which run `report --json` without `--findings`;
  - the review producer the control uses, unchanged: item 63's `v1-63.brief-review-pr`, `brief-review-pr-spoof`, `publish-fails-review-pr`, `reuse-review-pr`, item 54's cases and item 87's `v1-87.retry-review-pr`, `publish-fails`, `slot-control`, `slot-free`. Neither the review nor its brief changes to make the control pass.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item121.sh`. They run `report --findings` in a fixture repository with a fake `gh` first on `PATH` that answers each argument list from a table and logs its calls, as item 79's and item 113's do (with item 79's `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED`, process-group and environment checks not repeated). The comment bodies are recorded from real reviews and supplied by the coordinator with the RED brief: the `gh api --paginate --slurp repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/<n>/comments?per_page=100` answers of 2026-11-18, each comment's `body` trimmed to its first line, verdict line, `Blocking:` line, headings and Location lines (the rest is private project text), other comments replaced by `(other comment)`; the RED copies them into `acceptance/fixtures/item121/` unchanged: handsoff PR 152 (`## Blocking N —` and `### Blocking N —`/`:` headings, `**Location:**` and `**Locations:**`), handsoff PR 160 (`**Blocking N — ...**` headings, plain `Location:`) and draken PR 13 (`### Blocking N:` headings, Portuguese text); no case reaches the network. One control case runs `shipcraft review --pr <n>` with a fake reviewer whose fresh verdict is `CHANGES REQUESTED` with `Blocking: 1` and exactly one Blocking heading (so `review --pr` exits 1 with `review-changes-requested` after posting the comment, which the control expects), captures the comment body the fake `gh` received, serves it back as that PR's comments, and asserts that `report --findings` reads its finding. Estimate: 6 for point 1, 7 for point 2, 13 for point 3, 4 for point 4, 8 for point 5, 2 controls. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `main.rs` parses `report [--json] [--since <tag>] [--github]`, each flag once, any order; any other argument, a repeated flag or a `--since` failing the release grammar (item 99) prints `shipcraft: error: invalid arguments: <args>; ` and the usage text on stderr and exits 2, before any git or `gh` call. Keep. Change: `--findings` is accepted, with point 1's grammar for `--since` and its refusal with `--github`; the usage text's last line gains the `--findings` form.
- `report` without `--findings` reads the run log, `[report]` and git, prints `meta`, `lock`, `day`, `size`, `reds`, `review`, `checks`, `merged_pr`, `week`, `size_bucket`, `limit`, `dora`, `github` and `gap` lines as items 38 to 120 define, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep, byte for byte.
- `report --github`'s asker (items 79, 113, 114): its calls, 60 s limit, process group, environment, cache by argument list and timeout. Keep; this item uses it in its own run and changes none of its existing calls.
- `pr` and `shipcraft review --pr <n>` post the review comment through `gh::comment` (a `shipcraft-gh-*` folder with `corpo.md` under the caller's `TMPDIR`, removed afterwards), the `shipcraft/review` statuses and their `review` row, as item 113's current behavior lists. Keep; the control case reads the comment the fake `gh` received.
- `help`, `-h`, `--help` and no arguments print the usage text on stdout, exit 0, and may run git for item 61's setup hint on stderr; an invalid-argument error prints on stderr, exits 2 and looks up no hint. Startup removes `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` from the environment before dispatch (item 22), for `report --findings` and its refusals too. Keep; only the usage text's last line changes.
- `shipcraft review --pr <n>` with a `CHANGES REQUESTED` verdict prints the reviewer's start notice on stderr, posts the comment, posts a `failure` `shipcraft/review` status, prints `shipcraft: shipcraft/review failure: on this machine: CHANGES REQUESTED, Blocking 1 (<agent>)`, and refuses `review-changes-requested` (exit 1, three lines on stderr). Keep.
- `shipcraft review --pr <n>` (the control's path) also takes a review queue slot (folders and `slot-*` files under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/shipcraft/queue`, or the HOME fallback, a released slot truncated, not removed), runs the reviewer in a registered detached worktree under a `shipcraft-revisao-*` folder in the caller's `TMPDIR` (`brief.md`, `saida.txt`, `erros.txt`, possibly `ultima-mensagem.md`), and removes and prunes it afterwards, best effort. Keep; the control isolates `TMPDIR` and `XDG_STATE_HOME`, and its no-write assertions on `report --findings` take their snapshots after the review.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: `report --findings` runs no hook and appends no row; the control case that runs `review --pr` appends its row and posts to the fake `gh` as today. Keep.

### `report --findings` groups findings by the project's own classes (item 122)

Item 121 grouped the findings of `report --findings` by one built-in class table, tuned on Hands-off's and Draken's findings. A project with other kinds of code gets most of its findings in `Other`: on 2026-11-18 Hands-off's own ledger still had 58 of its 233 findings there. POC-050's RED reads the ledger to see which kinds of error keep coming back, so the grouping is what makes the ledger useful. This item lets a project declare its own classes in `.shipcraft.toml`. It is a reading only: `report --findings` prints the same lines as item 121 with a project that declares no class, and no other command changes except that an invalid `[findings]` table is refused where the config is validated (point 2).

1. **The keys.** In `.shipcraft.toml`:
   ```toml
   [findings]
   builtin_classes = true        # optional; false leaves out item 121's table

   [[findings.class]]
   name = "Money and rounding"
   keywords = ["round", "cents", '\bBRL\b']
   ```
   - `[findings]` is optional; its keys are `builtin_classes` (a boolean, default `true`) and `class` (an array of tables, written `[[findings.class]]`, default none). An empty array (`class = []`) is no class.
   - Each class has exactly the keys `name` (a string) and `keywords` (an array of strings, at least one). A keyword is a pattern of Rust's `regex` crate, built as item 121 builds its table's patterns: case-insensitive, Unicode, matched anywhere in the title. A class matches a title when any of its keywords matches.
   - **The class table in effect** is the declared classes in the file's order, then item 121's eleven classes in their order (only when `builtin_classes` is `true` or absent), then `Other`. Each finding gets the first class of that table that matches its title, or `Other`; item 121's point 4 otherwise holds unchanged. With `builtin_classes = false` and no declared class, every finding is `Other`.
   - The output's order (item 121 point 5) follows the table in effect: declared classes first, then the built-in ones, then `Other`; within a class, by PR from the largest number down, then in reading order, as today.
   - An older binary ignores `[findings]` (the config reader ignores unknown tables), so no `shipcraft_min` raise is needed; that binary groups by the built-in table.
2. **Validation.** `[findings]` is checked inside `Config::parse`, last: after item 102's `[review] guide` check, which comes after item 99's `[report]` check, so a config with an invalid `[report]` or an invalid `[review] guide` and an invalid `[findings]` gives the earlier refusal. `Config::parse_before_guide` does not gain the check, so `[review] guide`'s early check in the hook (`check_review_guide`, which runs only when `parse_before_guide` succeeds) decides exactly as today for every config, whatever its `[findings]`. No new call site is added: the check runs exactly where a full config parse runs today (the hook and `check` when they parse a staged config, `ci`, `red`, `pr`, `merge`, `status`, `review`, `report --github`'s whole-config read), and only then. So the hook's and `check`'s existing exemption holds: a commit that does not change `.shipcraft.toml` and has no lock to judge does not parse the staged config and is not refused for an invalid `[findings]` already on the branch (as for an invalid `[report]` today); the hook's preliminary checks (`shipcraft_min`, `[review] after`, `[review] guide`) do not gain it. The checks run on the file parsed as a TOML table, in this order, and the first problem found is the one named:
   1. `findings` that is not a table (`findings = 1`, `[[findings]]`): `[findings] is not a table`;
   2. a key other than `builtin_classes` and `class`, the first in byte order: `unknown key <key>`, where `<key>` is the key as written when it is made only of ASCII letters, digits, `_` and `-`, and otherwise the key as a JSON string (double quotes, with `serde_json`'s escapes, so a key holding a newline or an escape character prints on one line);
   3. `builtin_classes` that is not a boolean: `builtin_classes is not a boolean`;
   4. `class` that is not an array whose every element is a table: `class is not an array of tables`;
   5. then each class in order, numbered from 1 as `<i>`, every check of one class before the next class:
      - a key other than `name` and `keywords`, the first in byte order: `class <i>: unknown key <key>`, `<key>` written as in check 2;
      - `name` absent: `class <i>: name is missing`; not a string: `class <i>: name is not a string`; empty or only spaces and tabs: `class <i>: name is empty`; holding a control character (U+0000 to U+001F or U+007F): `class <i>: name has a control character`;
      - `name` equal, byte for byte, to `Other`, to the name of an earlier declared class, or, when the built-in table is in effect, to one of item 121's eleven class names (as `findings.rs`'s `CLASSES` and item 121's table write them, for example `Markdown, MDX and link handling (a syntax form that slips past a check)`): `class <i>: name <name> is already a class`;
      - `keywords` absent: `class <i>: keywords is missing`; not an array: `class <i>: keywords is not an array`; an empty array: `class <i>: keywords is empty`;
      - then each keyword in order, numbered from 1 as `<j>`: not a string: `class <i>: keyword <j> is not a string`; the empty string: `class <i>: keyword <j> is empty`; a pattern the `regex` crate does not build with item 121's options (a syntax error, or above the crate's default size limit): `class <i>: keyword <j> is not a valid pattern` (the crate's own message is not printed).

   A problem refuses `config-invalid` with today's three-line refusal shape for config errors:
   - `[findings] in .shipcraft.toml is invalid: <problem>`
   - `report --findings groups the review findings by these classes, and shipcraft does not guess a class it cannot read`
   - `give each [[findings.class]] a name and a non-empty list of valid keyword patterns, or remove [findings], in .shipcraft.toml and run again`

   A name is used as written (not trimmed). The checks read only the TOML table: text that is not TOML is refused earlier by item 31's check, as today.
3. **What `report --findings` reads and prints.**
   - **Reading the config.** After the root is found (item 121 point 2) and before any `gh` call, `report --findings` reads `<root>/.shipcraft.toml` from the working tree, as item 99's reader reads `[report]`: when the path is absent, is not a regular file, cannot be read, is not UTF-8 or is not TOML, the table in effect is the built-in one, exactly as item 121 (no line or warning about it). It reads only `[findings]`, with point 2's checks; an invalid `[report]` or any other key of the file does not change the ledger. When `[findings]` is invalid, no `gh` call is made and the output is item 121's not-read result with the reason `[findings] in .shipcraft.toml is invalid: <problem>` (`review findings not read: <reason>` in text, `{"kind":"findings_not_read","reason":"<reason>"}` with `--json`), nothing on stderr, exit 0. With no root, item 121's `no git repository found` stays as it is and no config is read.
   - **Text** (without `--json`): unchanged in form. A declared class is a `## <name> (<count>)` section like any other, in the order of the table in effect; the text does not say where a class came from.
   - **`--json`:** each `finding` object gains one key, `class_source`: `"project"` for a declared class, `"builtin"` for one of item 121's classes, and `null` for `Other`. Its keys are exactly `kind`, `pr`, `class`, `class_source`, `title`, `location`. The `pr_not_read` and `findings_not_read` objects do not change.
   - Everything else in item 121 holds unchanged: the arguments, the calls, the comments read, the headings, titles, locations, the count check, the not-read marker and the exit code 0. `report --findings` still writes no file or folder and appends no row.

**Known limits.** A keyword is a regular expression, so `.` and `(` mean what they mean there; a project that wants a literal writes `\.`. The classes are matched on the title only, as item 121's. A class's name is not checked against Markdown: a name with `#` or `*` prints as written. Declared classes that never match are not reported. The config check builds every keyword's pattern on each config read, a cost of microseconds for a few dozen keywords.

**Metric:** in Hands-off, after it declares its classes, the share of its findings in `Other` (58 of 233 on 2026-11-18 with the built-in table), read by hand once from `report --findings` on the release day, outside the battery. Revert: a later release that ignores `[findings]` again; the key is ignored by older binaries, so a project that set it reads the built-in table back.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.66.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
  - item 121's `--json` finding rows (`acceptance/lib/item121.sh`, the `finding(...)` helper, line 63, which the helper-built `--json` comparisons of the file use): each row gains `class_source`, `null` when the class is `Other` and `"builtin"` otherwise. No other line of `item121.sh` changes; its text-output assertions stay byte for byte, since no fixture of item 121 has a `.shipcraft.toml` with `[findings]`.
  - item 121's recorded oracles `acceptance/fixtures/item121/handsoff-152-expected.json`, `handsoff-160-expected.json` and `draken-13-expected.json`, read by `v1-121.recorded152`, `recorded160` and `recorded13`: each `finding` object gains `class_source`, by the same rule (`null` for `Other`, `"builtin"` otherwise); nothing else in those files changes.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every other case of item 121 (`acceptance/lib/item121.sh`), text and `--json`, including `v1-121.discovery`, `missing-root`, `bad-git`, `readonly`, `live`, `control-gh` and `control-live`: their fixtures declare no `[findings]`, so the built-in table applies;
  - item 99's `[report]` cases (`acceptance/lib/item99.sh`): `[report]` is checked before `[findings]`, and `v1-99.invalid-precedence` and `status-invalid` keep their refusal;
  - item 118's structural cases (`acceptance/lib/item118.sh`): `nucleo/src/config/` keeps exactly its ten files, so the new check goes in an existing file (`config/report.rs` is the natural one) and the pattern code in `nucleo/src/findings.rs`; every file stays at most 600 lines; `config/tests.rs` keeps its 11 tests (new unit tests go in `findings.rs`);
  - item 116's and item 119's structural cases: no file under `nucleo/src/relatorio/` or `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/` changes;
  - item 79's `report --github` cases, `v1-79.unobserved-config` included: their configs have no `[findings]`;
  - the config refusal and default cases item 118 names (`acceptance/lib/english.sh`, items 9, 30.1, 31, 42, 47, 59, 62, 64, 71, 80, 87, 90, 96, 99, 102, 105, 110, 112, 114, 115 and item 44's base-config cases): none of their configs has `[findings]`;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field;
  - the `status` cases that run the changed `Config::load` before printing: item 73's `v1-73.status-absent`, `status-string`, `status-table`, `status-dotted`; item 89's `status` cases (`v1-89.current-bare`, `current-local`, `early-invalid`, `early-missing`, `early-min` and the others of `acceptance/lib/item89.sh`); item 101's cases (`v1-101.order`, `config-fail`, `control-unset`, `control-literal` and the others): none of their configs has `[findings]`;
  - item 102's guide precedence cases (`acceptance/lib/item102.sh`): the guide check stays before `[findings]`'s, and its early hook check is unchanged (point 2);
  - the hook's close-mark and warning cases, which run the same staged-config parse: item 17's `v1-17.recusa-limpa-marca` and `v1-17.marca-falha` (the previous mark is removed, or `mark-failed` refused, before the staged config is parsed, so an invalid `[findings]` comes after both, as an invalid `[report]` does today), and item 28's `v1-28.pre-recusa`, `msg-recusa`, `pre-consulta-outros`, `msg-consulta-outros`, `pre-consulta-ignorados` and `msg-consulta-ignorados` (the outside-index warning before a refusal).
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item122.sh`, with item 121's fixture repository and fake `gh` (`acceptance/lib/item121.sh`'s helpers may be copied, not changed), never the network. Estimate: point 1, 9 (declared first; built-in after; `builtin_classes = false`; any keyword matches; case-insensitive and Unicode keyword; file order between declared classes; a declared class that wins over a built-in one on the same title; empty `class = []`; `builtin_classes = false` with no class gives `Other`); point 2, 17 (each problem of the list through `check` on a staged config, the precedence over `[report]`'s problem the other way round, one case through `status`, one through the hook's commit refusal); point 3, 8 (the four reading fallbacks giving the built-in table, an invalid `[findings]` as the not-read line in text and in `--json` with no `gh` call, `class_source` for project, built-in and `Other` rows, the text form of a declared class); 2 controls (a config with a valid `[findings]` passes `check`, which prints `shipcraft: no open lock; commit allowed.` and exits 0; an invalid `[report]` with a valid `[findings]` still prints the ledger with declared classes). The `status` case asserts only its refusal (it refuses at `Config::load`, before its first line and before any `gh` call); no case runs a `status` that succeeds. The `check`, `status` and hook cases run with no open lock, and each `check` and hook case stages a `.shipcraft.toml` that differs from the parent commit's, so the staged config is parsed (point 2). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `Config::parse` runs its checks in item 118's order, ending with item 99's `[report]` check and item 102's `[review] guide`; an unknown table such as `[findings]` is ignored. Change: `[findings]` is checked after both, last (point 2). Every other check, its order and its refusal text: keep.
- `report --findings` (item 121) finds the root by `report`'s discovery, runs no git subprocess, reads neither the run log nor `.shipcraft.toml`, groups by the built-in table, and prints the ledger or the not-read line, exit 0, writing nothing. Change: it reads `<root>/.shipcraft.toml`'s `[findings]` (point 3) and `--json` finding rows gain `class_source`. Everything else: keep.
- `report` without `--findings` reads only `[report]` of the config (item 99), and `report --github` validates the whole working-tree config and marks selected locks `config unreadable` when that fails (item 99 point 2). Keep; with an invalid `[findings]` the whole-config validation fails, so `--github`'s selected locks read `config unreadable`, as with an invalid `[report]`. `report` without `--github` and without `--findings` does not read `[findings]`.
- `status` loads the config with `Config::load` before it prints anything; a refusal there prints only the refusal (exit 1), with no `gh` call and no write; a success prints the branch, layers, roles, hooks, onboarding block, open locks and protection lines as items 64, 73, 89 and 101 define, queries protection through `gh`, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. The hook's and `check`'s staged-config parse refuse an invalid config with its code and three lines (exit 1; an operational failure exits 2). Keep; they refuse an invalid `[findings]` the same way, at the same point: after `check`'s outside-index warning (item 28) and, with `--commit-msg`, after the previous close mark is removed (item 17), whose failure still refuses `mark-failed` first.
- `check`, in both forms, is measured as `hook`: each run appends a `hook` row to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl`, creating `.git/shipcraft/` if needed, even with no lock, and a failed append changes no verdict. With no open lock and an accepted commit it prints `shipcraft: no open lock; commit allowed.` and exits 0. With an open lock it runs the locked cases (in the tree or on an index copy, with the caller's `TMPDIR`); when they pass, plain `check` closes the lock itself (writes the closed lock in the working tree, stages it, prints `shipcraft: all locked cases pass; lock <path> closes in this commit.`), while `check --commit-msg` writes the close mark and the post-commit hook then makes its `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>` commit. Keep both; this item's cases run with no open lock, so they meet only the row and the no-lock message.
- `red`, `red --redo`, `ci`, `pr`, `review` and `merge` reach `Config::parse` as today; their locks, rows, suite and `[build]` runs, publications and the other standing effects the spec-review brief lists are kept as their own items define them. This item's cases run none of them.
- `report --findings` runs no suite and no git, and appends no row. Keep.

### `report` shows the suite-red runs that went green on the same commit, by week (item 123)

The 2026-11-07 gaps review (private process repository, `prd/lacunas-e-proximos-2026-11-07.md`, track E) measured that about 40% of the adopters' red CI runs were flake-like: a `suite-red` `ci` row followed by a green `ci` row on the same commit, with no new commit, 12 of 29 in draken-platform (73 min of CI) and 5 of 16 in draken (32 min), and the agent investigated each one as real. Item 109 (2.58.0) already prints that count for the whole run log, as the `reds` line. What it cannot show is whether the share is falling or rising: a burst of flake-like reds in one week (machine load, a slow database) and a steady rate read the same over the whole log. This item adds the same reading per ISO week, beside item 120's week lines, so the decision on rerunning failed cases (below) is taken on a trend and not on one total. It is a reading of the run log only: nothing is rerun, nothing is refused, and no exit code, status, verdict, row or field changes.

1. **Which rows, and their week.** The reading is item 109's, unchanged: the same reader, **red rows** (`layer` `"ci"`, `result` `"refused"`, `code` `"suite-red"`, a non-empty string `commit`), **green rows**, **green again** (a later green row on the same commit, in the file's order), a red row's **CI time** (its `secs` when a JSON number of 0 or more, else 0) and its **cases** (the distinct strings of its `failed` array). A green-again red row is what this item calls *flake-like*. Two choices are settled here:
   - **Only `suite-red` counts.** A `ci` row with another refusal code is not a red row, as in item 109: `no-report` (a suite's build or pre-check failed and left no test report, so it names no case), `lock-not-closed`, `case-missing`, `case-skipped`, `test-changed`, `case-broke` (lock and contract refusals), and `error` rows (`timed-out` included). The 2026-11-07 measure counted `suite-red` only, and the week lines must add up to item 109's line.
   - **No "no new commit in between" condition.** POC-037's ground truth (2026-11-08) also required that the red row's branch had no `ci` or `hook` row on another commit between the red row and the green one. On draken's and draken-platform's run logs of 2026-11-18 that condition changes no row (17 of 17 green-again rows have no other commit in between), so this item keeps item 109's reading, and the week lines add up to the `reds` line by construction. The condition is a known limit below.
   - **The week of a red row** is the ISO 8601 week of the UTC day of its `at`, read as item 120 reads `merge_at` (a JSON number taken as the 64-bit float `serde_json` gives, rounded by that conversion before the range check and the day division, so a decimal written with more digits than a float holds is judged on its rounded value), by item 120's rule and writing (`YYYY-Www`, the day `floor(at / 86400)` days after 1970-01-01, weeks from Monday, week 1 holding the year's first Thursday): `at` 100 is `1970-W01`, `2026-11-18T12:00:00Z` is `2026-W47`. A red row has no week when its `at` is missing or not a JSON number, below 0, or at or above 253402300800 (`10000-01-01T00:00:00Z`); such a row still counts in the `reds` line, is in no week line, and adds no gap. Only the red row's `at` is read: the green row's `at`, and the time between the two, play no part.
2. **The lines.** When at least one red row has a week, `--json` prints, right after the `reds` object and before whatever follows it today (`review`, `checks`, `merged_pr`, `week`, ... `dora`), one object per week that at least one red row has, ordered by week from earliest to latest, with exactly these keys, each over the red rows of that week:
   - `kind`: `"reds_week"`; `week`: the week;
   - `suite_red`: how many red rows;
   - `green_again`: how many of them are green again;
   - `green_again_share`: `green_again / suite_red`, printed as item 109's share (a whole value as an integer: `0`, `1`; otherwise the fraction);
   - `green_again_secs`: the sum of the CI time of the week's green-again rows, printed as item 109's (`rows::num`), `0` when none;
   - `named`: how many of the week's green-again rows have a `failed` that is a JSON array, even an empty one;
   - `cases`: at most 5 objects `{"id": <string>, "rows": <integer>}`, by item 109's rule over the week's green-again rows only: one per distinct id, `rows` the number of the week's green-again rows naming it, most rows first, ties by id in byte order, the first 5; `[]` when none.

   So the weeks' `suite_red` add up to the `reds` line's `suite_red` less its red rows without a week, and the same holds for `green_again`, `named` and (up to floating-point addition order) `green_again_secs`. Counts are JSON integers. The text output prints, right after the `reds:` line, one line per week object in the same order:
   `reds <week>: <suite_red> suite-red, <green_again> green again on the same commit (<share>), <time> of CI; cases named in <named>: <cases>`
   with `<share>`, `<time>` and `<cases>` written exactly as the `reds:` line writes them (item 83's whole percent, item 83's duration, each case as its JSON string literal and ` (<rows>)`, joined by `, `, or `none`): `reds 2026-W45: 10 suite-red, 10 green again on the same commit (100%), 1 h 2 min 3 s of CI; cases named in 0: none`.
   - **No week line** when no red row exists (no `reds` line either: the output is item 109's, byte for byte) or when no red row has a week (the `reds` line alone, as today).
   - `--since` and `--github` do not change the week lines: they cover the whole run log, and nothing in them is filled from GitHub.
   - Every other line keeps its keys, values, text and relative order; only the `reds_week` lines are inserted. `report --findings` is not affected.

**Known limits.** Those of item 109 hold: the lines cover what ran on this machine, green again is not proof of a flaky test (a red caused by the machine that passes once it is fixed is green again too), a red row names up to 50 cases, and a later `ci` against another base that runs fewer suites can make a red green again. POC-037's "no other commit on the branch in between" condition is not applied: a red row whose commit is checked out again and passes after other commits on its branch counts as green again (0 such rows on the two Draken logs today). `no-report` rows are not counted although they can be flake-like too: on draken's log of 2026-11-18, 6 of its 8 `no-report` rows went green on the same commit (2.6 min of CI); they name no case, so a rerun of failed cases cannot reach them, and counting them is a later item if the numbers ask for it. A week is a UTC week, so a red row near midnight on Sunday in another time zone falls in the week UTC gives it.

**The follow-up that waits on these numbers.** Proposal E of the 2026-11-07 analysis: on `suite-red`, `ci` reruns only the failed cases once and records `flaky: [ids]` on the row, the verdict staying red until a green run. It is a behavior change, so it is not in this item; it waits for these week lines, read on the adopters for POC-050's window, to say whether the flake-like share holds or falls once the machine queue and timeouts are fixed.

**Metric:** on draken's and draken-platform's run logs (read only), on the release day: the `reds` line's totals equal the 2026-11-07 measure for the rows that existed then (12 of 29 and 72.5 min in draken-platform; 5 of 16 and 31.9 min in draken, whose log has 5 later red rows, none green again), and the week lines' `suite_red`, `green_again`, `named` and `green_again_secs` (the JSON values, the seconds within floating-point addition error) add up to the `reds` line's less its red rows without a week (none on either log today). Shares and case lists are not additive and are not compared. Revert: a later release that drops the `reds_week` lines; nothing is written by this item, so nothing needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.67.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-63.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version`, `v1-69.machine-version`, `v1-69.machine-nested-version`, `v1-65.main`, `v1-65.many` and `v1-66.passing`, which compare `--version` with the manifest, and the others that read section 11 or the manifest); their test code is unchanged and they pass once both say 2.67.0.
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them** (each has a red row with `at` 100, so a `reds_week` line for `1970-W01` now follows `reds`):
  - item 109's `v1-109.order-size`: the kinds become `['meta','lock','day','size','reds','reds_week','dora','gap']`, and in the text the line after `reds:` starts with `reds 1970-W01:` and the one after that is `DORA`;
  - item 109's `v1-109.order-day`: the kinds become `['meta','lock','day','reds','reds_week','dora']`, and in the text the line after `reds:` starts with `reds 1970-W01:` and the one after that is `DORA`;
  - item 112's `v1-112.order-reds`: `'reds_week'` is inserted after `'reds'` in the kinds, and in the text the line before `review:` starts with `reds 1970-W01:`;
  - item 120's `v1-120.order`: `'reds_week'` is inserted after `'reds'` in the kinds; its text assertions are unchanged.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every other case of item 109 (`acceptance/lib/item109.sh`): they read only the `reds` objects and the lines that start with `reds:`, which do not change;
  - every `report` case of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 79, 83, 99, 105, 107, 112, 113, 114 and 120 other than the four above, text and `--json`, and `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report`: their run logs hold no red row, or they do not assert the lines around `reds`;
  - items 121's and 122's `report --findings` cases: `--findings` prints no `reds` or `reds_week` line;
  - item 116's structural cases (`acceptance/lib/item116.sh`): the new code goes in the existing files of `nucleo/src/relatorio/` (no new file there), every table item keeps its file, every file stays at most 600 lines, and `relatorio/tests.rs` keeps its 13 tests (this item's unit tests go in a `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` of the file that holds its code);
  - item 97's, 43's, 81's and 33's `suite-red` cases: the refusal and its row are written as today;
  - item 40's and item 62's `ci` cases that write `docs_only` and `suite_in_checks` (`v1-40.raiz-md`, `v1-40.sem-pr`, `v1-62.ci-light` among them), and item 109's `v1-109.green-flags` and `v1-109.green-other`: the fields are written and read as today, so a green row that skipped the suite still makes no red row green again, in the week lines too;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field;
  - item 22's `v1-22.ambiente-red`, `ambiente-ci`, `ambiente-verifica`, `ambiente-build` and `ambiente-vazio`: startup still removes `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` before dispatch.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item123.sh` and write the run log by hand after the fixture's last commit, as item 109's do, and run `report --json` and `report`. Estimate: 14 for point 1 (the week of a red row at the ISO edges, `at` missing, a string, negative, at the upper bound; two weeks; green again across a week edge, judged on the red row's week; the sums against the `reds` line), 14 for point 2 (keys and integer types, share and secs forms, cases per week and their cut at 5, order of weeks, the text form, no week line without red rows or without a week, `--since` and `--github` unchanged, position against `review` and `dora`), plus the 4 changed assertions. Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `report` finds the repository by walking up from the current directory to the first `.git` (a directory, or a `gitdir:` file followed to its `commondir`), and reads `<common git dir>/shipcraft/runs.jsonl`, so a linked worktree or a subfolder of a monorepo reads the shared log; when no repository is found it reads an empty log, reports the git evidence as unavailable and exits 0, creating nothing. Keep; the week lines use the rows already read, with no lookup of their own.
- `report` reads the run log's bytes (a file it cannot read is an empty log; invalid UTF-8 replaced, never refused), one JSON object per line through `serde_json`, ignores blank lines and `escape: true` rows, strips a BOM and a trailing CR, and reports an unparsable line as a `gap`. Keep; the week lines read through the same reader.
- `report` prints `meta`, `lock`, `day`, `size`, `reds`, `review`, `checks` (with `--github`), `merged_pr`, `week`, `size_bucket`, `limit`, `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and `gap` lines as items 38 to 120 define, exits 0, writes nothing and appends no row. Keep. Change: the `reds_week` lines right after `reds`.
- Item 109's `reds` line and its `reds:` text line: keep, byte for byte.
- `report --findings` (items 121, 122), `report`'s git reads (item 76), `--since`'s refusal, `--github`'s calls, `[report]`'s config refusal path and the usage refusal: keep.
- `ci` writes a `suite-red` row with `commit`, `secs`, `failed` (at most 50 ids) and `failed_count` (item 97), and a passing row with `commit` and `secs`. Keep: this item adds no row and no field.
- Startup removes `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` from the environment before dispatch (item 22), for `report` and its git and `gh` children too. Keep.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the new cases run no hook after writing their log. Keep.

### `report` counts merges as deploys when the project tags no release (item 124)

Item 76's DORA block counts only release tags, and item 99 lets a project name its tags. Two of the three adopters deploy by merging to their default branch and tag nothing a release selection reads: on 2026-11-18 Draken (0 tags, 123 commits on `main`'s first-parent line) and draken-platform (one tag, `draken-platform-v0.1.0`, outside item 76's `vX.Y.Z`) both print `deploys: - (n 0, missing 0; no release read)`, and lead time, rework rate, time to recover and change failure rate are empty. DORA is the product's headline metric. This is proposal 5 of the 2026-11-07 analysis ("`report` com deploy = merge no main quando não há tag"). This item makes `report` count each merge to the default branch as a deploy when the project reads no release, and lets a project choose either reading. It is a reading only: no run-log row or field, lock field, exit code, status or refusal changes; the only new accepted input is the `[report] deploy` key (point 1), which today is refused as an unknown key.

1. **The key.** `[report] deploy = "merge" | "release"` in `.shipcraft.toml`, beside item 99's `release_tags`.
   - **Default:** with the key absent (or no `[report]`, or no `.shipcraft.toml`, or any of item 99's default-reading cases), the mode is chosen after the release tags are read: **release** when at least one release tag (item 76's tags, or item 99's pattern) is on the default branch, else **merge**. These are the `fallback` and `default` sources of point 4.
   - `deploy = "release"`: release mode whatever the tags, exactly today's reading (a project with no tag keeps `deploys: -`).
   - `deploy = "merge"`: merge mode whatever the tags.
   - **Validation**, by item 99's checks and in the same places (hook, `check`, `ci`, `red`, `pr`, `merge`, `status`, `review`, `report --github`'s whole-config read; `report` without `--github` reads `[report]` as item 99 point 2 says). The allowed keys of `[report]` become `release_tags` and `deploy`. Item 99's problems keep their order and text; one problem is added, checked last: a `deploy` that is not the string `merge` or the string `release` (any other string, any case variant, a non-string): `deploy is not "merge" or "release"`. So with an invalid `release_tags` and an invalid `deploy`, the `release_tags` problem is named. The refusal is item 99's `config-invalid`, its three lines unchanged except the problem in the first: `[report] in .shipcraft.toml is invalid: <problem>`, then item 99's `Why:` and `Next:` lines byte for byte. Under `report` without `--github`, an invalid `deploy` is item 99's invalid `[report]`: every git-derived value `null` with that reason, exit 0.
