   - **`red --fix --fixes-release <tag>`** (item 105): its selection check reads `[report]` through the same reader, so a valid `deploy` key is accepted there and changes nothing in that check (the tag is still checked against the release selection, `release_tags` or `vX.Y.Z`); an invalid `deploy` makes the selection invalid, as an invalid `release_tags` does today (`release tags: invalid: <reason>`).
   - An older binary refuses `deploy` as an unknown key of `[report]` wherever it validates the config; a project that sets it raises `shipcraft_min` to 2.68.0 itself. The release notes say so. Shipcraft does not raise it.
2. **Merge mode.** Everything item 76 and item 99 say holds, with these changes:
   - **The merges.** A *merge* is a commit on the default branch's first-parent line: the default branch's commit (item 76's ref), its first parent, that commit's first parent, and so on to a commit with no parent. So in a squash-merge project each squashed PR is one merge; in a merge-commit project each merge commit is one, and the PR branch's own commits are not; a commit pushed straight to the branch is one too. A merge's time is its committer date in Unix seconds, read with no signature verifier started (as item 76 reads a lightweight tag's commit). Merges are ordered along the line, oldest first; that order decides what a merge ships and which merge a fix names, while `deploys.first`, `last` and `per_day` keep item 76's minimum and maximum time (so a rebased line whose times are not monotonic gives the earliest and latest times, not the ends of the line). The line is git's own first-parent walk, as `git log --first-parent` gives it, so replacement refs and grafts count as git counts them by default, as for item 76's tag and ancestry reads. Every read keeps item 76's rules: `git -C <root>`, the scrubbed environment, no fetch; the partial and shallow clone checks and the default-branch lookup come first, unchanged; a failure (a non-zero exit, unreadable output, a time that is not a decimal integer) makes every git-derived value `null` with item 76's `git failed: ...` reason. The release tags are still read first, as today, in both modes (point 1's choice and `--since` need them). The merges are read only in merge mode, after the `--since` check below.
   - **What a merge ships.** A lock's merge is the earliest merge on the line whose tree holds its lock file (`.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`, item 76's lock file reading); a merge *ships* the locks whose merge it is. The DORA population is item 76's, with the merges in place of the release tags: every lock id in the run log, at the default branch's commit, or in any merge's tree.
   - **Fix data** (item 76 point 2, `change_kind`, `reported_at`, `fixes_release`), in merge mode: the first valid lock file among, in this order, the default branch's commit, the merge that ships the lock, and HEAD (an absent or invalid file is skipped, as item 76 skips one), else the earliest passed non-redo `red` row. The merge that ships the lock takes the place of item 76's release tags (every one that holds the lock, newest first); no other merge is a source. In release mode the sources stay item 76's.
   - **The merges read:** every merge, or with `--since <tag>` those whose time is at or after that release tag's time. `--since` keeps item 76's and item 99's checks: its grammar before any git read, then `report: --since <tag> is not a release tag on <default branch>`, exit 2, when the tag is not a release tag on the default branch. In both modes, every read `report` makes today (the tags, their trees, the default branch's and HEAD's trees, and the lock files read from them) happens before that membership check, in today's order, so a failure there still gives item 76's `null` evidence with `since` kept and exit 0, exactly as today. The merge reads (the line, the trees or lock files of the merges, and the lock files read from them) come after the check: a tag that is not a release exits 2 even if a merge read would have failed, and a merge-read failure after the check passed gives `null` evidence, exit 0. So in the fallback (no release tag) every `--since` exits 2, as it does today; with `deploy = "merge"` and release tags, `--since` starts the window at a release.
   - **The metrics** keep item 76's definitions with *merge* for *release*: `deploys` counts the merges read; `lead_time` is merge time minus the earliest passed non-redo `red` row's time over the shipped locks; `rework_rate` is the share of the merges read that ship a lock and ship a `fix` lock; `time_to_recover` is the time of the merge shipping a `fix` lock minus its `reported_at`. Every `n`, `missing` and null rule is item 76's.
   - **What a fix names.** `fixes_release` names a release tag; in merge mode there is no tag to name, and `red --fixes-release` cannot take a commit without a refusal change. So in merge mode `change_failure_rate` does not use `fixes_release`: a shipped `fix` lock names the merge that was on the default branch when its defect was reported, read from its `reported_at`. Among the merges strictly before, in line order, the merge that ships the fix, it is the latest in line order whose time is at or before `reported_at` (so a merge in the same second as the report counts; the fix's own merge and later ones never do). `fixes_release` is still read, validated, sourced and printed on lock rows exactly as today; only this metric ignores it. `change_failure_rate`'s value is the share of the merges read that some shipped `fix` lock names this way. `n` is the merges read. `missing` is the shipped locks with `change_kind: null`, plus the shipped `fix` locks with no `reported_at`, or whose `reported_at` names no merge (no merge before the fix's own merge has a time at or before it); when above 0, `value` is `null`. A named merge outside the merges read (before `--since`) is neither counted nor missing, as item 76 scopes its releases.
   - **Reasons** in merge mode (in release mode they are item 76's, byte for byte):
     - `deploys`: `no merge read`;
     - `lead_time`: `<k> shipped lock(s) without a passed red row in this run log, or merged before it`; `no shipped lock in the merges read`;
     - `rework_rate`: `<k> shipped lock(s) with an unknown change_kind` (unchanged); `no merge read ships a lock`;
     - `time_to_recover`: `<k> shipped fix lock(s) without reported_at, or merged before it`; `no shipped fix lock with reported_at` (unchanged);
     - `change_failure_rate`: `<k> shipped lock(s) with an unknown change_kind, or fix lock(s) with no merge before their reported_at`; `no merge read`.
3. **Release mode** is today's reading with no change in what it reads or counts; it reads no merge.
4. **What the report says.**
   - **`--json`:** the `dora` line's `git` object gains three keys, on every `dora` line, so it reads in byte order `default_branch`, `deploy`, `deploy_source`, `merges`, `reason`, `release_tags`, `releases`:
     - `deploy`: `"merge"` or `"release"`, the mode used. With git evidence unavailable (any of item 76's `null` reasons), the configured value when `[report] deploy` is set and valid, else `null`; with an invalid `[report]`, `null`.
     - `deploy_source`: `"config"` when `[report] deploy` is set and valid (also with git unavailable); `"fallback"` when the key is absent and no release tag is on the default branch; `"default"` when the key is absent and at least one is; `null` with git unavailable and no key, or with an invalid `[report]`.
     - `merges`: in merge mode, the number of merges on the line (the repository total, whatever `--since` says, as `releases` is); otherwise `null`.
     - `releases` keeps its meaning in both modes: the release tags on the default branch (so `0` in the fallback). Every metric object keeps its keys; `deploys.value` keeps `count`, `first`, `last`, `per_day`.
   - **Text**, in merge mode only (release mode and the git-unavailable line stay byte for byte):
     - the coverage line reads `git: default branch <ref>; <n> merge(s)<since>; lock rows without log times come from the branch tip; release tags: <tags>; deploys are merges to the default branch (<why>)`, where `<n>` is `deploys.n` (the merges read), `<since>` and `<tags>` are item 76's and item 99's, and `<why>` is `no release tag read` in the fallback or `[report] deploy = "merge"` with the key;
     - the deploys line reads `deploys: <count> merge(s), first at <time>, last at <time>, per day <x>` with item 83's forms and item 76's tail, or `deploys: -` with the tail when empty.
5. **Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
   - `report` reads item 76's git evidence and counts release tags only; a project with no release tag gets every DORA metric at `n 0`. Change: point 2's merge mode, chosen by point 1.
   - `[report]` accepts only `release_tags`; any other key is refused `config-invalid` (`unknown key <key>`) wherever the config is validated, and makes `report`'s git-derived values `null`. Change: `deploy` is accepted (point 1); every other key: keep.
   - The `dora` line's `git` object has four keys. Change: three more (point 4). The metric objects, the text block's run-log line, the git-unavailable line and the release-mode lines: keep.
   - `report --since`: grammar check before git, `not a release tag` exit 2, `since` kept when git is unavailable. Keep, in both modes.
   - `red --fixes-release`'s selection check and its refusal: keep (point 1).
   - `report`'s lock rows (state, `change_kind`, `reported_at`, `fixes_release`), the day rows, size, reds, review, checks, merged-PR, week and limit lines, the `--github` enrichment and the `--findings` ledger: keep. Change: in merge mode a lock's fix data sources are point 2's, so a lock row's `change_kind`, `reported_at` and `fixes_release` can differ from release mode for a lock with no valid file at the default branch's commit (its shipping merge's file comes before HEAD's, and the release tags are not sources). With a valid file at the default branch's commit, nothing changes.
   - `report` writes nothing, appends no run-log row, runs no suite, agent or network call without `--github`, and exits 0 except for its argument and `--since` refusals. Keep.
   - Validation order, on every path: the `deploy` check is the last check inside item 99's `[report]` check and runs wherever and whenever that check runs today; no other check moves. So `Config::parse`'s order (item 99's `[report]` before item 102's `[review] guide` and item 122's `[findings]`), and the hook's and `check`'s early `[review] after` and `[review] guide` checks on every present staged config (item 102), stay as they are: keep. `check` and the hook parse the staged config only when it differs from the parent commit's or a lock binds the commit; an unchanged config with no binding lock is not validated: keep, so an invalid `deploy` already on the branch does not refuse such a commit, as an invalid `release_tags` does not today. `report --findings` reads only `[findings]` and ignores an invalid `[report]` (`v1-122.control-gh`): keep.
   - The hook's and `check`'s steps before the staged config is parsed: the outside-index warning (item 28), and with `--commit-msg` the removal of the previous close mark (item 17), whose failure refuses `mark-failed` first and which is not rolled back by a later config refusal: keep. An invalid `deploy` refuses after them, as an invalid `release_tags` does today.
   - `red --fix --fixes-release <tag>`'s selection check exits 2 with its three lines before `red` opens the repository, appends no row and writes nothing (item 105): keep, also when the selection is invalid through `deploy`.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists (run-log rows of the commands the cases run, locks, the close commit, `TMPDIR`): keep.

**Known limits.** A merge's time is its committer date on the default branch, which for a squash or merge through GitHub is the merge time, but for a rebased or cherry-picked commit may be older. A commit pushed straight to the branch counts as a deploy, and so does the root commit. Merge mode attributes a defect to the merge that was live when it was reported, not to the change that caused it; a project that wants the exact link tags its releases and stays in release mode. `--since` takes only a release tag, so a project with no tag cannot narrow the window, and Draken's and draken-platform's rework rate and change failure rate stay `null` while their pre-item-75 locks (format 2 and 3, no `change_kind`) count as `missing`; a `--since <merge>` is a separate item. The `config-invalid` refusal's `Next:` line still says `release_tags` is the only key of `[report]`.

**Metric:** on Draken and draken-platform, with no config change, `report`'s `deploys` and `lead_time` have `n` above 0 and `missing` 0, read once on the release day outside the battery, and the numbers go in the item's metrics row. Revert: `[report] deploy = "release"` restores today's reading for a project; a later release can drop the fallback and the key.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.68.0 (item 123 takes 2.67.0): `v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest.
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them.** Measured on 2026-11-18 with a throwaway build that counted first-parent commits as deploys when no release tag is read: 854 of the 858 cases of items 38, 46, 75, 76, 79, 83, 99, 105, 107, 109, 112, 113, 114, 120 and 121 passed; the four that failed are the no-tag fixtures below.
  - `v1-76.no-releases` (`acceptance/lib/item76.sh`, the case that deletes every tag and asserts every metric `n 0`), and item 83's byte snapshots `v1-83.json-local`, `json-metrics` and `json-fraction` (`acceptance/lib/item83.sh`): each fixture writes `[report]\ndeploy = "release"` to the fixture's `.shipcraft.toml` (creating it when absent; item 83's and item 76's fixtures have none, so no other key is read), so they keep testing release mode with no tag. Their metric bodies, reasons and text stay byte for byte; the `git` object gains `"deploy":"release","deploy_source":"config","merges":null` between `default_branch` and `reason`.
  - `v1-83.json-release` and `json-github`: the `git` object gains `"deploy":"release","deploy_source":"default","merges":null` (their fixtures have release tags); nothing else changes.
  - `v1-99.coverage-default` (`acceptance/lib/item99.sh`, `list(d['git'])`): the key list becomes `['default_branch','deploy','deploy_source','merges','reason','release_tags','releases']`. Any other item 99 case that compares the whole `git` object or its key list changes the same way, and only that way; the RED lists each.
  - `acceptance/pontos/83.md` and `acceptance/pontos/99.md`: their frozen `git` object spellings follow the snapshots above.
  - `v1-120.control-no-merged` (`acceptance/lib/item120.sh`): its JSON golden SHA-256 is of an output whose `dora` line gains `"deploy":"release","deploy_source":"default","merges":null` (its fixture has two release tags); the text golden hash and every other assertion stay. Found by the PR's CI after the lock closed (the throwaway build above did not add the keys).
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every other case of items 38, 76, 79, 83, 99, 105, 107, 109, 112, 113, 114, 120 and 121 (measured above), item 46's `v1-46.report-zero` and `report-count`, `v1-87.report`, `v1-108.report`, and item 75's cases;
  - item 102's `v1-102.precedence` (an invalid `[report]` with an invalid guide, through `check`, `status`, `ci`, `review`, `review --pr`, `pr` and `merge`), item 17's `v1-17.recusa-limpa-marca` and `v1-17.marca-falha`, and item 28's `v1-28.pre-recusa` and `msg-recusa`;
  - item 122's `v1-122.control-precedence` (an invalid `[report]` still wins over an invalid `[findings]`) and `v1-122.control-gh` (`report --findings` ignores an invalid `[report]`);
  - item 43's lock validity and seal cases (`report` reads locks through the same reader);
  - item 31's staged-config cases `v1-31.1-hook-indice-invalido-arvore-valida` and `v1-31.1-hook-indice-valido-arvore-invalida`, and the config refusal cases of `acceptance/lib/english.sh` (item 99's refusal text is unchanged);
  - item 116's structural cases: `nucleo/src/relatorio/` keeps exactly its files, each at most 600 lines; `relatorio/tests.rs` is at 586 lines, so new unit tests go in another file of the folder (a `#[cfg(test)]` module inside it, with names that do not repeat item 116's test names);
  - item 118's structural cases: `nucleo/src/config/` keeps its ten files, and `config/tests.rs` its 11 tests.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item124.sh`, with real git fixture repositories (as item 76's and item 83's), never the network. Estimate: point 1, 7 (`deploy = "merge"` and `"release"` accepted by `check` on a staged config; each invalid value through `check` (a string variant, a non-string); the precedence of an invalid `release_tags`; one `status` refusal; `red --fix --fixes-release` with a valid `deploy` key and a tag outside the selection still refused as item 105; `report` with an invalid `deploy` giving `null` evidence); point 2, 15 (the fallback with no tag; the fallback with a tag outside the selection; `deploy = "merge"` with release tags; `deploy = "release"` with no tag; a merge-commit line whose side commits are not counted; a squash line; the root commit ships its locks; a lock removed after its merge still counts; fix data from the shipping merge; `--since` in the fallback exits 2; `--since` with `deploy = "merge"` and tags; a git failure in the merge read; a signed commit with `log.showSignature=true` and a recording `gpg.program` never started; a shallow and a partial clone keep item 76's reasons); point 2's metrics, 8 (lead time; rework rate; time to recover; change failure rate naming the live merge; a `reported_at` before every merge; a `reported_at` after the fix's own merge; an unknown `change_kind`; `fixes_release` not read in merge mode); point 4, 6 (the three `git` keys in each mode and source; the git-unavailable keys with and without the key; the merge-mode text lines; release-mode text byte for byte); 2 controls (a release-mode fixture with tags reads as today; `deploy = "release"` with tags reads as with no key). The `check` and `status` cases run with no open lock, and each `check` case stages a `.shipcraft.toml` that differs from the parent commit's. Cap: 40.

**Open, no case.** The GREEN may read the merges with a fixed number of git calls whatever their number (for example one `git log --first-parent` for the commits and times and one for the lock files each commit adds against its first parent), rather than one `ls-tree` per merge; no case pins the count. The cases pin only the output.

### `report --since` starts from a merge in merge mode (item 125)

Item 124 (2.68.0) counts each merge to the default branch as a deploy when a project reads no release tag. On 2026-11-18, with no config change, Draken and draken-platform read deploys and lead time, but rework rate and change failure rate stay empty: 79 and 100 of their shipped locks predate item 75 (lock formats 2 and 3, no `change_kind`), so both metrics have `missing` above 0 and `value` `null`. Item 124's known limits name the way out: `--since` takes only a release tag, so a project with no tag cannot start the window after those locks. This item lets `report --since` name a merge in merge mode, and makes the text say which merge would fill the two metrics. It is a reading only: no run-log row or field, lock field, config key, exit code or status changes, and every refusal `report` prints today is printed for the same input, byte for byte, except where point 1 accepts a value that today's grammar refuses.

1. **What `--since` takes.** A *merge id* is a string of 7 to 64 characters, each a lowercase ASCII hex digit (`0`-`9`, `a`-`f`); it names a merge when it is a prefix of exactly one merge's full commit id (item 124's merges: the commits of the default branch's first-parent line; the full id is the `%H` item 124's merge read already takes from git, with item 124's reader unchanged, so a SHA-1 and a SHA-256 repository both work and no new validation of git's output is added). A merge id is checked as git prints ids; `report` never asks git to resolve it (no `rev-parse`, no refs, no `HEAD~1`, no uppercase).
   - **The grammar check before git** (item 76 and item 99, in `main`, before any git read) accepts a value when today's check accepts it (the release grammar, or any value under an invalid `[report]`), or when it is a merge id and `[report] deploy` is not set to `"release"` (absent, or `"merge"`). Any other value: today's usage refusal, byte for byte, exit as today, before any git read. So with `deploy = "release"` nothing changes.
   - **After today's reads** (item 124 point 2's order: the tags, their trees, the default branch's and HEAD's trees and their lock files), with git evidence:
     1. a value that names a release tag on the default branch starts the window at that tag, in both modes, exactly as today (the merges read are those whose time is at or after the tag's time);
     2. otherwise, in merge mode, a merge id: the merges are read (item 124's merge reads); a failure there gives item 76's `null` evidence with `since` kept, exit 0, as item 124 says for any merge-read failure; then, when the id names a merge, the window starts at that merge: the merges read are that merge and every later merge on the line, in line order (not by time). The window filters only which merges the metrics count, as `--since <tag>` does: which merge ships a lock and which merge a fix names (item 124's `live_merge`) are read on the whole line, so a fix shipped inside the window that names a merge before it is neither counted nor missing, as item 124 scopes a named merge before `--since`; when it names none (no merge has it as a prefix, or more than one has), `report: --since <value> is not a merge on <default branch>`, exit 2, nothing on stdout, before any `--github` call;
     3. otherwise, a value of the release grammar: today's `report: --since <value> is not a release tag on <default branch>`, exit 2, before the merge reads (item 124's order kept);
     4. otherwise (release mode, and a merge id that is not of the release grammar): today's usage refusal, byte for byte, exit as today; nothing on stdout.
   - **With git evidence unavailable before the merge reads** (any of item 76's `null` reasons from today's reads, an invalid `[report]` included; a failure of the merge reads in step 2 is not this case: once merge mode is read and step 2 has started, a merge-read failure is always `null` evidence with `since` kept, exit 0, in the fallback too, and `--github` runs after it as it does after any `null` evidence today): `since` kept, every git-derived value `null`, exit 0, as today, when the value is of the release grammar, or `[report]` is invalid, or `[report] deploy = "merge"` is set; otherwise (a merge id outside the release grammar, with no `deploy` key) today's usage refusal, byte for byte. So every input refused today with git unavailable is still refused.
   - So with a merge id the refusal for a value that names no merge moves: in merge mode, a well-formed merge id that today gets the usage refusal (it is outside the default `vX.Y.Z` grammar) now gets `is not a merge on <branch>`, exit 2; this is the only refusal text change besides the one in point 3.
   - The JSON `since` and the text `, since <value>` keep the value as given (an abbreviated id stays abbreviated). `--since` scopes only the DORA metrics, as today: lock rows, day rows and every other line are unchanged. The `git` object's `merges` and `releases` stay repository totals.
2. **Which merge fills the two metrics** (text only, merge mode only, with git evidence). A *missing merge* is a merge on the whole line (whatever `--since` says) that ships a lock of the kind `change_failure_rate` counts in `missing` (item 124: a shipped lock with `change_kind: null`, or a shipped `fix` lock with no `reported_at` or whose `reported_at` names no merge). Whether a merge is missing does not depend on the window (a lock's shipping merge, its `change_kind` and the merge its `reported_at` names are all read on the whole line). This set holds every merge whose locks make `rework_rate`'s `missing` above 0 too. When `change_failure_rate`'s `missing` is above 0 (in the window), one line follows the `change failure rate:` line. With `<last>` the full commit id of the latest missing merge on the whole line, in line order:
   - when a merge follows `<last>` on the line, with `<next>` its full commit id: `to fill rework rate and change failure rate: report --since <next> (the first merge after <last>, the last merge that ships a lock counted missing)`;
   - when `<last>` is the default branch's commit: `to fill rework rate and change failure rate: no merge after <last>, the last merge that ships a lock counted missing`.
   The line is printed whatever the window, and names the same merges with or without `--since` (by tag or by merge). From `<next>`, no merge on the rest of the line ships a lock counted missing, so both metrics have `missing` 0; their `value` may still be `null` for item 124's other reasons (`no merge read ships a lock`). In release mode, with `missing` 0, or with git unavailable, no line is added. `--json` gains nothing: no key, no line; for inputs accepted today every `--json` line is byte for byte; with a merge id, only the `dora` line's metric values and `since` reflect the window.
3. **Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
   - `report --since <value>`: syntax check, then the release grammar before git (usage refusal otherwise), then today's reads, then the release-tag membership check (`is not a release tag on <branch>`, exit 2), then in merge mode the merge reads. Change: point 1's merge id, in merge mode only; every other value and mode: keep, byte for byte.
   - `--since <tag>` in merge mode (item 124, `v1-124.since` and `since-empty`): the window by the tag's time. Keep.
   - In the fallback (no release tag), every `--since` value exits 2 (`v1-124.control-since`, a `v9.0.0` value). Keep for every value that is not a merge id naming a merge.
   - Change, the one refusal text that moves: in merge mode, a value that is both a merge id and of the release grammar (item 99's valid patterns such as `a*` or `*f` can match hex; `*` alone is invalid by item 99) and names no release tag goes through point 1's step 2, so it is refused `is not a merge` after the merge reads instead of `is not a release tag` before them.
   - With git unavailable, a value of the release grammar keeps `since` and exits 0 (`v1-124.unavailable-config`, item 76's `since-partial` and `since-unavailable`). Keep.
   - The text DORA block: run-log line, coverage line, the five metric lines, in that order, then the lines after the block. Change: point 2's line after `change failure rate:`, in merge mode with `missing` above 0. Release mode, the git-unavailable block and every `--json` line: keep, byte for byte.
   - `report` writes nothing, appends no run-log row, runs no suite, agent or network call without `--github`, and its only non-zero exits are its argument and `--since` refusals (exit 2). Keep.
   - Lock rows and git evidence are all or nothing: `Facts` is built only once every read (merge reads included) succeeds; on any git failure the lock rows come from the run log alone, and the `--github` enrichment, the gaps and the line order follow as today. Keep: a merge-read failure after a merge id behaves as item 124's merge-read failure.
   - `main` removes `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` from the environment before dispatch, so `report`'s git and `gh` children never see them. Keep.
   - `report --findings --since <pr>` (item 121) reads a PR number and never this check. Keep.
   - `red --fix --fixes-release <tag>` (item 105) takes a release tag only. Keep.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists (run-log rows of the commands the cases run, locks, the close commit, `TMPDIR`): keep.

**Known limits.** A merge id is a commit id prefix only: a branch name, a tag outside the release grammar or a relative ref (`main~3`) is not resolved. A window that starts at a merge counts by line order, while a window that starts at a tag counts by time (item 124's `--since <tag>`), so on a rebased line whose times are not monotonic the two can differ. The fill line names the merge after the last missing one, which may leave few merges in the window on a project whose missing locks are recent. Release mode gets no fill line: a release project fills these metrics with `--since <tag>` as today. In merge mode a release tag wins over a merge id (point 1's order), so if a release tag were named exactly like `<next>`'s full id, `--since <next>` would start at that tag; the line does not check for it.

**Metric:** on Draken and draken-platform, with no config change, `report --since <next>` (the merge point 2 names) gives `rework_rate` and `change_failure_rate` with `missing` 0, read once on the release day outside the battery (read only, never writing to those repositories), and the numbers, with the window's merge count, go in the item's metrics row. Revert: drop the merge id from `--since` and the fill line in a later release; nothing else depends on them.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.69.0: `v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest.
- **Assertions that change:** none found. The spec review searched `acceptance/` for a case that requires a merge-mode DORA text block with `missing` above 0 to end at its five metric lines and found none (`v1-38.text` checks the opening line, `v1-120.control-no-merged` has release tags, item 123 compares two outputs of the same binary, item 124's `fallback` and `merge-tags` check substrings). The RED confirms with its own search (rule 11); if it finds one, it reports it before `red` instead of editing it.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every case of item 124 except the text assertions above, in particular `v1-124.control-since`, `since`, `since-empty`, `unavailable-config` and `invalid-report`;
  - item 76's `since`, `since-order`, `since-empty`, `since-unavailable`, `since-not-release`, `since-offline-tag`, `since-partial`, `since-text-coverage` and the `arguments-*` cases (usage refusals byte for byte);
  - item 99's `--since` cases `v1-99.since-release`, `since-missing`, `since-offbranch`, `since-mismatch`, its config-reader fallbacks `v1-99.fallback-malformed`, `fallback-utf8`, `fallback-directory`, `fallback-empty`, `fallback-unrelated` (`--since deploy-a` refused `usage:`, exit 2, with the default release reading), and `v1-99.invalid-since` (any value passes the grammar under an invalid `[report]`, `null` evidence);
