- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item125.sh`, with real git fixture repositories (item 124's helpers as a model, copied, not shared), never the network. Estimate: point 1, 16 (a full id; an abbreviated id of 7; a 64-character id that names none; an id naming a side commit of a merge-commit line, refused; an ambiguous prefix, refused; an uppercase id, usage refusal; a 6-character id, usage refusal (both under the default `vX.Y.Z` grammar); the window by line order on a line whose times are not monotonic; `--since <tag>` in merge mode unchanged; a merge id with `deploy = "release"`, usage refusal before git (a recording git never started); a merge id in release mode by default (tags present), usage refusal after the reads; a hex tag name that is also a merge id, under `release_tags = "a*"`, read as the tag; a merge-read failure after a merge id, `null` evidence, exit 0; git unavailable with no key, usage refusal; git unavailable with `deploy = "merge"`, `since` kept; the `is not a merge` refusal before any `gh` call with `--github`); point 2, 10 (the line with a following merge; with the last missing merge at the tip; a fix with no `reported_at` makes its merge missing; an unknown `change_kind` in the middle of the line; with `--since` before and after the last missing merge; `missing` 0 prints no line; release mode prints no line; `--json` unchanged by byte; `--since <next>` gives both metrics `missing` 0); 4 controls (a merge-mode fixture with `missing` 0 reads as today; `--since <tag>` in merge mode reads as today; a release-mode fixture's text byte for byte; the usage refusal of a non-id value byte for byte). The text cases compare whole lines. Cap: 40.

**Open, no case.** The GREEN may match the id against the merges it already reads (one pass over the line); no case pins the git calls.

### `report --findings` reads numbered and Portuguese Blocking headings (item 126)

Wrong-verdict note. Introduced in v2.65.0 (item 121, `nucleo/src/findings.rs`'s `heading`; item 122 kept it). Found on 2026-11-18 by the Hands-off PM and lead engineer: the ledger the RED reads held 5 of handsoff PR 164's 10 Blocking findings, because rounds 1 and 4 of that review number their headings (`**1. Title**`, `## 1. Title`), and Draken's reviews write `Bloqueante N` and `Bloqueio N` with `**Local:**`. Item 121's spec review should have caught it: it checked the grammar on three recorded PRs only (handsoff 152 and 160, draken 13), all in the `Blocking N` form, and its known limits named "another form" as a gap without measuring how many findings it lost. POC-050's script was fixed first (shipcraft-v2 PR 226, merge `8706785`); this item fixes the product the same way.

**Reproduction, on main (2.68.0), read only:** in `~/projects/handsoff`, `shipcraft report --findings --since 164` exits 0, prints 5 findings, all `(PR 164)`, and on stderr:
```
report: PR 164: the review says Blocking: 3, 0 Blocking heading(s) read
report: PR 164: the review says Blocking: 2, 0 Blocking heading(s) read
```
The two rounds it reads nothing from are the numbered ones. Over every merged PR on 2026-11-18, the fixed POC script reads 258 findings in Hands-off and 135 in Draken, with no count mismatch.

Everything in items 121 and 122 holds unless a point below changes it: the arguments, the calls, which comments are review comments, line splitting, the classes, the order, the `--json` objects, the not-read results and the exit code.

1. **The heading forms.** Item 121 point 3's *Blocking heading* gains forms. A line (after item 121's line splitting) is a **candidate heading** when it is one of these, starting at column 0, where `<word>` is `Blocking`, `Bloqueante` or `Bloqueio` (case-sensitive), `<n>` is 1 or more ASCII digits not followed by another digit, `<sep>` is one of `:`, `-`, `—` (U+2014) or `–` (U+2013), and `<rest>` is the rest of the line:
   - **Named, numbered** (item 121's forms, with the new words): 2 to 4 `#`, one or more spaces, `<word>`, one or more spaces, `<n>`, `<rest>`; or `**<word>`, one or more spaces, `<n>`, `<rest>`, `**` ending the line, with no `*` in `<rest>`. (`### Bloqueio 1 — Prévia`, `## Bloqueante 2: Título`, `**Bloqueio 3**`.)
   - **Named, unnumbered:** 2 to 4 `#`, one or more spaces, `<word>`, zero or more spaces, `<sep>`, `<rest>`; or `**<word>`, zero or more spaces, `<sep>`, `<rest>`, `**` ending the line, with no `*` in `<rest>`. It is a candidate only when `<sep><rest>`, with item 121's leading and trailing characters removed, is not empty (checked before `(no title)` would be substituted, so this form never gives `(no title)`). (`## Blocking: concurrent discard`, `### Blocking — Guest can pin`, handsoff PRs 20 and 27; `**Blocking:**` and `## Blocking findings` stay non-headings, as item 121's `heading-reject` case has them.)
   - **Numbered:** 2 to 4 `#`, one or more spaces, `<n>`, `.`, one space, `<rest>`; or `**<n>.`, one space, `<rest>`, `**` ending the line, with no `*` in `<rest>`. (`## 1. Live demotion permanently deletes a paragraph`, `**1. Search results cross the live identity boundary**`.) `## 1.Title`, `**1)** x` and `1. Title` are not candidates.

   A line that is a named form is never read as a numbered one. **The title** of a candidate is item 121's title rule applied to what follows `<n>` (named numbered), to `<sep><rest>` (named unnumbered), or to what follows `<n>.` (numbered): leading spaces, tabs, `:`, `.`, `-`, `—`, `–` removed, trailing spaces and tabs removed, `(no title)` when empty (which the named numbered and the numbered forms can give: `**Bloqueio 4**` and `**1. **` are `(no title)`, as `**Blocking 4**` is today; `## 1. ` is not a candidate, since item 121's line splitting removes its trailing space first).
   - **The location** gains two prefixes: `Local:` and `**Local:**` (case-sensitive), beside item 121's four, read the same way. A finding's text now ends at the next candidate heading of the comment (kept or not, point 2), at the end line (point 2), or at the comment's end. (`**Localização:**`, which draken PR 63 writes once, is not read: a known limit.)
2. **Which candidates are findings.**
   - **The end line.** The first line of the comment that matches, with the `regex` crate's default Unicode semantics, `(?i)^(?:#{2,4} +(?:Notes\b|Notas\b|Own checks\b|Non-blocking\b|Não bloqueante)|\*\*(?:Notes\b|Notas\b|Own checks\b|Non-blocking\b|Não bloqueante)[^*]*\*\*)` is the comment's end line (a heading that starts with one of the keywords, or a line that starts with a bold span that starts with one and closes; `**Notes service down` with no closing `**` is not one): no line from it on is a candidate (`## Notes — non-blocking`, `### Notas não bloqueantes`, `**Notes:** No additional non-blocking findings.`, `**Own checks**`, `### Notes — não bloqueantes`). A comment with no such line is read to its end, as today. The end line applies to all forms, item 121's included: a `## Blocking 1` heading after `## Notes` is no longer a finding.
   - **The cap.** The *declared count* `<d>` is item 121's (the first line that is exactly `Blocking: <d>`, 1 to 19 ASCII digits, anywhere in the comment, before or after the end line). Every named candidate (point 1's first two forms) before the end line is a finding, as item 121's headings are, whatever `<d>` says. The numbered candidates before the end line are findings, all of them, only when all three hold: the comment has a declared count, it has no named candidate before the end line, and the number of its numbered candidates equals `<d>`. Otherwise none of them is a finding. So a comment never mixes the two kinds, a numbered list of steps beside numbered findings reads no numbered finding (and warns, point 3 marks it), and a comment with no `Blocking:` line reads no numbered candidate. This is stricter than the POC script, which takes the first `<d>`: on Hands-off's and Draken's merged PRs of 2026-11-18 no review comment has numbered candidates beside named ones or a number of them other than its `<d>`, so the two read the same findings there, and where they would differ the product warns instead of guessing which numbered headings are findings.
   - **The count check** is item 121's, unchanged in text and condition, with `<k>` now this point's findings: `report: PR <n>: the review says Blocking: <d>, <k> Blocking heading(s) read` on stderr when `<k>` differs from `<d>`, the findings kept. So a review that declares 3 and names 4 still warns with `4` and keeps 4 (item 121's `v1-121.count`), and a review that declares 3 and numbers 5 reads none of the 5 and warns with `0`.
3. **The count mismatch in the ledger, and the exit code.**
   - **Text** (without `--json`): when at least one review comment's count check warns (point 2), one line follows item 121's header and its `Not read:` line (when there is one): `Count mismatch: PR <n> (Blocking: <d>, <k> read), PR <m> (Blocking: <d>, <k> read).`, one entry per warning comment in reading order (largest PR first, then the comments' order; a PR with two warning comments has two entries), `<d>` as written, joined by `, `, followed by an empty line. Then the class sections as today. So a ledger saved to `docs/review-findings.md` says in the file, where the RED reads it, that it is incomplete. With no warning, the text is byte for byte what item 122 prints.
   - **`--json`** gains nothing: no key and no line. The stderr warning stays its signal, as it is today: a caller of `--json` that needs to know the ledger is complete reads stderr for `report: PR <n>: the review says Blocking:` lines (the documented contract, not an inference). "Byte for byte" for `--json` here means the same objects, keys, values and line order as item 122; object key order stays outside the contract, as item 121 says. (Item 121's `v1-121.comments-failure`, `heading-reject` and `count` compare `--json` lines whole with a mismatching comment, so a JSON line would change three locked cases for a reader that already has stderr.)
   - **The exit code stays 0** in every case of items 121 and 122, a mismatch included. Settled here (the POC script exits 1 on an undercount): `report` is a reading whose only non-zero exits are argument refusals; a partial read already exits 0 with its `Not read:` marker in the file (item 121 point 5), and a count mismatch is the same kind of partial read, so it gets the same treatment, a marker in the file. An exit 1 would make an adopter's `shipcraft report --findings > docs/review-findings.md` step fail under `set -e` after writing a ledger that is still the best one available, and would change the exit contract item 121 locked (`v1-121.count`, `heading-reject` and `comments-failure` assert exit 0 with a mismatch).

**Known limits.** The end line is a keyword match: a finding whose own text has a line `**Notes:** ...`, or a `## Notes ...` heading before the findings, ends the comment early; the count check warns when the comment has a declared count, and nothing signals it when it has none. A named heading quoted inside a fenced code block counts (item 121's limit), so a comment that quotes one and numbers its real finding reads the quoted one, and with `Blocking: 1` the count agrees and nothing warns. Other words and forms (`Bloqueador`, `Achado 1`, `**Localização:**`) are not read. A comment's declared count is trusted as written. With `--json`, a mismatch is on stderr only.

**Metric:** on the release day, read only, outside the battery: `report --findings` and the POC script, run the same hour on `~/projects/handsoff` and `~/projects/draken`, give the same number of findings (258 and 135 on 2026-11-18; new merges since change both alike) and neither prints a count mismatch; `--since 164` in Hands-off gives PR 164's 10 findings with no warning. A difference is explained by hand before the row is written (the script reads one comments page per PR; the product paginates). The recorded fixtures, not this live read, are the reproducible oracle. The counts go in the item's metrics row. Revert: a later release that drops the new forms; nothing is written by this item.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.69.1 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change:** none found. Item 121's `heading-reject` lines (`## Blocking findings`, `**Blocking:**`, `## Blocking ١ Six`, `## Blocking1 Seven`, `## Blocking\t9 Nine` and the others) are none of point 1's forms; no item 121 or item 122 case has an end-line keyword before a heading, a numbered heading, a `Bloque` word or a `Local:` line; the only cases with a mismatching count (`v1-121.count`, `heading-reject`, `comments-failure`) run `--json`, which gains nothing; the text cases (`v1-121.text`, `empty`, `partial`, `all-unread`, item 122's text cases) have no mismatching comment. The RED confirms with its own search (rule 11); if it finds one, it reports it before `red` instead of editing it.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every case of item 121 (`acceptance/lib/item121.sh`), including `headings`, `heading-reject`, `title`, `location`, `location-empty`, `lines`, `count`, `count-invalid`, `pages`, `comments-failure`, `recorded152`, `recorded160`, `recorded13` (their recorded bodies read the same findings: their `### Notes` headings come after the last finding), `live` and `control-live`;
  - every case of item 122 (`acceptance/lib/item122.sh`), text and `--json`;
  - item 116's, item 118's and item 119's structural cases: the change stays in `nucleo/src/findings.rs` (no file under `nucleo/src/relatorio/`, `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/` or `nucleo/src/config/` changes), every file at most 600 lines;
  - the review comment as `pr` and `review --pr` post it, and their brief (`nucleo/src/brief_after_ci.md`): unchanged; this item only reads. The producer's cases keep passing as they are: item 63's `v1-63.brief-review-pr`, `brief-review-pr-spoof`, `publish-fails-review-pr`, `reuse-review-pr`; item 82's `v1-82.brief-review-pr`, `two-review-pr`, `publish-review-pr`; item 87's `v1-87.retry-review-pr`; no change to the producer or its brief makes a case of this item pass.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item126.sh`, with item 121's fixture repository and fake `gh` (item 121's helpers may be copied, not changed), never the network. Recorded bodies, supplied by the coordinator in item 121's trimmed form (first line, verdict line, `Blocking:` line, headings, bold lines, location lines and end lines; other comments `(other comment)`), go in `acceptance/fixtures/item126/` unchanged: `handsoff-164.json` (5 review comments: `**N. Title**` with a `**Notes:**` end line, `## Blocking N —`, `### Blocking N —`, `## N. Title`, and an APPROVE; 10 findings), `draken-73.json` (`### Bloqueante 1 —` after a `### Comandos` heading, `**Local:**`; 1), `draken-62.json` (`### Bloqueio 1 —` and two `### Blocking 1 —` with `**Local:**`, end lines `### Notas não bloqueantes` and `### Notes — não bloqueantes`; 3) and `draken-11.json` (`### Blocking 1 —` with `**Location:**` and `### Notes`, `## Bloqueio 1 —` after `## Suíte` with `**Local:**` and `## Notas não bloqueantes`; 2). Their expected `--json` oracles are written by the RED from this spec, as files beside them. Estimate: point 1, 12 (each named word in each form; the unnumbered form and its empty-title refusal; the numbered forms and their non-forms; titles; `Local:` and `**Local:**`; a finding's text ending at a candidate or the end line); point 2, 10 (each end-line keyword in each prefix, case-insensitive, Unicode `Não`; a `## Blocking 1` after the end line; named beside numbered reads only the named; more and fewer numbered than declared read none; no declared count; `Blocking:` after the end line; the warning text for an undercount and an overcount); point 3, 6 (the text line, its order with `Not read:`, two warning comments of one PR, no line without a warning, `--json` byte for byte with a mismatch, exit 0); recorded, 4 (one per fixture, `--json` against its oracle and the text form for handsoff 164); 2 controls (a review comment in item 121's form reads as today, text and `--json`; a numbered-only comment with no declared count reads no finding and prints no warning). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `findings.rs`'s `comment` splits a review comment's body into lines (item 121 point 3), reads every line `heading` accepts as a finding (`##`-`####` or `**`, `Blocking`, spaces, digits), its title by `title` and its location by `location` (four prefixes) from the lines up to the next heading, and warns on stderr when the first `Blocking: <d>` line differs from the headings read. Change: point 1's forms and prefixes, point 2's end line and cap; the warning text and condition: keep.
- `ledger` prints the header, the `Not read:` line when a PR's comments were not read, then the class sections; with `--json`, `pr_not_read` objects then `finding` objects. Change: point 3's text line; `--json`: keep, byte for byte.
- `report --findings` finds the root, reads `[findings]` (item 122), makes item 121's `gh` calls through the asker, writes nothing, runs no git, appends no row and exits 0 in every case of items 121 and 122. Keep. In detail, all keep, as items 121 and 122 define them: startup removes `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` before dispatch; invalid arguments print the usage error on stderr and exit 2 before discovery or any call; no root prints the not-read result without reading the config; an absent, unreadable, non-UTF-8 or non-TOML config selects the built-in classes silently, and an invalid `[findings]` prints the not-read result with no `gh` call; each `gh` call runs in the root with `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED=1`, the repository-selecting git variables removed, stdin from `/dev/null`, stderr discarded, in its own process group under the 60 s limit; a failed merged-PR list prints only the not-read result on stdout; a failed comments call prints its per-PR stderr line and the run goes on; the first timeout kills the process group and the later PRs are not read; the output is built whole and printed once on stdout; no repository write, lock, suite, check, build or temporary checkout.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: `report --findings` runs no hook and appends no row. Keep. This item's cases copy item 121's fake-`gh` setup only, not its live producer setup (`v1-121.live`'s `review --pr` run), so they meet no hook, review row, publication or review checkout.

### `report` splits the review numbers by the markers RED commits carry (item 127)

Two adopters mark their RED commits for a process trial. Since Hands-off PR 106 its RED commit messages carry a line `order cases: yes` or `order cases: no` (POC-048: did the RED lock cases on the order of concurrent events); since Hands-off PR 161 and Draken PR 104 they carry `ledger cases: <n>` (POC-050: how many cases came from the review findings ledger). Each trial's verdict compares review rounds between PRs whose RED used the new cases and PRs whose RED did not, and today that comparison needs a script outside Shipcraft. Item 120 (2.64.0) already reads each merged PR's review rounds from the run log. This item reads the marker lines of each merged PR's RED commit, adds them to item 120's `merged_pr` line, and prints item 120's review numbers per marker: PRs with the marker set, PRs with it unset, and PRs with no marker. It is a reading only: no run-log row or field, lock field, config key, refusal, exit code, status, brief or verdict changes.

1. **The RED commit of a merged PR.** For each merged PR (item 120 point 1: its merge row and its locks, read as item 120 reads them):
   - **Its first lock** is, among its locks that have a RED row, the one whose RED row has the smallest `at`; equal times keep the order of the merge row's locks. A lock's **RED row** is the one item 120's `red_at` reads, through the same reader (`evidence::red_row`, unchanged): among the rows whose `layer` is `"red"`, `result` `"passed"`, `redo` not JSON `true`, and whose locks include the lock, the one with the smallest `at` (a JSON number), equal times keeping file order; a row whose `at` is not a number is never a RED row (`v1-38.first-passed` keeps passing as it is). A PR with no lock that has a RED row has no first lock. Only the first lock is read: the later locks of a PR are the RED commits written in answer to its review rounds (Hands-off writes `review round <n>` in their subjects), and the trial's question is what the first RED did.
   - **Its parent** is the first lock's RED row's `commit` (the commit `HEAD` named when `red` wrote the row, whatever `--base` says; the RED commit is made on it) when it is a full commit id: 40 or 64 characters, each a lowercase ASCII hex digit; otherwise the PR has no parent and no RED commit.
   - **The RED commit** is a commit with exactly one parent, where that parent's full id equals the PR's parent, and that adds `.shipcraft/locks/<first lock>.json`: the path is absent from its parent's tree (no entry of any kind) and is a regular file in its tree (mode `100644` or `100755`; a file renamed into the path counts as added; a type change of an existing entry does not; a symlink or a gitlink never counts). Paths are compared as exact bytes, with no quoting or Unicode normalization: a path that is not valid UTF-8 is never a failure of the read and never matches a lock path. Commits are read as stored: git's replacement objects are not applied (`--no-replace-objects`). The commits searched are every commit reachable from `HEAD` or any ref of the repository (local branches, remote-tracking branches and tags, as `git log --all` reads them), with no history simplification, so a RED commit on a branch that a squash merge left off the default branch is still found while that branch or its remote-tracking ref exists. When several commits qualify (a squash commit made on the same parent adds the same lock file, or an amended RED is kept by another ref), the one with the smallest committer time (Unix seconds, as git prints `%ct`) is taken, then the smallest full id, whether or not the PR's merged history reaches it (a known limit below). When none qualifies (the branch is gone, the RED commit was rebased onto another parent), the PR has no RED commit.
   - **One git read for the whole run.** The RED commits come from one `git log` call (read only, through item 76's runner `Git`, in `<root>`, with its environment), whatever the number of PRs. The call starts no signature verifier, as item 76's `release_time` read does (`-c log.showSignature=false` and `--no-show-signature`; `v1-76.signed`), and prints every message with git's log output encoding set to UTF-8 (`-c i18n.logOutputEncoding=UTF-8`), so a message stored with an `encoding` header is read recoded, whatever the local configuration. Its output is framed so that no message content (any byte other than NUL, separators, tabs, lines that look like `commit <id>` or a file name) can end a message or start a record; output that does not parse as whole records, or ends inside one, is a failure of the whole read, never a parsed prefix. The call is made only when item 76's git evidence is available (and, in merge mode, item 124's merge reads succeeded), and at least one merged PR has a parent; it is made after the merge reads and `--since`'s checks and before any `--github` call. No call is made otherwise, so a log whose merged PRs have no valid `commit` in their first lock's RED row makes the same git calls as today. When the call cannot start, exits non-zero, ends by a signal or prints output that cannot be read, no PR has a RED commit read (every `markers` is `null`, below), no marker line is printed, no `gap` is added, every other line is unchanged, and the exit stays 0. A commit message that is not valid UTF-8 is read with each invalid sequence replaced by U+FFFD; it is never a failure.
2. **The markers.** The marker lines of a RED commit are read from its full message (git's `%B`: subject and body), split on `\n`, with one trailing `\r` removed from each line. A *marker line* is a whole line of the form `<word> cases: <value>`: `<word>` is 1 to 32 lowercase ASCII letters, followed by exactly one space, the text `cases:`, exactly one space, and `<value>`, 1 to 32 ASCII letters or digits, with nothing before or after (no indent, no trailing space). When a word has several marker lines in one message, the first counts. Any word is read: `ledger` and `order` are the two in use, and the reading names no key.
   - **`markers`** on item 120's `merged_pr` JSON line, after `red_to_merge`: an object from each word to its value as written (a string: `{"ledger": "11", "order": "yes"}`), `{}` when the PR's RED commit has no marker line, `null` when the PR has no RED commit (no first lock, no parent, none found, or the git read failed or was not made). Item 120's other keys and the text `pr #<n>:` line are unchanged.
   - **A marker's group** for a merged PR: `set` when its value is `yes` or a string of ASCII digits with at least one digit that is not `0` (`1`, `11`, `010`); `unset` when its value is `no` or a string of `0`s only (`0`, `00`); `none` when the PR's `markers` has no such word, is `{}` or `null`, or the value is anything else (`Yes`, `maybe`, `n2`). Values are compared case-sensitively.
3. **The marker lines.** The **window** is every merged PR when `--since` is not given. With `--since <value>` accepted (a release tag, or in merge mode a merge, by item 125 point 1), the window is the merged PRs whose item 120 `merge_at` is a number at or after the start time: the time item 76 reads for that release tag (the one `--since <tag>` starts DORA's window at), or the time item 124 reads for the named merge. A PR whose `merge_at` is `null` is outside any `--since` window. The window does not change the `merged_pr`, `week`, `size_bucket` and `limit` lines, which cover the whole run log as item 120 says. For each word that at least one PR of the window has in its `markers`, in ascending byte order of the word, three JSON objects, for the groups `set`, `unset` and `none` in that order, each over the window's merged PRs in that group, with exactly these keys:
   - `kind`: `"marker"`; `marker`: the word; `group`: `"set"`, `"unset"` or `"none"`; `since`: the `--since` value as given, `null` without it;
   - `merged_prs`, `reviewed_prs`, `round1_approved_share`, `changes_requested_per_pr`, `three_rounds` and `three_rounds_share`, each exactly as item 120 point 2 defines them for a week line (over the group's merged PRs instead of a week's; the ratios `null` when `reviewed_prs` is 0).

   A group with no PR is still printed (`merged_prs` 0). When no PR of the window has a word, no marker line is printed. The text output prints, at the same position, one line per object:
   `marker <word> cases <group>: <merged_prs> merged PR(s), <reviewed_prs> reviewed, approved on round 1 <share>, <per_pr> change requests per PR, 3 or more rounds <three_rounds> (<three share>)`
   with item 120's week-line forms for each value (whole percent, two decimals, `-` for `null`), followed by `; since <value>` when `--since` is given: `marker ledger cases set: 3 merged PR(s), 3 reviewed, approved on round 1 0%, 2.00 change requests per PR, 3 or more rounds 1 (33%); since deploy-20261118-134807`.
   - **Where the lines go.** In `--json` and in the text, after item 120's lines (after `limit`, or after the last `week` or `merged_pr` line when no PR is measured) and right before `dora` (in the text, before the `DORA` line).
4. **Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
   - `report`'s lines, in order: `meta`, `lock`, `day`, `size`, `reds`, `reds_week`, `review`, `checks` (with `--github`), item 120's `merged_pr`, `week`, `size_bucket` and `limit`, then `dora`, `github` (with `--github`) and `gap`. Change: the `marker` lines between item 120's lines and `dora`, only when a PR of the window has a marker word.
   - Item 120's `merged_pr` line has exactly ten keys. Change: it gains `markers`, on every `merged_pr` line (`null` on every fixture whose first lock's RED row has no full commit id, as every hand-written item 120 log does). Its text line: keep, byte for byte.
   - `report`'s git reads: item 76's (tags, trees, lock files), item 124's merge reads in merge mode; none without a repository or under an invalid `[report]`. Change: point 1's one `git log` call, only under point 1's condition. Lock rows, DORA and `Facts` do not read it, and its failure does not touch them (it is not item 76's all-or-nothing evidence).
   - `--since`'s grammar, its refusals (`is not a release tag on`, `is not a merge on`, the usage refusal), their order and exits (item 125 point 1): keep, byte for byte. A refused `--since` exits before point 1's call.
   - `--github`'s calls (items 79, 113 and 114) and their order: keep; point 1's call comes before them.
   - `report --findings` (items 121 and 122) reads no RED commit. Keep.
   - The `red` row: `red` writes `lock`, `result`, `redo`, `at` and `commit` (the commit it compared with). Keep: this item adds no row and no field.
   - `report` writes no file or folder, appends no run-log row, runs no suite or agent, and makes no network call without `--github`; its only non-zero exits are its argument and `--since` refusals (exit 2). Keep.
   - `main` removes `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` from the environment before dispatch. Keep.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists (run-log rows of the commands the cases run, locks, the close commit, `TMPDIR`): keep.

**Known limits.** A RED commit is found only while a ref still reaches it: on a project that merges by squash and deletes the branch and its remote-tracking ref, the PR reads as `none` (on 2026-11-18, 55 of Hands-off's 165 merged PRs in its run log, all before the trials). A RED commit rebased onto another parent after `red` ran is not found either. Only the first lock's RED commit is read, so a marker written only in a review-round RED does not count. The window compares the run log's `merge_at` with a git commit time, so a clock skew between them can move a PR merged within seconds of the start across it; item 125's merge window counts by line order instead. When several commits on the same parent add the lock (an amended RED kept by a backup ref, a squash commit with an older committer time), the earliest is read even if the PR merged another. The groups are small during a trial window: the lines report counts, not significance.

**Metric:** POC-048's and POC-050's verdicts read these lines instead of their scripts. Check once after the release, read only, never writing to those repositories: Hands-off with `--since deploy-20261118-134807` (its first release tag after PR 161's merge `db3b391`) and Draken with `--since ebb92e2` (PR 104's merge, merge mode); each marker table matches a by-hand count of the same PRs' RED commits and review rows, and the output goes in the item's metrics row. Revert: a later release that drops the `marker` lines and the `markers` key; nothing is written by this item, so nothing needs undoing.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.70.0: `v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-69.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest; they keep passing unchanged once the manifest says 2.70.0.
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else in them:**
  - item 120's shared schema check after every arm (`acceptance/lib/item120.sh`, the `for r in selected(s)` loop under `# Schemas only`): its exact key set of a `merged_pr` line gains `markers`. It runs in every item 120 case that prints a `merged_pr` line, so each of them changes there and nothing else: `v1-120.week-text`, `control-sample`, `limit-edge-grid`, `number-forms`, `control-github`, `basic`, `merge-choice`, `merge-types`, `pr-integers`, `locks`, `iso-weeks`, `floor-time`, `range-time`, `size-freeze`, `size-invalid`, `rounds`, `round-filters`, `red-selection`, `red-redo`, `nonfinite`, `reader`, `week-ratios`, `week-median`, `week-null`, `bucket-edges`, `bucket-ratios`, `limit-default`, `limit-minimum`, `limit-zero`, `limit-support`, `limit-strict`, `limit-share`, `limit-cumulative`, `limit-largest`, `order`, `since` and `later-failures`; `acceptance/pontos/120.md`'s lines that describe the exact key set (`v1-120.basic`'s `eleven-key`) are read as gaining `markers`;
  - item 120's `v1-120.basic`: its whole `merged_pr` object gains `markers=None` (its RED row's `commit` is `foreign120`, not a full id, so no RED commit is read and no git call is added, and its call-equality check stays as it is);
  - any other case that compares a whole `merged_pr` object or its exact keys: the RED searches `acceptance/` for `merged_pr` (items 79, 107, 108, 112 and 120 name it) and lists what it finds before `red`, changing only the `markers` key. Kind lists are not touched: no existing fixture's RED commit carries a marker line, so no case gains a `marker` line.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every other assertion of item 120, in particular its kind orders, `control-fixture`'s and `control-no-merged`'s byte hashes (no merged PR), and `basic`'s git-call equality;
  - `v1-38.first-passed` (the RED row is the earliest passing one by `at`, not the first in the file) and `v1-76.signed` (no signature verifier starts during `report`);
  - item 79's shared kind order (`acceptance/lib/item79.sh`, the list its cases sort by) and every case that reaches it: their logs' RED rows have no full commit id, or their RED commits have no marker line;
  - the git-call equality checks of items 107, 109, 112, 120 and 123 (`data(calls)==observed`): their RED rows carry no full commit id, so point 1 makes no call;
  - items 76, 99, 124 and 125's `--since` cases, refusals byte for byte, and their recording-git cases;
  - items 113 and 114's `gh` call lists, `v1-87.report` and `v1-108.report` (real `red`/`merge` rows: their PRs may read `markers` `{}` or `null`, and those cases read only their own lines);
  - item 83's byte snapshots and items 121 and 122's `--findings` cases;
  - item 116's structural cases: `nucleo/src/relatorio/` keeps exactly its files (no new file; the code goes in its existing modules), each at most 600 lines; `relatorio/tests.rs` is at 586 lines, so new unit tests go in another file's `#[cfg(test)]` module, with names that do not repeat earlier test names.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item127.sh`, with real git fixture repositories (item 125's helpers as a model, copied, not shared) whose RED commits are made on a recorded parent, and a run log written by hand after the fixture's commits with that parent as the RED row's `commit`, never the network. Estimate: point 1, 12 (the RED commit on a branch merged by a merge commit; on a branch a squash merge left off the line, reached by its branch ref; reached only by a tag or a remote-tracking ref; the branch deleted, `null`; a squash commit on the same parent, the earlier commit taken; the first lock by RED row time in a PR of three locks whose merge row lists them in another order; a redo row ignored; a RED row `commit` that is abbreviated, uppercase or missing, `null` and no extra git call; a parent that matches no commit; a git read failure, every `markers` `null`, no marker line, DORA unchanged, exit 0; the one call made once for many PRs; no call before a refused `--since`); point 2, 8 (two words read, `{}` with no marker line, the first of two lines for one word wins, an indented or trailing-space line ignored, a `\r\n` message, a 33-letter word or value ignored, a non-UTF-8 message read, each group's value forms `yes`, `010`, `no`, `00`, `Yes`, `maybe`); point 3, 10 (three groups per word in order, words in byte order, an empty group printed with `null` ratios, the ratios and the 3-or-more count against a by-hand count, `--since <tag>`, `--since <merge>` in merge mode, `merge_at` `null` outside the window, `since` in JSON and text, the text lines whole, the position before `dora` in both forms); 4 controls (a log with merged PRs and no marker reads as today except `markers`; a fixture with no merged PR byte for byte; `--json` kind order with markers; `--findings` unchanged). The text cases compare whole lines. Cap: 40.

**Open, no case.** The GREEN chooses the `git log` arguments (for example `--all --full-history --no-renames --diff-filter=A` with a record format and `--name-only`, filtered to `.shipcraft/locks/`), as long as point 1's set of commits is the one read; no case pins the call's arguments.

### `ci`, `pr` and `merge` say when the base has no config (item 128)

Found on 2026-11-18 by POC-051 (simulated adopters, run 2, excalidraw): the adopter's `master` had no `.shipcraft.toml` and the branch added one in its setup commit. `shipcraft ci --base master` exited 0 and ran the suite with the branch's own config, without a word. The README says "A PR is judged by the config on the base branch" and never says what happens when the base has none. The fallback itself is old and intended (2.0.0: `ci.rs`'s `Config::at(base).or(head_config)`; `Config::of_base` for `pr`, `merge` and the reviews; item 96's workflow reader "falls back to HEAD's, as today"); what is missing is that nobody is told.

**Reproduction, on main (2.70.0):** a scratch repository with one commit on `master` (no `.shipcraft.toml`), a branch whose one commit adds a `.shipcraft.toml` with one suite; `shipcraft ci --base master` prints `shipcraft: 1 commit(s) checked in <base>..HEAD`, `shipcraft: running the suite (s)`, `shipcraft: CI ok.` and the `ci publishes no status` line, and exits 0. No line says the base has no config.

**A hole or a note: a note, settled here.** The base-config rule exists so that a PR cannot switch off checks the base already has. A base with no `.shipcraft.toml` has none to switch off: Shipcraft checks nothing there yet, so the branch's config can only add checks. The way to reach a config-less base on a project that had one is a PR that deletes `.shipcraft.toml`, and that PR is judged by the base's config, which has it (item 31's `v1-31.1-ci-apaga-config`), and is reviewed as a config change. A refusal would block the first install PR of every adopter, which has no other config to be judged by. So the fallback stays, and this item makes it visible and documents it. Out of scope: which config "config changed" names in `red` (POC-051 queue item 1, its own item), and `review` and `review --pr`, which read `Config::of_base` too and stay silent (known limits).

Everything in items 1, 31, 59, 66 and 110 holds unless a point below changes it: which config judges the range, exit codes, refusals and their text, the order of the other lines, statuses, rows and files written.

1. **`ci`.** When the commit `--base` names has no `.shipcraft.toml` and HEAD has one (the case where `ci` today takes HEAD's config), `ci` prints exactly one line on stdout, before its `shipcraft: <n> commit(s) checked in <base>..HEAD` line and before any other line it prints:
   `shipcraft: the base <base> has no .shipcraft.toml, so this range is judged by HEAD's (a first install is judged by the config it adds).`
   `<base>` is the same short id the `checked in` line prints (`git.rs`'s `short`, 7 characters). The line is printed once per run, also with `--pr`, right after the config is chosen: after HEAD's config was read and validated (`Config::at(head)`, first, as today, so an invalid HEAD config refuses or fails as today with no line) and the base was found to have no `.shipcraft.toml` (`Repo::read` of the base snapshot returned no file). It is printed also when `ci` later refuses or fails (invalid lock bytes, `lock-not-closed`, `suite-red`, a build failure), even when no `checked in` line follows. When the base has a `.shipcraft.toml`, nothing is printed, byte for byte as today. When neither has one, `ci` refuses `no-config` as today, with no such line. The legacy-file check (`reject_legacy_file`, which refuses `config-invalid` with `Next: rename .sc2.toml to .shipcraft.toml` when a snapshot has `.sc2.toml` alone) runs on each snapshot before its absence is decided, as today: a base with `.sc2.toml` alone refuses as today and prints no line (`r-legacy-config-ci`). A base whose `.shipcraft.toml` cannot be read or parsed, or a git read of the base that fails (any failure `Repo::read` reports as an error, not as an absent file), fails as today, with no such line.
2. **`pr` and `merge`.** When `Config::of_base` takes the working tree's config, `pr` and `merge <n>` each print exactly one line on stdout, once per run, right after `Config::load` returned a valid config and before any later stdout line of that command (in `pr`, before `camadas()` and before a `pr-not-on-branch` refusal; in `merge`, before `camadas()` and the gate's refusals). It has two texts, by why the fallback happened, using the readers `of_base` uses today and their failure semantics:
   - `Repo::resolve("origin/<base>")` says absent (today's exit 1 of `git rev-parse -q --verify`): `shipcraft: origin/<base> is not in this clone, so this PR is judged by the working tree's .shipcraft.toml (fetch origin/<base> if it has one).`
   - the ref resolves and `Repo::read` of it returns no file: `shipcraft: origin/<base> has no .shipcraft.toml, so this PR is judged by the working tree's (a first install is judged by the config it adds).`

   `<base>` is the branch name `of_base` was given (`pr`: the default branch; `merge`: the PR's `baseRefName`). When `origin/<base>` has the file, nothing is printed, byte for byte as today. The legacy-file check runs on each snapshot `of_base` reads before absence is decided, as today: a refusal there prints no line. When the working tree has none either, or its `.shipcraft.toml` cannot be read (every case `Config::load` maps to `no-config` today) or is invalid, the refusal or failure as today, with no such line. A git failure of `resolve` or `read` other than absence fails as today, with no line, and never selects the working tree's config. `pr`'s local CI runs `shipcraft ci --base <base tip> --pr` with its stdout inherited, so on that path point 1's line also appears, from the child, where the child prints it; `pr`'s own line is still printed once. `pr --merge` prints `pr`'s line once and, when it reaches the merge, the merge's line once more (each command reads the config itself). `review` and `review --pr` do not print it. "Once" counts the lines Shipcraft itself prints in that process; a suite command that echoes the same text is not counted, and ordering is within each process's stdout (stderr diagnostics keep their place).
3. **The README.** The bullet "**A PR is judged by the config on the base branch,** not by its own. A PR can't switch off its own review." gains, after it: "When the base branch has no `.shipcraft.toml` yet (the PR that installs Shipcraft), there is nothing on the base to switch off, so another config judges the PR: `shipcraft ci` uses the one committed at the PR's HEAD, and `shipcraft pr` and `shipcraft merge` use the one in the working tree where they run. Each says so in one line." Nothing else in the README changes.

**Known limits.** `review` and `review --pr` fall back the same way and say nothing. `merge` falls back to the working tree of wherever it runs, which may not be the PR's head; this item names the fallback but does not change which tree it reads. GitHub's workflow (item 96's reader) falls back without printing this line; on GitHub, `ci`'s own line shows it. The line is information, not a gate: a branch of a project with no config on its base can still bring any config.

**Metric:** on the release, a rerun of POC-051's run-2 shape (a scratch repository, base with no config, branch adding one) prints point 1's line; no adopter (Hands-off, Draken) sees it on their PRs, whose bases have a config. Revert: a later release that drops the lines; nothing else changes.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.71.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-69.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Assertions that change:** none found by the spec's search: no case pins `ci`'s first line (`commit(s) checked` is asserted nowhere by position), and the cases whose base has no config are expected to be few (a fixture whose `ci --base` names a commit before `init`'s commit, or whose `origin/<base>` lacks the file). The RED searches `acceptance/` for `ci --base`, `shipcraft pr`, `shipcraft merge` and whole-stdout comparisons of them, lists every case whose base has no config and that compares stdout whole or by line count or position, and reports them before `red` instead of editing them (rule 11).
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - item 31's cases (`acceptance/lib/item31.sh`), `ci-apaga-config` included (base with a config, HEAD without: judged by the base, no line);
  - item 59's new-suite cases and item 66's base cases;
  - item 110's `pr --merge` cases (`acceptance/lib/item110.sh`), `changed-config` included, and items 62, 63, 73, 82, 87 and 103's `pr` and `merge` cases, whose bases have a config;
  - item 96's workflow reader cases (the workflow text is not changed);
  - item 44's CI tail and the child's position in `pr`'s output: `v1-44.{local,github,off}-{green,green-pr,docs,empty,empty-pr,red,base-pr}`, `refused`, `error`, `pr-local`, `pr-review-fails` (a leading line keeps their last success/guidance pair);
  - item 85's merge output, exits and rows: `v1-85.merge-default`, `merge-default-waiver`, `merge-other`, `merge-other-waiver`, `merge-control`, `merge-refused` (exactly three refusal lines; its base has a config), `merge-gh-fails-waiver` (exit 2, no merge row);
  - items 10 and 38's rows: `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`, `v1-38.pr-locks`, `pr-locks-many`, `pr-publication-passed`, `pr-publication-refused`, `ci-config-locks`, `ci-config-locks-many` (attribution on an invalid HEAD config stays before and without the line);
  - item 110's invalid-argument paths (empty stdout, before dispatch: no line);
  - the rename cases `r-legacy-config-ci` and `r-legacy-config-unstaged-hooks` (`acceptance/lib/rename.sh`): the legacy refusal keeps its place and text, with no line;
  - item 22's `v1-22.ambiente-ci`, `ambiente-verifica`, `ambiente-build` (`acceptance/tools/item22.py`): suites, checks and builds never receive inherited `SHIPCRAFT_CASES`/`SHIPCRAFT_FILES`;
  - `v1-5.5-ci-na-main-avisa-merges` (`acceptance/lib/v1.sh`): `ci` on main exits 0, warns only for the bypassed merge and writes its escape row;
  - item 116 to 119's structural cases: every file at most 600 lines, no new file under `nucleo/src/config/`, `nucleo/src/relatorio/` or `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/`.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item128.sh`, with real git fixture repositories and item 110's fake `gh` and bare `origin` (its helpers copied, not changed), never the network. Estimate: point 1, 7 (the line and its position before `checked in`, whole stdout of the reproduction; with `--pr`; printed once; before a later refusal, a lock left open on the branch; base with config, no line, byte for byte; neither has one, `no-config` and no line; HEAD deletes the config, no line); point 2, 8 (`pr` with `origin/<base>` lacking the file; `pr` with `origin/<base>` missing; `pr` with local CI, its own line once and the child's line; `pr` before a refusal; `merge` with the base lacking the file; `merge` with the base having it, no line; `pr --merge`, each line once; neither has a config, `no-config` and no line); point 3, 1 (the README sentence, exactly once); 2 controls (`ci` and `pr` on a base with a config, stdout as today line for line). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `ci::run` resolves `--base`, reads HEAD's config (`Config::at(head)`, which validates it), then the base's, takes the base's or else HEAD's, refuses `no-config` when neither has one, prints `shipcraft: <n> commit(s) checked in <base>..HEAD`, checks every commit, runs the build, the checks and the suite on HEAD (or the suite-checks path), and prints its result lines. Change: point 1's line when it takes HEAD's. Everything else: keep.
- Startup removes inherited `SHIPCRAFT_CASES` and `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` before dispatch. Keep.
- `ci::passed` on the default branch calls `warn_merges`: it prints bypass warnings and appends `layer: "merge", escape: true` rows for merges not yet recorded (or a warning when the GitHub query fails) and still exits 0. Keep; the line does not change it.
- `fila::enter` (local PR layers) creates `$XDG_STATE_HOME/shipcraft/queue/espera/` (or the HOME fallback), a waiter file and `slot-<i>` files, removes its waiter and unlocks the slot on drop and leaves the folders and slot files; these are outside the repository. Keep. The new cases that run a local layer set `XDG_STATE_HOME` to a folder of the case.
- `Config::of_base(repo, base)` reads `origin/<base>`'s config when that ref resolves and has the file, else `Config::load` (the working tree's, `no-config` when absent). Its callers: `pr::pr`, `merge::merge`, `pr::local`, `pr::of_pr`. Change: `pr::pr` and `merge::merge` print point 2's line when the working tree's was taken; `pr::local` and `pr::of_pr`: keep silent.
- `pr` fetches origin, reads the config, refuses `pr-not-on-branch`, pushes, opens or updates the PR, runs the layers, posts statuses and the summary, and appends its rows. `merge` views the PR, refuses `pr-not-open`, fetches the base, reads the config, checks the statuses and merges with `--match-head-commit`. Keep, except point 2's line.
- Rows: `medicao::record` appends to `runs.jsonl` in the `shipcraft` folder of the shared git directory (`.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl`, created when missing; in a linked worktree, the main repository's), the hook on every commit even with no lock, and a failed append changes no exit code. `pr` writes its PR row only once a PR number and the lock attribution exist (config, branch, push, size and create failures before that write none); `merge` writes its row only after `gh pr merge` succeeds (gate refusals and a failed merge write none). Exit 1 for refusals, 2 for failures. Keep all of it; the new line is stdout only and is in no row, status, PR body or summary comment.
- Standing effects the cases can meet: `init` writes `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, `red` writes the lock, the post-commit hook adds `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>`; suites, checks and `[build]` run with the caller's `TMPDIR`, in the tree on a clean tree and on a temporary copy (removed afterwards) on a dirty tree without `[build]`, a dirty tree with `[build]` refusing `build-dirty-tree`; JUnit reports go to a temporary file removed after reading; local PR layers take the per-machine queue slot (`fila.rs`); a local review makes and removes a temporary worktree. Keep all of it: the line changes no execution place or environment. The new cases keep the optional layers off (`ci`/`review` `off`) wherever the case does not need them, and say which fixtures run a local layer.

### `red`, `status` and `report` stop printing three false lines (item 136)

Wrong-verdict note. Three lines say something the run did not observe, each since a different release; one `--fixes-release` must cover all three, so it names the oldest (`git tag --contains <commit>`, the oldest tag of each):
- `status`'s branch protection line, since v2.0.0 (`nucleo/src/merge.rs`, commit `4f9d784`): any `gh api` error holding `404` prints `<base> has no branch protection`, and any holding `403` prints `this repository has no branch protection (GitHub free plan)`. GitHub answers `404 Not Found` (not `404 Branch not protected`) when the account is not an admin of the repository, so the line asserts an absence it never saw.
- `report`'s lock state, since v2.1.0 (item 38, commit `7afed8c`): `open` for every lock without a passed `merge` row, also when its close was observed.
- `report`'s DORA `per day`, since v2.34.0 (item 76, commit `88b79f8`): count ÷ span for any span above zero, so two merges minutes apart print thousands per day.
- `red`'s controls line, since v2.48.0 (item 98, commit `f620797`): one passing case anywhere in a suite that the mapper ties to no file or to two makes the whole suite's controls empty, so a full run prints `controls: 0` while cases of the changed test files pass.

Found on 2026-11-18 by POC-051 runs 3 (cli/cli: `controls: 0` while 29 cases of the changed files pass; `trunk has no branch protection` as a non-admin), 4 (httpx: the protection line), 5 (expense-compass: the protection line) and 6 (barbearia: `report` printed `lock ...: open; red at ..., close at ...` while `status` said no open lock, and `per day 4430.77` from two direct commits on main), recorded in the POC-051 card (`poc/051-simulated-adopters/FICHA.md`, item 7, commit `18ba6ee`, 2026-11-18T16:20-03:00). The layers that should have caught them: item 5's `status` cases, whose `gh` stub answers only `403 Upgrade to GitHub Pro` or `404 Branch not protected`, never a non-admin's `404 Not Found`; item 38's `v1-38.open` case, which pins `open` for a lock with a close row; item 76's and item 124's DORA cases, which pin a per-day rate over spans of 100 and 200 seconds; item 98's cases, which ran the controls only on fixtures where every passing case maps (and `v1-98.unmapped`, which pins the empty result as intended). A line that states what was not observed is a defect, so this is a fix (`kind = fix`, `red --fix --reported-at 2026-11-18T16:20-03:00 --fixes-release v2.0.0`; v2.0.0 is the oldest of the four releases above). Victor's decision (2026-11-18, "Pode resolver, tá?").

**Reproduction, on main (2.77.0, the shared binary):**
- (A) A copy of the POC-051 cli/cli clone (Go, gotestsum) with the RED's four files uncommitted and a base whose `.shipcraft.toml` differs from the branch's by one value, so `red` runs every suite: `red ran every suite: config changed`, then `controls: 0 passing case(s) of the changed test files`. The same files with the base config unchanged (the narrowed run): `controls: 29 ...`. The full run's 9,814 passing cases include `TestNormalizeReference` and its 8 subtests, defined in both `artifact_posix_test.go` (`//go:build !windows`) and `artifact_windows_test.go` (`//go:build windows`) of one package, which the Go mapper ties to two files.
- (B) A repository whose origin is any GitHub repository, `status` with a `gh` on `PATH` that answers the protection call with exit 1, stdout `{"message":"Not Found",...,"status":"404"}` and stderr `gh: Not Found (HTTP 404)` (what `gh api repos/cli/cli/branches/trunk/protection` answers a non-admin): `main has no branch protection: \`shipcraft merge\` is the gate`. A repository whose branch truly has no protection answers `{"message":"Branch not protected",...}` and `gh: Branch not protected (HTTP 404)`.
- (C) A local repository with no remote: two direct commits on `main` 5 seconds apart, `init`, a branch, a RED commit and a GREEN commit whose hook closes the lock. `status`: `open locks: none`. `report`: `lock <id>: open; red at ..., close at ...` and `deploys: 2 merge(s), first at ..., last at ..., per day 34560.00`.

The first RED case reproduces (B) on main: it fails on 2.77.0 because `status` prints `has no branch protection` for a `404 Not Found`.

Everything in items 5, 38, 76, 83, 98, 124 and 125 holds unless a point below changes it.

1. **`red`'s controls are held case by case, and the line says how many passing cases could not be held.** Wherever `red` or `red --redo` computes controls today (not with `--all`, not without a readable base, not on a redo's kept-controls path; item 98), a passing case of a suite is a control when the suite's mapper finds a map and that map ties the case to exactly one file, which is a changed path. The map is inferred exactly as today, once per suite from all of that suite's passing cases (item 81's `coverage_map`, its root and prefix choice, and the Go reader `go_tests`, which reads an unreadable or non-UTF-8 file as declaring no test, all unchanged); only the lookup that follows (`holder` per case) stops being all-or-nothing. An unknown pytest, vitest or jest case that prevents the inference still leaves its suite without a map, so the suite has no controls and all its passing cases count in `m`. A case the map ties to no file or to more than one (a Go test defined in two build-tagged files) is not a control, and no longer empties its suite's controls: the suite's other passing cases are judged one by one. A suite for which the mapper finds no map has no controls, as today. Let `m` be the passing cases of the suites judged that are not controls because of either of those two reasons (no map for the suite, or no single file for the case; cases held to one file that is not a changed path do not count). The controls line (printed where it is today: before item 132's optional vitest notice, which stays immediately before `RED locked at` / `RED redone at`) stays `shipcraft: controls: <n> passing case(s) of the changed test files; the hook runs them too` when `m` is 0, and becomes `shipcraft: controls: <n> passing case(s) of the changed test files; the hook runs them too; <m> passing case(s) could not be tied to a single test file, so they are not controls` when `m` is above 0. Same stream (stdout), same place. The lock's format, its `controls` list (byte order, as today) and the hook's use of it are unchanged; only which cases enter the list changes. With `--all`, without a readable base, and on the kept-controls path, the line is today's, with no `m` clause. `m` is shown only where today's line is printed (a successful write of a format 5 or 6 lock): a redo that writes format 3 (`v1-98.legacy-judge`), a refusal, an unchanged redo and a write failure print no controls line, as today.
2. **`status` says it could not read the protection when GitHub's answer does not prove there is none.** The protection call and its success path are unchanged. For a failed call, with `<e>` the error text as today (`gh`'s stderr, then its stdout, trimmed):
   - `<e>` contains `Upgrade to GitHub Pro`: today's `shipcraft: this repository has no branch protection (GitHub free plan): \`shipcraft merge\` is the gate`;
   - else `<e>` contains `Branch not protected`: today's `shipcraft: <base> has no branch protection: \`shipcraft merge\` is the gate`;
   - else `<e>` contains `HTTP 404`: `shipcraft: could not read branch protection on <base> (GitHub answers 404 when this account is not an admin of the repository, so protection may exist): use \`shipcraft merge\` as the gate`;
   - else (a `403` without the upgrade text, no remote, `gh` missing, any other failure): today's `shipcraft: could not read branch protection (<first line of <e>>): use \`shipcraft merge\` as the gate`.
   Matches are case-sensitive substrings of the whole `<e>`. Exactly one protection line, exit 0, no row, as today. `init --github`'s protection write (item 5.3) follows the same reading: only an error containing `Upgrade to GitHub Pro` prints `this repository has no branch protection on its GitHub plan: \`shipcraft merge\` is the gate.`; any other failure, a `403` included, prints today's `could not mark statuses as required (<e>); \`shipcraft merge\` is the gate.`
3. **`report`'s lock state and per-day rate say what was observed.**
   - **State.** A lock row's `state` is `merged` when it has a qualifying merge (as today: `flow::merge_row`, a passed `merge` row whose `base` equals its `default_branch`, with a readable time); else `merge_not_observed` when its lock file is at the default branch's tip (item 76, as today); else `closed` when its close was observed (`close_at` is not `null`, read as today: the earliest passed non-redo `close` row with a readable time, `passed("close")`); else `open`. The text prints the same word in the same place (`lock <id>: closed; red at ...`). `closed` locks are not counted in day rows (only `merged`, as today) and are not selected by `--github` (only `merged` and `merge_not_observed`, as today). This replaces item 38's reading "`state` (`open` or `merged`)" and item 76's "else `open`".
   - **Per day.** DORA's `deploys.value.per_day` is `null` when the time from the first to the last deploy read is under one day (86,400 seconds), as it is today with fewer than 2 deploys or a zero span; from exactly 86,400 seconds on it is today's count ÷ (span / 86,400). `count`, `first`, `last`, `n`, `missing` and `reason` are unchanged, in release and merge modes alike. The text prints `per day -` for a `null` rate as today, and, when the rate is `null` only because the span is above zero and under a day, `per day - (first to last under a day)` in its place: `deploys: 2 merge(s), first at <time>, last at <time>, per day - (first to last under a day) (n 2, missing 0)`.
   - Times stay UTC (item 83); the local offset is a later item.

**The version: 2.78.1.** A patch: three outputs now say what was observed. No command, flag, config key, lock format or refusal code changes. `report --json`'s `state` gains the value `closed` for a lock that printed `open` with a close time, and `per_day` is `null` in more cases; a reader that handles `null` and an unknown state word keeps working. Item 135 took 2.78.0 first, so this item is 2.78.1.

**Known limits.** The Go reader is lexical, as item 81's hook map already is: a test declared other than as gofmt writes it (a top-level `func Test...` not at column zero) is not seen, so in a package that also declares it at column zero in a build-tagged file a control may name the wrong file; gofmt'd sources, the norm, are read right. A closed lock whose close row is not in this machine's run log (closed elsewhere) stays `open`, as `close_at` is read only from the log; the `--all` controls line keeps saying `0` (item 98's rule); `status` cannot tell a non-admin's `404` from a deleted branch's; `report`'s UTC times; merge mode counts direct commits on the default branch as merges (item 124).

**Metric:** on the release, reproductions (A), (B) and (C) print `controls: 29 ...; 9 passing case(s) could not be tied ...`, `could not read branch protection on main (...)`, `lock <id>: closed` and `per day - (first to last under a day)`. POC-051's second arm counts these three lines read as false by a run: target 0. Revert: if a project's controls under point 1 refuse a GREEN commit (`control-failed`) for a case that point 1 added and that does not hold the file the map named, point 1 goes back to item 98's suite-level rule in a patch.

Lock-file `state` fields (`'open'`/`'closed'` inside `.shipcraft/locks/*.json`) are a different field and stay untouched.
