The RED may trade cases between points within 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `red::controls` returns no controls with `--all` or without a readable base, else, per suite, the passing cases `suite::holders` ties to changed paths, where `holders` returns `None` for the whole suite when the map is missing or any one case has no single holder. Change: per case, as point 1 says. `red::kept_controls` (a redo with no changed paths): keep. `print_controls` prints the line on stdout right before the lock line when the lock is format 5 or 6. Change: point 1's clause. The lock file, its seal and the run log row (`red`'s fields): keep.
- The hook runs the lock's failing cases and its controls (item 98), and refuses `control-failed` when a control fails: keep.
- `merge::status` prints branch, layers, roles, hooks, the `AGENTS.md` line, `open locks`, item 134's Python lines, then one protection line from `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/<base>/protection`; exit 0, no row. Change: only the line chosen for a failed call, as point 2 says.
- `init::protect` (with `--github`) writes the protection with `gh api -X PUT`; on success it prints `GitHub now requires ...`; on an error containing `403` or `Upgrade to GitHub Pro`, the plan line; else `could not mark statuses as required (<e>)`; `init` goes on and exits as today. Change: only the `403` match, as point 2 says.
- `report` reads the run log and git (and `gh` with `--github`), prints text or `--json`, writes nothing and exits 0 (or 2 for its usage and `--since` refusals). The lock row's `state` (change, point 3), the day rows, size, reds, review, checks, merged-PR, week, limit and markers lines, the `--github` enrichment and the DORA object other than `per_day` (change, point 3): keep.
- Also kept: `red::run` and `redo`'s other output and its order (`print_dropped`, `LeftOut::print`, `warn_absent_suites`, `warn_default_branch`, the final `next: the GREEN` line), and the hook's close mark (`check --commit-msg` removes and rewrites the per-worktree `shipcraft/close` mark, `mark-failed` when it cannot), the post-commit close with its retry guidance when it cannot close, and its index/tree failure messages.
- Also kept, on the touched paths: `red::with_lists`/`suite::write_lists` create `.git/shipcraft/tmp` and NUL selection files and remove them (a list-write failure omits the selection variables); temporary JUnit reports are removed after reading; `red::replace` writes a sibling `redo-<pid>.tmp`, removes it on failure, keeps the old lock and refuses `redo-write-failed`; `red` runs `[build]` in the working tree before suites and its output stays; `gh::with_input` writes a temporary `shipcraft-gh/corpo.json` for `init --github`; `init`'s core-write rollback (`undid what this run created`) happens before the protection write, whose failure rolls nothing back and keeps `init`'s exit.
- Standing effects the cases can meet, all kept: `main::measured` and `medicao::record` create `.git/shipcraft/` and append to `runs.jsonl` best effort; the hook writes a row on every commit, even with no lock; `red` and `red --redo` write `.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`; the post-commit hook makes `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>` when a commit's locked cases all pass and `close` appends one row per lock it closes; `init` writes `.shipcraft.toml`, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, the hooks, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.shipcraft/briefs/`; suites run with the caller's `TMPDIR`, in the tree or a checkout copy as today; `status` and `report` write nothing.

### `report --github`'s code split into modules (item 119)

This item changes Shipcraft's own code, not the product: no command, config key, lock, refusal, row, `gh` call or output of `shipcraft` changes. `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh.rs`, the code that asks GitHub through `gh` for `report --github` (items 79, 113 and 114), has 716 lines on `origin/main` `ec1880f` (525 of code and 191 of unit tests); since items 116 and 117 it is one of the larger files in `nucleo/src`, and items 113 and 114 each grew it. This item moves its code, unchanged, into modules along its own seams, by item 116's method. It is a refactor: a patch release, 2.63.4 (item 118, running beside it, takes 2.63.3; whichever of the two merges second rebases onto the other).

1. **The modules.** `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh.rs` stays the module root (Rust's `relatorio_gh.rs` beside a `relatorio_gh/` folder) and its submodules go in `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/`, exactly these files, each holding the items named, defined nowhere else:

   | File | What it holds | Items (each defined in this file only) |
   |---|---|---|
   | `relatorio_gh.rs` | the module root: the reasons every reader gives, the PR a lock went through and a call's outcome; it declares the six submodules | `const FAILED`, `const UNREADABLE`, `const TIMED_OUT`, `enum Pr`, `type Outcome` |
   | `relatorio_gh/asker.rs` | the asker: one lock's waiting and merge time (item 79), a commit's Blocking count (item 113), a PR's check runs (item 114), the once-per-run config reader and the call cache with its timeout | `struct Asker` (with its one `impl` block, every method in it unchanged) |
   | `relatorio_gh/answers.rs` | the shapes of `gh`'s answers that several readers share (item 79) | `struct PrView` (with its `impl`), `fn pages`, `fn check_run`, `fn oid`, `fn time` |
   | `relatorio_gh/checks.rs` | item 114's reading of a PR's commits and check runs | `type Tally`, `enum Verdict`, `struct Run`, `fn pr_commits`, `fn suite_runs`, `fn tally` |
   | `relatorio_gh/blocking.rs` | item 113's reading of the review statuses | `fn latest_count`, `fn blocking_count` |
   | `relatorio_gh/process.rs` | running `gh` in its own process group under the 60 s limit | `const LIMIT`, `enum Ran`, `fn run` |
   | `relatorio_gh/tests.rs` | the unit tests, as `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` in `relatorio_gh.rs` | the 7 `#[test]` functions of today's `mod tests`: `pr_commits_are_distinct_shas_in_answer_order`, `suite_runs_read_completed_runs_of_the_names`, `each_red_run_is_green_again_fixed_by_a_new_commit_or_still_red`, `a_pr_is_read_in_full_or_gives_its_one_reason`, `blocking_counts_follow_the_grammar`, `the_latest_count_wins_ties_to_the_first`, `times_are_github_utc_real_dates` |

   - **The folder** `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/` holds exactly the six files named, and nothing else: no other file of any name or extension, no subfolder. `nucleo/src/relatorio.rs` and `nucleo/src/relatorio/` (item 116) are not touched; in particular `relatorio/github.rs`, what `report` asks through the `Asker`, stays where it is.
   - **The submodules are declared** in `relatorio_gh.rs`, each on its own line at column 0: `mod <name>;` for the five code modules (`asker`, `answers`, `checks`, `blocking`, `process`; a `pub(crate) ` or `pub(super) ` prefix allowed), and `mod tests;` on the line right after a line `#[cfg(test)]`.
   - **A definition** is read line by line, with no Rust parser, by item 117's reader: a line at column 0 of the form `[pub ][pub(crate) |pub(super) ][unsafe ](fn|struct|enum|const|type) <name>` followed by a character that cannot continue a Rust identifier (`(`, `<`, `:`, ` `, `;`, `{`, `=`), or `impl <name>` followed by ` ` or `{`. `impl<'a> Asker<'a> {` and `impl<'v> PrView<'v> {` define nothing in this sense (the struct line does). Methods, nested functions and test helpers inside a block are indented, so they are not definitions, and neither are `use` lines (a `pub use` or `pub(crate) use` re-export defines nothing). A test is a line at column 4 or less of the form `fn <name>(` (any leading spaces) whose previous non-blank line is `#[test]` at the same indent.
   - **Every item keeps its name,** and each table item is defined in exactly one of the six code files (`relatorio_gh.rs` and the five code modules), the one its row names: the same table name defined at column 0 in two of them fails. `tests.rs` is read only for its 7 test names: its helpers sit at column 0 once out of the `mod tests` block (`fn sha`, `fn run`, `fn page`), and its `fn run` is the test helper, not `process.rs`'s, so `tests.rs` is left out of the "defined in two files" check.
   - **A file is at most 600 lines,** counted as the number of `\n` bytes plus one when the file is non-empty and does not end in `\n`. This applies to `relatorio_gh.rs` and to each file in the folder; today's sizes put every file well under (the largest, `asker.rs`, about 210).
   - **The two `impl` blocks.** The definition reader leaves generic `impl` lines out, so the two existing ones are checked by their exact lines: `impl<'a> Asker<'a> {` appears at column 0 in `asker.rs` once and in no other of the six code files, and `impl<'v> PrView<'v> {` at column 0 in `answers.rs` once and in no other. No other column-0 line starting with `impl` that names `Asker<` or `PrView<` exists in the six code files.
   - **Reading fails closed:** a file or the folder that is missing, unreadable, not UTF-8, or not listable fails the case with its path, never passes it.
2. **The code moves unchanged.** Each item's body, attributes (`#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]` on `Verdict`, `#[cfg(test)]` on `Asker::answered` and `Asker::configured`) and doc comments move as they are, the error texts and the `gh` arguments byte for byte. Today's four-line `//!` header stays at the head of `relatorio_gh.rs`. The only edits are the ones the move needs: `mod` declarations and `use` lines; visibility widened to `pub(super)` or `pub(crate)` where another module of `relatorio_gh` now uses the item, its struct fields or its variants (for example `Ran`'s variants, `Run`'s fields, `PrView`'s fields and `read`); one `//!` line at the head of each new file saying what it holds; and the formatting `cargo fmt` gives. The 7 unit tests keep their names and bodies; only their `use` line changes. Nothing else under `nucleo/` changes, besides the version (the item's own new acceptance file and fixed points are the RED's, as usual): the callers keep compiling byte for byte, so `relatorio_gh.rs` re-exports (`pub use` or `pub(crate) use`) every item they reach at its path today: `main.rs` (`mod relatorio_gh;`, unchanged), `relatorio.rs` and `relatorio/tests.rs` (`crate::relatorio_gh::Asker`, with `Asker::new`, `lock`, `blocking`, `suite_checks`, `pr_checks`, `answered` and `configured`), and `relatorio/github.rs` (`crate::relatorio_gh::{oid, Asker, Pr}`). `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass (item 111's `lint` check), with no new `allow`.
3. **No behavior change.** `report --github`'s stdout, stderr and exit codes, its `github` line, item 113's `blocking` keys and item 114's `checks` object and line, every `missing` and `not read` reason, and its `gh` calls (arguments, order, environment, stdin, the 60 s limit, the process group, the cache by argument list, no retry, the timeout that stops every later call) stay exactly as today, as does `report` without `--github`, which makes no call. The proof is the whole battery, unchanged except the version, and the 7 unit tests passing with the rest (`cargo test` in `nucleo/`; CI does not run unit tests, so the coordinator runs them on the PR head before `pr` and says so in the metrics row). The version moves to 2.63.4 (a patch: no contract change), in section 11 and in `nucleo/Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`; whatever prints the running version prints 2.63.4, and every other byte is the same as with the release before it.

**Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14), all keep:
- `report --github` (item 79): for each selected lock, `gh pr view <n> --json number,state,mergedAt,headRefOid,mergeCommit` called directly (no `api`, no pagination) and read as one JSON object, then the combined `status` and, per `suite_checks` name, `check-runs?check_name=<name>&filter=latest`, these two through `gh api --paginate --slurp`; `.shipcraft.toml` read at most once per run by `Asker::config`; `merge_at`, `close_to_merge` and `red_to_merge` filled on `merge_not_observed` rows after `git merge-base --is-ancestor`; the `github` summary line with `selected`, `merge_filled`, `waiting_filled` and `missing`. Keep.
- Item 113's `statuses` calls per reviewed commit and its `blocking` keys and suffix; item 114's PR `commits` and `check-runs?filter=all` calls and its `checks` object and line, a PR read in full or not at all. Keep.
- Every call: in `<root>`, `GH_PROMPT_DISABLED=1`, the repository-selecting git variables removed (`REPOSITORY_VARS`), stdin from `/dev/null`, stderr discarded, its own process group killed at 60 s or on failure; `gh failed`, `unreadable answer from gh`, `gh timed out` and the other reasons word for word. Keep.
- `report --github` writes nothing and appends no run-log row; `report` without `--github` makes no `gh` call. Keep.
- A failed enrichment never fails the report: `report --github` exits 0 and prints no GitHub error on stderr; each lock, commit or PR it could not read gets its `missing` or `not read` reason. Keep.
- Partial enrichment stands: `Asker::lock` fills `merge_at`, `close_to_merge` and `red_to_merge` of a `merge_not_observed` row (counted in `merge_filled`) before a later step of the same lock (`config unreadable`, `ci off`, a component missing or not green) gives its reason, and the row keeps them. Keep.
- A timeout takes precedence over cached answers: once one call reaches 60 s, every later lock, call and PR gives `gh timed out`, even when its answer is already in the cache. Keep.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists (the hook's rows, `red`'s lock, the closing commit, `TMPDIR`): keep. The new cases run no `shipcraft`, no `cargo`, no git and no network.

**Touches.**
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.63.4 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest); their test code is unchanged and they pass once both say 2.63.4.
- **Items 116 and 117:** `acceptance/lib/item116.sh` and `item117.sh`'s reader is the model; their cases read only `relatorio.rs`, `relatorio/`, `init.rs` and `init/`, which this item does not touch. Keep.
- **Item 118** (the config's split, beside this item): it touches `config.rs` and its folder, which this item does not; only the version lines and section 11's sentence meet, resolved at the rebase (`process/merge-main.sh`).
- **Assertions that change:** none.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:** the whole battery; CI runs it in full (a PR that changes `nucleo/src` runs every case). Named because they run the moved code: every case of `acceptance/lib/item79.sh` (the lock calls, their order, environment, process group, limit, cache and reasons), `item113.sh` (the review statuses, the count grammar, the timeout) and `item114.sh` (the PR commits and check runs, a PR read in full), item 107's `v1-107.github` and `v1-107.control-github` and item 109's `v1-109.github` and `v1-109.control-github` (the direct `pr view` argument list, `pr not merged` and `gh failed`, local measurements kept when enrichment fails), item 112's `report` cases that pass `--github`, and item 111's `lint` cases. Item 116's cases are structural and run no `report`.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item119.sh`, core profile, static reads of the repository under test (`$ACEITE/..`) in Python, like item 117's: one per row of point 1's table (the file exists and defines each item named, by point 1's reader; for `asker.rs` and `answers.rs` also their `impl` line by point 1's exact-line check; for `relatorio_gh.rs` also the five `mod` lines and `mod tests;` after `#[cfg(test)]`, so that case fails at RED too; for `tests.rs`, each of the 7 test names as a test), one for the folder's exact contents, one for "no table item defined in two of the six code files" (with the two `impl` lines in no other file), one for the 600-line ceiling over `relatorio_gh.rs` and every file in the folder, and controls that prove the helpers on fixture text in the case's temp directory (a 600-line file passes and a 601-line one fails, with and without a final `\n`; a missing, unreadable or non-UTF-8 file fails with its path; `impl<'a> Asker<'a> {` defines nothing and `pub struct Asker<'a> {` defines `Asker`; an indented method of a table name is not a definition; `fn times(` does not define `time`; a `pub use asker::Asker;` line defines nothing; an `impl<'a> Asker<'a> {` line in the root fails the `impl` check; a column-0 `fn run(` in `tests.rs` does not count against `process.rs`'s `run`; a subfolder or a stray file in the folder fails the contents check). At RED the folder does not exist, so every case but the controls fails; a missing file or folder fails with its path, never passes. About 19 cases.

**Known limits.** The ceiling covers `report`'s, `init`'s and `report --github`'s files only; `suite.rs`, `red.rs`, `lock.rs`, `deps.rs` and `pr.rs` are still over 600 lines, and a repository-wide ceiling is a later item. The module stays `relatorio_gh`, beside `relatorio/` and not inside it: moving it into `relatorio/` would change `main.rs`'s `mod` list and the `crate::relatorio_gh` paths of `relatorio.rs`, `relatorio/github.rs` and `relatorio/tests.rs`, and the spec's references to `relatorio_gh.rs` and `Asker::config`, for no reader's gain; `relatorio/github.rs` is what `report` asks, `relatorio_gh` is how it is asked. The structural cases pin this item's layout: a later `report --github` item that adds a function or a file updates `item119.sh` in its own RED, as any item that changes an earlier item's assertion does. Unit tests run only where someone runs `cargo test`; making CI run them is its own `ci` item.

**Metric:** the line counts before and after (716 in one file; the largest file after), in the metrics row, and the review of the next `report --github` item (rounds and Blocking findings), noted in that item's row. Revert: none needed; a later item can merge files back.

### The config's code split into modules (item 118)

This item changes Shipcraft's own code, not the product: no command, config key, default, lock, refusal, row, file written or output of `shipcraft` changes. `nucleo/src/config.rs`, the code that reads and validates `.shipcraft.toml`, has 1,247 lines on `origin/main` `ec1880f` (978 of code and 269 of unit tests); since item 117 it is the largest file in `nucleo/src`. A file that size is slow to read and to review, and every new config key lands in it. This item moves its code, unchanged, into modules along the config's own seams, by the method of items 116 and 117. It is a refactor: a patch release, 2.63.3.

1. **The modules.** `nucleo/src/config.rs` stays the module root (Rust's `config.rs` beside a `config/` folder) and its submodules go in `nucleo/src/config/`, exactly these files, each holding the items named, defined nowhere else:

   | File | What it holds | Items (each defined in this file only) |
   |---|---|---|
   | `config.rs` | the entry points: the file's name and the binary's version, the legacy file name, `Config` with its loading, its checks in their order and its accessors, and the missing config; it declares the ten submodules | `const CONFIG_FILE`, `const VERSION`, `fn reject_legacy_file`, `struct Config`, `impl Config`, `fn no_config` |
   | `config/suites.rs` | the suites and the build (items 9, 16a, 22.4, 59) | `struct Suite`, `enum Filtra`, `impl Suite`, `fn suite_names`, `fn owner`, `struct Build`, `fn dot` |
   | `config/paths.rs` | the form of the config's paths and globs (items 30.1, 67) | `fn normal_path`, `fn normal_form`, `fn normal_glob`, `fn normal_globs`, `fn invalid` |
   | `config/reading.rs` | reading the TOML text: the legacy keys and the text that is not TOML (E1, item 31) | `fn check_legacy_keys`, `fn legacy_keys`, `fn not_toml` |
   | `config/execution.rs` | `[execution]` and `[machine]`, and the layers (items 1, 42, 47, 62) | `struct Execucao`, `struct Maquina`, `enum Camada`, `fn camada`, `fn any_value`, `fn check_execution`, `fn check_suite_checks` |
   | `config/roles.rs` | `[roles]` (item 64) | `struct PapeisRaw`, `struct Papel`, `impl Papel`, `struct Papeis`, `const ROLE_AGENTS`, `const ROLES_MIN`, `fn check_roles`, `fn role`, `fn shape`, `fn role_invalid`, `fn a_type`, `fn escaped`, `fn quoted` |
   | `config/review.rs` | `[review]` (items 87, 96, 102) | `struct Revisao`, `fn check_retry_wait`, `fn check_review_after`, `fn check_review_guide` |
   | `config/pr.rs` | `[pr]` (items 11, 24, 115) | `struct Pr`, `enum NewDependencies`, `struct DependencyDays` |
   | `config/report.rs` | `[report]` (items 99, 105) | `fn report_release_tags`, `fn report_invalid`, `fn check_report` |
   | `config/version.rs` | `shipcraft_min` and version numbers (item 9) | `fn minimum_version`, `fn min_reaches`, `fn check_min`, `fn version_parts` |
   | `config/tests.rs` | the unit tests, as `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;` in `config.rs` | the 11 `#[test]` functions of today's `mod tests`: `legacy_document_keys_refuse_before_deserialization`, `english_config_keys_reach_their_consumers`, `new_dependencies_and_their_days_are_checked`, `raiz_and_globs_are_normalized_once`, `absolute_or_parent_paths_and_empty_globs_are_invalid`, `not_toml_or_a_suite_without_comando_is_a_refusal`, `role_checks_come_after_the_existing_refusals`, `suite_checks_need_github_ci_and_a_2_18_minimum`, `retry_wait_defaults_to_120_and_any_explicit_value_needs_2_41`, `report_release_tags_is_validated_in_order`, `review_after_defaults_to_ci_and_any_explicit_value_needs_2_46` |

   - **The trait impls go with their type:** `impl Default for Execucao` in `execution.rs`, `impl Default for Papeis` in `roles.rs`, `impl Default for Revisao` in `review.rs`, `impl Default for Pr`, `impl Default for DependencyDays` and `impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for DependencyDays` in `pr.rs`. They are not table items (the reader below reads `impl Default for X` as an `impl` of `Default`, a name in no row, and `impl<'de>` as no definition), so the cases do not pin them; the move keeps them beside their type. `fn dot` and `fn any_value` stay in the module of the struct whose serde attributes name them (`#[serde(default = "dot")]`, `deserialize_with = "any_value"`), because serde resolves those paths from that module.
   - **The folder** `nucleo/src/config/` holds exactly the ten files named, and nothing else: no other file of any name or extension, no subfolder.
   - **The submodules are declared** in `config.rs`, each on its own line at column 0: `mod <name>;` for the nine code modules (a `pub(crate) ` or `pub(super) ` prefix allowed), and `mod tests;` on the line right after a line `#[cfg(test)]`.
   - **A definition** is read line by line, with no Rust parser, by item 117's reader: a line at column 0 of the form `[pub ][pub(crate) |pub(super) ][unsafe ](fn|struct|enum|const|type) <name>` followed by a character that cannot continue a Rust identifier (`(`, `<`, `:`, ` `, `;`, `{`, `=`), or `impl <name>` followed by ` ` or `{`. Methods, nested functions and struct fields are indented, so they are not definitions in this sense, and neither are `use` lines (a `pub use` or `pub(crate) use` re-export defines nothing) nor `#[...]` attribute lines. A test is a line at column 4 or less of the form `fn <name>(` (any leading spaces) whose previous non-blank line is `#[test]` at the same indent. In `tests.rs` the test helper `fn suite(` sits at column 0 and so counts as a definition of `suite`, a name in no row (the table's `Suite` differs in case). A column-0 definition inside a block comment or a string would be counted; the moved code has none.
   - **Every item keeps its name,** and each table item, the tests included, is defined in exactly one of the eleven files, the one its row names. Uniqueness is by name, whatever the kind, as item 117's: a table item's name defined at column 0 (or as a test) in a file other than its row's fails, even as another kind (`struct Camada` in one file and `enum Camada` in another). A type and its `impl` (`struct Config` and `impl Config`) share their row's file.
   - **A file is at most 600 lines,** counted as the number of `\n` bytes plus one when the file is non-empty and does not end in `\n`. This applies to `config.rs` and to each file in the folder; today's sizes put every file well under (the largest, `tests.rs`, about 270).
   - **Reading fails closed:** a file or the folder that is missing, unreadable, not UTF-8, or not listable fails the case with its path, never passes it.
2. **The code moves unchanged.** Each item's body, attributes (the `#[derive(...)]` and `#[serde(...)]` lines, field by field) and doc comments move as they are, every refusal text and default value byte for byte. Today's two-line `//!` header stays at the head of `config.rs`. The only edits are the ones the move needs: `mod` declarations and `use` lines; visibility widened to `pub(super)` or `pub(crate)` where another module of `config` now uses the item or its struct fields (for example `PapeisRaw`, a private field of `Config`; `Execucao`'s `suite_checks_raw` and `Revisao`'s `after_raw`, which `Config::parse_before_guide` reads; the `check_*`, `normal_path`, `normal_globs`, `not_toml` and `camada` functions `config.rs` calls); one `//!` line at the head of each new file saying what it holds; the unit tests' indentation, one level less outside the `mod tests { }` block, with their names and bodies kept and only their `use` lines changing; and the formatting `cargo fmt` gives. Types that already are `pub` stay `pub` (they appear in `Config`'s public fields). Nothing else under `nucleo/` changes, besides the version (the item's own new acceptance file and fixed points are the RED's, as usual): the callers keep compiling byte for byte, so `config.rs` re-exports (`pub use` or `pub(crate) use`) every item they reach at its path today: `Suite` and `Filtra` (`suite.rs`, `check.rs`), `owner` (`contract.rs`, `narrow.rs`, `red.rs`), `suite_names` (`ci.rs`, `red.rs`), `Camada` (`ci.rs`, `merge.rs`, `pr.rs`), `Papel` (`pr.rs`), `NewDependencies` (`deps.rs`), `check_review_after` and `check_review_guide` (`check.rs`, `review_guide.rs`), `report_invalid` and `report_release_tags` (`relatorio.rs`, `relatorio/selection.rs`), `normal_glob` (`init/custom.rs`), `minimum_version` (`init/config_file.rs`, `init_msg.rs`), `version_parts` (`init_msg.rs`), `min_reaches` (`review_guide.rs`) and `check_min` (`check.rs`); `CONFIG_FILE`, `VERSION`, `Config`, `reject_legacy_file` and `no_config` stay defined in `config.rs`. `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass (item 111's `lint` check), with no new `allow`.
3. **No behavior change.** Every command's stdout, stderr, exit codes, files and run-log rows written, git calls and `gh` calls stay exactly as today, for every config: the accepted keys and their serde names, the defaults, the legacy key and legacy file refusals, the order of the checks, and every refusal's code and three lines. The proof is the whole battery, unchanged except the version, and the 11 unit tests passing (`cargo test` in `nucleo/`; CI does not run unit tests, so the coordinator runs them on the PR head before `pr` and says so in the metrics row). The version moves to 2.63.3 (a patch: no contract change), in section 11 and in `nucleo/Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`. The version is the one byte-level difference this item allows: whatever today's code writes or prints from the running version keeps doing so with 2.63.3 (`--version`, `shipcraft-min`'s `this is <version>`, the `<!-- written by shipcraft <version> -->` stamp of new phase briefs, the `differs from this version's, kept` notice; `minimum_version()` and so `init`'s `shipcraft_min` stay `2.63`); every other byte and message is the same as with 2.63.2. The build SHA that `nucleo/build.rs` embeds in `--version` keeps its mechanism; its value is the new commit's, as for any build.

**Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14), all keep:
- `Config::parse` (through `Config::load`, `Config::at` and `Config::of_base`), wherever a command reads the config (the hook, `red`, `ci`, `pr`, `merge`, `status`, `review`, `report` and the others that call it): the checks in this order, each refusing with its code and three lines: the legacy keys (`config-invalid`, E1, the first legacy key in TOML's table order); `shipcraft_min` above the binary (`shipcraft-min`); text that is not TOML or a `[[suite]]` without `command` (`config-invalid`, item 31, with the line it points at); `[execution] ci`, `review`, `merge` (`execution-invalid`, items 42 and 47) and `suite_checks` (`execution-invalid`, item 62); the paths and globs (`config-invalid`, item 30.1); `[roles]` (`config-invalid`, item 64: the `shipcraft_min` rule, then `red`, `green`, `review`); `[review] retry_wait`'s minimum (item 87); `[review] after` (item 96); `[report]` (item 99); `[review] guide` (item 102, `review_guide::check`). Keep.
- The readers and their failures: `Config::load` reads the tree's file with `fs::read_to_string`, so a missing, unreadable or non-UTF-8 `.shipcraft.toml` is `no-config` (exit 1); `Config::at` reads a snapshot (index or commit) through `repo.read` and parses `String::from_utf8_lossy` of its bytes, returns no config when the snapshot has none, and propagates a git read error as it does today; `Config::of_base` resolves `origin/<base>` (a git call), reads that commit's config with `at`, and falls back to `load` when the ref is absent or the commit has no config, while a failed lookup propagates its error. Each one checks `reject_legacy_file` first, on its own snapshot. Keep.
- `reject_legacy_file` (`.sc2.toml` without `.shipcraft.toml`, `config-invalid`), checked first by `load` and `at` and by `init` and `check`; `no_config` (`no-config`) when the tree has no `.shipcraft.toml`; `camadas()`'s `execution-invalid` and `review-github` refusals. Keep.
- The defaults: a suite's `root` `.`, `filter` `shipcraft`; `[execution]` `ci = "local"`, `review = "local"`, `ci_timeout = 1800`, `merge = "merge"`, `suite_checks` empty; `[review]` `timeout = 1200`, `retry_wait = 120`, `after` `ci`, no `spec`, no `guide`; `[pr]` `warn_lines = 500`, no `shape_lines`, `new_dependencies = "warn"`, `new_dependency_days = 30` (serde's `new_dependency_days must be from 1 to 36500, not <n>` outside the range); `[roles]` review `codex`, no RED or GREEN role; `[machine]` no `slots`. Keep.
- The helpers other modules call: `suite_names` and `owner` (item 59), `normal_glob` (item 67's `init --glob`), `minimum_version`, `version_parts`, `min_reaches`, `check_min`, `check_review_after` and `check_review_guide` (the hook's early checks of a staged config), `report_release_tags` and `report_invalid` (`report`, `red --fixes-release`, item 105): same results, same texts. Keep.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists (the hook's rows, `red`'s lock, the closing commit, `TMPDIR`): keep. The new cases run no `shipcraft`, no `cargo`, no git and no network.

**Touches.**
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.63.3 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest); their test code is unchanged and they pass once both say 2.63.3.
- **Items 116 and 117:** `acceptance/lib/item116.sh` and `acceptance/lib/item117.sh`'s reader is the model; their cases read only `relatorio.rs`, `relatorio/`, `init.rs` and `init/`, which this item does not touch. Keep.
- **Assertions that change:** none.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:** the whole battery; CI runs it in full (a PR that changes `nucleo/src` runs every case). Named because they run the moved code: every case that writes a `.shipcraft.toml` and runs `shipcraft` reads it through `Config::parse`; the config refusal and default cases in particular: `acceptance/lib/english.sh` (legacy keys and the legacy file), items 9 (`shipcraft_min`), 30.1 (paths and globs), 31, 42, 47, 59, 62, 64, 71, 80, 87, 90, 96, 99, 102, 105, 110, 112, 114 and 115 (`[pr] new_dependencies` and `new_dependency_days`), item 44's base-config cases `v1-44.local-base-pr`, `v1-44.github-base-pr` and `v1-44.off-base-pr` (the PR judged by the base's config through `Config::of_base`), and item 111's `lint` cases.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item118.sh`, core profile, static reads of the repository under test (`$ACEITE/..`) in Python, like items 116 and 117: one per row of point 1's table (the file exists and defines each item named, by point 1's reader; for `config.rs` also the nine `mod` lines and `mod tests;` after `#[cfg(test)]`, so that case fails at RED too; for `tests.rs`, the 11 test names as tests), one for the folder's exact contents, one for "no table item defined in two files", one for the 600-line ceiling over `config.rs` and every file in the folder, and controls that prove the helpers on fixture text in the case's temp directory (a 600-line file passes and a 601-line one fails, with and without a final `\n`; a missing, unreadable or non-UTF-8 file fails with its path; a `pub(super) struct PapeisRaw {` line defines `PapeisRaw`; an indented method or struct field of a table name is not a definition; `fn normal_globs(` does not define `normal_glob`; `impl Default for Pr {` defines no `Pr` and `impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for DependencyDays {` defines nothing; a `pub use suites::{Filtra, Suite};` line defines nothing; `mod tests;` without `#[cfg(test)]` on the line before it does not count; a subfolder or a stray file in the folder fails the contents check). At RED the folder does not exist, so every case but the controls fails; a missing file or folder fails with its path, never passes. About 22 cases.

**Known limits.** The ceiling covers `report`'s, `init`'s and the config's files only. Other files are over 600 lines today (`suite.rs` 977, `red.rs` 875, `lock.rs` 836, `deps.rs` 771, `relatorio_gh.rs` 716, `pr.rs` 637); a repository-wide ceiling, and splitting those, are later items. ### `pr --merge` merges when the PR is ready (item 110)

POC-031 (private process repository, `poc/031-pr-then-merge`, 2026-11-07) chained `shipcraft pr` and `shipcraft merge <n>` in a script for Shipcraft's own items: over PRs 116 to 120 the `merge` row came 5 to 7 s after the `pr` row (median 6 s, against 12 to 42 s by hand), and no merge went through that the gate would refuse. Where the chain was not used, the merge waited for whoever came back: PR 124 waited 15 min and PR 126 24 min after `pr` passed. Now `pr` takes the chain itself. The gate does not move: the merge is `shipcraft merge`'s, with the same checks, refusals, pin and row.

1. **The flag.** `shipcraft pr` accepts `--merge`, a flag with no value, in any option position among `--title <t>` and `--body <file>` (`pr --merge`, `pr --title T --merge --body f`, `pr --body f --merge`). The arguments are read left to right as today: `--title` and `--body` each take the next argument as their value whatever it is, so `pr --title --merge` is a PR titled `--merge` without the flag, and `pr --body --merge` reads a file named `--merge`; only a `--merge` in an option position sets the flag. `--title` or `--body` with no argument after it, `--merge` given twice in option positions, a value after it that is not one of the options (`pr --merge yes`), or any other unknown argument is the invalid-arguments error it is today (`shipcraft: error: invalid arguments: ...; usage: ...` on stderr, exit 2), before anything runs: no fetch, no push, no `gh` call, no row. The usage text names it: the `pr` entry becomes `shipcraft pr [--title <t>] [--body <file>] [--merge]`; nothing else in the usage text changes. Without `--merge`, `pr` behaves exactly as today, byte for byte, in its output, exit codes, GitHub calls and rows.
2. **When it merges.** With `--merge`, `pr` runs as today up to and including its last line: ``shipcraft: PR #<n> ready: enabled layers passed; merge with `shipcraft merge <n>`.`` is printed unchanged, and the `pr` row is written as today (same fields, `result` `"passed"`, its `secs` not counting the merge), best effort: a failed append changes neither the merge attempt nor the exit code. Only then, and only when `pr` would have exited 0, it attempts the merge of that PR exactly as `shipcraft merge <n>` without `--no-review` does, in the same process, where `<n>` is the PR number `pr` just printed: the `gh pr view` and its permissive reading as today (a missing or wrong-typed field reads as empty, the number as 0; no new schema check), the fetch of the base, the base's config, the status reads, the suite checks of item 62, the refusals `pr-not-open`, `merge-without-ci` and `merge-without-review`, the `--match-head-commit` pin to the `headRefOid` the merge's own gate reads, `[execution] merge` (`"merge"` or `"squash"`, item 42), the `merge` row, and its two success lines (`shipcraft: PR #<n> merged into <base>.` and item 85's cleanup line), printed on stdout right after the `ready` line. The output after the `ready` line and the exit code are exactly the merge's: 0 when merged; 1 with the merge's refusal on stderr; 2 with `shipcraft: error: ...` where `shipcraft merge` errors (its `gh pr view`, the base fetch, the status read, `gh pr merge`). Item 62's suite-check reads keep their meaning: an unreadable check is the `merge-without-ci` refusal, exit 1, not an error. The gate is read afresh: a base whose config changed while `pr` ran (a new suite check, a layer turned on) may refuse though every layer of this `pr` passed. The `pr` row already written stays `"passed"` whatever the merge does.
3. **When it does not merge.** When `pr` refuses (any code: `pr-not-on-branch`, a CI or review refusal, ...) or errors, `--merge` changes nothing: the same output, exit code and rows as without it, no merge attempted, no `merge` row, no further `gh` call. `--merge` never waives a layer: an off layer passes exactly as `shipcraft merge` already lets it, and there is no `--no-review` for `pr`.

**Known limits.** With `[execution] ci = "github"` and review off, `pr` does not wait for GitHub's CI (items 62 and 73), so the merge that follows usually finds `shipcraft/ci` not yet `success` and refuses `merge-without-ci`; the PR stays open and `shipcraft merge <n>` merges it later. When the merge refuses or errors after `pr` passed, Shipcraft makes no merge and undoes nothing `pr` did (push, PR body, statuses, comment, `pr` row); the PR's state on GitHub is whatever it is (someone else may have closed or merged it, which is `pr-not-open`). The merge reads the PR's head again from GitHub and pins that head, as `shipcraft merge` does: a push that lands after that read is refused by the pin, but a newer head pushed before it, by another session, is merged when it carries every required status at `success`, exactly as a standalone `shipcraft merge` would merge it; `--merge` does not insist on the head this `pr` reviewed. The AGENTS.md block and the briefs do not mention the flag (no block change in this item); the `ready` line still names `shipcraft merge <n>`, which stays valid.

**Metric:** over the next 5 items of our own flow run with `pr --merge` in place of `process/pr-merge.sh`: the gap from the `pr` row to the `merge` row (their `at`), median ≤ 10 s, and 0 merges the gate would refuse (a merge whose head lacks a required status at `success`). Revert: a release that drops the flag; nothing is written by this item that a later binary must read, and the process goes back to `process/pr-merge.sh`.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.59.0 (`v1-62.version`, `v1-63.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-78.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-61.hint-version`, `v1-69.machine-version`, `v1-69.machine-nested-version`, and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **Must keep passing unchanged** (none passes `--merge`):
  - every case that runs `shipcraft pr`, among them items 24, 25, 27, 38, 40, 41, 44, 62, 63, 72, 73, 90, 91, 96, 100, 103, 107 and 108 (`acceptance/lib/item*.sh` and `acceptance/lib/v1.sh`): output, exit codes, rows and GitHub calls unchanged without the flag;
  - every case that runs `shipcraft merge`, among them item 5 (`acceptance/lib/v1.sh`), item 85 (`acceptance/lib/item85.sh`: `v1-85.merge-default`, `merge-default-waiver`, `merge-other`, `merge-other-waiver`, `merge-control`, `merge-refused`, `merge-gh-fails-waiver`, with the cleanup line and its absence on failure), 42 (`v1-42.*`), 62 (`v1-62.reader-run-error` and `v1-62.reader-status-error-call` among them: an unreadable suite check refuses, exit 1, with no merge call), 64 (`v1-64.reader-merge`), 73 (`v1-73.control-merge`) and 91: `merge` itself is unchanged;
  - item 10's `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: no row gains or loses a field; a `pr --merge` run writes a `pr` row and, when it merges, a `merge` row, each with the fields it has today;
  - the invalid-arguments cases, where they read the usage text: only the `pr` entry changes, as point 1 says.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item110.sh`, on the fake `gh` and state directory the `pr` and `merge` cases of items 73 and 42 use, and run `shipcraft pr ... --merge`. Estimate: 8 for point 1 (positions, twice, a value after it, unknown argument, usage text), 14 for point 2 (local CI and review green then merged with both lines in order and both rows; squash; layers off; the merge refused for each of its three refusals and for a red suite check; `gh pr merge` failing; the `pr` row unchanged), 8 for point 3 (CI red, review requests changes, review error, `pr-not-on-branch`, a `pr` error, each with no `merge` row and no merge call), 2 controls (the same fixture without `--merge` ends at the `ready` line with no merge call). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `main.rs` parses `pr`'s arguments as `--title <t>` and `--body <file>` in any order, each taking the next argument; anything else is invalid arguments, exit 2, printed with the usage text, before any work. Keep. Change: `--merge` is accepted once, with no value.
- `pr` fetches, reads the base's config, refuses `pr-not-on-branch`, reads the `--body` file, pushes with `--force-with-lease`, prints the published-head line, opens or edits the PR, prints `shipcraft: PR #<n>` and the size lines (items 103 and 108), runs the layers (local CI through the queue, GitHub CI wait, the review beside CI of item 96, off layers published as `success` with `off in .shipcraft.toml`), posts the summary comment, prints the `ready` line or refuses. The `pr` row is written last, whatever ended the command, only once the PR number and its locks are known (after the size lines): a failure before that (the fetch, the config, `pr-not-on-branch`, the `--body` read, the push, the PR lookup, the count, `gh pr create`) writes no `pr` row. Keep all of it, the `ready` line's text included; with `--merge` a merge is attempted only after the `ready` line, so it never follows a run without a `pr` row.
- The queue (item 41): local CI and the review take a slot under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/shipcraft/queue` (its folders and slot files stay after release); the review runs in a detached worktree with its brief and output under `TMPDIR`, removed and pruned when it ends normally, and an error while preparing the brief can leave it. Keep; the merge takes no slot.
- `merge <pr>` refuses `no-review-without-reason` before any call, reads the PR (`number,state,headRefOid,headRefName,baseRefName`), refuses `pr-not-open`, fetches the base, reads the base's config, refuses `merge-without-ci` (status or suite check) and `merge-without-review`, fetches the head when it is missing, reads the locks the PR delivers, runs `gh pr merge <n> --merge|--squash --match-head-commit <head>`, writes its `merge` row only after `gh` succeeds, then prints its two lines. Keep, and `pr --merge` runs exactly this path with no `--no-review`.
- Refusals exit 1 with the refusal on stderr; errors exit 2 with `shipcraft: error:`; a `gh` failure is an error. Keep.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: rows appended best effort to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl`, the post-commit closing commit, `gh` input through temporary files under the caller's `TMPDIR`, and local CI as section 2 runs it (a clean tree in the working tree, so suite, check and `[build]` outputs can stay in the checkout; a dirty tree without `[build]` on an export of HEAD under `TMPDIR`; a dirty tree with `[build]` refused `build-dirty-tree`). Keep; this item adds no file, no status and no comment of its own.

### `pr` checks origin before it pushes, and `pr` and `merge` refuse with no origin (item 130)

Found on 2026-11-18 by POC-051 (simulated adopters, `poc/051-simulated-adopters/FICHA.md`, queue item 3, seen in all six repositories). Run 5 (a vibe-coded app cloned from its author's public repository): `shipcraft pr` pushed first, to `origin` as it was, with no check that the user can write there, and stopped on git's raw error. Run 6 (a local-only repository, no remote): `pr` stopped on `git fetch -q origin`'s raw error and `merge 1` on `gh pr view`'s `no git remotes found`, with no Why and no Next, and nothing said that the work is local and `shipcraft ci` is its finish line. Run 4 (a contributor clone of `encode/httpx`): origin was the real upstream, and only the run's own stop rule kept `pr` from pushing there. This is a new guard, not a fix: no earlier contract said `pr` checks origin (`kind = feature`).

**Reproduction, on main (2.70.0):**
- No remote: a scratch repository with a commit on `main` and a branch `feat` with one more, no remote. `shipcraft pr --title t` prints ``shipcraft: error: `git fetch -q origin` failed: fatal: 'origin' does not appear to be a git repository`` (and git's three further lines) and exits 2; `shipcraft merge 1` prints ``shipcraft: error: `gh pr view 1 --json number,state,headRefOid,headRefName,baseRefName` failed: no git remotes found`` and exits 2.
- Origin not writable: the same shape with `shipcraft init` committed on `main`, `origin` = `https://github.com/encode/httpx.git` (for which `gh repo view https://github.com/encode/httpx.git --json viewerPermission` answers `READ`), and `remote.origin.pushurl` set to a local bare repository of the test. `shipcraft pr --title t` prints `shipcraft: feat published to origin (<sha>)`: the branch reached the push URL with no check, and only then did `pr` stop (exit 2).

**Refuse or warn when the permission cannot be proven: refuse, settled here.** The threat is a branch pushed into a repository that is not the user's (someone else's project, or a real upstream). When `gh` cannot answer (not installed, not logged in, offline, origin not on a GitHub host), `pr` cannot tell a writable origin from a foreign one. A warning would push anyway, which is the harm this item exists to stop. And refusing loses nothing that works today: every step of `pr` after the push needs `gh` (`gh pr list`, `gh pr create`, the statuses), so a `pr` whose `gh` cannot read origin's permission already fails today, only after it has pushed. So `pr` refuses, before any fetch, push or `gh` write.

Everything in items 3, 5, 100, 103 and 110 holds unless a point below changes it: the other refusals and their order, exit codes, stdout and stderr of a `pr` or `merge` that passes the check, statuses, comments, rows and files written.

1. **No origin.** "No origin" means `git remote get-url --push --all origin`, run in the repository root, exits with code 2 (git's `error: No such remote 'origin'`). Any other nonzero exit, a git that cannot be started or that ends on a signal, is a failure of shipcraft as `Repo::run` reports it today (`shipcraft: error: ...`, exit 2), never `no-origin`. `<base>` in this point is `main` when `git rev-parse -q --verify refs/heads/main^{commit}` succeeds, else `master` when the same check on `refs/heads/master` succeeds, else the literal text `<base branch>` (so the `Next:` line reads ``shipcraft ci --base <base branch>``); a failure of those checks counts as the ref not existing.
   - Argument parsing and finding the repository keep their precedence: an invalid `pr` or `merge` command line and a directory outside git are refused or fail as today, before this check.
   - `pr` (with or without `--merge`, `--title`, `--body`) checks this first, before any other git call that reaches a remote, before reading the config, before reading the `--body` file and before the `pr-not-on-branch` refusal, so a repository without origin refuses this way even on its default branch or on a detached HEAD. It refuses `no-origin`:
     `shipcraft refused: this repository has no remote named origin, so shipcraft pr has nowhere to open a PR`
     `Why: pr pushes the branch to origin and opens the PR on GitHub; nothing was fetched, pushed or sent`
     ``Next: the work stays local, and `shipcraft ci --base <base>` is your finish line; to open PRs, add a GitHub repository you can push to: git remote add origin <url>``
   - `merge <n>` (with or without `--no-review "<reason>"`) checks it after the `no-review-without-reason` refusal (which stays first, as today) and before any `gh` call or fetch. It refuses `no-origin`:
     `shipcraft refused: this repository has no remote named origin, so shipcraft merge has no PR to merge`
     `Why: merge reads the PR and its statuses on GitHub through origin; nothing was fetched or sent`
     and the same `Next:` line as `pr`'s.
   - No `gh` is run, no row is written, nothing is printed on stdout.
2. **Origin you can push to.** "You can push" here means what GitHub says about the account `gh` is logged in with (`viewerPermission`), not who owns the repository and not the identity git will push with (see Known limits). When origin exists, `pr` then takes the push URLs: point 1's output, decoded as UTF-8 with invalid bytes replaced (`String::from_utf8_lossy`), split on `\n`, each line with a trailing `\r` removed and nothing else trimmed, empty lines dropped (`pushurl`, `insteadOf` and `pushInsteadOf` apply as for the push; with several `remote.origin.pushurl` entries, `git push origin` pushes to each, so each is checked). With no URL left, `pr` refuses `origin-unverified` with `<reason>` `origin has no push URL`. Then, before any fetch, push or other `gh` call, for each URL in order, `pr` runs one `gh repo view <url> --json nameWithOwner,viewerPermission` in the repository root, and stops at the first URL that does not pass, which decides the refusal below; the other URLs get no call. `<url>` is the URL with one change: when it has the form `<scheme>://<userinfo>@<rest>` (a `@` before the first `/` after `://`), the `<userinfo>@` part is removed, so a user name and password or token written there is not in `gh`'s arguments (nor in a `gh` message that echoes them); an scp-like URL (`git@github.com:owner/repo.git`) is passed as it is. No other part of the URL is changed. The JSON is read with `serde_json` (`gh::json`'s reader; on a duplicate key its last value counts). The answer for one URL decides:
   - `gh` exits 0 and prints a JSON object whose `viewerPermission` is the string `ADMIN`, `MAINTAIN` or `WRITE`: the check passes, prints nothing, and `pr` goes on exactly as today (fetch, config, `pr-not-on-branch`, push, ...).
   - `gh` exits 0 and prints a JSON object whose `viewerPermission` is any other string (`READ`, `TRIAGE`, `NONE`, `""`) or `null`: `pr` refuses `origin-not-writable`:
     `shipcraft refused: origin is <name>, where your GitHub permission is <permission>, so shipcraft pr cannot push to it`
     `Why: pr pushes the branch to origin before anything else, and a branch pushed to a repository that is not yours fails or lands in someone else's project; nothing was fetched, pushed or sent`
     ``Next: point origin's push URL at a GitHub repository you can push to, such as your fork (git remote set-url origin <url>; when git remote -v shows other push URLs, git remote set-url --push origin <url>), or keep the work local: `shipcraft ci --base <base>` is your finish line``
     `<name>` is the answer's `nameWithOwner` when it is a nonempty string, else the words `a GitHub repository`. `<permission>` is the string as `gh` gave it, or `none` when it is `""` or `null`. In `<name>`, `<permission>` and `<reason>` below, each control character (Unicode category Cc, `char::is_control`) is replaced by one space, so the refusal stays three lines. `<base>` here is `Repo::default_branch()`, the base `pr` uses today, when `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` resolves; otherwise point 1's local rule (`main`, `master`, or `<base branch>`), since no fetch has run.
   - Anything else: `gh` cannot be run, exits nonzero, prints something that is not a JSON object, or a JSON object without a `viewerPermission` key, or with one that is neither a string nor `null`. `pr` refuses `origin-unverified`:
     `shipcraft refused: shipcraft pr could not confirm that you can push to origin: <reason>`
     `Why: pr pushes only to a repository GitHub says you can write to, and every later step needs gh too; nothing was fetched, pushed or sent`
     ``Next: check that origin is a GitHub repository (git remote get-url origin) and that gh is logged in (gh auth status), then run shipcraft pr again; or keep the work local: `shipcraft ci --base <base>` is your finish line``
     `<reason>`: when `gh` could not be run or exited nonzero, the first nonempty line of the error string `gh::run` returns (stderr and stdout, each trimmed, joined by one space, then trimmed: so one-line stderr and one-line stdout make one line), trimmed, or `gh failed with no message` when there is none; when it exited 0, `gh did not return your permission on origin`. `<base>` as in the previous bullet.
   - In every refusal of this point: no fetch, no push, no other `gh` call, no row (`.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl` byte for byte as before the command), nothing on stdout; the refusal is on stderr, exit 1, as every refusal.
   - `merge` does not run this check (point 1 only). `pr --merge` runs it once, in `pr`; its merge step does not run it again (it still runs point 1's check, which passes).
3. **The docs.** Section 7's table gains three rows, in its alphabetical order: `no-origin` (`pr` or `merge` in a repository with no remote named `origin`, item 130), `origin-not-writable` (`pr` when GitHub says your permission on origin is below write, item 130), `origin-unverified` (`pr` when `gh` cannot say whether you can push to origin, item 130). The README's requirement line "Optional, for `shipcraft pr` and `shipcraft merge`: a GitHub remote named `origin` and `gh` logged in." becomes "Optional, for `shipcraft pr` and `shipcraft merge`: a GitHub remote named `origin` and `gh` logged in; for `shipcraft pr`, also write access to `origin` for the `gh` account, which `pr` checks before it pushes anything. Without a remote, the work stays local and `shipcraft ci --base <base>` is its finish line." Nothing else in the README changes.

**Known limits.** A config with `[execution] suite_checks` makes `shipcraft ci` leave the suite to GitHub checks (item 62), so for such a project with no origin the local finish line the refusals name runs no suite; this item does not change `ci`. The check reads the permission of the `gh` account: a contributor with `WRITE` on the upstream passes and `pr` pushes there as today (contributor mode, below, is its own item), and a user who pushes with a deploy key or another SSH account while the `gh` account has `READ` is refused `origin-not-writable` (the PR could not be opened with that `gh` account either). Only `<scheme>://` userinfo is removed from the lookup's argument; a credential in a query string, and whatever git prints on a later push, are as today. `init` and `status` still say nothing about origin (run 4's other half). `merge` does not check the permission: a `gh pr merge` without it fails as today. A push URL that is not on a GitHub host (a local mirror, another forge) is refused as `origin-unverified` even when the user can push there; `pr` could not open a PR there anyway. When other remotes exist (a fork clone with `upstream`), the later `gh` calls resolve their repository as today, which may not be origin; keeping Shipcraft's files out of an upstream branch (contributor mode, POC-051 queue item 2) is its own later item. The check costs one `gh` call per `pr` run.

**Metric:** on the release, POC-051's shapes again: a repository with no remote (`pr` and `merge 1` refuse `no-origin`, exit 1, three lines); a scratch clone whose origin is `git@github.com:encode/httpx.git`, run with `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=false` so no git network call can succeed (`pr` refuses `origin-not-writable` with permission `READ`, and nothing is pushed or fetched). Over the next 5 items of Shipcraft's own flow and the adopters' `pr` runs (Hands-off, Draken): 0 refusals of this item on an origin the `gh` account can push to. Revert: a release that drops the check; nothing is written that a later binary must read.

**Touches.** Read first:
- **The version:** the contract goes to 2.73.0 (items 128 and 129, run beside this one, merged first with 2.71.0 and 2.72.0) (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-67.version`, `v1-69.version`, `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest).
- **The `gh` stub** (`acceptance/stubs/gh`, test code): its `repo view` already ignores positional arguments and answers `viewerPermission` `ADMIN`, so every existing `pr` case passes the check unchanged. The RED may extend it so a case chooses the answer (another permission, `null`, a missing key, a non-JSON answer, a nonzero exit with a message), keeping `ADMIN` as the default and every other answer as today, and updates the surface table in `acceptance/README.md`.
- **Assertions that change (found after item 128 merged):** `v1-128.readme-once` pins the hash of the whole README without item 128's sentence; point 3's README line changes it, so its expected hash becomes the same formula over this item's README (a mechanical update; every other assertion of the case stays).
- **Rules that change:** section 7's "Each refusal has a code, recorded in the run log" now names the refusals that write no row (point 2's "no row"); the row shapes of `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl` and fixed point 19 still hold for every row written.
- **Assertions that change:** none found. The spec review searched the `calls.log` readers (items 42, 62, 73, 85) and item 110's `trace110`: they filter merge and API calls and none pins the whole `gh` call list of a `pr` run or its first call, though the new `repo view` call is the first `gh` call of every `pr` run that reaches it. If the RED finds one, it reports it before `red` instead of editing it (rule 11). `writes.log` is unchanged (`repo view` is not a mutation).
- **Must keep passing unchanged:**
  - every case that runs `shipcraft pr` or `shipcraft merge` on the bare `origin` (items 3, 5, 24, 25, 27, 38, 40, 41, 42, 44, 54, 62, 63, 72, 73, 85, 87, 90, 91, 96, 97, 100, 103, 107, 108, 110 in `acceptance/lib/item*.sh` and `acceptance/lib/v1.sh`), with their output, exit codes, rows and GitHub writes; the gh-failure wrappers of items 38, 42, 54 and 97 fail only calls that are not `repo view`;
  - item 85's `v1-85.merge-refused` (exactly three refusal lines) and `merge-gh-fails-waiver` (exit 2, no merge row): their repositories have an origin;
  - `v1-5.*`'s `no-review-without-reason` cases: that refusal stays first;
  - item 55's `no-base` case (`git remote remove origin` before `red`): `red` is not touched;
  - item 115's `pr` cases (`acceptance/lib/item115.sh`: `v1-115.added`, `scoped`, `dedup`, `base-config`, `off`, `create-fail`, `body-fail`, `late-error`, `reuse`, `count-fail` and the rest of the family): registry requests, bodies, reviewer input, errors and rows as today; the check runs before the dependency count and makes no registry call;
  - item 110's invalid-argument cases (`v1-110.duplicate`, `flag-value`, `unknown`, `missing-title`, `missing-body`, `no-review`): no external work, the check is not reached;
  - item 128's cases when it lands first (its `pr` and `merge` cases have an origin);
  - item 116 to 119's structural cases: every file they cap at most 600 lines, no new file under `nucleo/src/config/`, `nucleo/src/relatorio/` or `nucleo/src/relatorio_gh/`.
- **New cases** go in `acceptance/lib/item130.sh`, with real git fixture repositories, the bare `origin` and the `gh` stub, never the network. Estimate: point 1, 8 (`pr` with no remote, the three lines exactly and exit 1; no `gh` call (`calls.log` absent or empty) and nothing on stdout; on `main` and on a detached HEAD, `no-origin` rather than `pr-not-on-branch`; `<base>` is `master` when only `master` exists, and `<base branch>` when neither exists (a tag named `main` does not count); `pr --merge`; `merge 1` with no remote, its three lines, and no `gh` call; `merge 1 --no-review ""` still refuses `no-review-without-reason` first); point 2, 12 (`READ`, `TRIAGE`, `""` shown as `none`, `null` shown as `none`: refusal lines exact, the bare repository's refs unchanged, no fetch (a ref created in the bare repository after setup is not fetched), `calls.log` holds only the `repo view` call; `nameWithOwner` missing gives `a GitHub repository`; `WRITE` and `MAINTAIN` pass and push; the `pushurl` is the URL `gh` receives; two `pushurl` entries, the first `ADMIN` and the second `READ`: refused, two `repo view` calls, nothing pushed; userinfo removed from `https://user:secret@...` (the secret appears in no argument and no output); `gh` exits 1 with a message, first line as `<reason>`; `gh` exits 0 with non-JSON, and with no `viewerPermission`; `gh` not on `PATH`); point 3, 2 (the README sentence once; the three table rows); 2 controls (`pr` on an `ADMIN` origin: stdout and GitHub writes as today; `merge` with an origin: as today). Cap: 40.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `pr::pr` takes `Repo::default_branch()`, runs `git fetch -q origin` (an error, exit 2, when it fails), reads `Config::of_base`, takes the layers, refuses `pr-not-on-branch`, reads the `--body` file, pushes with `--force-with-lease` to `origin`, prints `shipcraft: <branch> published to origin (<sha>)`, looks up and opens or edits the PR, counts the product lines and the dependencies, prints the PR and size lines, runs the layers, posts statuses and the summary comment, and appends its rows. Change: points 1 and 2 run first. Everything else: keep.
- `merge::merge` refuses `no-review-without-reason` before any call, reads the PR with `gh pr view`, refuses `pr-not-open`, fetches the base, reads the config, checks the statuses and suite checks, and merges with `--match-head-commit`. Change: point 1 runs right after the `no-review-without-reason` check. Everything else: keep.
- `gh::run` runs `gh` in the repository root and returns stdout, or stderr then stdout trimmed as the error text (`run gh: <io error>` when it cannot start). Keep; the check uses it.
- Rows: `pr` writes its rows only once a PR number exists, and `merge` only after `gh pr merge` succeeds; refusals before that write none (`pr-not-on-branch` today). Keep: this item's refusals write no row. Exit 1 for refusals (on stderr), 2 for failures (`shipcraft: error:`).
- `status`, `init`, `red`, `ci`, `review` and `review --pr` do not read origin's permission. Keep.
- `pr`'s `git fetch -q origin` and `merge`'s base fetch, once reached, update remote-tracking refs and `.git/FETCH_HEAD` (the common git directory's in a linked worktree), and a later refusal does not roll them back. Keep; this item's refusals run before them, so a refused run leaves both unchanged.
- `merge` with `--no-review "<reason>"` posts its waiver comment before `gh pr merge`, which stays when the pinned merge then fails (exit 2, no merge row, `v1-85.merge-gh-fails-waiver`); it fetches a missing PR head best effort before reading the locks, prints its success lines and its cleanup line. Keep.
- Standing effects the cases can meet, all kept:
  - every `hook`, `red`, `ci`, `review`, `pr` and `merge` run that reaches its row appends to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl` (creating `.git/shipcraft/` when missing; in a linked worktree, the main repository's), the hook on every commit even with no lock, so setup commits already write rows; `close` appends one row per lock it closes; a failed append changes no exit code. This item's refusals add no row: a case compares the log's bytes just before and after the command, never expects it absent;
  - `init` writes `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, the hooks, the briefs and the AGENTS.md block; `red` and `red --redo` write `.shipcraft/locks/<id>.json`; the post-commit hook adds `chore(shipcraft): close lock <id>`;
  - local CI runs suites, checks and `[build]` with the caller's `TMPDIR`: on a clean tree in the working tree (their outputs, such as a TypeScript `dist/`, stay there), on a dirty tree without `[build]` on an export of HEAD under `TMPDIR`, and a dirty tree with `[build]` refuses `build-dirty-tree`; JUnit reports go to temporary files removed after reading;
  - local PR layers take the per-machine queue slot under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/shipcraft/queue` (its folders and slot files stay after release); the local review runs in a detached worktree under `TMPDIR` with its brief and output, removed and pruned when it ends normally (an error while preparing the brief can leave it);
  - `gh` bodies go through temporary files under the caller's `TMPDIR`.

  The new cases keep `ci` and `review` `off` wherever a case does not need them, set `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `TMPDIR` to folders of the case where a local layer runs, and say which fixtures run one.

### The review

- **A separate session:** always a fresh session of the `[roles] review` agent, without the author's context. It is a different agent even with the same model. The brief is built into the binary and carries the project spec (`[review] spec`), the project's review guide when set (`[review] guide`, item 102), the PR body and the raw diff.
- **The verdict is the first line:** `APPROVE at <sha>` or `CHANGES REQUESTED at <sha>`, then `Blocking: N`. Anything else, including a blank line first or text after the sha, is malformed. `APPROVE` gives `success`, `CHANGES REQUESTED` gives `failure`.
- **What blocks (item 88):** a Blocking item is reproduced, breaks the spec or the intent or crashes on valid input, and is reachable or a risk to data or access. Any other reproduced defect is a note.
- **Fail closed:** timeout, quota, a non-zero exit, a reviewer refusal or a malformed verdict give `error` with the reason, never approval. The reviewer runs once per `shipcraft pr`; only a non-zero exit whose reason holds `Please retry` is tried again (item 87). A `pending` status older than the timeout is redone by the next `shipcraft pr`.
- **Reuse:** a verdict is reused when the diff and the PR's text are both unchanged (item 54 added the text). The diff key (`diff_sha256`) is the SHA-256 of `git diff --raw -z --no-abbrev --no-renames --ignore-submodules=none <base> <head>`, as before: every changed file has the same versions on both sides. If the key cannot be computed, the review runs.
- **The PR's text is part of the key (item 54).** The brief carries a title and a body, so a verdict is reused only when they are the same too.
  - **The text key.** `text_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the title's UTF-8 bytes, one NUL byte, then the body's UTF-8 bytes: the same title and body this run's brief receives, before the brief's own substitutions (an empty body is the empty string, not the brief's placeholder). For a PR they are the decoded JSON strings `gh` returned, with no trimming or newline or Unicode normalization; for `review` without a PR, the title and body it builds today (its rows get the key; whether it reuses is unchanged: it runs every time). If the title or body could not be read (a failed call, a missing, null or non-string field), there is no text key: the review runs and its row has no `text_sha256`. How the review behaves when the text cannot be read is unchanged.
  - **Lookup.** As today, the newest earlier `review` row in the run log that ran a reviewer (not `reused`, result `passed` or `refused`) and whose keys match gives the verdict. It now needs both `diff_sha256` and `text_sha256` equal to this run's; a row whose `text_sha256` is missing or is not 64 lowercase hex characters (every row written before item 54) is never a source, so the first review after the upgrade runs once. Matching is by exact keys only: if the text goes back to an earlier version, that version's verdict can be reused, as a diff that goes back already can.
  - **Rows.** Every `review` row with a text key records it in `text_sha256`, a reused one included; a row without one omits the field. `diff_sha256` keeps its meaning. The reused status, its description, `reused: true` and the round counting are unchanged.
  - **Why.** On draken-platform#29 (head a8bb3ef) the review refused because the code disagreed with a rule written in the PR body; after the body was fixed with `gh pr edit`, `shipcraft review --pr 29` answered `verdict from a8bb3ef reused (same file versions)`, and only a docs commit (3a5ea1c) made it review again. Wrong-measurement note: since 1.0 (item 4, rule 4), the key covered the diff only while the brief also carried the PR text.
  - **Known limits.** Statuses belong to the head commit: editing the PR's text after an approval does not by itself undo `shipcraft/review` (run `shipcraft review --pr <n>` again; `shipcraft merge` reads the status as before), and two PRs with the same head share one status. The text is read when the brief is built, so an edit made after that (during a long CI run) is not seen by that review. The project spec (`[review] spec`) is in the key only through the diff.

### The review after a green CI (item 63)

The QA brief told the reviewer to run the whole suite. In `shipcraft pr` the review only starts after CI passed at the same head, so it reran what CI had just judged. Measured on this repository on 10-29 and 10-30: 1374, 1319 and 1301 s per review, most of it the battery. That alone breaks the 14-minute RED-to-main bar. In the same reviews, 8 battery cases failed only because the reviewer inherited variables that `shipcraft pr` sets for its own run log.

1. **After green CI.** In `shipcraft pr`, with `[execution] ci = "local"` or `"github"`, the review runs only after the CI gate passed at HEAD (local CI, or item 62's CI-green rule). That review is *after green CI*. Only how `shipcraft pr` got there counts: no PR number, status or environment variable is read to decide it. With `ci = "off"`, in `shipcraft review` and in `shipcraft review --pr <n>`, the review is not after green CI.
2. **What green CI means here.** CI was green at this exact commit. That is not a claim that the suite ran: CI may judge a PR docs only and skip the suite (item 40 for local CI, or the project's own rules in its `suite_checks`). The brief and the output say "CI is green", never "the suite passed".
3. **The brief after green CI.** Shipcraft keeps two built-in briefs and chooses one. The brief after green CI says, everywhere it mentions the suite (the opening, the steps, the review format), that CI is green at this commit, that the suite is CI's job, and that the reviewer must not run the suite commands as a check. The reviewer may still install what the project needs, and may run the smallest selection the runner offers (a file, a package, a test name) to reproduce something it suspects, even if that selection happens to cover every case. Not being able to isolate a test is never a Blocking item by itself. That choice is the reviewer's: Shipcraft classifies no file. The suite commands stay in the brief so the reviewer knows how the suite runs. The format's suite line becomes `The suite: not rerun (CI green at <short sha>)`, where `<short sha>` is the first 7 characters, as elsewhere. The verdict parser does not change. The other brief is today's, byte for byte: install, run the suite, record passed/total. Title, body, spec, diff and battery go into both briefs as today.
4. **The reviewer's environment.** The reviewer's command, for every agent and in every case (after green CI or not), starts with no environment variable whose name begins with the bytes `SHIPCRAFT_`. Names are compared as raw OS bytes, so a name or value that is not valid UTF-8 is never decoded, and every other variable is passed unchanged. This covers `SHIPCRAFT_PRODUCT_LINES`, `SHIPCRAFT_PR_LOCKS`, `SHIPCRAFT_SHAPE_WARNINGS` and any that a later item adds. Only the reviewer's command loses them. Shipcraft's own process keeps them, so its CI, review, `pr` rows and summary record `product_lines`, `shape_warnings` and `locks` as before.
5. **What it prints.** After green CI, the line that starts the review ends with `; CI is green at <short sha>, so the suite is not rerun`.
6. **The run log.** Every `review` row that is not reused gains `after_green_ci`: `true` after green CI, otherwise `false`. It goes on exactly the rows written today: a reviewer that could not start or gave no verdict still has its row, with the field; a failure that writes no row today (no queue slot, no checkout, no brief) still writes none. A reused row (`reused: true`) does not have it. Reuse does not change: the same keys, the same lookup and the same rows, in `pr` and in `review --pr`. A reused verdict starts no reviewer, so no suite runs.
7. **Measure and revert.** The metric is the `secs` of non-reused `review` rows with `after_green_ci: true`, over the first 5 PRs merged into main after this item. The coordinator names the 5 PRs by number and reads their rows by `pr` from this machine's run log; every round counts, and a PR with no such row is reported as not observed, never counted as fast. `secs` is wall time, including any wait for a queue slot, because that is what the PR waits. The bar: the review fits inside the 14-minute RED-to-main bar, with a median of 5 minutes or less. If it is slower, or a defect that a full suite run would have caught escapes a review, the coordinator reports it and the project may turn the layer off (`review = "off"`). Nothing switches by itself.
8. **Known limits.** A test that passes in CI but is wrong is still only something the reviewer reads, as it was before. CI is judged at HEAD only: if the base moved after CI, the review does not run the merged tree, the same as the merge gate. When `shipcraft review --pr <n>` starts a reviewer, it uses today's brief and runs the full suite, because it does not read CI.

### The review beside GitHub CI (item 96)

Since item 63 the reviewer does not rerun the suite after green CI, so waiting for CI before the review starts only adds the review's time to every PR. Measured here (run log, PRs 103 and 105 to 110, the full battery): `pr` took 341 to 632 s, median 379 s, and a non-reused review 29 to 92 s (one 351 s). From item 85 to 91 no CI went red after a green GREEN slice, so a review spent on a head CI then rejects should be rare. This item lets a project start the review at once, beside GitHub CI. The default does not change. It amends item 63 points 1 and 3: under `"push"` there is a third brief (point 2), chosen by how `shipcraft pr` got there, as item 63 chooses its two.

1. **`[review] after`.** `"ci"` (the default when the key is absent): `shipcraft pr` behaves as today. `"push"`: the review starts beside GitHub CI, as point 2 says. Any other value, a non-string included, refuses `config-invalid` naming `[review] after` and its value, wherever the config is read, like `[execution] ci`: every command that reads the config validates the key, and only the scheduling below is limited to `shipcraft pr`. Any explicit value, `"ci"` included, needs `shipcraft_min` of `2.46` or more, refused `config-invalid` the way item 87's `retry_wait` is, because an older binary ignores the key. `shipcraft pr` reads it from the same config it reads `[execution]` from. With `"push"`, nothing changes unless `[execution] ci = "github"` and `review = "local"`: with local CI the CI and the review would compete for the same machine slots, so local CI, `ci = "off"` and `review = "off"` behave exactly as with `"ci"`, and print nothing new. `shipcraft review` and `shipcraft review --pr <n>` validate the key but do not change how they run. The config table gains the row. Fixed point 22 (`acceptance/README.md`) holds with the key absent or `"ci"`; `acceptance/pontos/96.md` adds the exception: under `[review] after = "push"` the local review starts beside the wait and does not depend on its result.
2. **The review beside CI.** With `"push"`, `ci = "github"` and `review = "local"`, `shipcraft pr` starts the review (or reuses a verdict, item 54, unchanged) at the point where today it starts the wait for GitHub CI, and runs that wait (items 62 and 73: rules, output, polling and limit, unchanged) at the same time. The wait and its `ci_timeout` timer start at once, whether or not the review has a machine slot yet; the review queues as today (item 7), prints its queue lines as today, and keeps item 87's attempts and retry waits. Every attempt of a review started this way is "beside CI".
   - The brief is item 63's built-in brief after green CI, changed in the template before the title, body, spec, commands, battery and diff are inserted (inserted text is never rewritten), with exactly these changes: `CI is green at this commit (<short>)` becomes `CI is running at this commit (<short>)`; `CI already ran them` becomes `CI runs them`; `CI is green at this commit and the suite is CI's job` becomes `CI is running at this commit and the suite is CI's job`; the format line becomes `The suite: not rerun (CI running at <short>)`. Every other byte is the same, and the verdict parser does not change. The line that starts the review ends with `; CI is running at <short sha>, so the suite is not rerun`.
   - The review publishes as today, in today's order (pending status, row, review comment, final status; a reused verdict: row, then status), whenever it ends, whatever CI is doing then; a publication failure behaves as today. `shipcraft pr` waits for both sides, the CI wait and the review, before it reports, whichever ends first. Then, in this order: if either side ended in an error that today ends `pr` with exit 2 (a failed or unreadable `gh` call in the wait; a review that could not queue, check out, write its brief or publish), `pr` ends with that error as today, the CI side's when both did; else if the CI wait stopped (item 62's stops: `ci-github`, `ci-github-no-status`), the refusal is the CI's code and reason, without the words `; review did not run`, even when the review approved; else if the review did not approve, the refusal is the review's code and reason as today; else the PR is ready. The summary comment is posted as today, wherever today's code posts it. `shipcraft merge` does not change: both statuses at the head.
3. **The run log.** Every `review` row that is not reused gains `beside_ci`: `true` when it started beside CI under point 2, otherwise `false`; `after_green_ci` is `false` on a row with `beside_ci: true`. A reused row has neither field, as today. Every `pr` row gains `review_after`: the value point 1 read, `"ci"` or `"push"` (absent key: `"ci"`). Rows are written exactly where they are today: a `pr` that fails before today's first row writes none. The `pr` row's `secs` stays the command's whole wall clock, as today; only its gate part becomes the longer of the two sides instead of their sum.
