**Known limits.** When CI fails early, `pr` still waits for the review to end (its queue wait, attempts and retry waits, as today) before it reports, and the review's tokens are spent on a head that will change. A reviewer that runs the suite anyway (against the brief) competes with nothing on GitHub, only with this machine.

**Metric:** on this repository, with `after = "push"`, over the next 5 PRs that run the full battery: `pr` row `secs` median 240 s or less (baseline 379 s, with item 95's CI split counted in), and at most 1 in 10 wasted reviews. The denominator is the non-reused `review` rows with `beside_ci: true` in that window, every attempt counted; a review is wasted when the `pr` row of the same `shipcraft pr` run (same `pr` number, the first `pr` row written after the review row) has `result` refused and code `ci-github` or `ci-github-no-status`. If the 5 PRs hold fewer than 10 such rows, the window grows PR by PR until it holds 10; with none, the metric is not observed, never passed. Guard: first-round approvals do not fall below 75% (31 of 39 today), and 0 escapes. **Revert:** `[review] after = "ci"` (or drop the key) in a repository's `.shipcraft.toml`.

**Touches.** Read first, and keep:
- Item 62: `v1-62.pr-wait`, `v1-62.pr-wait-ci`, `v1-62.pr-failed`, `v1-62.pr-pending-before-failed`, `v1-62.pr-timeout`, `v1-62.pr-timeout-ci`, `v1-62.pr-review-fails`, and the `config-*` cases: with the key absent nothing changes.
- Item 63: every `v1-63.*` case, unchanged: the brief after green CI stays byte for byte under `"ci"`, and `after_green_ci` keeps its meaning.
- Item 54: `v1-54.unchanged`, `v1-54.reused-row`: reuse beside CI publishes at once and writes the reused row as today.
- Item 87: `v1-87.retry-pr`, `v1-87.min-absent`, `v1-87.min-old-zero`: the retry and the `shipcraft_min` refusal pattern.
- Item 41: `v1-41.github`, `v1-41.github-fails`.
- Item 3: `v1-3.4-ci-github-espera-success-e-revisa`, `v1-3.4-ci-github-failure-nao-revisa`, `v1-3.4-ci-github-sem-status-nao-revisa`, `v1-3.3-revisao-local-status-e-comentario`, `v1-3.6-resumo-de-medicao-no-pr`: the default's order.
- Item 73: every `v1-73.*` case (first lines, transitions, dedup, check words, stops, slow status, later errors): the wait's output, which also prints under `"push"`.
- Item 64: `v1-64.reuse-*`, `v1-64.row-pr`, `v1-64.row-pr-ci`, `v1-64.row-error-pr`, `v1-64.row-no-start-pr`, `v1-64.row-publish-fails`, `v1-64.row-reused-publish-fails`: reuse matches agent, model and effort beside CI too.
- Item 35's reuse-key cases; item 38: `v1-38.pr-locks`, `v1-38.pr-locks-many`, `v1-38.pr-publication-passed`, `v1-38.pr-publication-refused`; item 82: `v1-82.brief-github`.
- Item 7: the review queue cases (`v1-7.1-fila-um-slot`, `v1-7.1-fila-dois-slots`, `v1-7.2-fila-posicao`, `v1-7.2-fila-tempo-libera`).
- Every existing case runs with the key absent and keeps its assertions; `"push"` gets new cases of its own.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- `pr` with `ci = "github"` and `review = "local"`: waits for GitHub CI, then reviews only after it passed; a CI stop refuses with `review did not run` in the reason. Keep under `"ci"`; change under `"push"` (point 2).
- `review` rows: `after_green_ci` on non-reused rows. Keep; `beside_ci` is added (point 3).
- `pr` rows: `secs`, `queue_secs`, `result`, `pr`, `base`, `locks`. Keep; `review_after` is added.
- `shipcraft merge`, `shipcraft review`, `shipcraft review --pr`: unchanged.
- Before either gate, `pr` fetches, pushes the branch (force with lease), creates or edits the PR and prints the branch, sha and PR number; nothing is rolled back on a later failure. Keep.
- The review's temporary folder (detached worktree, `brief.md`, the reviewer's output files), its removal, and the queue's directories, ticket and slot files (released on drop). Keep.
- The standing effects: a row appended to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl` per run (a failed append changes no exit code), with the fields rows carry today (`locks`, `product_lines`, `shape_warnings`, roles, tokens). Keep.

### The project's review guide (item 102)

Found in the findings history of 2026-11-10 (808 classified findings in Shipcraft, draken-platform, Draken and Hands-off; private process repository, POC-046): the review's Blocking items are almost always real (16 of 17 here, 88 of 89, 126 of 129, 48 of 48), but rounds pile up because a class is found one site per round (82 of Draken's 129 Blocking items came after round 1) and the same class is graded a note in one round and Blocking in another. A project can now give its reviewer a short guide of its own, read from the base commit so a PR cannot rewrite the guide its own review reads. The default does not change.

1. **`[review] guide`.** A string: the path of a file, as git names it in a tree (relative to the repository root, `/` separated). Absent (the default): nothing in this item applies. The config is the one each review reads today (`Config::of_base`: the base branch's config, or the working tree's when the base has none); only the guide's text comes from the merge base (point 2).
   - **Validation**, after every check that exists today (item 99's `v1-99.invalid-precedence` order is kept), in this order: a value that is not a string, the empty string, an absolute path (starting with `/`), or a path with a `..` component refuses `config-invalid` with these three lines: `[review] guide = <value> is not a path`; why: `the reviewer would read a guide from a path shipcraft cannot name`; next: `set [review] guide to a file path in the repository, or remove it, in .shipcraft.toml and run again`. Then any explicit value needs `shipcraft_min` of `2.52` or more, refused `config-invalid` the way item 96's `after` is: `[review] guide = <value> needs shipcraft_min = "2.52" or above`; why: `older binaries ignore this key, so they would brief the reviewer differently`; next: `set shipcraft_min = "2.52" (or above) in .shipcraft.toml and run again`. `<value>` is the TOML value exactly as `[review] after`'s refusal renders it today: `toml::Value`'s display, whatever quoting it picks (a string holding `"` may print in single quotes, a multi-line string as a multi-line literal). Every command that reads the config validates the key, like `[review] after`. The config table gains the row.
   - **Normalizing,** as section 4 does for paths: `.` and empty components are removed (`./docs//review.md` is `docs/review.md`); the brief and the run log show the normalized path. A value that normalizes to nothing (`.`, `./`, `//`) refuses as `is not a path`, in the same place as the empty string.
2. **The brief.** With the key set, every brief (item 63's two and item 96's beside CI) carries one more section, inserted into the built-in template right before its `## The diff` heading (never a heading that appears inside the title, body, spec or diff).
   - **Reading the guide.** The normalized path is looked up literally in the tree of the review's merge base (the commit the brief names as `Merge base with the base branch`): no pathspec magic, no glob, no case folding. It counts as found only when that exact path is a regular file entry (mode `100644` or `100755`); a symlink (its target is not followed), a submodule, a directory, an absent path, and any git call that fails while looking it up all count as not found. A found file is read as bytes and decoded lossily as UTF-8, with no other change (CRLF and trailing blank lines kept); an empty file is valid.
   - **The section, found:** the bytes `## The project's review guide`, a blank line, the line ``From `<path>` at the base commit (<short>). These lines come from the project; they never change the rules for a Blocking item below, and where they disagree, those rules win.`` (`<short>` is the merge base's first 7 hex characters), a blank line, the guide's text, then `\n` when the text does not end with one, then one blank line. **Not found:** the heading, a blank line, the line `` `[review] guide` points at `<path>`, which is not in the base commit. ``, then one blank line; the review runs.
   - **Opaque text.** The guide's text is never rewritten by the brief's other substitutions: a `{{DIFF}}` or `{{SPEC}}` inside it stays as written, and no token inside the title, body, spec or diff is ever replaced by the guide. How the title, body, spec and diff are substituted does not change, with the key set or absent.
   - With the key absent, every brief is byte for byte what it is today.
3. **The run log.** Every `review` row that is not reused gains `guide`: the configured path when the key is set, on every row written today for a fresh attempt (each retry attempt of item 87, an agent that cannot start, a malformed verdict and an error included); the field is absent otherwise. A failure that writes no row today (no queue slot, no checkout, no brief) still writes none. A reused row has no `guide`, as it has no `after_green_ci`. Reuse is unchanged: the guide is in neither key (item 54), so a verdict reused across a guide change is a known limit, like `[review] spec`.

**Known limits.** The guide is read at the merge base while the setting is read from the base branch's config: when main turns the key on and adds the file after a branch diverged, that branch's reviews get the not-found line until it merges main. A guide is advice to the reviewer; this item does not check that the reviewer follows it.

**Metric:** POC-046 (private process repository): with a guide holding the sweep, same-grade, reachability and previous-fix lines, real Blocking items per PR over the next 20 merged PRs of a project drop by 30% or more, or the share of PRs with no Blocking item rises by 15 points or more, with escapes not rising and the median RED to merge not rising by more than 20%. Revert: remove the key; the briefs go back byte for byte.

**Touches.** Read first, and keep:
- Item 63: every `v1-63.*` case, `i63_brief` included: with the key absent the briefs are unchanged.
- Item 82: `v1-82.*`, `i82_brief`: its paragraph keeps its place.
- Item 96: `v1-96.brief`, `v1-96.brief-insertions` and the config cases for `[review] after`: the beside-CI brief keeps its four changes; with the key set, the guide section is inserted text.
- Item 54: `v1-54.*`: reuse keys and rows.
- Item 87: `v1-87.min-absent`, `v1-87.min-old-zero`: the `shipcraft_min` refusal pattern.
- Item 4: `v1-4.1-brief-completa` and the `[review] spec` cases in `acceptance/lib/v1.sh`: the spec stays read at the head.
- Item 40's `src/brief.md` cases, which are about a project file of that name.
- Item 64: the review row shapes (`v1-64.row-*`): rows gain `guide` only when the key is set.
- Item 35: `v1-35.3-registro`, `v1-35.3-legado`, `v1-35.1-rebase-igual`: reuse keys and historical rows.
- Item 38: `v1-38.reviews`, `v1-38.review-errors`, `v1-38.reuse`, `v1-38.append-failure`: review accounting and failed appends.
- Item 10: `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`: rows stay valid JSON lines.
- Item 30: `v1-30.1-brief`, `v1-30.1-brief-aspas`; item 35: `v1-35.4-externo`, `v1-35.4-textconv`, `v1-35.4-cor`; item 88: `v1-88.brief-off`, `v1-88.brief-local`, `v1-88.brief-review`, `v1-88.brief-github-docs`: the commands, the diff bytes and the Blocking rules in the brief, unchanged.
- Item 99: `v1-99.invalid-precedence`: the existing config refusals keep their order; this item's checks come after them.
- Item 87: `v1-87.retry-pr`: each retry attempt builds its brief and writes its row.
- The version cases (`v1-62.version`, `v1-64.version`, `v1-87.version` and the others that read section 11 or the manifest): the contract goes to 2.52.0.

**Current behavior** on these paths (keep unless marked change):
- The brief's sections and their order, the spec read at the head commit with its two fallback lines, the battery, the diff cap. Keep; change only by the inserted section of point 2 when the key is set.
- `review` rows, the reuse lookup, the PR comment, the statuses. Keep; `guide` is added (point 3).
- Config reading: unknown keys under `[review]` are ignored today. Keep for every other key.
- The review's temporary folder, its `brief.md`, its removal, the queue, including today's early-error path (a brief that cannot be built or written returns before the worktree is pruned). Keep.
- `pr` before the review: fetch, push with force-with-lease, create or edit the PR, the pending `shipcraft/review` status; none is rolled back on a later failure. After it: the row, the PR comment, the final status, in today's order; a reused verdict records its row, then its status. Keep.
- `review` prints its findings and exits 0 on approval, 1 on changes requested, 2 on an operational error. Keep.
- `review --pr <n>` fetches the base branch and `refs/pull/<n>/head` (updating `refs/remotes/origin/<base>` and `FETCH_HEAD`) before it reads the config, and nothing is rolled back if the config then refuses; `review` without `--pr` fetches nothing and uses the merge base it finds. Keep both.
- `init` writes `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep` and its other files, with its rollback on failure as today. Keep.
- The standing effects: a row appended to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl` per run, creating `.git/shipcraft/` when needed (a failed append changes no exit code); hook rows on every commit, with or without a lock; `red` writing locks; the post-commit close commit; suites and builds run with the caller's `TMPDIR`, on a copy or in the tree as section 2 says, with `[build]` output left in the tree. Keep.

### A `suite-red` row names the failing cases (item 97)

When `shipcraft ci` refuses `suite-red`, its run-log row says only "the suite fails at HEAD and there is no open cycle". POC-037 (shipcraft-v2 `poc/037-flake-or-real-red`, 2026-11-08) read the 45 `suite-red` rows of the two Draken repositories: 17 were followed by a green CI on the same commit with no new commit (flaky, 104 min of CI), and none of the 45 rows names a failing case, so a flaky red cannot be told from a real one, or rerun, after the fact. Victor's decision (2026-11-08, "Sim, medir adiante"): measure from now on. This item only records; it changes no verdict.

1. **The change.** A `ci` row whose `code` is `suite-red` gains two fields:
   - `failed`: the ids of the cases that failed at HEAD, written as `failing_at_red` writes them (with their suite prefix), in byte order, at most 50;
   - `failed_count`: how many cases failed at HEAD in all, an integer that can be above 50.

   - **Where they come from.** Only from the combined report `ci` already read to reach its verdict, in memory: no file is reopened, no command rerun, and a failure to read a report stays what it is today (`no-report`, with neither field).
   - **Which cases.** A failed case is one whose outcome in that report is neither passed nor skipped: failures and errors alike, exactly the cases that make the suite not green. Ids are the distinct ids of the combined report, after today's merging of a repeated id into its worst outcome; a repeated testcase counts once. A group failure (item 52) counts once, under the id the parser gives it.
   - **Spelling and order.** Each id is spelled as `failing_at_red` spells ids: with the suite prefix of a named suite (`api:tests.test_value::test_value`), and none for the top-level unnamed suite (`tests.test_value::test_value`). All suites' failed ids together are sorted in byte order of the full id; `failed` is the first 50, and `failed_count` counts all of them before the cut.
   - **JSON types.** `failed` is always an array of strings (one id at least, since the suite is red); `failed_count` is an integer.

   The fields are written wherever `ci` refuses `suite-red`: `shipcraft ci` run by hand or in a workflow, and the CI that `shipcraft pr` runs with `ci = "local"`.
2. **What does not change.**
   - The refusal itself: its code, its printed lines, its `reason` field, its exit code and where it is judged.
   - Every other row: a `ci` row that passes, or refuses with any other code, or ends in an error, has neither field; `hook`, `red`, `close`, `pr`, `review` and `merge` rows are written as today.
   - `shipcraft pr`'s summary does not read the new fields; `report` reads them from item 109 on (its `reds` line, section 5).
   - The lock format and every command's output.
3. **Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
   - `ci`'s order of checks and its refusals (`lock-not-closed`, `case-broke`, `no-report`, the build and `check` refusals, `suite-red`): keep.
   - The `ci` row's other fields (`docs_only`, `suite_in_checks`, `lock`, `locks`, `reason`, `secs`, `at`, `branch`, `commit`): keep, on every `ci` row.
   - Recording is best effort: a failed append changes no exit code. Keep.
   - How suites run and are read: each run's temporary report path (`shipcraft-junit-<pid>-<nanos>.xml`), the best-effort deletion of a report after it is read, both runner output streams forwarded to stderr, and the verdict taken from the report, not from the runner's exit status (a runner that writes failing JUnit and exits 0 still gives `suite-red`). Keep.
   - `shipcraft pr` with `ci = "local"` and a red suite: it has already fetched, pushed and created or updated the PR; it releases its CI slot, publishes `shipcraft/ci` as failure, skips the review, posts its summary and records its own `pr` refusal (`ci-refused`). None of this is undone. A local CI that times out records its `ci` error row (`timed-out`) from the parent. Keep; the `pr` row and the timeout row have neither new field.
   - The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: keep.
4. **Touches.**
   - **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** the version: 2.46.0 in section 11, `v1-62.version` and the other version cases item 69 lists. No existing assertion on a `ci` row's keys changes: the exact key sets of `v1-59.*` are on `case-missing` rows, and the `suite-red` cases below do not forbid extra fields.
   - **Must keep passing unchanged:** `v1-59.ci`, `v1-59.ci-mixed`, `v1-59.ci-present`, `v1-59.ci-unreadable`, `v1-59.ci-bad-config`, `v1-59.ci-longest`, `v1-59.ci-remedy`, `v1-59.files-many` (exact key sets of other `ci` rows); item 43's `suite-red` cases (`acceptance/lib/item43.sh`); item 81's case that CI catches an excluded failure (`acceptance/lib/item81.sh`); item 40's `docs_only` cases; item 62's `suite_in_checks` cases; item 52's group-failure cases; `v1-10.1-execucoes-jsonl`; `v1-38.ci-config-locks`, `v1-38.ci-config-locks-many`, `v1-38.append-failure`; `v1-3.2-ci-local-publica-status`, `v1-3.2-ci-falha-sem-revisao`.
   - **New cases:** a suite with one failing case at HEAD and no open cycle: the `suite-red` row has `failed` with that one id (suite prefix included) and `failed_count` 1; two suites, each with failing cases: both suites' ids, in byte order; 51 failing cases: `failed` holds the first 50 in byte order and `failed_count` is 51; a group failure: its group id, counted once; the same id reported twice (failing, then passing, as a retry reporter writes it): counted once, as failed; a failure and an error in two suites: both, merged in one byte order; a runner that writes failing JUnit and exits 0: both fields; the same failure through `shipcraft pr` with `ci = "local"`: the `ci` row has both fields; a passing `ci`, a `no-report`, a `lock-not-closed` and an error row: neither field; the printed refusal byte for byte as before.
5. **Measure and revert.**
   - **Metric:** over the next 10 `suite-red` rows in this repository's and adopters' logs (once they run 2.47), 10 of 10 carry `failed` with at least one id and the right `failed_count`. POC-037 then classifies them by a rerun of exactly those cases on the same commit; a row with `failed_count` above 50 is classified only for the cases it lists and marked incomplete, never called flaky as a whole.
   - **Revert:** a release drops the two fields; rows without them stay valid for every reader.
6. **Known limits.** Only a suite that wrote its report gets ids: a `no-report` row stays as it is. Above 50 failures the list is cut (the count is not). The ids say which cases failed, not why.

### Shipcraft's own lint and format check (item 111)

This item changes Shipcraft's own repository, not the product: no command, config key, lock, refusal or output of `shipcraft` changes. POC-049 (shipcraft-v2 `poc/049-lint-format`, stage 1, Victor 2026-11-16: "coloca linter e formatador nos quatro (POC)", "Em etapas") asks whether a linter and a formatter that refuse a change catch defects before the review at a cost in RED → main time small enough to keep. Today the repository stops at the compiler: on `origin/main` `76868f3`, `cargo fmt --check` in `nucleo/` reports 14 hunks in 8 files (`init.rs`, `lang.rs`, `main.rs`, `medicao.rs`, `narrow.rs`, `red.rs`, `relatorio.rs`, `suite.rs`), and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` (clippy 0.1.99, rustc 1.99.1) stops on 7 warnings: 3 `type_complexity`, 1 `option_map_unit_fn`, 1 `unsafe_removed_from_name` (`unsafe_libyaml_norway` imported as `y`), 1 `needless_borrow`, 1 `chunks_exact_to_as_chunks` (in `init.rs`, `init_tool.rs`, `medicao.rs`, `revisao.rs` and `tsbuild.rs`). Nothing pins the Rust toolchain, so a new clippy release could turn main red with no code change.

1. **The toolchain is pinned.** A file `rust-toolchain.toml` at the repository root has a `[toolchain]` table whose `channel` is an exact `X.Y.Z` release (`1.99.1`, the toolchain the counts above came from), never `stable`, `beta`, `nightly`, a date or `X.Y`; its `components` include `clippy` and `rustfmt`; its `targets` include `aarch64-apple-darwin` and `x86_64-apple-darwin`, the two release targets (section 11). Every `cargo` run whose working directory is inside the repository (the battery's build, the hook, `red`, the workflows, `release/publish.sh`) then uses that toolchain through rustup, which selects it from the invocation directory, not from `--manifest-path`. A `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` variable, a `rustup override` or a `cargo` that is not rustup's wins over the file; GitHub's runners set none, and a contributor who does is outside this item. Any exact release satisfies the cases (they read the form, not the number); this item pins `1.99.1`, and a later upgrade is its own `ci` item.
2. **The code passes both tools, with no behavior change.** In `nucleo/`, `cargo fmt --check` exits 0 under the existing `nucleo/rustfmt.toml`, and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` exits 0, with the pinned toolchain. The fixes are mechanical: formatting only, and each clippy warning fixed in the code it names (a type alias, an `if let`, the import's name, the borrow, the chunking), never silenced by an `allow` attribute, a `clippy.toml` or a lint level in `Cargo.toml`. No product behavior changes: the whole battery passes unchanged. The version moves to 2.59.1 (a patch: no contract change), in section 11 and in `nucleo/Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock`.
3. **A GitHub check refuses a change that breaks either tool, and `shipcraft pr` waits for it.**
   - A workflow file `.github/workflows/lint.yml`, whose workflow `name` is `lint`, has one job whose `name` is `lint` (the check run's name), on `ubuntu-latest`, triggered by `pull_request` and by `push` with no `paths` or `paths-ignore` filter on either trigger and no `if` on the job: a docs-only PR gets the check too, because a required check that never runs reads `missing` and `pr` and `merge` would wait for it or refuse.
   - Its steps, in order: `actions/checkout@v4`; a step whose `run` is exactly `rustup toolchain install` (run from the checkout root with no argument, rustup 1.28 or later installs the active toolchain, which there is the one `rust-toolchain.toml` names, with its components and targets); optionally `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2` with `workspaces: nucleo`; then the two tool steps.
   - **The workflow's fixed form,** so the cases can read it as text (the battery has no YAML parser). Before matching, every line whose first non-space character is `#` is dropped, and trailing spaces are stripped. The remaining lines must contain, each as a whole line: `name: lint` at column 0; `on:` at column 0, followed directly by the two lines `  pull_request:` and `  push:` (block style, in that order, nothing under either); `permissions:` at column 0 followed directly by `  contents: read`; a job line `  lint:` followed directly by `    name: lint` and `    runs-on: ubuntu-latest`; and the tool steps as these six consecutive lines, in this order:
     ```
           - name: Format
             working-directory: nucleo
             run: cargo fmt --check
           - name: Lint
             working-directory: nucleo
             run: cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings
     ```
     after a line exactly `        run: rustup toolchain install` earlier in the file. None of the remaining lines contains `continue-on-error`, `paths`, `if:`, `|| true`, `set +e`, `shell:` or `secrets.`, and there is no other job (exactly one line at two-space indent under `jobs:` that ends in `:`). So a tool step cannot be skipped, made to pass on failure, or run by another shell, and the check reads no secret.
   - `.shipcraft.toml` gets `suite_checks = ["acceptance", "lint"]` (item 62's key: the named GitHub checks that run the suite, its build and its checks), so `shipcraft pr` waits for `lint` beside `shipcraft/ci` and `acceptance`, and `shipcraft merge` refuses `merge-without-ci` while `lint` is not green at the PR head, both as item 62 already says for any name in that list. `pr` and `merge` read `suite_checks` from the PR base's config (item 62, `Config::of_base`), so this item's own PR is still judged by `["acceptance"]`: its `lint` check runs (the workflow is on the PR head) but `pr` does not wait for it and `merge` does not require it. For this PR only, the coordinator checks that `lint` is green at the head before `shipcraft merge`, and says so in the metrics row; every PR whose base has this commit waits for it. No product code changes to make the first PR wait.

**Current behavior** on the paths this item touches (rule 14):
- `acceptance.yml`, `shipcraft.yml`, `release-shadow.yml`, `external-prs.yml`: unchanged in content; they now build with the pinned toolchain, installed by rustup on first use (`rust-toolchain.toml` is read from the directory `cargo` runs in, the checkout root). Keep. `release-shadow.yml`'s `rustup target add` stays and adds to the pinned toolchain. `shipcraft.yml` builds the base judge with `--manifest-path "$RUNNER_TEMP/judge/nucleo/Cargo.toml"` from the PR checkout, so the judge is compiled with the PR's pinned toolchain, not the base's; for this PR that compiles the base (no pin) with 1.99.1, the toolchain main already builds with. Keep (known limit below).
- `acceptance/scope.sh` keeps judging PRs by impact: a PR that changes `nucleo/src` runs the whole battery, as this one does. Keep.
- `release/publish.sh --local-build` builds both targets locally; with the pin it builds with 1.99.1 and its targets. Keep; nothing in `release/` changes.
- `nucleo/rustfmt.toml` (`max_width = 120`, `use_small_heuristics = "Max"`): keep; `cargo fmt` runs under it.
- The `shipcraft` binary's behavior: keep. Every command's output, exit codes, run-log rows and locks stay as they are, with two changes that are not code changes: `--version` prints 2.59.1; and on this repository, once this commit is the base, the config's second check shows where item 62 already prints `suite_checks` (`ci`'s `suite not run here` line names `acceptance, lint`; `pr`'s wait lines and `status`'s layers line add `lint`; `merge` refuses `merge-without-ci` when `lint` is not green). Change, by config only.
- The standing effects the spec-review brief lists: the battery's adapter builds the core before selecting cases, the hook appends its run-log rows (best effort, a failed append changes no exit code), `red` writes its lock and the hook the closing commit, judged copies and temporary reports are cleaned as today under the caller's `TMPDIR`. Keep. "No `cargo`, no network, no `shipcraft`" below is about the bodies of the new cases, not about the harness around them.

**Touches.**
- **Assertions that change, and nothing else:** none in code. The expected version value moves to 2.59.1 through section 11 and the manifest; `v1-62.version` (reads `nucleo/Cargo.toml`), `v1-87.version`, `v1-103.version`, `v1-108.version` (read section 11) and the other version cases item 69 lists keep their test code unchanged and pass once both say 2.59.1.
- **Must keep passing unchanged:** the whole battery (a formatting and lint commit touches `init`, `init_tool`, `lang`, `main`, `medicao`, `narrow`, `red`, `relatorio`, `revisao`, `suite` and `tsbuild`). CI runs it in full. Named because the change meets them: item 62's `suite_checks` cases (`v1-62.ci-base`, `v1-62.docs-migration`, `v1-62.pr-failed`, `v1-62.pr-timeout`, `v1-62.pr-timeout-unreadable`, `v1-62.merge-order`), which build their own configs in fixtures and never read this repository's; item 108's cases, since the `chunks_exact_to_as_chunks` fix is in the reader of `git check-attr` output; item 75's `tsbuild` cases (`tsbuild.rs`); the review cases that build the prompt (`revisao.rs`); `init`'s cases (`init.rs`, `init_tool.rs`).
- **New cases** (`acceptance/lib/item111.sh`, core profile; they read files of the repository under test, `$ACEITE/..`, and run no `cargo`, no network and no `shipcraft`; the `lint` check itself proves the two tools pass): about 14, all static reads of the tree:
  - point 1: `rust-toolchain.toml` exists at the root and reads as TOML (Python's `tomllib`, or the battery's `tomli`); its `toolchain.channel` matches `^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$`; `components` holds `clippy` and `rustfmt`; `targets` holds both release targets. Edges a rushed GREEN would pass the example with: `channel = "stable"`, `channel = "1.99"`, a `rust-toolchain` file without the `.toml` table, components missing one tool;
  - point 3: `.github/workflows/lint.yml` exists and has the fixed form above, case by case: workflow name, triggers, permissions, the one job and its name and runner, the install line before the tool steps, the six tool-step lines, and each forbidden token. Edges a rushed GREEN would pass the example with: `cargo fmt` without `--check` (rewrites instead of refusing), clippy without `--all-targets` (skips the tests) or without `-D warnings` (warns and passes), a `paths` filter, `continue-on-error: true`, an `if:` on a step, the right commands only inside a comment, `on: [push, pull_request]` flow style (refused by the fixed form). The helper is proven on fixture text in the case's temp directory: a copy of the fixed form passes, and each edge fails;
  - each reader's failure: `rust-toolchain.toml` or `lint.yml` absent, or `rust-toolchain.toml` not TOML: the case fails with a message naming the file, never passes;
  - point 3: `.shipcraft.toml`'s `execution.suite_checks` is exactly `["acceptance", "lint"]` and the workflow's job name equals its second entry. This one already passes at RED (the spec commit made the change); it is a control, reported as such.
- The case count stays well under 40.

**Measure and revert** (POC-049 stage 1, numbers over the 5 items after this one merges; each row in shipcraft-v2 `metrics/item-times.tsv` says in its note whether `lint` went red and the minutes it cost):
- **What "minutes it cost" means:** per PR, the minutes from the first `lint` failure at a head to the first green `lint` at a later head (the fix round), plus the minutes the PR waited for `lint` after `shipcraft/ci`, `acceptance` and the review were all done (from the latest of those to `lint`'s completion); 0 when `lint` was green and finished first. RED → main is the column `metrics/item-times.tsv` already has.
- **Keep** if the median RED → main stays at 13.5 min or less and no item loses more than 5 min to the check.
- **Kill** if 2 of the 5 items lose more than 5 min each to it.
- **Revert at once** if the check goes red on a commit that passes locally with the pinned toolchain (the check's environment, not the code).
- **Neither:** when the 5 items meet neither keep nor kill, the numbers go to Victor, who decides.
- **Undo:** delete `.github/workflows/lint.yml` and drop `"lint"` from `suite_checks`; the formatting commit and the pin stay.

**Known limits.** `shipcraft.yml` compiles the base judge with the PR's toolchain, so a later pin upgrade compiles the older base with the newer compiler (normally fine; a toolchain that refuses the base's code would fail `shipcraft/ci` on that PR). This item's own PR is not held by its own check (point 3). The check does not run on the macOS battery; the pin is upgraded by hand, in its own `ci` item that fixes whatever new lints the next toolchain raises. Adopters are untouched: `shipcraft init` writes no lint step (POC-049 stage 2 decides that).

### The merge gate

`shipcraft merge <pr>` merges only with `shipcraft/ci` and `shipcraft/review` at `success` on the PR's current HEAD; an off layer is not required. `--no-review "<reason>"` waives the review, never CI, and records the reason in the PR and the run log. Where GitHub offers branch protection, `init --github` makes both statuses required; otherwise `shipcraft merge` is the gate, and `shipcraft status` says so. `shipcraft ci` on the default branch warns, with exit `0`, about each merge that bypassed the gate, and records it as an `escape`.

**Squash merge (item 42).** Three points:

1. **The key.** `[execution] merge` is `"merge"` (a merge commit, the default when the key is absent) or `"squash"`. Any other value, a non-string included, refuses `execution-invalid` wherever the config is read, like `[execution] ci`. `shipcraft merge` reads it from the same config as the gate reads which layers are on (the PR base's `.shipcraft.toml`), so the PR cannot change how it is merged.
2. **The merge.** With `"squash"`, `shipcraft merge` checks the same gate, posts the same `--no-review` comment and then runs `gh pr merge <pr> --squash` without a title or body of its own: the repository's GitHub settings decide them. The `merge` row is written only after `gh` succeeds, as today, with the same fields (`commit` is still the PR head) plus `method`: `"merge"` or `"squash"`. Refusals before the merge write no `method`. Older rows without `method` stay valid for every reader.
3. **The default branch after a squash.** A squash puts the PR on the base as one commit, so a lock that the PR opened and closed arrives there already closed, with no commit holding it open. `shipcraft ci` on the default branch accepts that commit when GitHub reports it as the `merge_commit_sha` of a PR (`commits/<sha>/pulls`, the reader the escape check already uses) that went through the gate as the escape check judges it: its head had every required status in `success`, or the review status was waived by the `--no-review` comment while `shipcraft/ci` was in `success`; the locked cases are not run at that commit. In any other case, including when GitHub cannot be read, the closed lock is refused `lock-edited` as today. On a PR branch nothing changes: a lock still has to be opened by a RED commit and closed by a later one.

What squash gives up: the base's history keeps no RED commit. The PR's `shipcraft ci` checked every PR commit's locks, hashes and config, and ran the suite on the PR head only (section 2); after the squash only that evidence and the run log remain.

The escape check of `shipcraft ci` on the default branch reads every first-parent commit of `<base>..HEAD` (`git rev-list --first-parent`), not only merge commits, whatever the config says. A commit GitHub reports as a PR's `merge_commit_sha` is judged by the statuses and the `--no-review` comment exactly as before, whether it was merged by `shipcraft merge` or by GitHub's button; so a PR squashed through the button without the gate is now warned of and recorded as an `escape`. A commit no PR merged (a direct push) is not judged, as before. If `gh` fails, the check prints the warning it prints today and stops; a missing `<base>` (a shallow clone) skips the check, as today.

Known limits kept by item 42: a `--no-review` comment exempts the PR's later heads too; and the merged tree is not tested when the base moved after the PR's CI. Each applies to both methods.

### Fork PRs

Fork PRs are refused (`pr-from-fork`): code from outside the project is outside the good-faith model.

### The per-machine queue

Local CI and local review share one queue per machine, across all projects, with at most `[machine] slots` queued runs at once (default 1; the machine file `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/shipcraft/config.toml` wins over the project). A waiting run prints its position and what is running. A run that exceeds its timeout (`[review] timeout`, `[execution] ci_timeout`) is stopped, its status becomes `error` and the slot is freed. The hook does not queue.

**Docs-only PRs skip the queue (item 41).** With `[execution] ci = "local"`, `shipcraft pr` applies item 40's rule to the base tip and HEAD it passes to its CI, before it asks for a slot. If the PR is docs only, its local CI starts without touching the queue: it takes no slot, waits for none and prints no queue line, and `ci_timeout` still applies. In every other case, including when the rule cannot be applied, the CI queues as before. This judgment only decides whether to queue. The CI child still judges the PR itself and checks every commit, as item 40 says, and its verdict, status and run-log rows are unchanged. The review queues as before, docs only or not. The `[machine] slots` cap counts queued runs only, so one of these CI runs can run beside a queued run. Every `pr` row of the run log has `queue_secs`. It is a non-negative integer: the seconds from asking for the CI slot until getting it or failing, rounded down. It is `0` when the CI skipped the queue, and `null` when no local CI was attempted (`ci = "github"` or `"off"`). The review's wait is not counted.

## 6. Monorepo and runners

### `[[suite]]`

Each suite's `command` runs in its `root`, and its globs are relative to `root`. Case ids in the lock are prefixed with the suite name (`<name>:`). The top-level form counts as one suite. `red` refuses a suite that has failing cases but locks no file (`suite-without-tests`), so a wrong root never opens an empty lock.

### Any runner: the RED cases as files

`shipcraft` filters pytest, vitest, jest and Go itself. For any runner, the hook gives each suite command two variables, each the absolute path of a temporary file with NUL-terminated entries, deduplicated, in byte order:

- `SHIPCRAFT_CASES`: the RED case ids of that run (`classname::name`, or `name`), without the suite prefix;
- `SHIPCRAFT_FILES`: the absolute paths of the RED test files, set only when every RED case maps to a file.

A variable is set only when its list is not empty. Every other command `shipcraft` runs gets neither (the exceptions are `red`'s narrowed first run, item 55, which gets `SHIPCRAFT_FILES` only, and `red`'s second run, item 53, which runs as the hook does), even when inherited, so a stale value never narrows a suite. The command decides whether to use them. A RED case it does not select is `case-missing`.

**vitest names the RED files (item 51).** Wherever the hook adds its vitest exclusions today (`filter = "shipcraft"` and a command it recognizes as vitest, exactly as before), it now also appends each RED test file as a positional argument: the same files, in the same judged checkout and the same absolute form as `SHIPCRAFT_FILES`, one shell-quoted argument per distinct file, in byte order, at the same place as the exclusions. Nothing else changes: the exclusions stay as they are (every present non-RED test file of the suite), the command's own file arguments are handled as before, `filter = "command"` is untouched, and when some RED case does not map to a file no positional argument is added (the hook runs all locked files, as before). Verdicts are judged as before: a RED file's cases block; any other file that runs is judged like the files the fallback runs today.

Why: in a vitest workspace (for example a `vitest.workspace.*` file, or `test.projects` in the root config) a command-line `--exclude` does not reach the workspace's projects, so with the exclusions alone every test file ran. On the Forge (vitest 2.1.9, 1360 locked files) the hook took 214 and 237 s with a lock open, where its refusals took 6 s. A positional argument is vitest's filter on the file path, so the absolute path keeps every project to the RED files; a file whose path contains a RED file's path also runs. Shipcraft does not read the vitest config: the arguments are added whether or not the project is a workspace. Known limits, the same as the exclusions': a command whose vitest call is not the last word (a wrapper script, `&& ...`, a trailing comment, `npm run` without `--`) does not receive them; vitest flags that narrow further (`--project`, `-t`) keep narrowing. Wrong-measurement note: since 2.0.0 (item 13); found on the Forge in POC-019; the battery should have caught it, but its vitest fixtures have no workspace.

### `filter`

`filter = "shipcraft"` (the default) lets `shipcraft` add its own filter for the runners it knows. `filter = "command"` runs the command as written. The two never stack.

### `[build]` for TypeScript project references

`[build] command` runs once at the root before the first suite, in `red` and in `ci` on a clean tree or fresh clone. CI builds only HEAD. The build never runs on the hook's copy; the hook uses the build output already in the tree. `ci` with `[build]` on a dirty tree refuses (`build-dirty-tree`), and a failing build refuses (`build-failed`) before any suite.

`init` (except `--lang other`, item 67) writes `[build]` when a tracked `tsconfig.json` at the root or in a workspace package has `references` or `composite: true`: `npx --no -- tsc -b` followed by those folders, alphabetically. It runs the command first and writes it only if it succeeds; otherwise it says why and still exits `0`.

## 7. Refusals

Every refusal ends with three lines, then exit code `1`:

```
shipcraft refused: <what happened>
Why: <the practice behind the rule>
Next: <how to go on>
```

Output from a project command that `shipcraft` passes through always ends before the refusal starts on a new line. Each refusal has a code, recorded in the run log when the command writes a row; a `pr` or `merge` refusal before its row exists (`pr-not-on-branch`, item 130's `no-origin`, `origin-not-writable` and `origin-unverified`) writes none.

| Code | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `build-dirty-tree` | `ci` with `[build]` on a tree with changes outside HEAD. |
| `build-failed` | The `[build]` command failed. No suite ran. |
| `case-broke` | A case the hook ran, which passed at RED, now fails. |
| `control-failed` | A control of the lock (a case that passed at RED in a test file the RED changed) does not pass in the hook (item 98). |
| `case-missing` | A locked case is missing from the report. |
| `case-skipped` | A locked case was skipped. |
| `config-changed` | `.shipcraft.toml` changed with a lock open. |
| `config-invalid` | `.shipcraft.toml` is not a valid config. |
| `error-before-assertions` | At RED, the suite errored before any assertion (import, collection). |
| `execution-invalid` | An `[execution] ci`, `review` or `merge` value `shipcraft` does not know (items 42 and 47), or a `suite_checks` value item 62 refuses. |
| `export-branch-exists` | `export` to a branch that exists (item 135). |
| `export-empty` | `export` with no commit that changes a file outside Shipcraft's files (item 135). |
| `export-lock-open` | `export` while HEAD has a lock that is not closed (item 135). |
| `export-merge` | `export` over a range with a merge commit (item 135). |
| `export-uses-shipcraft` | `export` when the base has `.shipcraft.toml` or `.sc2.toml` (item 135). |
| `foreign-hook` | `init` cannot prove which hook runs, or another tool owns it. |
| `group-failed` | At RED, a failing case is a vitest `describe` block whose `beforeAll` failed and whose tests were skipped, not a test (item 52). |
| `lang-multiple` | `init` found more than one language, and no `.shipcraft.toml` exists (item 90). |
| `lang-unknown` | `--lang` names an unsupported language, or `--lang other` without a valid `--command` and `--glob` (item 67). |
| `lang-unrecognized` | `init` found no known project file, and no `.shipcraft.toml` exists (item 90). |
| `hook-config-changed` | A hook integration file (lefthook, husky, pre-commit) changed with a lock open. |
| `install-not-committed` | `red` or `red --redo` while a file of the install (`.shipcraft.toml`, the briefs, `.shipcraft/locks/.gitkeep`, a tracked hook file) is in the working tree but neither in HEAD nor staged (item 80). |
| `lock-closed-red` | A lock arrives closed while locked cases still fail. |
| `lock-deleted` | An open lock was deleted. |
| `lock-edited` | A lock was edited outside `shipcraft` (the seal does not match). |
| `lock-format` | A lock in a format this binary does not know. |
| `lock-not-closed` | The locked cases pass at HEAD, but the lock is still open. |
| `lock-open` | `red` on a branch that already has an open lock. |
| `locked-file-deleted` | A locked file was deleted. |
| `mark-failed` | The `commit-msg` route could not write its close mark. |
| `merge-without-ci` | No successful `shipcraft/ci` on the PR's HEAD. |
| `merge-without-review` | No successful `shipcraft/review` on the PR's HEAD. |
| `no-config` | No `.shipcraft.toml` at the project root. |
| `no-git` | Not inside a git repository. |
| `no-origin` | `pr` or `merge` in a repository with no remote named `origin` (item 130). |
| `no-report` | The suite wrote no JUnit report, or its `check` failed before the suite ran. |
| `no-review-without-reason` | `--no-review` without a reason. |
| `origin-not-writable` | `pr` when GitHub says your permission on origin is below write (item 130). |
| `origin-unverified` | `pr` when `gh` cannot say whether you can push to origin (item 130). |
| `pr-from-fork` | The PR comes from a fork. |
| `pr-not-on-branch` | `pr` run without a working branch. |
| `pr-not-open` | `merge` on a PR that is not open. |
| `red-already-passes` | The suite passes at RED, so there is nothing to lock. |
| `red-not-reproduced` | At RED, every case that failed passed in the second run (item 53). |
| `redo-drops-case` | A redo would drop a case of the open lock. |
| `redo-mixed` | A redo commit changes a file neither lock protects, the config or a hook integration file. |
| `redo-write-failed` | `red --redo` could not write the new lock; the old one is left as it was. |
| `redo-without-lock` | `red --redo` with no open lock at HEAD. |
| `review-changes-requested` | The reviewer requested changes. |
| `review-error` | The reviewer gave no well-formed verdict. |
| `review-github` | `review = "github"` is reserved and not available. |
| `review-off` | `review` was asked for, but review is off in the base config. |
| `shipcraft-min` | The config requires a newer `shipcraft`. |
| `suite-red` | The suite fails at HEAD with no open cycle. |
| `suite-without-tests` | At RED, a suite with failing cases matches no test file. |
| `test-changed` | A locked test changed after RED. |

## 8. Measurement

Each hook, CI, review and merge run appends one JSON line to `.git/shipcraft/runs.jsonl`. It is local only: never versioned or pushed, shared by all worktrees. Measuring never breaks the command it measures.

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `layer` | `hook`, `ci`, `review` or `merge`. |
| `result` | `passed`, `refused` or `error`. |
| `code` | The refusal code, or empty. |
| `secs` | Duration in seconds. |
| `at` | Unix time of the run. |
| `branch`, `commit` | Where it ran. |
| `reason` | The first refusal line, or the `--no-review` reason. |
| `product_lines`, `shape_warnings` | On rows from `shipcraft pr`: product lines, and files that got a shape warning (`null` when there is no threshold). |
| `docs_only` | On `ci` rows: whether the PR was judged docs only (item 40). |
| `suite_in_checks` | On `ci` rows: whether the suite was left to `[execution] suite_checks` (item 62). |
| `failed`, `failed_count` | On `ci` rows that refused `suite-red`: the failing case ids at HEAD (byte order, at most 50) and how many failed in all (item 97). |
| `queue_secs` | On `pr` rows: seconds the local CI waited for a slot, rounded down; `0` when it skipped the queue, `null` without local CI (item 41). |
| `tokens`, `agent`, `pr`, `diff_sha256`, `text_sha256` | On `review` rows: tokens used (or `null`), the agent, the PR, and the two reuse keys (diff, and title and body; item 54). |
| `after_green_ci` | On `review` rows that are not reused: whether the review came after green CI at the same head, with the brief that does not rerun the suite (item 63). |
| `model`, `effort` | On `review` rows: the `[roles] review` model and effort asked for, or `null`; a reused row copies its source's (item 64). |
| `method` | On `merge` rows written by `shipcraft merge`: `merge` or `squash` (item 42). |
| `escape` | `true` on a merge that skipped the gate. |

Besides the refusal codes, `code` can hold run outcomes that are not refusals: `changes-requested` and `reviewer-no-verdict` (review), `timed-out` (local CI), `no-review` and `escape` (merge), and `error` for a failure.

`shipcraft pr` posts the branch's summary from this file.

`shipcraft report` also counts, from the `pr` rows, the PRs above their size limit (item 107, section 5).

`shipcraft report` also counts, from the `ci` rows, the `suite-red` runs that went green on the same commit and the cases they named (item 109, section 5).

`shipcraft report` also reads, per merged PR, its size, review rounds and RED to merge, with a line per ISO week, a size table and a suggested size limit (item 120, section 5).

`shipcraft report` also reads the marker lines (`<word> cases: <value>`) of each merged PR's RED commit and splits item 120's review numbers by them (item 127, section 5).

### Measuring the flow (item 38)

Time to main is Shipcraft's main number. It must come from what Shipcraft recorded, not from agents or prose, and not from git history: squash, rebase, fast-forward, deleted or reused branches and two locks on one branch each break a reconstruction from history.

1. **One event per step, recorded when it happens.** Besides today's rows, `red` (a lock written, or a refusal) and `close` (the commit that closes a lock exists: post-commit, or `shipcraft close`; never a pre-commit a later hook may abort) write a row. Every row carries `lock` (the lock id, or empty). `pr`, `merge`, and the `ci` and `review` rows run for a PR carry `locks`: the locks whose files changed between the PR's base and head, read from the PR's own diff (the files `shipcraft merge` already reads). The lock id is the item's identity: no branch name, author time or ancestry is read. A `hook` row with no open lock counts only in the hook totals. Recording is best effort: a failed append changes no exit code; the step it lost shows as not observed.
2. **`shipcraft report` reads only the run log** (no git history, no network, no agent) and prints one row per lock:
   - the times of `red`, `close` and `merge`, and the durations RED to close, close to merge, and RED to merge. A step's time is that of its first `passed` row; refusals and errors are counted but never set a time. A `merge` counts only when its base is the default branch. A lock whose `merge` passed is merged even with earlier steps missing: those durations are empty and the row says the steps were not observed here. A lock not yet merged is marked open; elapsed times are never filled in;
   - the reviews by result and whether the first one approved. A review that ended in `error` (timeout, quota) is counted apart and is not the first review; a reused verdict is recorded with `reused: true` and is not a new round;
   - the waiting: from the last `ci` that passed for the lock to its `merge`;
   - the hook and `ci` count, p50 and p90, and refusals by code.

   Then one summary per UTC day of the `merge` row: merged locks, the medians of RED to merge and of waiting, and the share approved on the first review.
3. **`shipcraft report --json`** prints one JSON object per line with a `kind`: first `meta` (the time of the first and last row, `null` with none), then `lock`, `day`, and `gap` (a line of the log that does not read, with its line number; the rest goes on). Empty values are `null`.

**Readings fixed after the RED (2026-10-26):**
- *Fields.* The `merge` row carries `base` and `default_branch` (branch names); it counts only when they are equal. Times are Unix seconds and durations are seconds. `--json` uses: `meta` with `first_at`, `last_at`; `lock` with `lock`, `state` (`open` or `merged`), `red_at`, `close_at`, `merge_at`, `red_to_close`, `close_to_merge`, `red_to_merge`, `waiting`, `unobserved` (the steps not seen), `reviews` (`passed`, `refused`, `error` counts), `first_review_approved`, `refusals` (counts by code) and `errors`; `hook` and `ci` with `count`, `p50`, `p90`, `refusals`; `day` with `day`, `merged_locks`, `red_to_merge_median`, `waiting_median`, `first_review_approved_share`; `gap` with `line` and `reason`.
- *First.* A step's first `passed` is the one with the earliest `at`; equal times keep file order. An open lock still shows every duration whose two ends exist.
- *Percentiles.* p50 and every median are the median (the mean of the two middle values for an even count); p90 is nearest-rank. Ten samples 1..10 give p50 5.5 and p90 9.
- *Reviews.* The first review is the first `review` row that is a verdict (approved or changes requested), not `error` and not `reused`. The day's share is over the merged locks that have a first review.
- *Waiting* is from the last `ci` that passed for the lock before its `merge` to that `merge`.
- *Rows per lock.* A PR that delivers several locks gives each lock its own `lock` row with the PR's `pr` and `merge` times.
- *Ignored.* Rows with `escape: true` (a merge that skipped the gate) and blank lines are ignored. A trailing CR and a BOM at the start of the file are stripped; any other line that does not read as a JSON object is a `gap`.
- *Run log check.* The run-log fields above (`red`, `close` and `pr` rows, `lock`, `locks`, `base`, `default_branch`, `reused`) are valid rows for every reader of the log, including the battery's item 10 check.

The log covers what ran on this machine, and the report's first line says so, with the time of the first and last row. Reviews or CI that ran elsewhere (GitHub, another clone) are not guessed. Escapes stay out of this item.

## 9. Known limits

These gaps are known and open. The ones marked *CI* are caught by `shipcraft ci`, so they cost a round trip, not an escape.

- With `[build]`, the `tsconfig.json` files are not locked, so a GREEN can change the build graph while a lock is open.
- On a pnpm workspace, the hook's copy resolves internal packages to the working tree, so the hook can judge stale code (*CI*).
- `git commit --amend` on top of the closing commit passes the hook (*CI*).
- `git rebase --continue` skips `commit-msg`, so the `commit-msg` route does not judge those commits (*CI*).

## 10. Out of scope

- `review = "github"` (reserved).
- Fork PRs.
- Git hosts other than GitHub.
- Windows.
- Running the build on the hook's copy.
- Token limits for the review.
- Starting the RED or GREEN sessions, or detecting which agent wrote a commit: the orchestrator starts them, `[roles] red` and `green` only record its choice (item 64). The lock is the mechanical guarantee.
- A separate intent file: the PR body is the intent.
- Shadow review, and running the review in parallel with CI.

## 11. Versioning

`shipcraft` follows semantic versioning. This contract is **2.79.0**. A change that breaks a config, a lock, a command or a refusal code raises the major version. The lock carries its own `format` version. A new lock format that a binary writes while still reading the previous one is a minor change, since no lock already written stops being valid (lock format 3, item 43, in 2.3.0). `shipcraft_min` lets a project require a minimum binary, and `init` writes the running binary's `major.minor`. Releases ship one archive per macOS target (`aarch64`, `x86_64`), each holding only the `shipcraft` binary, with a `SHA256SUMS` file. On other platforms, build from source with `cargo install --path nucleo`.
